他向琼伸出一只空手供她查验，随后朝空中轻轻一拢，像是捉住了什么飞掠之物。待他再度摊开手，掌心里已闪着一枚小小的钻石星饰。

===Original===
“Take it, Joan,” said Sir Clinton in his natural voice. “I meant to send it to you tomorrow; but at the last moment I thought I might as well bring it with me and have the pleasure of giving it to you myself. It’s your birthday present. I’m an old enough friend to give you diamonds on a special occasion like this.”

“You took me in completely,” Joan admitted, after she had thanked him. “I couldn’t make out who you were; and I thought you were the limit in insolence when you began talking about my private affairs.”

“It’s Michael Clifton, of course?” Sir Clinton asked.

===Chinese===
“拿着吧，琼，”克林顿爵士（Sir Clinton）用他本来的嗓音说道。“我本打算明天差人送去；可临了转念一想，不如干脆随身带来，亲手送你，也图个欢喜。这是你的生日礼物。论交情，我够得上老朋友了，遇上这样特别的日子，送你钻石是应当的。”

“您把我骗得滴水不漏，”琼道过谢后承认道。“我实在猜不出您是谁；您一开口谈论我的私事时，我只当您无礼到了极点。”

“是迈克尔·克利夫顿（Michael Clifton），没错吧？”克林顿爵士问道。

===Original===
“Why ‘of course’? One would think he’d been my last chance, by the way you put it. This living on a magic island has ruined your manners, my good Prospero.”

“Well, he won’t let you down, Joan. You﻿—shall I say, even you, to be tactful﻿—couldn’t have done better in the raffle.”

Before Joan could reply, a girl in Egyptian costume came past their chairs. Joan stopped her with a gesture.

===Chinese===
“凭什么‘没错’？听您这口气，人家还以为他是我最后的机会呢。在魔岛上住久了，把您的礼数都住坏了，我的好普洛斯彼罗。”

“放心，他不会委屈你的，琼。你——为了说得客气些，我该说，即便是你——就算去摸彩，也摸不着比这更好的彩头了。”

琼还没来得及答话，一个穿埃及装束的姑娘从他们座椅旁走过。琼打了个手势把她叫住。

===Original===
“Pin this pretty thing in the front of my band, please, Cleopatra. Be sure you get it in the right place.”

She held out the diamond star. Cleopatra took it without comment and fastened it in position hastily.

“Sit down,” Joan invited, “your next partner will find you here when he comes. Tell us about Caesar and Antony and all the rest of your disreputable past. Make it exciting.”

===Chinese===
“劳驾，把这漂亮玩意儿别在我发带正面，克娄巴特拉（Cleopatra）。千万要别对位置。”

她递出那枚钻石星饰。克娄巴特拉一声不吭地接过，匆匆别在了位置上。

“坐下吧，”琼相邀道，“你的下一任舞伴来了，自然会在这里找到你。给我们讲讲凯撒呀、安东尼呀，还有你那段声名狼藉的往事里其余的角儿。讲得精彩些。”

===Original===
Cleopatra shook her head.

“Sorry I can’t stop just now. Neither Julius nor Antony put in an appearance tonight, so I’m spending my arts on a mere centurion. He’s a stickler for punctuality﻿—being a Roman soldier.”

She glanced at her wristwatch.

===Chinese===
克娄巴特拉摇了摇头。

“抱歉，这会儿不能久留。尤利乌斯和安东尼今晚一个都没露面，我只好把本事实现在一个区区百夫长身上。他守时守得一丝不苟——毕竟是罗马军人嘛。”

她瞥了一眼腕表。

===Original===
“I must fly at once. O reservoir! as we say in Egypt, you know.”

With a nod of farewell, she hastened along the alley and out of the winter-garden.

“She seems a trifle nervous about something,” Sir Clinton commented, indifferently.

===Chinese===
“我得立刻飞走了。噢，水库！——你知道，我们埃及人就是这么说的。”

她颔首作别，匆匆沿着小径走去，出了温室花园。

“她像是有点儿心神不宁，”克林顿爵士心不在焉地评论了一句。

===Original===
Joan smoothed down her filmy tunic.

“Isn’t it time we moved?” she asked. “I see Falstaff’s gone away, so you can’t turn him into a white rabbit now; and there doesn’t seem to be anything else you could enchant just at present. The orchestra will be starting in a moment, anyhow.”

She rose as she spoke. Sir Clinton followed her example, and they made their way out of the winter-garden.

===Chinese===
琼抚平了身上薄纱似的舞衣。

“咱们是不是该挪动了？”她问道。“我看福斯塔夫已经走开了，这下您变不成白兔了；眼下场子里别的似乎也没什么可施法的了。反正乐队马上就要奏起来。”

她说着便站起身。克林顿爵士依样起身，两人一同走出温室花园。

===Original===
“What costume is Michael Clifton wearing tonight?” asked Sir Clinton as the orchestra played the opening bars of the dance. “I ought to congratulate him; and it’s easier to pick him up at a distance if I know how he’s dressed.”

“Look for something in eighteenth-century clothes and a large wig, then,” Joan directed. “He says he’s Macheath out of the *Beggar’s Opera*. I suppose he’s quite as like that as anything else. You’ll perhaps recognize him best by a large artificial mole at the left corner of his mouth. I observed it particularly myself.”

She noticed that her partner seemed more on the alert than the occasion required.

===Chinese===
“今晚迈克尔·克利夫顿穿的是什么行头？”乐队奏响舞曲开头几小节时，克林顿爵士问道。“我该去道个喜；知道他怎么打扮，隔着老远也好认出他。”

“那您就找穿十八世纪衣裳、戴大假发的那位，”琼指点道。“他说自己扮的是《乞丐歌剧》（*Beggar’s Opera*）里的麦基希（Macheath）。我估摸着他扮这个跟扮别的什么一样像。不过您多半最容易凭他嘴角左边那颗大假痣认出他来。我自己可是特意留神看过的。”

她注意到，她的舞伴似乎比这种场合所需要的更多着几分警觉。

===Original===
“What are you worried about?” she demanded. “You seem to be listening for something; and you can’t hear anything, you know, even if you tried, because of the orchestra.”

Sir Clinton shook off his air of preoccupation.

“The fact is, Joan, I’ve been worried all evening. I’m really afraid of something happening tonight. I don’t much like this mask business with all that stuff in the collections. I’ve a feeling in my bones that there might be trouble.”

===Chinese===
“您在操心什么？”她追问。“您像是在留神听什么动静；可您什么都听不见的，您知道，就算竖起耳朵也白搭——乐队的声音太响了。”

克林顿爵士抖落了一脸的心事重重。

“实话说，琼，我整晚都不安生。我真怕今晚出什么事。一屋子人蒙着脸，藏品里又尽是那些物件，我实在不喜欢这么办。我骨头缝里有一种感觉：只怕要出乱子。”

===Original===
Joan laughed at his gloomy premonitions.

“You won’t be kept on the rack much longer, that’s one good thing. There’s just this dance, then the march-past for judging the costumes, and then it will be midnight when everybody must unmask. So you’ll have to make the best of your fears in the next half-hour. After that there’ll be no excuse for them.”

“Meanwhile, on with the dance, eh?” said Sir Clinton. “I see it’s no use trying to give you a nightmare. You’re too poor a subject to repay the labour and trouble. Besides, this music’s terribly straining on the vocal cords if one tries to compete with it.”

===Chinese===
琼笑话他这阴郁的预感。

“您也受不了多久的吊心之苦了，这是件好事。只剩这一支舞，接着是评比服装的列队巡行，然后就到午夜，人人都得摘下面罩。所以接下来的半个钟头，您就和您的担忧将就着相处吧。过了那阵子，它们可就没理由再赖着了。”

“眼下嘛，还是接着跳舞，嗯？”克林顿爵士说。“我看，想让你做一场噩梦是白费劲。你这样的对象太不经吓，不值得费那个力气。再说，这音乐吵得吓人，谁想跟它比嗓子，声带可吃不消。”

===Original===
As he spoke, however, the orchestra reached a diminuendo in the score and sank to comparative quietness. Joan looked here and there about the room as they danced and at last detected the figure for which she was searching.

“That’s Michael over there,” she pointed out, “the one dancing with the girl dressed as﻿ …”

Across the sound of the music there cut the sharp report of a small-calibre pistol fired in some adjacent room. On the heels of it came the crash and tinkle of falling glass, and, almost simultaneously, a cry for help in a man’s voice.

===Chinese===
然而就在他说话的当口，乐队奏到乐谱上的渐弱处，沉入相对的安静。琼一面跳舞一面朝大厅四处张望，终于找到了她搜寻的那个身影。

“那边那个就是迈克尔，”她指给他看，“正跟一个扮成……的姑娘跳舞的那个。”

一声小口径手枪的锐响从隔壁某个房间穿透乐声传来。紧随其后的是玻璃坠落的碎裂与叮当，几乎同时，一个男人的声音高声呼救。

===Original===
Sir Clinton let Joan’s hand go and turned to the door; but before he could take a step, the lights above them vanished and the room was plunged in darkness. Joan felt a hand come out and grip her arm.

“That you, Joan?”

“Yes.”

===Chinese===
克林顿爵士放开琼的手，转向门口；可他还没迈出一步，头顶的灯骤然熄灭，整个大厅陷入一片漆黑。琼感到一只手伸来，紧紧抓住了她的手臂。

“是你吗，琼？”

“是我。”

===Original===
“They’ve taken out the main switch,” Sir Clinton said hurriedly. “Get hold of some man at once and show him where it is. We want the lights as quick as possible. I can trust you not to lose your head. Take a man with you for fear of trouble. We don’t know what’s happening.”

“Very well,” Joan assured him.

“Hurry!” Sir Clinton urged.

===Chinese===
“他们把总闸拉开了，”克林顿爵士急促地说。“你马上去拉住一位男宾，带他到总闸那儿去。我们要尽快把灯弄亮。我信得过你，你不会慌了神。带个男人同去，以防有变。眼下是什么情形，我们还不清楚。”

“好的，”琼应道。

“快去！”克林顿爵士催促道。

===Original===
His hand dropped from her arm as he moved invisibly away towards the door. In the darkness around her she could hear movements and startled exclamations. The orchestra, after mechanically playing a couple of bars, had fallen to silence. Someone blundered into her and passed on before she could put out her hand.

“Well, at least I know where the door is,” she assured herself; and she began to move towards it.

Meanwhile the cries for help continued to come from the museum. Then, abruptly, they were hushed; and she shuddered as she thought of what that cessation might mean. She moved forward and came to what seemed an unobstructed space on the floor, over which she was able to advance freely.

===Chinese===
他的手从她臂上松开，人影隐形般朝门口去了。四周的黑暗里，她听得见挪动声和惊惶的喊叫。乐队机械地奏完两小节，便归于沉寂。有人一头撞上她，还没等她伸手，那人已经走过去了。

“好在至少我知道门在哪儿，”她给自己壮胆；随即朝门口挪去。

与此同时，博物馆那边的呼救声仍在继续。随后，那声音陡然止息；想到这骤然的安静意味着什么，她不由得打了个寒噤。她继续向前，走到一片似乎没有遮拦的空地上，总算能畅行无阻了。

===Original===
Her whole senses were concentrated on reaching the exit; but her mind appeared to work independently of her own volition and to conjure up the possibilities behind this series of events. Sir Clinton had evidently expected some criminal attempt that night; and he had assumed that the museum would be the objective. But suppose he were wrong. Perhaps the affair in the museum was only a blind to draw towards it all the men outside the ballroom. Then, when they were disposed of, there might come an incursion here. Most of the women had taken advantage of their fancy dress to deck themselves out with jewellery, and a few armed men could easily reap a small fortune in a minute or two. Despite the soundness of her nerves, she began to feel anxious, and to conjure up still more appalling pictures.

Suddenly her eyes were dazzled by a flash of light as a man beside her struck a match. Almost at the same moment she felt a hand on her shoulder and she was pulled backwards so brusquely that she almost lost her balance and slipped.

“Put out that match, you fool!” said Michael’s voice. “Do you want to have these girls’ dresses in a blaze?”

===Chinese===
她全部的心神都集中在抵达出口上；可她的脑子却仿佛不听意志调遣，自顾自推演起这一连串变故背后的种种可能。克林顿爵士显然预料到今夜会有什么犯罪图谋；而且他认定目标就是博物馆。可倘若他错了呢。博物馆里的事也许只是调虎离山，为的是把舞厅外的男人统统引过去。等这批人被牵制住了，这里说不定就会闯进一伙人来。大多数女宾都借着化装舞服之便，浑身缀满珠宝；几条带枪的汉子不消一两分钟就能发上一笔小财。尽管她素来胆壮，也不由得焦虑起来，脑子里浮出一幅幅愈发骇人的图景。

突然，身旁一个男人划亮火柴，一道火光晃得她睁不开眼。几乎同一瞬间，她肩头落下一只手，猛地把她往后一拽，拽得她几乎站不住脚，险些滑倒。

“把火柴掐灭，你这蠢货！”是迈克尔的声音。“你想让这些姑娘的裙子烧起来吗？”

===Original===
The flare of the match had revealed a circle of startled faces. The room was filled with excited voices and a sound of confused movements. Over at the orchestra a music-stand fell with a clash of metal. Then, close beside her in the darkness, Joan heard a girl’s voice repeating monotonously in tones of acute fear: “What does it mean? Oh, what does it mean?”

“Much good *that* does anyone,” Joan muttered, contemptuously. Then, aloud, she called: “Michael!”

Before he could reply, there came a sharp exclamation in a man’s voice:

===Chinese===
火柴的光焰照出一圈惊惶的脸。大厅里满是激动的呼喊和乱糟糟的挪动声。乐队那边，一只谱架哐啷一声金属脆响倒在地上。接着，黑暗中就在琼的身旁，她听见一个姑娘的声音带着极度的惊恐，单调地反复念叨：“这是什么意思？哦，这是什么意思？”

“那样对谁也没半点用处，”琼轻蔑地低声咕哝。随即扬声喊道：“迈克尔！”

他还没来得及应声，一个男人厉声喊了起来：

===Original===
“Stand back, there! My partner’s fainted.”

The possibilities involved in a panic suddenly became all too clear in Joan’s mind. If half a dozen people lost their heads, the girl might be badly hurt.

Michael’s voice was lifted again, in a tone that would have carried through a storm at sea:

===Chinese===
“让开，都让开！我的舞伴晕倒了。”

一场恐慌会招致什么后果，骤然在琼心里变得再清楚不过。只要有半打人慌了神，那姑娘就可能受重伤。

迈克尔又扬起嗓门，那嗓音即便在海上风暴里也传得出去：

===Original===
“Everybody stand fast! You’ll be trampling the girl underfoot if you don’t take care. Stand still, confound you! Pull the blinds up and throw back the curtains. It’s a moonlight night.”

There was a rustling as those nearest the windows set about the execution of his orders. Light suddenly appeared, revealing the strained faces and uneasy attitudes of the company. Joan turned to Michael.

“Come with me and put in the switch, Michael. Sir Clinton’s gone to the museum. We must get the lights on quick.”

===Chinese===
“全体站定别动！不当心的话，你们要把这姑娘踩到脚底下了。站住，该死的！把百叶窗卷上去，窗帘都敞开。今晚是个月夜。”

离窗最近的人窸窸窣窣地动手照办。光亮蓦地出现，照出众人格外紧绷的脸和惶惶不安的姿态。琼转向迈克尔。

“跟我来合闸，迈克尔。克林顿爵士去博物馆了。我们必须赶紧把灯弄亮。”

===Original===
Michael, with a word to his partner, followed his fiancée towards the door. A thought seemed to strike him just as he was leaving the room:

“Wait here, everybody, till we get the lights on again. You’ll just run risks by moving about in the dark outside. It’s nothing. Probably only a fuse blown.”

“Now then, Joan, where’s that switch?” he added as they passed out of the door.

===Chinese===
迈克尔向自己的舞伴交代了一句，便跟着未婚妻朝门口走去。就在跨出大厅的当口，他似乎猛然想起一件事：

“各位都在这儿等着，等我们把灯重新弄亮。摸黑在外面走动，只会自讨风险。没事的。多半只是保险丝烧了。”

“好了，琼，闸在哪儿？”出门时他又补了一句。

===Original===
It was pitch-dark in the rest of the house; but Joan knew her way and was able to grope along the corridors without much difficulty. As they came near the switch-box, the lights flashed up again. One of the servants appeared round a corner.

“Someone had pulled out the switch, sir,” he explained. “It took me some time to make my way to it and put it in again.”

“Stout fellow!” said Michael, approvingly.

===Chinese===
宅子其余的地方黑得伸手不见五指；好在琼认得路，沿着走廊摸索前行并不太费事。他们快走到配电箱时，灯光又刷地亮了。一个仆人从拐角处现身。

“有人把闸拉开了，先生，”他解释道。“我费了好一阵才摸过去，把它重新合上。”

“好样的！”迈克尔赞许道。

===Original===
At that moment, a voice shouted above the confused noises of the house:

“Come on, you fellows! He’s got away. Lend hand to chase him.”

And a sound of running steps filled the hall, as the male guests poured out in answer to the summons.

===Chinese===
就在这时，一个声音盖过满宅子的嘈杂，高声喊道：

“来呀，各位！那家伙跑了。搭把手追他去。”

男宾们应声涌出，奔跑的脚步声顿时充满了门厅。

===Original===
“You don’t need me any longer, Joan?” Michael questioned. “Right! Then I’m off to lend a hand.”

He ran to join the rest.

Left alone, Joan retraced her steps to the ballroom; but instead of reentering it, she passed on in the direction of the museum, whither a number of the guests were making their way also.

===Chinese===
“这里不再需要我了吧，琼？”迈克尔问。“好！那我也去搭把手。”

他跑着去和大伙会合了。

只剩琼一人。她循原路折回舞厅，却没有进去，而是继续朝博物馆方向走去——不少宾客也正往那边去。

===Original===
“I hope nobody’s got badly hurt,” she thought to herself as she hurried along. “I do wish I’d taken the hint and not asked to have that collection thrown open tonight.”

Much to her relief, she found Sir Clinton sitting on a chair beside the museum door. In the doorway stood the keeper, looking none the worse and busying himself with fending off the more inquisitive among the guests who wished to enter the room. Joan noticed that the museum itself was in darkness though the lights were burning in the rest of the house.

“You’re not hurt, are you, Sir Clinton?” she asked as she came up to him.

===Chinese===
“但愿没有人受重伤，”她一路疾走，心里暗想。“真希望我当初听了那话，没有开口要求今晚把那些藏品开放。”

让她大感宽慰的是，克林顿爵士正坐在博物馆门旁的椅子上。门口站着管理员，看上去毫发无损，正忙着拦挡宾客中那些好奇心重、想往屋里钻的人。琼注意到，宅子其他地方灯火通明，博物馆本身却是一片漆黑。

“您没受伤吧，克林顿爵士？”她走近后问道。

===Original===
“Nothing to speak of. The fellow kicked me on the ankle as he came out. I’m temporarily lamed, that’s all. Nothing to worry about, I think.”

He rubbed his ankle as he spoke.

“Are you all right, Mold?” Joan inquired.

===Chinese===
“不值一提。那家伙冲出来时在我脚踝上踢了一脚。我暂时成了瘸子，仅此而已。我看没什么可担心的。”

他一边说，一边揉着脚踝。

“你没事吧，莫尔德（Mold）？”琼问道。

===Original===
The keeper reassured her.

“No harm done, Miss Joan. They didn’t hurt me. But I’m sorry, miss, I didn’t manage to get hold of them. They were on me before I could do anything, me being so taken aback by the lights going out.”

“What’s happened?” Joan questioned Sir Clinton. “Has anything been stolen?”

===Chinese===
管理员让她宽心。

“没伤着，琼小姐。他们没把我怎么样。只是对不住，小姐，我没能抓住他们。灯一灭我整个人都懵了，还没来得及动作，他们就扑到了我身上。”

“到底出了什么事？”琼问克林顿爵士。“有东西被偷了吗？”

===Original===
“We don’t know yet what’s gone,” he replied, answering her last question first. “The bulk of the lamp’s smashed in there”﻿—he nodded towards the museum﻿—“and until they bring a fresh one, we can’t find out what damage has been done. As to what happened, it seems rather confused at present; but I expect we shall get it cleared up eventually. There seems to have been a gang at work; and I’m afraid some things may be missing when we begin to look over the collection.”

“I wish I’d taken your hint,” Joan admitted, frankly. “It’s partly my blame, I feel, for neglecting your advice. I was silly to laugh at you when you spoke about it.”

“I shouldn’t worry about it, if I were you, Joan,” Sir Clinton reassured her. “It was really only one chance in a million that anything of the sort would happen tonight. Besides, if we manage to nail this fellow that they’re all after, we may be able to get some clue to his confederates. Quite evidently there was a gang at work, and he may be induced to split on his friends if we can lay hands on him; and then we’ll get the stuff back again without much trouble, I hope.”

===Chinese===
“丢了什么，眼下还不知道，”他答道，先回答了她后面那个问题。“里面那盏大灯已经砸碎了”——他朝博物馆那边点了点头——“换上新灯之前，查不清到底损失几何。至于经过嘛，目前看还相当乱；不过我想，最终总能理清的。看样子是一伙人作的案；只怕等我们清点藏品时，会发现有些东西已经不在了。”

“我真希望当初听了您的话，”琼坦诚地说。“没把您的劝告当回事，我想我也得担一部分责任。您说起这事的时候我还笑话您，真是傻。”

“我要是你，就不为这个发愁，琼，”克林顿爵士宽慰她。“今晚真出这种事，其实不过是万分之一的可能。再说，要是我们能抓住大家都在追的那家伙，说不定就能摸到他同伙的线索。明摆着是一伙人作案；只要我们能把他弄到手，兴许能撬开他的嘴，让他招出同伙；那样的话，东西也不难追回来——我希望如此。”

===Original===
He glanced at her, as though to see the effect of his words; then, as his eyes caught her mask, he seemed struck by another idea.

“That reminds me,” he said, “we must get these masks off. Send someone round at once, please, Joan, to order everyone to unmask now. And have all the outer doors shut, too. It’s a futile precaution, I’m afraid; because anyone could slip out during the confusion when there was no light: but we may as well do what we can even at this stage.”

He removed his own mask as he spoke, and pulled away the false beard which he had worn as Prospero. Joan loosened her mask and went off to give the necessary orders. In a few moments she returned.

===Chinese===
他瞥了她一眼，像是想看看这番话的效果；接着，目光落到她的面罩上，他似乎又想到了什么。

“这就提醒我了，”他说，“得把这些面罩都摘下来。琼，请你马上派人传话，叫大家现在就摘面罩。还有，把所有通外面的门也都关上。恐怕这是白费劲的防范；因为趁刚才没灯那阵乱，谁都可能溜出去了：不过事到如今，能做的我们还是做一做。”

他一边说，一边摘下自己的面罩，扯掉了扮普洛斯彼罗时贴的假胡子。琼解下面罩，去传必要的吩咐。不多时她就回来了。

===Original===
“Now tell me what did happen,” she demanded.

“There’s no one killed, or even hurt,” Sir Clinton assured her. “This ankle of mine’s the only casualty, so far as I know; and I expect I’ll be able to limp about quite comfortably by tomorrow.”

“I’m thankful it’s no worse,” said Joan, with relief.

===Chinese===
“现在给我讲讲，到底出了什么事，”她要求道。

“没有人丧命，连受伤的都没有，”克林顿爵士让她放心。“据我所知，唯一的伤情就是我这只脚踝；估计到明天，我就能相当自在地一瘸一拐走动了。”

“没有更糟，真是谢天谢地，”琼如释重负地说。

===Original===
“All I know about the business comes from Mold, here,” Sir Clinton went on. “It seems he was patrolling the museum at the time the thing happened, under your brother’s orders. Perhaps half a dozen people﻿—under a dozen, he says, at any rate﻿—were in the place then. Some of them were examining the cases in the bays; some of them were looking at the things in the big centre case. Mold doesn’t remember what costumes they were wearing. I don’t blame him. People had been passing in and out all through the evening; and there was no reason why he should take particular note of the guests at that special moment.”

Sir Clinton glanced up at the keeper, who was looking rather ashamed at his inability to furnish better information.

“Don’t you worry, Mold. I doubt if I’d have had any more to tell, myself, if I’d been there. One can’t be expected to remember everything.”

===Chinese===
“这件事的经过，我知道的都来自这位莫尔德，”克林顿爵士接着说。“出事的时候，他奉你哥哥的吩咐，正在博物馆里巡查。当时屋里约有半打人——他说，反正不到一打。有的在察看凹室里的展柜，有的在看中央大柜里的东西。莫尔德记不得他们穿的是什么戏服。这不怪他。整个晚上人进人出；他没有任何理由偏偏在那个当口特别留意宾客。”

克林顿爵士抬眼看了看管理员，后者正为自己提供不出更多情况而面露愧色。

“你别往心里去，莫尔德。换作我在场，只怕我也说不出更多的东西来。总不能指望谁把每件事都记住。”

===Original===
He turned back to Joan.

“The next thing that happened was a pistol-shot, and the light went out. Some light filtered in from the door of the room, for the lamps in the hall here were still blazing; but before Mold could do anything, someone gripped him from behind and got his wrists twisted behind his back. In the struggle Mold was swung round, so that he couldn’t see the central case even in what light there was. Then the lights outside were switched off and he heard a smashing of glass. There was a bit of a struggle, apparently; and then all at once he felt himself let loose. As soon as he got free, he lit a match and posted himself at the door to prevent anyone getting away; and he stayed there until the lights went on again. Then he made all his prisoners unmask and those whom he didn’t recognize himself he kept there until someone he knew came to identify them. They’re all people you know quite well, Joan. More than half of them were girls, who seem rather unlikely people to go in for robbery with violence, to put it mildly. Mold made a list of them, if we happen to need it. But I don’t think we’re likely to find the criminal amongst them. This affair was too well planned for that. The real gang have got clean away, I’m pretty sure.”

“And what about your ankle?” demanded Joan.

===Chinese===
他转回琼这边。

“接下来发生的是一声枪响，跟着灯就灭了。门厅这边的灯还亮着，屋里多少透进些光来；可莫尔德还没来得及有所动作，就有人从背后扼住他，把他的双腕反扭到背后。扭打当中，莫尔德被抡得转了个身，于是哪怕就着那点光，他也看不见中央展柜了。随后外面的灯也给关了，他听见玻璃砸碎的声音。看样子还搏斗了一小阵；然后，他忽然觉得自己被松开了。他一脱身，就划亮一根火柴，守住门口不让任何人跑掉；一直守到灯重新亮起。接着，他让扣下的这些人统统摘下面罩，其中有他自己认不出的，就扣在那里，等他熟识的人来指认。这些人你都相当熟，琼。他们过半数是姑娘——说得客气些，实在不像会去犯暴力抢劫案的人。莫尔德把他们记了一张单子，万一我们用得上。不过我看，罪犯多半不在他们中间。这案子干得太周密，不会留这种破绽。真正的匪伙已经逃得干干净净，这一点我相当肯定。”

“那您的脚踝又是怎么回事？”琼追问道。

===Original===
“Oh, that? I happened to arrive at the door fairly quickly after the lights went out. Just as I got to it, a fellow came dashing out; and I made a grab at him as well as I could in the dark. But one can’t see what one’s doing; and I didn’t get a decent grip on him as he charged out on top of me. He landed me a fairly effective kick﻿—right on the anklebone, by bad luck﻿—and then, before I could get my hands on him properly he tore himself clear and was off down the hall towards the front door. I hobbled after him as best I could; and there he was﻿—a fellow dressed in Pierrot costume﻿—running quite leisurely over the gravel sweep and making for the woods. I couldn’t go after him; but he was quite clear in the moonlight and he’d a long way to go before getting into cover; so I raised a hue and cry at once, and quite a crowd of stout fellows are after him. He’ll have to run a bit faster than he was doing, if he expects to get off. These pinewoods have no undergrowth to speak of; and he’ll find it difficult to conceal himself in a hurry.”

As Sir Clinton ended his narrative a servant came hurrying up the hall, bringing a tall pair of steps with him.

“Is that the new lamp?” Sir Clinton demanded. “All right. Light a match or two, Mold, to let him see where to put the steps. And don’t tramp about too much while you’re fixing them up, please. I want to see things undisturbed as far as possible.”

===Chinese===
“哦，那个呀？灯灭之后，我算到得比较快，赶到了门口。我刚到那儿，就有一个家伙夺路冲出来；我趁着黑暗，尽力朝他抓了一把。可是黑灯瞎火的，谁也看不见自己在干什么；他迎面撞将出来，我没能结结实实抓住他。他回敬了我相当见效的一脚——偏偏踹在踝骨上——随后不等我再度上手，他已挣脱身子，冲下门厅直奔前门去了。我竭尽全力一瘸一拐地追出去；只见他——一个穿皮埃罗（Pierrot）戏服的家伙——正不慌不忙地跑过砂石车道，朝树林而去。我追不动了；可月光下他的身影清清楚楚，离可以藏身的地方还远着呢；于是我立刻大喊捉人，一帮精壮的汉子已经追出去了。他要想脱身，跑得就得比方才快上几分。这片松林里几乎没有什么下层灌木；他一时半会儿想藏住自己，可没那么容易。”

克林顿爵士刚讲完，一个仆人就匆匆穿过门厅走来，还扛着一副高梯子。

“新灯拿来了？”克林顿爵士问道。“好。莫尔德，划一两根火柴，让他看清楚梯子该架在哪儿。还有，架梯子的时候，请别把现场踩得太乱。我要一切都尽量保持原样。”

## IV The Chase in the Woods / 第四章 林中追捕

===Original===
In earlier days, Michael Clifton had been reckoned among the more creditable runners in the School Mile; and he had never allowed himself to fall out of training. Thus as he joined the throng of would-be pursuers emerging from the house, he felt a certain confidence that the fugitive would at any rate have to put his best foot foremost if he was to avoid being run down. Before he had covered twenty yards, however, Michael found himself handicapped by his costume. The full-bottomed wig dropped off almost immediately, and the shoes were not so troublesome as he had feared; but the sleeves of his coat interfered with his movements, and the long skirts hampered his legs.

===Chinese===
早年间，迈克尔·克利夫顿（Michael Clifton）在学校一英里赛跑里还算得上有数的好手；此后他也从不肯让身子荒疏下来。因此，当他汇入从宅子里涌出的追捕人潮时，心里颇有几分底气：那逃犯要想不被追上，无论如何都得拿出拼命的脚力才行。然而没跑出二十码，迈克尔就发觉自己吃了戏服的亏。那顶蓬蓬的全罩假发几乎立刻就掉了下去，鞋子倒不像他担心的那么碍事；可上衣的袖子碍手碍脚，长长的下摆又拖累双腿。

===Original===
“I wonder if these coves in the eighteenth century ever ran a step,” he grumbled. “If they did it in this kit, they must have been wonders. I must get rid of the truck.”

===Chinese===
“真不知道十八世纪那帮家伙有没有跑过一步路，”他嘟囔道。“要是穿着这身行头还能跑，那他们准是些奇人了。这堆劳什子得赶紧脱掉。”

===Original===
He pulled up and stripped off the full-skirted coat; then, as an afterthought, he removed the long waistcoat as well. While doing this, he glanced ahead to see how the chase was progressing. The light of the full moon, now at its highest in the cloudless heavens, lit up the whole landscape before him almost as clearly as daylight. Far ahead, he could see the white figure of the escaping thief as it ascended the long, gentle slope towards the pinewoods.

“I wonder what tempted the beggar to choose that particular costume on a night like this,” Michael speculated. “It’s the most conspicuous affair he could have put on. Well, all the better for us.”

===Chinese===
他收住脚步，脱掉那件宽摆上衣；转念一想，又把长马甲也扒了下来。忙这些的时候，他抬眼看了看前面追捕的进展。一轮满月正高悬在万里无云的夜空，把眼前的整片景致照得几乎同白昼一样分明。远远的前方，他望见那窃贼逃跑的白色身影，正沿着长长的缓坡向松林爬去。

“真不明白这家伙哪根筋搭错了，偏挑这么个晚上穿这身行头，”迈克尔思忖道。“这简直是他所能挑的最扎眼的一身了。也好，对我们反倒更有利。”

===Original===
The quarry had evidently secured a fair start, for the nearest group of pursuers was still a considerable distance behind him. The hunters were strung out in an irregular file, knotted here and there with groups of three or four runners; and the line extended back almost to Michael’s position. Behind him, he could hear fresh reinforcements emerging from the house, shouting as they came.

“They’d better save their breath,” Michael commented critically to himself. “That long rise’ll take it out of a good many of them.”

===Chinese===
那逃犯显然抢得了先机，因为离他最近的一拨追兵也还远远落在后面。追的人群拉成一条不规则的散列，这里一簇那里一簇地三五成群；这条长队几乎一直延伸到迈克尔所在的位置。在他身后，还能听见新加入的生力军从宅子里涌出来，一路嚷嚷着。

“他们还是省着点力气好，”迈克尔挑剔地自语道。“那段长坡就够一大半人受的。”

===Original===
He settled down to his favourite stride; and very soon began to overtake the laggards at the tail of the chase. In front of him he saw a Cardinal Richelieu with kilted cassock; but the Cardinal found his costume too much for him and pulled out of the race as Michael passed him. Shortly after, Michael drew level with an early nineteenth-century dandy and for a few seconds they raced neck and neck. The dandy, however, was unable to stay the pace.

“It’s these damned Johnny Walker boots,” he gasped, as he fell behind.

===Chinese===
他稳住步子，迈开了自己惯用的大步；不多时便开始赶上落在队尾的迟缓者。他前面是一个把法衣下摆掖起来的黎塞留红衣主教（Cardinal Richelieu）；可这位主教实在吃不消自己那身行头，迈克尔超过他时，他便退出了竞逐。稍后，迈克尔又与一个十九世纪早期的花花公子并了肩，两人齐头并进地赛了几秒钟。然而那公子哥儿撑不住这个配速。

“都怪这该死的约翰尼·沃克靴子，”他落在后面时喘着气说。

===Original===
Michael, running comfortably, began to take a faint amusement in the misfortunes of his colleagues. He could not help smiling as he passed a Minotaur, sitting beside the track and making furious efforts to disentangle himself from his pasteboard bull’s head which seemed to have become clamped in position. But as he found two more of the hunters by the wayside, a fresh point of view occurred to him.

“If they’re going to drop out at this rate, there won’t be many of us left at the finish to tackle the beggar; and he’s armed. We’ll need all the men we can scrape up, if we’re to make sure of him.”

===Chinese===
迈克尔跑得从从容容，渐渐从同伙们的狼狈里品出几分乐趣来。路过一个弥诺陶洛斯（Minotaur）时，他忍不住笑了——那家伙坐在道旁，正拼命想把脑袋从纸板牛头里退出来，那牛头像是卡得死死的。可等他又见两个追兵撂在路边，心里便转出一个新的念头。

“照这个掉队的速度，到终点还能剩下几个去对付那家伙可难说；何况他身上有枪。要想十拿九稳地拿下他，咱们能搜罗多少人手就得搜罗多少。”

===Original===
Glancing ahead again, he was relieved to see that he had gained a fair amount of ground on the fugitive; and now he began to pass runner after runner, as the rising slope told on the weaker pursuers. He reached the group at the head of the chase just as the escaping burglar dashed into the shadow of the woods a hundred yards in advance.

“He’ll dodge us now, if he can,” Michael warned his companions, who evidently were unacquainted with the ground. “Keep your eyes on him at any cost.”

===Chinese===
再往前一望，他欣慰地发现自己已从那逃犯身上追回了不小的一段距离；上坡路开始拖垮后段的弱者，他便一个接一个地超人。待他赶到追捕队伍的最前头时，那逃跑的窃贼恰好窜进百码开外的林影里。

“现在他要是有办法，就该跟咱们兜圈子了，”迈克尔提醒同伴们说——他们显然不熟悉这里的地形。“无论如何盯紧了他。”

===Original===
But as they entered the pine arcades, Michael found that he was mistaken. The quarry maintained his lead; but he made no effort to leave the beaten track. Ahead of them they could see his white-clad figure dappled with light and darkness as he sped up the broad pathway.

Suddenly, Michael remembered what lay beyond the pinewood. Without raising his voice, for fear the runner in front should hear him, he explained the situation.

“He doesn’t know what he’s running into. There’s a big quarry up there, with barbed wire fences on each side. If we can keep him straight for it, we’ll have him pinned.”

===Chinese===
可等进了松林的拱廊，迈克尔发现自己想岔了。那逃犯依旧领先；却丝毫没有离开正路的意思。他们能望见他那一身白影，在宽阔的林道上疾驰，明暗的光影在他身上斑斑驳驳地闪动。

忽然，迈克尔记起了松林那头是什么地方。他压低了嗓门——生怕前面那奔逃的家伙听见——把情况讲了个明白。

“他不知道自己正往什么地方跑。上面有座大采石场，两侧都围着铁丝网。只要能让他照直跑下去，咱们就能把他堵死在那儿。”

===Original===
On went the fugitive, still maintaining his lead and glancing over his shoulder from time to time, as though he were gauging the distance which separated him from his closest pursuers.

“The beggar can run, certainly,” Michael admitted to himself. “But running isn’t going to help him much in a minute or two. We have him on toast.”

===Chinese===
那逃犯继续奔逃，仍旧领先，还不时回头张望，像是在掂量自己同追得最近的人之间还隔着多远。

“这家伙确实能跑，”迈克尔心里承认。“可再过一两分钟，能跑也帮不了他多少忙了。他已是瓮中之鳖。”

===Original===
In a few moments the moon shone bright through the trees ahead. As they reached the edge of the wood, the white figure in front of them showed up clearly as it sprinted across the strip of open ground, straight for the spinney which bounded the quarry cliff. With a gesture, Michael called his motley group to a halt.

“Wait a minute,” he ordered. “You, Mephistopheles, get off to the left there, outside the spinney. Go on until you strike barbed wire. Take this Prehistoric Man—oh, it’s you, is it, Frankie? Well, both of you get down there and act as stoppers, so that he can’t sneak off along the fence. Oliver Cromwell and you in the funny coat! You’re to do the same over yonder on the right. Put some hurry into it, now! And don’t move in towards him till you get the word. The rest of you, extend a bit along the near edge of the spinney. Not too close; give yourselves a chance of spotting him if he breaks cover. And don’t yell unless you actually see him. We’ve got him shut in now, and we can afford to wait for reinforcements. Here they come!”

===Chinese===
不多时，前方的树隙间月色大亮。他们赶到林缘时，前面那道白影看得清清楚楚：它正冲过一条开阔的空地，直奔那片紧挨着采石场崖壁的小树林。迈克尔一抬手，叫这支五花八门的队伍停下。

“先等一下，”他下令道。“你，梅菲斯托费勒斯（Mephistopheles），到左边去，绕到小树林外面。一直走到碰上铁丝网为止。带上这位史前人（Prehistoric Man）——哦，原来是你呀，弗兰基（Frankie）？好吧，你们俩下到那边去把口子堵住，别让他顺着围栏溜走。奥利弗·克伦威尔（Oliver Cromwell），还有穿那件滑稽外套的！你们到那头右边去，也一样。抓紧点儿，现在就去！没听到号令，不许朝他逼近。其余的人，沿着小树林近侧的边缘稍微散开。别贴得太近；他一旦窜出来，得留出能望见他的余地。还有，不真真切切看见他，不许嚷嚷。现在他已经叫咱们关起来了，等增援也等得起。喏，这不就来了！”

===Original===
Two panting runners breasted the hill as he spoke. At this moment there came from beyond the spinney the sound of a splash. Michael was taken aback.

“The beggar can’t have dived over, surely. It’s full of rocks down below. We’ll have to hurry up. He might get away, after all, if he’s extra lucky.”

===Chinese===
正说话间，两个气喘吁吁的跑者翻上了坡顶。就在这时，小树林那边传来一声落水响。迈克尔愣住了。

“那家伙总不见得跳下去了吧。下面全是乱石。咱们得赶紧。他要是运气格外好，说不定还真能跑掉。”

===Original===
A fresh group of pursuers gave him the reinforcements he needed; and he fed them into his cordon at its weak points.

“Pass the word for the whole line to close in!”

The cordon began to contract around the spinney, the wide gaps in it closing up as it advanced.

===Chinese===
又来了一拨追兵，正好补上他需要的人手；他把他们填进包围圈的薄弱处。

“传话下去，全线收拢！”

包围圈围着小树林开始收缩，随着推进，圈上的大缺口渐渐合拢。

===Original===
“The beggar’s probably got a pistol; look out for yourselves among the trees,” Michael cautioned them as they reached the boundary of the plantation. “Don’t hurry. And keep in touch, whatever you do.”

He himself was at the centre of the line and was the first to enter the tiny wood. The advance was slow; for here there was some undergrowth which might offer a hiding-place to the fugitive; and this was carefully scrutinized, clump by clump, before the line moved forward as a whole. Michael meant to make certain of capturing the burglar; and he could afford now to go about the matter deliberately. Fresh reinforcements in twos and threes were still streaming in from the pinewood.

===Chinese===
“那家伙多半带着枪；进林子后都留神着点自己，”队伍到达林子边界时，迈克尔告诫众人。“别急。还有，千万保持彼此联络。”

他自己处在线列中央，头一个进了这片小林子。推进得很慢；因为这里有些下层灌木，可能给逃犯藏身；每挪一步之前，都得一丛一丛地仔细搜过，整条线才能齐进。迈克尔打定了主意要稳稳拿住这窃贼；如今尽好整以暇、从容行事。松林那边，增援的人还在三三两两源源不断地涌来。

===Original===
It took only a few minutes, however, to draw his screen through the spinney; for the belt of trees was a narrow one. Every instant he expected to hear a shout indicating that the quarry had been run to earth; but none came. His line emerged intact from the trees, forming an arc of which the cliff-face was the chord; and as his men came out into the moonlight Michael had to admit to himself that no one could well have crept through any gap in the cordon.

“He must be out here, hiding among these seats,” he shouted. “Don’t break your line any more than you can help. Advance to that balustrade in front. Rush him, if he shows up.”

===Chinese===
不过几分钟工夫，他这张搜网便拉过了小树林；因为这带林子本就窄。他时时刻刻都在等着那一声宣告猎物被逼进死角的呼喊；可始终没有。他的队列完完整整地出了树林，拉成一道弧线，崖壁正是这弧的弦；看着手下的人走进月光里，迈克尔不得不对自己承认：谁也不可能从包围圈的哪个空隙里爬过去。

“他准是在外头，藏在这些座位中间，”他扬声喊道。“队形能不散就不散。朝前面那道石栏推进。他一露头，就扑上去。”

===Original===
Now that he was sure of his quarry, Michael at last had leisure to note the tincture of the bizarre in the scene before him. The high-riding moon whitened the terrace and touched with glamour the motley costumes of the hunters preparing for their final swoop. Here Robin Hood and a hatless Flying Dutchman were stooping to peer below one of the marble seats. Farther along the line Lohengrin and a Milkman discussed something eagerly in whispers. On the left the Prehistoric Man loomed up like a Troglodyte emerging from his cave; while beyond him Mephistopheles leaned upon the railing, scanning the water below. From the inky shadow of the spinney Felix the Cat stole softly out to join the cordon.

“A weird-looking gang we are,” Michael commented to himself as he gazed about him.

===Chinese===
既然猎物已十拿九稳，迈克尔总算有闲心留意眼前这一幕里那几分古怪的意趣了。高悬的明月把露台照得一片银白，又给这些准备最后一击的猎手们五花八门的行头镀上一层魔魅的光彩。这边，罗宾汉（Robin Hood）和一个光着脑袋的飞翔的荷兰人（Flying Dutchman）正弯腰往一张大理石座底下张望。队列再过去些，罗恩格林（Lohengrin）和一位送奶工凑在一处急切地低声商量着什么。左边，那位史前人的身影赫然耸起，活像刚从洞里钻出来的穴居人；在他更远处，梅菲斯托费勒斯倚着栏杆，俯瞰下面的水面。菲力猫（Felix the Cat）从小树林墨黑的阴影里悄没声地溜出来，加入了包围圈。

“咱们这一伙，模样可真够古怪的，”迈克尔环顾四周，自语道。

===Original===
Only a few steps separated the hunters from the clear floor of the terrace. In a second or two at most, the man they were chasing must break cover and make a dash for liberty or else tamely surrender. Slowly the line crept forward.

“We’ve got him now!” a voice cried, exultantly.

But the living net swept on past the marble tier without catching anything in its meshes. Between it and the balustrade was nothing but the untenanted paving of the terrace.

===Chinese===
猎手们离露台空旷的地面只剩几步了。至多再过一两秒，他们追的那人就必定要现出身来，或是夺路拼死一搏，或是乖乖就擒。队列缓缓地向前挪动。

“这回他跑不了了！”一个声音得意地喊道。

可这张活网扫过大理石座那一排，网眼里却什么也没兜住。它同石栏之间，只剩下露台上空无一人的石面。

===Original===
“He’s got away!” ejaculated someone in tones of complete amazement. “Well, I’m damned if I see how he managed it.”

The chain broke up into individuals, who hurried hither and thither on the esplanade searching even in the most unlikely spots for the missing fugitive. All at once Michael’s eye caught something which had been concealed in the shadows thrown by the moon.

“Here’s a rope, you fellows! He’s gone down the face of the cliff. Swum the lake, probably.”

===Chinese===
“他跑掉了！”有人惊愕万分地脱口而出。“得，他到底是怎么得手的，我算是看不出来了。”

长链散作了一个个单独的人，在平台上跑来跑去，连最不可能的地方也搜遍了那失踪的逃犯。忽然间，迈克尔的目光触到了一样藏在月影里的东西。

“这儿有根绳子，伙计们！他从崖壁下去了。多半是游过湖去了。”

===Original===
Mephistopheles dissented in a languid drawl.

“Not he, Clifton. I’ve had my eye on the water ever since I got up to the barbed wire. You could spot the faintest ripple in this moonshine. He didn’t get off that way.”

“Sure of that?” demanded Michael.

“Dead sure. I watched specially.”

===Chinese===
梅菲斯托费勒斯懒洋洋地拖长了调子表示异议。

“他才没有呢，克利夫顿。打我到铁丝网那儿起，我就一直盯着水面。这月光底下，一丝最细的波纹也逃不过眼睛。他没走那条道。”

“这话有把握？”迈克尔追问。

“十成把握。我特意盯着的。”

===Original===
Michael hesitated for a moment or two, considering the situation. Then his face cleared.

“I see it! I remember there’s a cave right below here, in the cliff-face. He’s gone to ground there. Half of you get through the barbed wire on the right; the rest take the left side. Line up on the banks when you get down to the water. He may swim for it yet if we don’t hurry.”

===Chinese===
迈克尔迟疑了片刻，琢磨着眼前的局面。随即他脸上豁然开朗。

“我明白了！我记得正下方崖壁里有个山洞。他钻进洞藏起来了。你们一半人从右边钻过铁丝网；其余的走左边。下到水边就沿岸排开。咱们动作不快的话，他说不定还要泅水逃走。”

===Original===
They raced off to carry out his instructions, while Michael pulled up the rope and flung it on the terrace.

“That cuts off his escape in this direction,” he said to himself. “Now we can dig him out at leisure.”

Without hurrying, he made his way down to the water.

===Chinese===
众人飞奔而去执行他的调度，迈克尔则把绳子拽上来，扔在露台上。

“这就断了他这个方向的逃路，”他自语道。“现在咱们可以从容不迫地把他掏出来了。”

他不慌不忙地朝水边走去。

===Original===
“There used to be a raft of sorts here,” he explained. “If we can rout it out, we’ll be able to ferry across to the cave-mouth without much bother. I doubt if he’ll show fight once we lay our hands on him; for he hasn’t an earthly chance of getting away.”

He poked about among the sedge on the rim of the lakelet and at last discovered the decrepit raft.

“This thing’ll just bear two of us. Do we dig the beggar out or starve him out? Dig him out, eh? Well, I want someone to go with me. Here, you, Frankie”—he turned to the Prehistoric Man—“you’d better come along. If it comes to a ducking, you’ve got fewer clothes to spoil than the rest of us.”

===Chinese===
“这儿原先有只凑合能用的木筏，”他解释道。“要是能把它翻出来，咱们就能不费什么劲渡到洞口去。等我们真把手搭上他，我看他未必还敢抵抗；反正他是半点逃走的指望也没有了。”

他在小湖边沿的莎草丛里拨弄了一阵，总算找出了那只破旧的木筏。

“这玩意儿勉强载得动咱们两个。是把他掏出来，还是把他困死在里头？掏出来，对吧？好，我得有个人跟我走。喂，你，弗兰基”——他转向那位史前人——“你最好来一个。真要落水的话，你比我们谁都少几件衣裳可以糟蹋。”

===Original===
Nothing loath, the Prehistoric Man scrambled aboard the raft, which sank ominously under the extra weight.

“I can’t find anything to pole with,” grumbled Michael. “Paddle with your flippers, Frankie. It’s the only thing to do. Get busy with it.”

===Chinese===
史前人正中下怀，手脚并用地爬上木筏，筏子在添了这点分量后往下沉了沉，颇为不祥。

“找不着撑篙的家伙什，”迈克尔抱怨道。“用你的脚蹼划水吧，弗兰基。只有这个办法了。使劲划。”

===Original===
Under this primitive method of propulsion, the progress of the raft was slow; but at last they succeeded in bringing it under the cliff-face, after which they were able to work it along by hand. Gradually they manoeuvred it into position in front of the cave-mouth, which stood only a yard or so above water-level. Michael leaned forward to the entrance.

“You may as well come out quietly,” he warned the inmate. “It’s no good trying to put up a fight. You haven’t a dog’s chance.”

===Chinese===
靠了这原始的推进法子，木筏走得缓慢；但他们终于把它弄到了崖壁底下，之后便能用手推着它沿岸挪动。他们一点一点地把筏子摆到洞口前面；洞口离水面只有一码上下。迈克尔朝洞口俯过身去。

“你还是安安静静出来得好，”他警告洞里那位。“想顽抗是没有用的。你连半分胜算都没有。”

===Original===
There was no reply of any sort.

“Hold the damned raft steady, Frankie! You nearly had me overboard,” expostulated Michael. “I’m going to light a match. The cave’s as black as the pit, and I can see nothing.”

He pulled a silver matchbox from his trousers pocket.

“Lucky I hadn’t this in my coat; for you don’t look as if you had a pocket of any sort on you, Frankie.”

===Chinese===
没有任何回音。

“把那该死的筏子扶稳了，弗兰基！差点把我晃下水去，”迈克尔抗议道。“我要划根火柴。洞里黑得像深渊，我什么都看不见。”

他从裤子口袋里掏出一只银火柴盒。

“幸亏这玩意儿没放在上衣里；瞧你这模样，身上怕是连一个口袋都没有吧，弗兰基。”

===Original===
The first match, damped by the moisture on his hands, sputtered and died out.

“*Hurry* up, Guvnor,” shouted Mephistopheles, cheerfully, from the bank. “Don’t keep us up all night with your firework display. It’s getting a bit chilly, paddling about amongst this sedge. Not at all the temperature I’m accustomed to at home.”

===Chinese===
第一根火柴叫手上的潮气洇了，噼啪一响便灭了。

“*快*着点儿吧，头儿，”梅菲斯托费勒斯在岸上兴冲冲地喊道。“别让你这场焰火表演耗掉我们一整夜。在这莎草丛里蹚来蹚去，身上有点冷了。这跟我老家习惯的温度，可完全不是一回事。”

===Original===
Michael felt for another match and lighted it successfully. Standing up on the raft, he held the light above his head and peered into the cavity in the rock. The Prehistoric Man heard him exclaim in amazement.

“Damnation, Frankie! He’s not here! It’s hardly a cave at all.”

===Chinese===
迈克尔又摸出一根火柴，顺利划着了。他站上木筏，把火举过头顶，朝岩壁上那个洞窟里张望。史前人听见他惊呼出声。

“见鬼，弗兰基！他不在里头！这压根算不上什么山洞。”

===Original===
He put his hands on the cave floor.

“Hold tight with the raft. I’m going in to make sure.”

He scrambled up into the hollow; but almost immediately his face appeared again in the moonlight.

“Nothing here. The hole’s barely big enough to take me in.”

===Chinese===
他双手撑上洞底。

“把筏子抓牢了。我进去看个究竟。”

他手脚并用地爬进那凹洞；可几乎立刻，他的脸又出现在月光里。

“这儿什么都没有。这洞勉强才塞得下我一个人。”

===Original===
“Then where’s he gone?” demanded the Prehistoric Man, who was a creature of few words.

“I dunno! Must have given us the slip somehow. If he isn’t here, he must be somewhere else. No getting round that.”

He shouted the news to the watchers on the banks; and a confused sound of argument rose from amongst the sedge.

“Not much use hanging round the old home, Frankie. Pull for shore, sailor. We’d best manhandle her along the face of the cliff. I’ve had enough of that paddling.”

===Chinese===
“那他去哪儿了？”史前人质问道——他是个话不多的人。

“我哪儿知道！反正不知怎么叫咱们落了空。他要是不在这儿，就准在别处。这个赖不掉。”

他把这消息朝岸上守望的人喊了出去；莎草丛里顿时腾起一片乱糟糟的争论声。

“守着这老窝也没什么用处了，弗兰基。水手，朝岸边划吧。咱们最好用手推着筏子沿崖壁走。那脚蹼划水我是受够了。”

===Original===
When they touched firm ground again they were surrounded by their friends, most of whom seemed to doubt whether the search of the cave had been properly carried out.

“I tell you,” declaimed the exasperated Michael, “I got right into the damned hole! It’s so small that I nearly broke my nose against the back wall as I heaved myself inside. It would have been a tight fit for me and a squirrel together. He’s not there, whether you like it or not. … I can’t help your troubles, Tommy; you can go and look for yourself, if you like the job of lying on your tummy on a raft that’s awash. I shan’t interfere with your simple pleasures.”

===Chinese===
等他们再踏上坚实的地面，就被伙伴们围住了；其中大多数人似乎都在怀疑，那山洞到底搜得妥不妥当。

“我告诉你们，”被惹恼的迈克尔高声道，“我整个人钻进那破洞里去了！那洞小得很，我往里拱的时候，鼻子差点撞上后壁。我和一只松鼠挤在一处都嫌转不开身。他不在那儿，你们爱信不信。……汤米（Tommy），你的难处我帮不上；你要是喜欢趴在一只吃水的木筏上，自管去看。你这点小小乐趣，我不搅扰。”

===Original===
“But …”

“We’ve lost him. Is that plain enough? There’s nothing to be done but go home again with our tails between our legs. I’m going now.”

He accompanied his friends to the top of the cliff again; but when he reached the terrace a fresh thought struck him, and he loitered behind while the others, soaked and disconsolate, made their way down into the pinewood. When the last of them had disappeared, Michael retraced his steps to the edge of the cliff.

===Chinese===
“可是……”

“咱们把他丢了。这话够明白了吧？除了夹着尾巴回家，再没别的可干。我这就走。”

他随伙伴们重新上了崖顶；可一到露台，心头又掠过一个新念头，他便落在后面磨蹭，任凭旁人一个个浑身湿透、垂头丧气地下坡进了松林。等最后一个人的身影消失，迈克尔又折回崖边。

===Original===
“He reached here all right,” he assured himself. “And he didn’t break back through the cordon.”

He stooped down, picked up the rope, and refastened it round one of the pillars of the balustrade.

“Everyone knows there are secret passages about Ravensthorpe,” he mused. “Perhaps this beggar has got on to one of them. And quite possibly the end of the passage is in that cave down there. That would explain the rope. I’ll slide down and have another look round.”

===Chinese===
“他确确实实是到了这儿的，”他给自己吃准。“也没有倒穿回去冲出包围圈。”

他俯下身，拾起绳子，重新把它拴在石栏的一根柱子上。

“谁都知道雷文索普（Ravensthorpe）这一带有些密道，”他沉吟道。“说不定这家伙撞上了其中一条。那条密道的尽头，也完全可能就在下面那个洞里。那样绳子就说得通了。我滑下去再仔细看看。”

===Original===
He got into the cave-mouth without difficulty and used up the remainder of his matches in a close examination of the interior of the cavity; but even the closest scrutiny failed to reveal anything to his eyes.

“Nothing there but plain rock, so far as I can see,” he had to admit to himself as the last match burned out. “That’s a blank end in more senses than one.”

Without much difficulty he swarmed up the rope again, untied it from the balustrade, and coiled it over his arm.

===Chinese===
他毫无困难地进到洞口，把剩下的火柴全用上，将洞窟内部细细察看了一遍；可即便这样细看，也没能让他的眼睛发现任何东西。

“依我看，那里头除了光秃秃的岩石什么都没有，”最后一根火柴烧尽时，他不得不对自己承认。“这是个死胡同——往不止一层意思上说都是。”

他不甚费劲地又攀绳而上，把绳子从石栏上解下来，盘在小臂上。

===Original===
“A nice little clue for Sir Clinton Driffield to puzzle over,” he assured himself. “Sherlock Holmes would have been on to it at once; found where it was sold in no time; discovered who bought it before five minutes had passed; and paralysed Watson with the whole story that same evening over a pipeful of shag. We shall see.”

He threw a last glance round the empty terrace and then moved off into the spinney. As he passed into the shadow of the trees he saw, a few yards to one side, the outline of the Fairy House dappled in the moonshine which filtered through the leaves overhead. Half-unconsciously, Michael halted and looked at the little building.

===Chinese===
“给克林顿·德里菲尔德爵士（Sir Clinton Driffield）伤伤脑筋，倒是个不错的小线索，”他自得其乐地想道。“换了夏洛克·福尔摩斯（Sherlock Holmes），早一眼盯上它了；转眼就查出它是在哪儿卖的；不出五分钟就摸清是谁买的；当天晚上就能就着一斗粗烟丝，把这整桩事讲得华生（Watson）目瞪口呆。咱们走着瞧。”

他朝空荡荡的露台投去最后一瞥，随即走进小树林。跨进树影时，他看见几步开外的一旁，仙女屋（Fairy House）的轮廓映在从头顶叶隙间筛落的月光里，斑斑驳驳。迈克尔半是不自觉地停下脚，打量起这座小屋来。

===Original===
“They could never have overlooked that in the hunt, surely. Well, no harm in having a peep to make certain.”

He dropped his coil of rope, stepped across to the house, and, stooping down, flung open the door. Inside, he caught a flash of some white fabric.

“It’s the beggar after all! Here! Come out of that!”

===Chinese===
“追人的时候，总不至于把这个也漏了吧。也好，进去瞄一眼确认一下，不费什么。”

他放下那盘绳子，跨步走到小屋前，俯身推开了门。屋内，一角白色的什么东西在他眼前一闪。

“那家伙到底在这儿！喂！快出来！”

===Original===
He gripped the inmate roughly and hauled him by main force out of his retreat.

“Pierrot costume, right enough!” he said to himself as he extracted the man little by little from his refuge. Then, having got his victim into the open:

“Now we’ll turn you over and have a look at your face … Good God! Maurice!”

===Chinese===
他一把揪住屋里那位，硬生生把他从藏身处拖了出来。

“皮埃罗（Pierrot）戏服，没错！”他一边把那人一点一点从躲处往外拽，一边自语道。随后，他把这位猎物拖到了空地上——

“现在该把你翻个身，瞧瞧你的脸了……天哪！莫里斯！”

===Original===
For as he turned the man on his back, it was the face of Maurice Chacewater that met his eyes. But it was not a normal Maurice whom he saw. The features were contorted by some excessive emotion the like of which Michael had never seen.

“Let me alone, damn you,” Maurice gasped, and turned over once more on his face, resting his brow on his arm as though to shut out the spectacle of Michael’s astonishment.

===Chinese===
因为把那人仰面翻过来时，映入他眼帘的竟是莫里斯·蔡斯沃特（Maurice Chacewater）的脸。可他看见的不是一个正常状态下的莫里斯。那张脸被某种过度的情绪扭曲着，那模样迈克尔生平未曾见过。

“别碰我，你这该死的，”莫里斯喘着气说，重新翻过身去俯卧着，额头抵在手臂上，像是要把迈克尔那副惊愕的样子挡在眼外。

===Original===
“Are you ill?” Michael inquired, solicitously.

“For God’s sake leave me alone. Don’t stand there gaping. Clear out, I tell you.”

Michael looked at him in amazement.

“I’m going to have a cheerful kind of brother-in-law before all’s done, it seems,” he thought to himself.

===Chinese===
“你是不是病了？”迈克尔关切地问。

“看在上帝份上，别来烦我。别站在那儿瞪着眼。滚开，听见没有。”

迈克尔惊愕地望着他。

“看来到头来，我要摊上一位够『快活』的内兄了，”他心里暗想。

===Original===
“Can I do anything for you, Maurice?”

“Oh, go to hell!”

Michael turned away.

“It’s fairly clear he doesn’t like my company,” he reflected, as he stepped across and picked up his coil of rope from the ground. “But I’ve known politer ways of showing it, I must say.”

===Chinese===
“我能帮你点什么吗，莫里斯？”

“哦，你下地狱去吧！”

迈克尔转过身去。

“很清楚，他不喜欢我作陪，”他一边跨过去从地上拾起那盘绳子，一边想道。“不过话说回来，表示这意思，我倒见过比这客气些的方式。”

===Original===
With a final glance at the prostrate figure of Maurice, he walked on and took the road back to Ravensthorpe. But as he went a vision of Maurice’s face kept passing before his mind’s eye.

“There’s something damned far wrong with that beggar, whether it’s an evil conscience or cramp in the tummy. It might be either of them, by the look of him. He didn’t seem to want any assistance from me. That looks more like the evil conscience theory.”

===Chinese===
他又看了俯卧在地的莫里斯最后一眼，便继续赶路，取道回雷文索普去。可一路走着，莫里斯那张脸的影子总在他眼前晃来晃去。

“那家伙极不对劲，不管是良心坏了还是肚子抽筋。瞧他那模样，两样都有可能。他似乎不想从我这里得到任何帮助。这更像是良心坏了一说。”

===Original===
He dismissed this with a laugh; but gradually he grew troubled.

“There he was, in white—same as the burglar. He’s in a bit of a bate at being discovered, that’s clear enough. He didn’t half like it, to judge by his chat.”

A discomforting hypothesis began to frame itself in his mind despite his efforts to stifle it.

===Chinese===
他笑着把这念头打发掉了；可渐渐地，心里又不安起来。

“他就在那儿，一身白——同那窃贼一模一样。被撞破了，他老大不自在，这够明白的。听他那些话，他可是恼火得很。”

一个叫人心里发毛的臆想，尽管他竭力按捺，还是在脑子里渐渐成了形。

===Original===
“He’s the fellow, if there is one, who would know all these secret passages about here. Suppose there really is one leading out of that cave. He could have swarmed down the rope, got into the cave, sneaked up the subterranean passage, and got behind us that way.”

A fresh fact fitted suddenly in.

“And of course the other end of the passage may be in that Fairy House! That would explain his being there. He’d be waiting to see us off the premises before he could venture out in his white costume.”

===Chinese===
“要真有谁知道这一带的这些密道，那就非他莫属。假如那条洞里当真通着一条。他完全可以顺绳子溜下去，钻进洞里，摸黑走上那条地下通道，这么着绕到咱们背后去。”

一件新事实忽然严丝合缝地扣了上来。

“还有，那条通道的另一头，八成就通到那座仙女屋里！这就能解释他为什么在那儿了。他定是等着看咱们离开这地界，才敢穿着那身白衣服出来活动。”

===Original===
He pondered over the problem as he hurried with long strides towards the house.

“Well,” he concluded, “I’m taking no further steps in the business. It’s no concern of mine to go probing into the private affairs of the family I’m going to marry into. And that’s that.”

===Chinese===
他迈开大步匆匆往宅子赶，一路琢磨着这个问题。

“得，”他下了结论，“这事我不再插手了。去刨根问底地探我未来姻亲家的私事，与我无关。就这么定了。”

===Original===
Then, as a fresh aspect of the matter came to his mind, he gave a sigh of relief.

“I must be a stricken idiot! No man would ever dream of burgling his own house. What would he gain by it, if he did? The thing’s ridiculous.”

And the comfort which this view brought him was sufficient to lighten his steps for the rest of his way.

===Chinese===
随后，这事的另一个方面浮上心头，他如释重负地舒了口气。

“我准是个不折不扣的傻瓜！天底下没有人会做梦去偷自己家的。真要偷，他能图着什么？这事实在荒唐。”

这个念头带来的宽慰，足以让他剩下的一路脚步都轻快起来。

## V Sir Clinton in the Museum / 第五章 博物馆中的克林顿爵士

===Original===
“There’s the light on again in the museum,” Sir Clinton observed. “I think we’ll go in and have a look round, now, to see if the place suggests anything.”

Mold stood aside to let them pass, and then resumed his watch at the door to prevent anyone else from entering the room. The servant had just finished fitting the new globe in its place and was preparing to remove the steps which he had used, when Sir Clinton ordered him to leave them in position and to await further instructions.

The museum was a room about forty feet square, with a lofty ceiling. To judge by the panelling of the walls, it belonged to the older part of Ravensthorpe; but the parquet of the floor seemed to be much more modern. Round the sides were placed exhibition cases about six feet high; and others of the same kind jutted out at intervals to form a series of shallow bays. In the centre of the room, directly under the lamp, stood a long, flat-topped case; and the floor beside it was littered with broken glass.
===Chinese===
“博物馆的灯又亮了，”克林顿爵士（Sir Clinton）说道。“我看咱们这就进去转上一圈，看看这地方能不能给人一点启发。”

莫尔德（Mold）侧身让他们进去，随后回到门口继续守望，防止再有旁人进屋。仆人刚把新灯泡装回原位，正要搬走他方才用过的梯子，克林顿爵士却吩咐他把梯子留在原处，听候下一步指示。

博物馆是间约四十英尺见方的屋子，顶棚很高。从墙上的木护壁来看，它属于雷文索普（Ravensthorpe）较老的那部分建筑；但地板的拼花镶木看上去却新得多。四面墙边摆着约六英尺高的展柜；另有同式的展柜每隔一段向外凸出，围出一连串浅浅的凹室。屋子正中、吊灯正下方，立着一座长形平顶柜；柜旁的地板上散落着砸碎的玻璃。

===Original===
“I think we’ll begin at the beginning,” said Sir Clinton.

He turned to the servant who stood waiting beside the steps.

“Have you got the remains of the broken lamp there?

“You can go now,” he added. “We shan’t need you further.”

When he had received the smashed lamp, he examined it.
===Chinese===
“我看咱们就从头上查起，”克林顿爵士说。

他转向在梯子旁侍立等候的仆人。

“碎灯的残骸就在你手里吧？”

“你现在可以走了，”他补充道。“用不着你再伺候了。”

接过那盏砸碎的灯，他细细察看。

===Original===
“Not much to be made out of that,” he admitted. “It’s been one of these thousand candlepower gas-filled things; and there’s practically nothing left of it but the metal base and a few splinters of glass sticking to it.”

He looked up at the fresh lamp hanging above them.

“It’s thirty feet or so above the floor. Nothing short of a fishing-rod would reach it. Evidently they didn’t smash it by hand.”
===Chinese===
“从这上头瞧不出多少名堂，”他承认道。“这原是那种一千烛光的充气灯；如今几乎片甲不剩，只剩个金属灯座，上头还粘着几片碎玻璃。”

他抬头望了望头顶新挂上的灯。

“离地板约莫三十英尺。不借钓竿一类的家什，休想够得着。可见他们不是用手砸的。”

===Original===
He stooped down and sorted out one or two small fragments of glass from the debris at his feet.

“These are more bits of the lamp, Joan,” he said, holding them out for her to look at. “You see the curve of the glass; and you’ll notice that the whole affair seems to have been smashed almost to smithereens. There doesn’t seem to be a decent-sized fragment in the whole lot.”
===Chinese===
他俯下身，从脚边的碎屑里挑出一两小片玻璃。

“这些也是灯上的碎片，琼，”他说着，递到她眼前让她看。“你瞧这玻璃的弧度；你也注意到了吧，整盏灯几乎砸了个粉碎。整堆里竟挑不出一块够尺寸的碎片。”

===Original===
He turned to the keeper.

“I think we’ll shut the door, Mold. We’d better conduct the rest of this business in private.”

The keeper closed the door of the museum, much to the disappointment of the group of people who had clustered about the entrance and were watching the proceedings with interest.
===Chinese===
他转向看管人。

“我看把门关上吧，莫尔德。往下的事，咱们最好关起门来办。”

看管人关上了博物馆的门，这让聚在门口、正饶有兴味围观的一群人大失所望。

===Original===
“Now, Joan, would you mind going round the wall-cases and seeing if anything has been taken from them?”

Joan obediently paced round the room and soon came back to report that nothing seemed to have been removed.

“All the cases were locked, you know,” she explained. “And there’s no glass broken in any of them. So far as I can see, nothing’s missing from the shelves.”
===Chinese===
“现在，琼，麻烦你沿墙边的展柜走一圈，看看有没有被拿走什么？”

琼依言绕屋走了一圈，很快回来报告说，似乎什么都没有被拿走。

“要知道，展柜全都是上了锁的，”她解释道。“哪一柜的玻璃也没有碎。依我看，柜里架上一件不缺。”

===Original===
“What about that safe let into the wall over yonder?” Sir Clinton inquired.

“It’s used to house one or two extra valuable things from time to time,” Joan explained. “But tonight everything was put on show, and the safe’s empty.”

She went over and swung the door open, showing the vacant shelves within.

“We do take precautions usually,” she pointed out. “The museum door itself is iron-plated and has a special lock. It was only tonight that we had everything out in the showcases.”
===Chinese===
“那边嵌进墙里的保险柜呢？”克林顿爵士问道。

“平日里有一两件格外贵重的东西，时不时会收进去，”琼解释道。“可今晚所有东西都摆出来展览了，保险柜是空的。”

她走过去拉开柜门，亮出里面空空的搁板。

“平常我们并不是不设防，”她指了出来。“博物馆这扇门本身就包着铁皮，还上着特制的锁。唯独今晚，才把所有的东西都摆进了展柜。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton refrained from comment, as he knew the girl was still blaming herself for her share in the catastrophe. He turned to examine the rifled section of the central case.

“What’s missing here, Joan, can you make out?”

Obediently, Joan came to his side and ran her eye over the remaining articles in the compartment.

“They’ve taken the Medusa Medallions!” she exclaimed, turning pale as she realized the magnitude of the calamity. “They’ve got the very pick of the collection, Sir Clinton. My father would have parted with all the rest rather than with these, I know.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士没有置评——他知道，这场大祸里自己那份责任，姑娘还记着在心里自责呢。他转身去察看中央展柜遭劫的那一格。

“这儿丢了什么，琼，你看得出来吗？”

琼顺从地走到他身旁，把这格里剩下的物件扫视了一遍。

“他们把美杜莎徽章（Medusa Medallions）拿走了！”她失声叫道，一想到祸事之大，脸色顿时发白。“他们拿走的，正是整批收藏里的头等精华，克林顿爵士。我知道，哪怕舍出其余全部，我父亲也不肯撒手这几件的。”

===Original===
“Nothing else gone?”

Joan looked again at the case.

“No, nothing else, so far as I can see. Wait a bit, though! They’ve taken the electrotype copies as well. There were three of each: three medallions and an electrotype from each that Foxton Polegate made for us. The whole six are gone.”
===Chinese===
“别的没丢？”

琼又看了看展柜。

“没有，依我看别的都没丢。等一下，还有！他们把电铸复制品也拿走了。原先每样各有三件：三枚徽章，外加福克斯顿·波尔盖特（Foxton Polegate）给我们做的、每枚各配的一件电铸件。六件全没了。”

===Original===
She cast a final glance at the compartment.

“No, there’s nothing else missing, so far as I can see. Some of the things are displaced a bit; but everything except the medallions and the electros seems to be here.”

“You’re quite sure?”

“Certain.”

Sir Clinton seemed satisfied.
===Chinese===
她朝这格里又投去最后一瞥。

“没有了，依我看再没有缺什么。有几件东西挪动过一点；可除了徽章和电铸件，其余的似乎都在。”

“你完全确定？”

“确定。”

克林顿爵士像是放了心。

===Original===
“Of course we’ll have to check the stuff by the catalogue to make sure,” he said, “but I expect you’re right. The medallions alone would be quite a good enough haul for a minute or two’s work; and probably they had their eyes on the things as the best paying proposition of the lot.”

“But why did they take the electros as well?” Joan demanded.

Then a possible explanation occurred to her.

“Oh, of course, they wouldn’t know which was which, so they took the lot in order to make sure.”
===Chinese===
“当然，回头还得对照藏品目录清点核实一遍，”他说，“不过我料你说得没错。单是这几枚徽章，一两分钟的活儿就有这样的进账，也尽够了；多半他们早把眼光落在这几件上，认定是整批里最值钱的货色。”

“可他们为什么连电铸件也一并拿走呢？”琼诘问道。

接着她想到了一个说得通的解释。

“哦，当然了，他们分不清哪件是哪件，索性一股脑儿全拿走，图个稳妥。”

===Original===
“Possibly,” Sir Clinton admitted. “But don’t let’s be going too fast, Joan. We’d better not get ideas into our minds till we’ve got all the evidence, you know.”

“Oh, I see,” said Joan, with a faint return of her normal spirits, “I’m to be Watson, am I? And you’ll prove in a minute or two what an ass I’ve made of myself. Is that the idea?”

“Not altogether,” Sir Clinton returned, with a smile. “But let’s have the facts before the theories.”
===Chinese===
“也许是，”克林顿爵士承认。“不过咱们也别走得太快，琼。要知道，证据没凑齐之前，最好别先往脑子里塞想法。”

“哦，我明白了，”琼说道，平日的兴致恢复了些许，“我这是扮华生（Watson），对吧？再过一两分钟，您就来证明我把自己弄成了多大的蠢驴。是这么个章程吧？”

“倒也不尽然，”克林顿爵士含笑应道。“不过先摆事实，后立理论。”

===Original===
He turned to the keeper.

“Now we’ll take your story, Mold; but give us the things in the exact order in which they happened, if you can. And don’t be worried if I break in with questions.”

Mold thought for a moment or two before beginning his tale.
===Chinese===
他转向看管人。

“现在来听你陈述，莫尔德；不过，尽量把事情按先后次序原原本本讲给我们。我中途插话发问，你不必着慌。”

莫尔德想了片刻，才开讲他的经过。

===Original===
“I’m trying to remember how many people there were in the room just before the lights went out,” he explained at last, “but somehow I don’t quite seem able to put a figure on it, Sir Clinton. I’ve a sort of feeling that some of ’em must ha’ got away before I stopped the door﻿—sneaked off in the dark. At least I know I felt surprised when I saw how few I’d got left when they began to come up to me to be let out. But that’s all I can really say, sir.”

Sir Clinton evidently approved of the keeper’s caution.

“Now tell us exactly what happened when the light went out. This is the bit where I want you to be careful. Tell us everything you can remember.”
===Chinese===
“我正想回忆灯灭之前，屋里到底有多少人，”他终于解释道，“可不知怎么的，我实在报不出个数目来，克林顿爵士。我隐隐觉得，有几位准是在我守住门之前就脱了身——趁黑溜掉了。至少我还记得，等他们陆续凑上前来求我放行的时候，一看剩下的人那么少，我心里还纳闷来着。可我实实在在说得上来的，就只有这些，先生。”

克林顿爵士显然赞许看管人的这份谨慎。

“现在给我们讲讲灯灭那一刻到底发生了什么。这一段，我要你格外仔细。想起什么，一句别落，全讲出来。”

===Original===
Mold fixed his eye on the corner of the room near the safe.

“I was patrollin’ round the room, sir, most of the night. I didn’t stand in one place all the time. Now just when the light was about to go out, I was walkin’ away from this case here”﻿—he nodded towards the rifled central case﻿—“and as near as may be, I’d got to the entrance to that second-last bay, just before you come to the safe. I just turned round to come back, when I heard a pistol goin’ off.”
===Chinese===
莫尔德把目光定在保险柜附近那个屋角上。

“一晚上大半的工夫，我都在屋里来回巡查，先生。我没有在一个地方站死不动。就在灯快灭的那会儿，我正从这口柜子边上走开”——他朝被劫的中央展柜点了点头——“差不离，我已经走到倒数第二个凹室的口上，再往前就是保险柜。我刚转身要往回走，就听见一声手枪响。”

===Original===
“That was the first thing that attracted your attention?” questioned Sir Clinton. “It’s an important point, Mold.”

“That was the first thing out o’ the common that happened,” Mold asserted. “The pistol went bang, and out went the light, and I heard glass tinkling all over the place.”

“Shot the light out, did they?” Sir Clinton mused.
===Chinese===
“那是头一件引起你注意的事？”克林顿爵士盘问道。“这是要紧的一点，莫尔德。”

“那是头一件出了常轨的事，”莫尔德一口咬定。“枪砰的一响，灯就灭了，跟着我就听见满屋子玻璃叮叮当当。”

“把灯打灭的，是吧？”克林顿爵士沉吟道。

===Original===
He glanced up at the carved wooden ceiling, but evidently failed to find what he was looking for.

“Have you a pair of race-glasses, Joan? Prismatics, or even opera-glasses? Tell Mold where he can get them, please.”

Joan gave the keeper instructions and he left the room.

“Knock when you come back again,” Sir Clinton ordered. “I’m going to lock the door to keep out the inquisitive.”
===Chinese===
他抬眼扫过雕花的木顶棚，却显然没找到他要找的东西。

“琼，家里有望远镜吗？赛马用的棱镜双筒的，观剧镜也行？请告诉莫尔德上哪儿去拿。”

琼给看管人指点了地方，他便出了屋。

“回来的时候敲敲门，”克林顿爵士吩咐道。“我打算把门锁上，挡开那些好事之徒。”

===Original===
As soon as the keeper was out of earshot, Sir Clinton turned to Joan.

“This fellow Mold, is he a reliable man? Do you know anything about him, Joan?”

“He’s our head keeper. We’ve always trusted him completely.”

She glanced at Sir Clinton, trying to read the expression on his face.
===Chinese===
看管人一走到听不见的地方，克林顿爵士便转向琼。

“这个莫尔德，人可靠吗？你了解他些什么，琼？”

“他是我们的看管人领班。我们一向完全信得过他。”

她瞥了克林顿爵士一眼，想读懂他脸上的神情。

===Original===
“You don’t think *he’s* at the bottom of the business, do you? I never thought of that!”

“I’m only collecting facts at present. All I want to know is whether you know Mold to be reliable.”

“We’ve always found him so.”

“Good. We’ll make a note of that; and if we get the thing cleared up, then we’ll perhaps be able to confirm that opinion of yours.”
===Chinese===
“您不会是觉得这桩事的根子在*他*身上吧？我可从没往那儿想过！”

“眼下我只管搜集事实。我想知道的，无非是你是否确知莫尔德靠得住。”

“我们一向觉得他靠得住。”

“好。记下这一条；等案子查个水落石出，说不定就能印证你这句话。”

===Original===
In a few minutes a knock came at the door and Sir Clinton admitted the keeper.

“Prismatics?” he said, taking the glasses from Mold. “They’ll do quite well.”

Adjusting the focus, he subjected the ceiling of the room to a minute scrutiny. At last he handed the glasses to Joan.

“Look up there,” he said, indicating the position.
===Chinese===
不多几分钟，门上响起敲门声，克林顿爵士放看管人进来。

“棱镜的？”他从莫尔德手里接过望远镜，说道。“完全够用了。”

他调好焦距，对屋子的顶棚做了一番细致入微的察看。末了，他把望远镜递给琼。

“往那儿看，”他说着，指明了位置。

===Original===
Joan swept the place with the glasses for a moment.

“I see,” she said. “That’s a bullet-hole in the wood, isn’t it?”

Sir Clinton confirmed her guess.

“That’s evidently where the bullet went after knocking the lamp to pieces. Pull the steps over there, Mold. I want to have a closer look at the thing.”
===Chinese===
琼举着望远镜朝那边扫视了片刻。

“看见了，”她说。“木头里那是弹孔，对吧？”

克林顿爵士证实了她的猜测。

“那显然是子弹击碎吊灯之后去的方向。把梯子挪过去，莫尔德。我要凑近看看这东西。”

===Original===
With some difficulty, owing to his injured ankle, he ascended the steps and inspected the tiny cavity.

“It looks like a .22 calibre. One could carry a Colt pistol of that size in one’s pocket and no one would notice it.”

His eye traced out the line joining the bullet-mark and the lamp.

“The shot was evidently fired by someone in that bay over there,” he inferred. “Just go to where you were standing when the light went out, Mold. Can you see into this bay here?”
===Chinese===
他忍着伤踝的种种不便，费力地登上梯子，端详那个小小的孔洞。

“看样子是 .22 口径的。这种尺寸的柯尔特手枪，揣在口袋里带走也没人瞧得出来。”

他的目光沿着弹痕与吊灯之间的连线推移。

“这一枪，显然是那边凹室里的什么人开的，”他推断道。“站到你灯灭时待的地方去，莫尔德。从那儿能看见这个凹室吗？”

===Original===
Mold looked around and discovered that a showcase interposed between him and the point from which the pistol had been fired.

“They evidently thought of everything,” Sir Clinton said, when he heard Mold’s report. “If a man had brandished his pistol in front of Mold, there was always a chance that Mold might have remembered his costume. Firing from that hiding-place, he was quite safe, and could take time over his aim if he wanted to.”

He climbed down the steps and verified the matter by going to the position from which the shot had been fired. It was evident that the shooter was out of sight of the keeper at the actual moment of the discharge.
===Chinese===
莫尔德四下里看了看，发现一只展柜正隔在他与开枪的位置之间。

“他们显然方方面面都盘算到了，”听完莫尔德的报告，克林顿爵士说道。“要是有人当着莫尔德的面挥动手枪，莫尔德总还有可能记住他那身戏服。躲在那个藏身处开枪，就稳妥多了，愿意的话还可以从容瞄准。”

他爬下梯子，又亲自走到开枪的地点核实了一遍。显然，枪响的那一刻，开枪的人正处在看管人的视线之外。

===Original===
“Now what happened after that, Mold?” Sir Clinton demanded, coming back to the central case again.

Mold scratched his ear as though reflecting, then hurriedly took his hand down again.

“This pistol went off, sir; and the lamp-glass tinkled all over the place. I got a start﻿—who wouldn’t?﻿—with the light going out, and all. Before I could move an inch, someone got a grip of my wrists and swung me round. He twisted my arms behind my back and I couldn’t do anything but kick﻿—and not much kickin’ even, or I’d have gone down on my face.”
===Chinese===
“那之后又怎样了，莫尔德？”克林顿爵士重新回到中央展柜跟前，追问道。

莫尔德挠了挠耳朵，像是在回想，随即又慌忙把手放了下来。

“这枪一响，先生；灯玻璃就碎得满屋叮当。灯一灭，我吓了一跳——谁个不吓？——还没等我挪动一寸，就有人攥住我的双腕，把我抡了个转身。他把我的两条胳膊反扭到背后，我除了踢腿什么也做不了——连踢也踢不了几下，不然早扑倒在地了。”

===Original===
“Did you manage to get home on him at all?”

“I think I kicked him once, sir; but it was only a graze.”

“Pity,” Sir Clinton said. “It would have always been something gained if you’d marked him with a good bruise.”

“Oh, there’ll be a mark, if that’s all you want, sir. But it wouldn’t prevent him runnin’ at all.”
===Chinese===
“你到底蹭着他没有？”

“我好像踢着他一下，先生；不过是擦了个边儿。”

“可惜，”克林顿爵士说。“要是能给他留下一块结实的乌青，好歹总算是有所斩获。”

“哦，印记总会有的，只要您要的是这个，先生。可那一点儿也不碍他跑路。”

===Original===
“And then?” Sir Clinton brought Mold back to his story.

“Then, almost at once when the lights went out, I heard glass breakin’﻿—just as if you’d heaved a stone through a window. It seemed to me﻿—but I couldn’t take my oath on it﻿—as if there was two smashes, one after t’other. I couldn’t be sure. Then there was a lot of scufflin’ in the dark; but who did it, I couldn’t rightly say. I was busy tryin’ to get free from the man who was holdin’ me then.”
===Chinese===
“后来呢？”克林顿爵士把莫尔德拉回他的叙述。

“后来，灯一灭，几乎立刻，我就听见玻璃碎——就跟往窗户里扔了块石头似的。我觉着——可我不敢起誓——像是碎了两响，一响接着一响。我说不准。再后来，黑地里一阵乱蹬乱抓；可那是谁干的，我实在说不上来。我那会儿正忙着挣脱揪住我的那个人呢。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton moved over to the rifled compartment and inspected the broken glass thoughtfully for a moment or two.

“Are you looking for fingermarks?” asked Joan, as she came to his side.

Sir Clinton shook his head.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士踱到被劫的那一格展柜前，对着碎玻璃沉吟察看了一两分钟。

“您在找指纹吗？”琼走到他身边，问道。

克林顿爵士摇了摇头。

===Original===
“Not much use hunting for fingermarks round here. Remember how many people must have leaned on this case at one time or other during the evening, when they were looking at the collection before the robbery. Fingerprints would prove nothing against anyone in particular, I’m afraid, Joan. What I’m really trying to find is some evidence confirming Mold’s notion that he heard two smashes after the light went out. It certainly looks as if he were right. If you look at the way that bit of glass there is cracked, you’ll see two series of lines in it. It might have been cracked here”﻿—he pointed with his finger﻿—“first of all: long cracks radiating from a smash over in this direction. Then there was a second blow﻿—about here﻿—which snapped off the apices of the spears of glass left after the first smash. But that really proves nothing. The same man might easily have hit the pane twice.”
===Chinese===
“在这儿找指纹用处不大。想想看，失窃之前，整个晚上看收藏的时候，必定有多少人或早或晚在这柜子上倚过。只怕指纹落在谁身上都定不了罪，琼。我真正想找的，是能证实莫尔德那个说法的证据——他说灯灭之后听见了两响砸玻璃声。看样子他确实没说错。你细看那边那块玻璃的裂纹，就能看出上面有两套纹路。它可能是先在这儿裂的”——他用手指点了点——“头一记砸在这个方向，留下放射状的长裂纹。接着又挨了第二记——大约在这儿——把头一回砸剩的玻璃尖茬齐齐崩断。不过这其实证明不了什么。同一个人往玻璃上砸两下，也是轻而易举的事。”

===Original===
He turned back to the keeper.

“Can you give me an estimate, Mold, of how long it was between the two crashes you heard?”

Mold considered carefully before replying.

“So far’s I can remember, Sir Clinton, it was about five seconds. But I’ll not take my oath on it.”
===Chinese===
他转回看管人这边。

“莫尔德，你听见的那两声响，中间隔了多久，能估个大概吗？”

莫尔德仔细掂量了一番才作答。

“凭我记着的，克林顿爵士，约莫五秒钟。可我不敢起誓。”

===Original===
“I wish you could be surer,” said the Chief Constable. “If it really was five seconds, it certainly looks like two separate affairs. A man smashing glass with repeated blows wouldn’t wait five seconds between them.”

He scanned the broken glass again.

“There’s a lot of jagged stuff round the edge of the hole but no blood, so far as I can see. The fellow must have worn a thick glove if he got his hand in there in the dark without cutting himself in the hurry.”
===Chinese===
“你要能说得更准些就好了，”郡警察总长（Chief Constable）说道。“倘若真是五秒钟，那就分明是两桩事。一个人抡着胳膊连砸玻璃，两下之间不会歇上五秒钟。”

他又扫视了一遍碎玻璃。

“窟窿边上一圈尽是参差的玻璃茬，可我看没有血迹。那家伙若是摸黑把手伸了进去、又急急慌慌没割着自己，手上必定戴着厚手套。”

===Original===
He turned back to the keeper.

“You can go outside, Mold, and keep people off the doorstep for a minute or two. Perhaps we shall have news of the manhunt soon. If anyone wants to see me on business; let him in; but keep off casual inquirers for the present.”

Obediently Mold unlocked the door and took his stand on the threshold outside, shutting the door behind him as he went. When he had gone, Sir Clinton turned to Joan.
===Chinese===
他又转向看管人。

“你到外头去吧，莫尔德，在门口挡上一两分钟，别让人围过来。追捕那伙人的消息，也许很快就有了。真有事要见我的，放他进来；闲散打听的一干人等，眼下先挡在外面。”

莫尔德依言开了锁，到门外门槛上站定，出去时随手带上了门。他走后，克林顿爵士转向琼。

===Original===
“Were these medallions insured, do you know?”

Fortunately, Joan was able to supply some information.

“Maurice insured them, I know. But I’ve heard him say that he wasn’t content with the valuation put on them by the company. It seems they wouldn’t take his word for the value of the things﻿—they thought it was a speculative one or something﻿—and in case of a loss they weren’t prepared to go beyond a figure which Maurice thought too small.”
===Chinese===
“这几枚徽章上过保险没有，你知道吗？”

幸好琼还能提供些情况。

“莫里斯（Maurice）给它们保过险，这我知道。可我听他说过，保险公司的估价他并不满意。他们不肯听信他对这些东西身价的说法——觉得那数目带着投机味儿还是怎么的——真出了损失，他们赔的数目不肯超出一个莫里斯嫌太小的数。”

===Original===
“The electros weren’t insured for any great amount, I suppose?”

Joan shook her head.

“I don’t think they were specially insured. They were just put under the ordinary house policy, I think. But you’d better ask Maurice. He knows all about it.”
===Chinese===
“电铸件没保多大的数额吧，我猜？”

琼摇了摇头。

“我想没单独保过。大概只归在寻常的房屋保单里。不过您最好还是问莫里斯。他一清二楚。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton glanced round the room once more.

“I doubt if there’s much more to find out here,” he concluded. “It doesn’t give us much to go on, does it? Perhaps we’ll have better luck when these fellows come in from their hunt. They may have some news for us. But as things stand, we can’t even be sure whether it was two men or two gangs that were at work. One can’t blame Mold for not giving us better information; but what he gave us doesn’t seem to amount to very much at present.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士又把屋子环视了一遍。

“我看在这儿再挖不出多少东西了，”他下了结论。“这现场没给我们多少凭据，是吧？等追捕的那些人回来，运气或许会好些。他们可能捎回些消息。但就眼下的情形，作案的到底是两个人还是两伙人，我们连这个都拿不准。莫尔德给不出更好的情况，怨不得他；可他说的这些，目前看也顶不了大用。”

===Original===
He turned, as though to leave the room; but at that moment the door opened and Mold appeared.

“There’s a Mr. Foss wants to see you, sir. He says he’s got something to tell you that won’t wait. He’s been looking for you all over the house.”
===Chinese===
他转过身，像是要出屋去；正在这时，门开了，莫尔德露了面。

“有位福斯（Foss）先生要见您，先生。他说有件事要告诉您，耽搁不得。他满宅子找您，都找遍了。”

===Original===
“That’s the American, isn’t it?” Sir Clinton asked Joan in a low voice.

“Yes. He’s been here for a day or two, consulting with Maurice about these medallions.”

“Well, if he can throw any light on this business, I suppose we’d better let him in and see what he has to say. You needn’t go, Joan. You may as well hear his story, whatever it may be.”

He turned to the keeper.

“Let Mr. Foss in, Mold; and wait outside the door yourself.”
===Chinese===
“就是那位美国人，对吧？”克林顿爵士低声问琼。

“是的。他来了一两天了，正为这几枚徽章的事跟莫里斯接洽。”

“好吧，他要是能给这桩事透出一线光亮，我们还是让他进来，听听他有什么话说。你不必走开，琼。他的话不管是什么名堂，你不妨也听听。”

他转向看管人。

“放福斯先生进来，莫尔德；你自己就在门外守着。”

## VI Mr. Foss’s Explanation / 第六章 福斯先生的解释

===Original===
Mr. Foss had nothing distinctively American in his appearance, Sir Clinton noted; and when he spoke, his accent was so faint as to be hardly detectable. He was a stout man of about fifty, with a clean-shaven face and more than a trace of a double chin: the kind of man who might readily be chosen as an unofficial uncle by children. Sir Clinton’s first glance showed him that the American was troubled about something.

Foss seemed surprised to find the Chief Constable in the guise of Prospero. He himself, in preparation for an official interview, had exchanged his masquerade costume for ordinary evening clothes.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士注意到，福斯先生的外表没有什么鲜明的美国特征；开口说话时，口音也淡得几乎难以察觉。他是个年约五十的壮实汉子，脸刮得干干净净，双下巴已不止隐约一线：属于那种很容易被孩子们选作“编外叔叔”的类型。克林顿爵士第一眼便看出，这位美国人心事重重。

福斯见郡警察局长竟是普洛斯彼罗的行头，似乎颇感意外。他自己为着这场正式谈话，已把化装舞会的戏服换成了寻常晚礼服。

===Original===
“We haven’t met before, Sir Clinton,” he explained, rather unnecessarily, “but I’ve something to tell you”﻿—his face clouded slightly﻿—“which I felt you ought to know before you go any further in this business. I’ve been hunting all over the house for you; and it was only a minute or two ago that I got directed in here.”

“Yes?” said Sir Clinton, interrogatively.
===Chinese===
“我们从前不曾谋面，克林顿爵士，”他解释道，这话未免多余，“但我有件事要告诉您”——他的神色微微阴沉下来——“依我看，在这桩事上再往前走之前，您应当知情。我满宅子找您找了半天；就在一两分钟前，才有人把我指到这儿来。”

“是吗？”克林顿爵士带着盘问的口气应道。

===Original===
Foss glanced at Joan and seemed to find some difficulty in opening the subject.

“It’s a strictly private matter,” he explained.

Joan refused to take the implied hint.
===Chinese===
福斯瞥了琼一眼，似乎颇觉此话难以启齿。

“这是一桩纯属私人的事，”他解释道。

琼偏不肯领会这层暗示。

===Original===
“If it has any connection with this burglary, Mr. Foss, I see no reason why I should not hear what you have to say. It’s a matter that concerns me as one of the family, you know.”

Foss seemed taken aback and quite evidently he would have preferred to make his confidence to Sir Clinton alone.

“It’s rather a difficult matter,” he said, with a feeble endeavour to deflect Joan from her purpose.
===Chinese===
“福斯先生，这事只要同这桩盗窃案有半点干系，我就看不出有什么理由不该听听您的高见。要知道，我也是家中一分子，这事同我相干。”

福斯显得颇为错愕；明摆着，他原意只想单独向克林顿爵士一人剖白。

“这事相当难启齿，”他说，有气无力地想把琼的念头岔开。

===Original===
Joan, however, took no notice of his diffidence.

“Come, Mr. Foss,” she said. “If it’s really important, the sooner Sir Clinton hears of it the better. Begin.”
===Chinese===
然而琼对他的局促视若无睹。

“行了，福斯先生，”她说。“这事当真要紧的话，就越早让克林顿爵士知道越好。说吧。”

===Original===
Foss glanced appealingly at Sir Clinton; but apparently the Chief Constable took Joan’s view of the matter.

“I’m rather busy at present, Mr. Foss,” he said, dryly. “Perhaps you’ll give us your information as concisely as possible.”

Having failed in his attempt, Foss made the best of it; though it was with obvious reluctance that he launched into his subject.
===Chinese===
福斯向克林顿爵士投去恳求的一瞥；可郡警察局长显然同琼一个看法。

“我眼下相当忙，福斯先生，”他干巴巴地说。“您的情况，或许可以讲得尽量简洁些。”

此计落空，福斯只得顺其自然；只是开讲正题时，那副不情愿仍是一望而知。

===Original===
“Last night after dinner,” he began, “I went into the winter-garden to smoke a cigar. I had some business affairs which I wanted to put straight in my mind; and I thought I could stow myself away in a corner there and be free from interruption. So I sat down at one side of the winter-garden behind a large clump of palms where no one was likely to see me; and I began to think over the points I had in mind.”

“Yes?” prompted Sir Clinton, who seemed anxious to cut Foss’s narrative down to essentials.
===Chinese===
“昨天晚上吃过晚饭，”他开腔道，“我到温室花园里去抽雪茄。有些生意上的事，我想在心里理出个头绪；我想着不如在那儿的角落里寻个地方安顿下来，图个不受搅扰。于是我就在温室花园的一侧、一丛高大棕榈的背后坐了下来，那里不太会有人瞧见；我便开始琢磨心里盘算的那几件事。”

“然后呢？”克林顿爵士催了一句，他似乎急着要把福斯的叙述删削到只留筋骨。

===Original===
“While I was sitting there,” the American continued, “some of the young people came into the winter-garden and sat down in a recess on the side opposite to where I was. At first they didn’t disturb me. I thought they’d be almost out of earshot, on the other side of the dome. I think you were one of them, Miss Chacewater: you, and your brother, and Miss Rainhill, and someone else whom I didn’t recognize.”

“I was there,” Joan confirmed, looking rather puzzled as to what might come next.
===Chinese===
“我坐在那儿的当口，”这位美国人接着说，“几位年轻人走进温室花园，在对面一侧的凹室里坐了下来。起初他们并没有搅扰我。我想，隔着穹顶那一头，他们说话我多半听不真切。我记得您也是其中一位，蔡斯沃特小姐：您，还有令兄，还有雷恩希尔小姐，另外一位，我没认出来。”

“我确是在场的，”琼证实道，神情相当困惑，不知下文会是什么。

===Original===
“You may not know, Miss Chacewater,” Foss continued, “that your winter-garden is a sort of whispering-gallery. Although I was quite a long way off from your party, your voices came quite clearly across to where I was sitting. They didn’t disturb me at all﻿—I’ve got the knack of concentration when I’m thinking about business affairs. But although I wasn’t listening intentionally, the whole conversation was getting in at my ear while I was thinking about other things. I suppose I ought to have gone away or let you know I was there; but the fact is, I’d just got to a point where I was seeing my way through a rather knotty tangle, and I didn’t want to break my chain of thought. I wasn’t eavesdropping, you understand?”
===Chinese===
“您也许不知道，蔡斯沃特小姐，”福斯接着说，“您家这座温室花园，简直像座耳语廊（whispering-gallery）。尽管我离你们那一伙坐得相当远，你们的说话声却清清楚楚地传到我坐的地方。这丝毫没有搅扰我——我琢磨生意上的事时，自有凝神聚念的本事。只是，虽说我并非有意偷听，那一整场谈话还是一边往我耳朵里灌，一边我脑子里想着别的事。按理我应当走开，或者让你们知道我在那儿；可实情是，我刚进行到一处节骨眼上，正看准了门道、要解开一团相当棘手的乱麻，不想半截打断自己的思路。我不是存心偷听，这层二位明白么？”

===Original===
“Yes?” repeated Sir Clinton, with a slight acidity in his tone. “And then?”

But the American failed to take the hint. Evidently he laid great stress on explaining exactly how things had fallen out.
===Chinese===
“是么？”克林顿爵士重复了一遍，语调里透出些微尖酸。“后来呢？”

可这位美国人没接这个茬。明摆着，他极看重把事情原委一五一十交代清楚。

===Original===
“After a while,” he went on, with an evident effort to be accurate, “Miss Chacewater and someone else left the party.”

“Quite true,” Joan confirmed. “We went to play billiards.”

The American nodded.
===Chinese===
“过了一阵，”他继续说道，明显下着力求精确的功夫，“蔡斯沃特小姐和另一位离席走了。”

“一点不假，”琼证实道。“我们去打台球了。”

这位美国人点了点头。

===Original===
“When you had gone,” he continued, “someone else joined the party﻿—a red-haired young man whom they called Foxy.”

Sir Clinton glanced at Joan.

“That’s Foxton Polegate,” Joan explained. “He’s a neighbour of ours. He made these electrotypes of the medallions for us.”
===Chinese===
“你们二位走后，”他接着说，“又有一个人加入进来——一个红头发的年轻人，他们管他叫福克西。”

克林顿爵士瞥了琼一眼。

“那是福克斯顿·波尔盖特，”琼解释道。“是我们的邻居。徽章的那些电铸复制品，就是他替我们做的。”

===Original===
Foss waited patiently till she had finished her interjection. Then he resumed his narrative.

“Shortly after that, my ear caught the sound of my own name. Naturally my attention was attracted, quite without any intention on my part. It’s only natural to prick up your ears when you hear your own name mentioned.”

He looked apologetically at them both as if asking them to condone his conduct.
===Chinese===
福斯耐心等她把这句插话说完，才重新拾起话头。

“那之后没多久，我的耳朵便捕捉到了我自己的名字。我的注意力自然而然被吸引了过去，全无半点故意的成分。听见有人提起自己的名字，谁都会竖起耳朵来，人之常情嘛。”

他满怀歉意地望了望他们两位，仿佛请求他们宽宥他这一番行径。

===Original===
“The next thing I heard﻿—without listening intentionally, you understand?﻿—was ‘Medusa Medallions.’ Now, as you know, I’ve been sent over here by Mr. Kessock to see if I can arrange to buy these medallions from Mr. Chacewater. It’s my duty to my employer to get to know all I can about them. I wouldn’t be earning my money if I spared any trouble in the work which has been put into my hands. So when I heard the name of the medallions mentioned, I﻿ ﻿… frankly, I listened with both ears. It seemed to me my duty to Mr. Kessock to do so.”
===Chinese===
“接下来我听到的——并非有意去听，您明白吧？——是‘美杜莎徽章’几个字。诸位知道，我是受凯索克先生差遣过海来的，看看能不能设法从蔡斯沃特先生手里买下这几枚徽章。把徽章的底细打探个通透，是我对雇主应尽的本分。交到我手里的事，我若怕麻烦、惜力气，这份钱便挣得问心有愧。所以一听见有人提起徽章的名目，我……恕我直言，我便两只耳朵一齐支了起来。在我看来，如此行事，才算对凯索克先生尽了职分。”

===Original===
He looked appealingly at their faces as though to plead for a favourable verdict on his conduct.

“Go on, please,” Sir Clinton requested.
===Chinese===
他恳切地望着两张脸，仿佛恳求对他的行为下一句有利的判词。

“请往下讲，”克林顿爵士要求道。

===Original===
“I hardly expected you’d look on it as I do,” Foss confessed rather shamefacedly. “Of course, it was just plain eavesdropping on my part by that time. But I felt Mr. Kessock would have expected me to find out all I could about these medallions. To be candid, I’d do the same again; though I didn’t like doing it.”

Sir Clinton seemed to feel that he had been rather discouraging.

“I shouldn’t make too much of it, Mr. Foss. What happened next?”
===Chinese===
“我原不敢指望二位会同我一般看待这事，”福斯面带愧色地招认。“当然，到了那步田地，我这已经是彻头彻尾的偷听。可我觉得，凯索克先生必定指望我把这几枚徽章的底细摸个通透。说句实话，再来一回我还是会照办；尽管我并不乐意干这种事。”

克林顿爵士似乎自觉方才太不给人留情面。

“这事儿不必过分放在心上，福斯先生。后来怎样？”

===Original===
Foss’s face showed that he was at last coming to a matter of real difficulty.

“It’s rather unfortunate that I came to be mixed up in the thing at all,” he said, with obvious chagrin. “I can assure you, Miss Chacewater, that I don’t like doing it. I only made up my mind to tell you about it because it seems to me to give a chance of hushing this supposed burglary up quietly before there’s any talk goes round.”
===Chinese===
福斯的脸色表明，他终于说到了真正的难处。
