“我竟会被卷进这桩事里，实在相当不幸，”他说，懊恼之色溢于言表。“我向您担保，蔡斯沃特小姐，我并不乐意这样办。我之所以拿定主意说出来，只因依我看，这还来得及趁闲话未曾传开，把这桩所谓的盗窃案悄没声儿地捂过去。”

===Original===
“*Supposed* burglary,” exclaimed Joan. “What’s your idea of a real burglary, if this sort of thing is only a supposed one?”

She indicated the shattered showcase and the litter of glass on the floor.
===Chinese===
“‘*所谓*的盗窃案，’琼失声道。‘这种事都只算所谓的，那在您看来，什么样才算真正的盗窃？’”

她指了指砸碎的展柜和满地狼藉的玻璃碴。

===Original===
Foss evidently decided to take the rest of his narrative in a rush.

“I’ll tell you,” he said. “The next thing I overheard was a complete plan for a fake burglary﻿—a practical joke﻿—to be carried out tonight. The light in here was to be put out; the houselights were to be extinguished: and in the darkness, your brother and this Mr. Foxy How-d’you-call-him were to get away with the medallions.”
===Chinese===
福斯显然打定主意，剩下的话索性一股脑儿倒完。

“我这就奉告，”他说。“我接下来无意中听到的，是一整套假盗窃的完整计划——一场恶作剧——定在今晚实施。这屋里的灯要弄灭；全宅的灯火要掐断：然后趁着黑暗，令兄同这位福克西——怎么称呼来着——先生，便把徽章弄到手。”

===Original===
“Ah, Mr. Foss, now you become interesting,” Sir Clinton acknowledged.

“I heard all the details,” Foss went on. “How Miss Rainhill was to see to extinguishing the lights; how Mr. Chacewater was to secure the keeper; and how meanwhile his friend was to put on a thick glove and take the medallions out of the case there. And it seems to me that it was a matter that interested me directly,” he added, dropping his air of apology, “for I gathered that the whole affair was planned with some idea of making this sale to Mr. Kessock fall through at the last moment.”
===Chinese===
“啊，福斯先生，您这下子可讲到有意思的地方了，”克林顿爵士颔首道。

“详情我一概听见了，”福斯继续道。“雷恩希尔小姐如何负责掐灯灭火；蔡斯沃特先生如何制住管理员；他的朋友又如何趁这个当口戴上厚手套、把徽章从那口柜子里取出来。而且依我看，这桩事同我有直接的利害，”他补上一句，卸下了那副告罪的架势，“因为我听得出，整件事的盘算里，便存着几分要在最后关头搅黄这桩卖给凯索克先生的买卖的意思。”

===Original===
“Indeed?”

Sir Clinton’s face showed that at last he saw something more clearly than before.

“That was the motive,” Foss continued. “Now the whole thing put me in a most awkward position.”
===Chinese===
“哦？”

克林顿爵士的神色表明，他终于把某些事情看得比先前清楚了一层。

“那便是动机，”福斯接着说。“这么一来，整桩事便把我架在了一个万分尴尬的位置上。”

===Original===
“I think I see your difficulty,” Sir Clinton assured him, with more geniality than he had hitherto shown.

“It was very hard to make up my mind what to do,” Foss went on. “I’m a guest here. This was a family joke, apparently﻿—one brother taking a rise out of another. It was hardly for me to step in and perhaps cause bad feelings between them. I thought the whole thing was perhaps just talk﻿—not meant seriously in the end. A kind of ‘how-would-we-do-it-if-we-set-about-it’ discussion, you understand.”
===Chinese===
“我想我明白您的难处了，”克林顿爵士向他宽解，语气比先前和悦了许多。

“要拿定主意怎么办，实在难上加难，”福斯继续说。“我是府上的客人。这明摆着是场家宅玩笑——兄弟之间一个捉弄另一个。我实在不便插手其间，闹得他们兄弟失和。我当时想，整件事兴许只是嘴上说说——末了未必当真。就是那种‘咱们真要下手，会怎么个干法’的空谈，您明白吧。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton nodded understandingly.

“Difficult to know what to do, in your shoes, undoubtedly.”

Foss was obviously relieved by the Chief Constable’s comprehension.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士会意地点了点头。

“换在您的境地，进退确实难决，毫无疑问。”

郡警察局长这般体谅，福斯显然松了口气。

===Original===
“I thought it over,” he continued, with a less defensive tone in his voice, “and it seemed to me that the soundest course was to let sleeping dogs lie﻿—to let them lie, at any rate, until they woke up and bit somebody. I made up my mind I’d say nothing about the matter at all, unless something really did happen.”

“Very judicious,” Sir Clinton acquiesced.
===Chinese===
“我前思后想，”他继续说道，话音里自我辩解的意味淡了些，“依我看，最稳妥的路子便是莫惹睡狗——至少由它睡下去，等它自己醒了、咬了人再说。我拿定主意，这事只字不提，除非当真出了什么事。”

“极有分寸，”克林顿爵士表赞同。

===Original===
“Then came tonight,” Foss resumed. “Their plan went through. I don’t know what success they had﻿—the house is full of all sorts of rumours. But I heard that the Chief Constable was on the spot and was taking up the case himself; and as soon as I heard that, I felt I ought to tell what I knew. So I hunted you out, so as to avoid your taking any steps before you knew just how the land lay. It’s only a practical joke and not a crime at all. I don’t know anything about your English laws, and I was afraid you might be taking some steps, doing something or other that would make it impossible to stop short of the whole affair coming out in public. I’m sure the family wouldn’t like that.”
===Chinese===
“接着便到了今晚，”福斯重新讲起。“他们的计划付诸实施了。成效如何我不得而知——满宅子沸沸扬扬，什么传言都有。可我听说郡警察局长就在现场，还亲自接手了案子；我一听得这话，便觉得我该把知道的讲出来。所以我四处寻您，免得您在摸清底细之前先采取了什么步骤。这不过是场恶作剧，压根儿算不上罪案。贵国的法律我一窍不通，我怕您也许正在采取步骤、办着什么事儿，以致到头来非把这桩事全盘张扬出去不可。我敢说，府上上下谁也不愿见到那步田地。”

===Original===
He glanced at Joan’s face, but evidently found nothing very encouraging in her expression.

“It’s been a most unfortunate position for me,” he complained.

Sir Clinton took pity on him.

“It was very good of you to give me these facts,” he said with more cordiality than he had hitherto shown. “You’ve cleared up the thing and saved us from putting our foot in it badly, perhaps. Thanks very much for your trouble, Mr. Foss. You’ve been of great assistance.”
===Chinese===
他瞥了一眼琼的脸，可显然没从她的神情里寻到半点鼓舞。

“我这处境实在倒霉透顶，”他抱怨道。

克林顿爵士对他动了恻隐之心。

“您肯把这些实情告诉我，实在难得，”他说，态度比先前恳切得多。“您把事情理清了，兴许还使我们免于结结实实捅一回娄子。多谢您费心，福斯先生。您帮了大忙。”

===Original===
His tone showed that the interview was at an end; but, tactfully, as though to spare the obviously ruffled feelings of the American, he accompanied him to the door. When Foss had left the room, Sir Clinton turned back to Joan.

“Well, Joan, what about it?”
===Chinese===
他的口气表明，接谈到此为止；不过他很识趣地——像是顾全这位美国人显然已受挫的体面——把他送到了门口。福斯出屋之后，克林顿爵士转回琼这边。

“怎么样，琼，你怎么看？”

===Original===
“Oh, it sounds accurate enough,” Joan admitted, though there was an undercurrent of resentment in her tone. “Foss couldn’t have known what sort of person Foxy is; and it’s as clear as daylight that Foxy was at the bottom of this. He’s a silly ass who’s always playing practical jokes.”

She paused for a moment. Then relief showed itself in her voice as she added:

“It’s rather a blessing to know the whole affair has been just spoof, isn’t it? You can hush it up easily enough, can’t you? Nobody need know exactly what happened; and then we’ll be all right. If this story comes out, all our little family bickerings will be common talk; and one doesn’t want that. I’m not exactly proud of the way Maurice has been treating Cecil.”
===Chinese===
“哦，听着倒够确实的，”琼承认道，只是话音底下潜着一股怨气。“福斯哪里知道福克西是号什么样的人；明摆着，这事的根子在福克西身上。他是个成天价搞恶作剧的蠢货。”

她顿了片刻。接着，如释重负之意浮上了她的声音：

“得知这整桩事不过是一场哄弄，实在是一件幸事，对吧？您轻轻松松就能把它压下去，不是吗？谁也不必知道究竟出了什么事；这样我们就都太平了。这事一旦传扬出去，我们家里那些小小的龃龉就要变成人人嘴边的谈资；谁也不想那样。莫里斯向来对待塞西尔的那套做法，我可说不上有什么光彩。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton’s face showed that he understood her position; but, rather to her surprise, he gave no verbal assurance.

“It *is* all right!” she demanded.

“I think we’ll interview your friend Foxy first of all,” Sir Clinton proposed, taking no notice of her inquiry.

Going to the door, he gave some orders to the keeper.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士的神情表明，他体谅她的处境；可让她相当意外的是，他没有给一句口头上的保证。

“这*当然*没事！”她追问。

“我想，咱们还是先见一见你那位朋友福克西，”克林顿爵士提议道，全不理会她的追问。

他走到门口，向管理员交代了几句。

===Original===
“You were rather stiff with our good Mr. Foss,” he said, turning to Joan as he closed the door again. “What would you have done yourself, if you’d been in his position?”

Joan had her answer ready.

“I suppose he couldn’t help overhearing things; but when this affair came to light, I think if I’d been in his shoes I’d have gone to Cecil instead of coming to us with the tale. Once Cecil found the game was up, he’d have been able to return the medallions in some way or other, without raising any dust.”
===Chinese===
“你对咱们那位好福斯先生，未免太生硬了点，”他掩上房门，转身对琼说道。“换你处在他那个位置，你自己会怎么办？”

琼早备好了答案。

“我想，无意之间听见些什么，那怪不得他；可这事一旦败露，要是我处在他那步田地，我就去找塞西尔，而不是揣着这套话跑来讲给我们听。塞西尔一见把戏拆穿，总归有法子把徽章送回去，不惊不扰。”

===Original===
“That was one way, certainly.”

“What I object to is Foss coming to you,” Joan explained. “He didn’t know you’re an old friend of ours. All he knew was that you were the Chief Constable. So off he hies to you, posthaste, to give the whole show away; when he might quite well have come to me or gone to Cecil. I don’t like this way of doing things﻿—no tact at all.”
===Chinese===
“那固然是一种办法。”

“我不赞成的是福斯跑来向您告状，”琼解释道。“他不知道您是我们的老朋友。他只当您是郡警察局长。于是他火烧火燎地奔了您来，把整台戏都抖搂出去；其实他满可以来找我，或者去找塞西尔。我不喜欢这种办事的路数——一点分寸也没有。”

===Original===
“I can’t conceive how Cecil came to take up a silly prank like this,” said Sir Clinton. “It’s a schoolboy’s trick.”

“You don’t know everything,” said Joan, in defence of her brother.

“I know a good deal, Joan,” Sir Clinton retorted in a decisive tone. “Perhaps I know more than you think about this business.”
===Chinese===
“我实在想不明白，塞西尔怎么会沾手这样一场蠢恶作剧，”克林顿爵士说。“这是学童的淘气勾当。”

“您并非什么都知道，”琼说，护着她哥哥。

“我知道的可不少，琼，”克林顿爵士斩钉截铁地回道。“关于这桩事，我知道的兴许比你以为的还多。”

===Original===
In a few minutes the keeper knocked at the door.

“Well?” demanded Sir Clinton, opening it.

“I can’t find Mr. Polegate anywhere, sir,” Mold reported. “No one’s seen him; and he’s not in the house.”
===Chinese===
不多几分钟，管理员来叩门。

“怎么样？”克林顿爵士开了门，问道。

“哪儿都寻不着波尔盖特先生，长官，”莫尔德回禀道。“没人见过他；他也不在宅子里。”

===Original===
“He was here tonight,” Joan declared. “I recognized him when I was dancing with him. You can’t mistake that shock of hair; and of course his voice gave him away when he spoke.”

Sir Clinton did not seem perturbed.

“Bring Mr. Cecil, Mold,” he ordered, and locked the door again as the keeper went off on his fresh errand.
===Chinese===
“他今晚分明在，”琼断言。“我同他跳舞的时候就认出他来了。那么一头乱蓬蓬的红发，错不了；他一开口，那嗓音自然更瞒不住人。”

克林顿爵士并不见慌乱。

“去把塞西尔先生请来，莫尔德，”他吩咐道；管理员领了这趟新差出去后，他又把门锁上了。

===Original===
This task Mold completed in a very short time. Sir Clinton opened at his knock and Cecil Chacewater came into the museum. He was dressed as a Swiss admiral and behind him came Una Rainhill in the costume of Cleopatra.

Sir Clinton wasted no time in preliminaries.

“I’ve sent for you, Cecil, because I want to know exactly what part you played in this business tonight.”
===Chinese===
这趟差事，莫尔德顷刻办妥。克林顿爵士闻叩开门，塞西尔·蔡斯沃特走进博物馆。他扮的是一位瑞士海军上将，身后跟着一身克娄巴特拉装束的尤娜·雷恩希尔。

克林顿爵士不在客套上虚耗工夫。

“我把你请了来，塞西尔，是因为我要确切知道，今晚这桩事里你担当的是什么角色。”

===Original===
Cecil Chacewater opened his eyes in astonishment.

“You seem to be a bit of a super-sleuth! How did you spot us so quickly?”

Quite obviously Cecil was not greatly perturbed at being found out, as Sir Clinton noted with a certain relief. So far as he was concerned, the thing had been only a prank.
===Chinese===
塞西尔·蔡斯沃特惊讶得睁大了眼睛。

“您倒真有几分超级神探的本事！怎么这么快就盯上我们了？”

明摆着，塞西尔对被拆穿并不怎么惶恐，克林顿爵士看在眼里，倒松了口气。就他而论，这事自始至终不过一场胡闹。

===Original===
“Tell me exactly what happened after you came in here before the lights went out,” the Chief Constable demanded in a curt tone.

Cecil glanced at Una. Sir Clinton caught the look.

“We know all about Miss Rainhill’s part in the affair,” he explained bluntly.
===Chinese===
“原原本本告诉我，灯灭之前你进来之后都出了什么事，”郡警察局长简促地命令道。

塞西尔瞟了尤娜一眼。克林顿爵士捕捉到了这道目光。

“雷恩希尔小姐在这桩事里担任的角色，我们已经一清二楚，”他直截了当地说明。

===Original===
“Oh, in that case,” said Cecil, “there’s no particular reason why I should keep back anything. Una, Foxy, and I planned it between us. I take full responsibility for that. I wanted to upset this sale, if I could. I’m not ashamed of that.”

“I know all about that,” Sir Clinton pointed out, coldly. “What I wish to know is exactly what happened after you came in here to steal these medallions.”

Cecil seemed impressed by the Chief Constable’s tone.
===Chinese===
“哦，既然如此，”塞西尔说，“我就没什么特别的理由再隐瞒了。是我、尤娜和福克西三个一起谋划的。这个我全盘担待。我当时想，只要办得到，就搅黄这笔买卖。这一点我不觉得羞耻。”

“这些我全知道了，”克林顿爵士冷冷点破。“我要知道的是，你进来偷这几枚徽章之后，究竟出了什么事。”

郡警察局长这副口吻，似乎把塞西尔镇住了。

===Original===
“I’ll tell you, then. We’ve nothing to conceal. I came in here at about twenty to twelve and sauntered about the room, pretending to look at the cases as if I’d never seen them before. My part was to mark down Mold and prevent him interfering.”

Sir Clinton nodded to show that he knew all this.
===Chinese===
“那我就说吧。我们没什么可遮掩的。十一点四十前后，我进了这里，在屋里信步闲逛，装作端详那些展柜，好像生平头一回见着似的。我分担的角色是盯死莫尔德，不叫他碍事。”

克林顿爵士点了点头，示意这些他已尽知。

===Original===
“Rather before I expected it, the light went out. Oh, there was a shot fired just then. I didn’t understand that part of it, but I supposed that Foxy had brought a pistol with him and fired a blank cartridge just to add a touch of interest to the affair. It wasn’t on the bill of fare, so I imagine it must have been one of these last-minute improvements. Anyhow, I did my part of the business: jumped on Mold and held him while Foxy got away with the stuff. Then, when he’d had time to get away, I let Mold go and made a beeline for the door myself. I could swear no one spotted me in the dark, and I was well mixed up in the mob before the lights went on again.”
===Chinese===
“灯灭了，比我预料的早了些。哦，那时还响了一枪。那一段我没有弄明白，只当是福克西随身带了手枪，放了一发空包弹，给这桩事添些趣味。原定的节目单上并没有这一出，所以我琢磨，多半是临场添的彩头。不管怎样，我把我那份活儿干完了：扑上去按住莫尔德，让福克西带着东西脱身。然后，估摸他跑得够远了，我放开莫尔德，自己也直奔门口而去。我可以起誓，黑暗里没有人瞧见我，灯再亮起来之前，我早已混进人堆里了。”

===Original===
“Did you pay particular attention to what Polegate was doing while you were busy with the keeper?”

“No. Mold gave me all I wanted in the way of trouble.”

“You’re sure it was Mold you got hold of? You didn’t make any mistake?”

Cecil reflected for a moment.

“I don’t see how I could have gripped the wrong man. I’d marked him down while the light was on.”
===Chinese===
“你制着管理员那阵子，有没有格外留意波尔盖特在干什么？”

“没有。光是莫尔德一个，就够我招架的了。”

“你拿住的确乎是莫尔德？没弄错人？”

塞西尔思忖了片刻。

“我看不可能抓错了人。灯亮着的时候，我就把他盯死了。”

===Original===
“Can you remember anything about sounds of breaking glass?”

Cecil pondered before replying.

“It seemed to me that there was a lot of glass-breaking﻿—more than I’d expected. The light was hardly out before there was a smash and tinkle all over the place. Foxy must have got to work quicker than I’d allowed for. And I remember hearing quite a lot of hammering and smashing going on after that, as if he’d found it difficult to make a big enough hole in the glass of the case. I thought he’d bungled the business, and it was all I could do to keep my grip on Mold long enough to get the thing safely through.”
===Chinese===
“碎玻璃的声响，你能记起什么吗？”

塞西尔沉吟了一阵才作答。

“依我听来，碎玻璃的动静着实不少——远超我的预想。灯几乎才灭，四下里便响起一片砸碎声、叮当声。福克西动手必定比我预算的快。而且我记得，那之后还有好一通敲砸捶打，仿佛他在柜子玻璃上开一个够大的洞颇费了些力气。我还当他把事情办砸了；为把这一局稳稳当当送过去，我死死按住莫尔德、撑足工夫，已是使尽了全力。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton dismissed that part of the subject. He turned to Una.

“Now, Miss Rainhill, I believe your part in the affair was to pull out the main switch of the house?”

“Yes,” Una admitted, looking rather surprised at the extent of his knowledge.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士把这一层撇开，转向尤娜。

“那么，雷恩希尔小姐，我记得你在这桩事里的差事，是拉掉全宅的总闸？”

“是的，”尤娜承认道，对他知道得如此之细相当惊讶。

===Original===
“Did you carry out your part of the arrangement punctually, or were you late in getting the current off?”

“I pulled out the switch to the very second. I had my hand on it and my eye on my wristwatch; and when it came to 11:45 I jerked it out and the lights went off. I was absolutely right to a second, I’m sure.”

“And you thought Miss Rainhill had been a shade before her time, Cecil?”

“So it seemed to me. I hadn’t a chance of looking at my watch; and of course after the lights went off I couldn’t spare time to look.”
===Chinese===
“你那份差事办得准时吗，还是断电流断晚了？”

“我拉闸拉得分秒不差。我一手搭在闸上，一眼盯着腕表；一到 11:45，我便一把拉下，灯应声而灭。我敢说，准得连一秒都不差。”

“而你觉得雷恩希尔小姐拉得略早了一线，塞西尔？”

“我感着是那样。我没腾出工夫看表；灯灭之后，自然更抽不出空看了。”

===Original===
At this moment another knock came to the door and Foxy Polegate burst into the museum. Sir Clinton noticed that he was masquerading as a Harlequin.

“Heard you’d been asking for me, Sir Clinton,” he broke out as he came into the room. “Seems the keeper had been inquiring for me. So I came along as soon as I heard about it.”

He glanced inquisitively at Cecil and Una, as though wondering what they were doing there.
===Chinese===
正当此时，门上又是一叩，福克西·波尔盖特一阵风似的闯进博物馆。克林顿爵士注意到，他扮的是哈里昆（Harlequin）。

“听说您一直在找我，克林顿爵士，”他一进屋便嚷开了。“好像是那位管理员一直在打听我。所以我一得着信儿就赶过来了。”

他好奇地瞟了瞟塞西尔和尤娜，像是纳闷他们两位在这儿做什么。

===Original===
Sir Clinton wasted no words.

“The medallions, Mr. Polegate, please.”

Foxy made a very good pretence of astonishment at the demand; but Cecil cut him short.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士半句废话不叙。

“请把徽章交出来，波尔盖特先生。”

对这个要求，福克西把一出惊讶的好戏演了个十足；可塞西尔一句话截断了他。

===Original===
“You may as well hand them over, Foxy. They seem to know all about the joke.”

“Oh, they do, do they?” Foxy exclaimed. “They seem to have been mighty swift about it. That little joke’s gone astray, evidently.”

He seemed completely taken aback by the exposure.

“The medallions?” he repeated. “I’ll get ’em for you in a jiffy.”
===Chinese===
“你索性交出来吧，福克西。这玩笑的底细，他们像是全知道了。”

“哦，全知道了，是吗？”福克西失声道。“他们办起事来可真够麻利的。瞧这光景，那个小玩笑是走岔了道儿。”

被当面拆穿，他显得全然措手不及。

“徽章？”他重复了一遍。“我一眨眼的工夫就给您取来。”

===Original===
He walked across to the showcase, fumbled for a moment at the flat base near one of the legs, and from below this he drew out three medallions.

“Stuck ’em there with plasticine as soon as I’d got ’em. After that anyone would have turned out my pockets if they’d wanted, see?”

Sir Clinton held out his hand and took the medallions from Foxy. For a moment or two he examined them, then he passed them to Cecil.
===Chinese===
他走到展柜跟前，在一条柜腿近旁的平底座上摸索片刻，从那底下摸出了三枚徽章。

“一到手，我就用橡皮泥把它们粘在了那儿。这么着，往后谁爱翻我口袋只管翻，明白吧？”

克林顿爵士伸出手，从福克西手里接过徽章，端详了一两刻，随即递给塞西尔。

===Original===
“Have you any way of telling easily whether these are the real things or the replicas?”

Cecil inspected them one by one with minute care.

“These are the real things,” he announced. “What else could they be?”

“You’ve no doubt about it?” questioned Sir Clinton.

“Not a bit,” Cecil assured him. “When Foxy made the replicas, my father had a tiny hole﻿—just a dot﻿—drilled in the edge of each electrotype so as to distinguish the real things from the sham. There are no holes here; so these are the real Leonardos.”
===Chinese===
“有没有便捷的法子，分辨这些是真品还是复制品？”

塞西尔逐枚细验，一丝不苟。

“这些是真品，”他宣布。“还能是别的什么？”

“你毫无疑虑？”克林顿爵士问。

“一丝也不疑，”塞西尔向他保证。“福克西做复制品那会儿，我父亲叫人在每枚电铸件的边缘钻了一个小孔——针尖大的一个点——好把真品同赝品区分开来。这几枚上没有孔；所以它们是真正的列奥纳多。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton swung round suddenly on Foxy.

“Now, Mr. Polegate,” he said, sternly, “you’ve given a lot of trouble with this silly joke of yours. I’m not concerned with your taste in humour, or I might say a few things you wouldn’t care to hear. But you can repair the damage to some extent if you give me a frank account of your doings in here tonight. I want the whole story, please.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士霍地转身，逼向福克西。

“听着，波尔盖特先生，”他严词道，“你这场蠢玩笑，惹出的乱子着实不少。你的幽默品位不归我管，不然我倒要说几句你不爱听的话。不过，你若肯把今晚你在这屋里的所作所为坦白讲来，还能把损失挽回几分。我要听全部经过，请吧。”

===Original===
Foxy was evidently completely taken aback by Sir Clinton’s tone.

“Come, we’re waiting. There’s no time to lose,” Sir Clinton said, curtly, as Foxy seemed to hesitate. Joan and the others showed by their faces that they could not quite understand the reason for the Chief Constable’s asperity.

“We planned that﻿ ﻿…”

“I know all about that,” said Sir Clinton, brusquely. “Begin at the point where you came in here at twenty to twelve or so.”

Foxy pulled himself together. The Chief Constable’s manner was not encouraging.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士这副口吻，显然把福克西彻底镇住了。

“快，我们等着呢。一刻也耽误不起，”福克西面现迟疑，克林顿爵士简促地说。琼同其余几人脸上都露出不解之色：郡警察局长这股疾言厉色，所为何来。

“我们盘算的是……”

“那些我全知道了，”克林顿爵士生硬地说。“从十一点四十前后你进这屋里讲起。”

福克西定了定神。郡警察局长这副做派可算不上鼓舞人心。

===Original===
“I came in here as arranged, and worked my way over to the central case there﻿—slowly, so as not to attract the keeper’s attention. One or two other people were hanging round it then, too. I remember noticing a chap in a white Pierrot costume alongside me. Suddenly there was a pistol-shot and the light went out according to plan.”

“How do you account for the pistol-shot?” demanded Sir Clinton.
===Chinese===
“我按约定进了这里，慢慢挪到那边中央展柜跟前——脚下放缓，免得引管理员留意。当时柜边还闲晃着一两位旁人。我记得注意到身旁有个穿白色皮埃罗戏服的家伙。忽然一声枪响，灯按计划灭了。”

“那一声枪响，你怎么解释？”克林顿爵士追问。

===Original===
“Try next door,” said Foxy. “I thought it was a fancy tip that Cecil had thrown in at the last moment. It wasn’t in the book of words.”

“You were ready to get to work when the light went out?” inquired Sir Clinton.

Foxy considered for a moment.

“It took me rather by surprise,” he admitted. “I’d counted on having at least another minute, according to the timetable.”

“What happened next? Be careful now.”
===Chinese===
“这个嘛，您上隔壁问去吧，”福克西说。“我当时只当是塞西尔临了甩进去的花点子。原来的戏本子里可没这一段。”

“灯一灭，你就准备动手了？”克林顿爵士询问。

福克西寻思了一下。

“这倒让我有些措手不及，”他承认。“照时刻表算，我原指望至少还富余一分钟。”

“接下来呢？这回仔细说。”

===Original===
“As soon as the light went out, I pulled on a thick pair of gloves and got a bit of lead pipe out of my slapstick. But there was a bit of a scuffle in the dark round the showcase, and someone must have put their elbow through the glass. I heard it go crash in the dark. I shoved along till I was opposite the medallion section of the case﻿—luckily someone made way for me just then﻿—and I got to work with my lead pipe. The glass smashed easily﻿—it must have been cracked before. So I put my hand in and groped about. I could find only three medallions instead of six; but I hooked them out, slabbed on some plasticine, stuck them under the case for future reference, and cut my stick for the door. Someone was ahead of me there, and I heard some sort of mix-up in the dark. Then I wandered out into the garden by the east door, as soon as I could find it in the dark. And I’ve been out there having a smoke till now. When I came in again, I heard you’d been asking for me, so I came along.”
===Chinese===
“灯一灭，我戴上一副厚手套，从滑稽棒（slapstick）里抽出一截铅管。可黑暗里展柜周围有一小阵推搡混乱，准是谁用胳膊肘把玻璃捅穿了。我听见它在黑暗里哗啦一声碎响。我挤着挪到正对柜中徽章那一格的位置——幸亏那时有人正好让开——便抡起铅管干了起来。玻璃一砸便碎——先前必定已有了裂纹。于是我伸手进去四下摸索。六枚徽章，我统共只摸到三枚；我把它们钩了出来，抹上些橡皮泥，粘到柜子底下以备后用，便撒腿朝门口开溜。那儿有人抢在我前头，黑暗里我还听见一阵纠缠混乱。随后我摸到东门，一寻着便遛达到花园里去了。我在外头抽烟，一直抽到方才。再进来的时候，听说您一直在找我，我就过来了。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton considered for a moment.

“I want to be quite clear on one point,” he said with no relaxation of his manner. “You say that you heard the glass crack before you began your work. Are you certain of that?”

“Quite,” said Foxy.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士思量了片刻。

“有一点，我须弄得一清二楚，”他说，神色没有半分松缓。“你说，动手之前你就听见玻璃碎了。这一点你拿得准吗？”

“拿得准，”福克西说。

===Original===
“And when you got your hand into the case you could find only three medallions?”

“That was all. I was groping for the top row of the six; and naturally it surprised me when I felt only three altogether. I’m quite certain about it.”

“So you were evidently the second thief at the case tonight?” Sir Clinton concluded.

Foxy flushed at the word “thief” but a glance at the face of the Chief Constable evidently persuaded him that it would be best not to argue on philology at that moment. He contented himself with nodding sullenly in response to Sir Clinton’s remark.
===Chinese===
“还有，你把手伸进柜子之后，只摸到了三枚徽章？”

“就那些。我是照着上排六枚去摸的；一摸统共只有三枚，自然吃了一惊。这一点我确凿无疑。”

“这么说，今晚在柜前，你显然是第二个贼了？”克林顿爵士下了断语。

“贼”字一出，福克西涨红了脸；可看一眼郡警察局长的脸色，他显然认定，此刻还是别在字眼的训诂上多作争辩为妙。他只闷闷地点了点头，算是应了克林顿爵士这话。

===Original===
Joan relieved the tension.

“Anyhow, we’ve got the medallions safe, and that’s all that really matters,” she pointed out. “Let’s have a look at them, Cecil.”

She took them from his hand and scrutinized them carefully.

“Yes, these are the real Leonardos,” she affirmed, without hesitation. “That’s all right.”

“Quite all right,” admitted Sir Clinton, with a wry smile, “except for one point: Why were the replicas stolen and the real things left untouched?”
===Chinese===
琼把这一触即发的紧绷缓了下来。

“不管怎么说，徽章安然寻回，这才是真正要紧的，”她指出。“塞西尔，给我瞧瞧。”

她从他手里接过徽章，仔细端详。

“没错，这些是真品列奥纳多，”她毫不迟疑地断言。“这就妥了。”

“相当妥了，”克林顿爵士苦笑着承认，“只除了一点：何以复制品遭了窃，真品反倒安然无恙？”

===Original===
“That certainly seems to need explaining,” Una admitted. “Can you throw any light on it, Foxy? You’re the only one of us who was near the case.”

There was no hint of accusation in her tone; but Foxy seemed to read an insinuation into her remark.

“I haven’t got the replicas, if that’s what you mean, Una,” he protested angrily. “I just took what was left﻿—and it turns out to be the real things. Whoever was ahead of me took the duds.”
===Chinese===
“这一点确乎需要解释，”尤娜承认。“福克西，你能指点一二吗？咱们中间，只有你一个人挨着柜子。”

她话音里并无半点指摘之意；可福克西似乎从这话里品出了弦外之音。

“尤娜，你要是指这个，我可没拿复制品，”他怒气冲冲地抗辩。“我拿的不过是剩下的——结果还偏是真品。抢在我前头的那个，拿走的才是赝货。”

===Original===
Cecil considered the point, and then appealed to Sir Clinton.

“Doesn’t that seem to show that an outsider’s been at work﻿—someone who knew a certain amount about the collection, but not quite enough? An outsider wouldn’t know we had the replicas in the case alongside the real things. He’d just grab three medallions and think he’d got away with it.”
===Chinese===
塞西尔把这层想了想，随即向克林顿爵士求解。

“这岂不表明，有外来人动过手——一个对藏品略知一二、却又知之未尽的人？外来人不会知道我们把复制品同真品并排放在柜里。他只管抓上三枚，还自以为得手了呢。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton shook his head.

“Your hypothetical outsider, Cecil, must have had a preliminary look at the case before the lights went out﻿—just to make sure of getting to the right spot in the dark. Therefore he must have seen the six medallions there; and he’d have taken the lot instead of only three, when he had his chance.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士摇了摇头。

“你那位假想的外来人，塞西尔，灯灭之前必定先来看过这口柜子——为的是保险起见，黑灯瞎火里一步到位。那么，柜里六枚徽章他必定看见了；一旦机会到手，他会整窝端走，而不是只拿三枚。”

===Original===
“That upsets your applecart, Cecil,” said Joan. “It’s obvious Sir Clinton’s right. Unless”﻿—a fresh idea seemed to strike her﻿—“unless the thief knew of the replicas and had wrong information, so that he imagined he was taking the Leonardos when he really was grabbing the replicas. I mean he may have thought that the replicas were in the top row instead of the lower one.”

She glanced at Sir Clinton’s face to see what he thought of her suggestion; but he betrayed nothing.

“Wouldn’t you have taken the whole six, Joan, if you had been in his shoes?”

Joan had to admit that she would have made certain by snatching the complete set.

“There’s more in it than that,” was all that Sir Clinton could be induced to say.
===Chinese===
“这下你的如意算盘砸了，塞西尔，”琼说。“明摆着克林顿爵士说得对。除非”——她似乎灵光一闪，又生一计——“除非那贼知道有复制品，却得了讹误的信儿，自以为拿走的是列奥纳多，实则抓的是复制品。我是说，他兴许以为复制品放在上排，而不是下排。”

她瞥了克林顿爵士的脸，想看他对此议作何想法；可他丝毫不露口风。

“琼，换你处在他那步田地，难道不会把六枚全拿走？”

琼只得承认，她会把整套尽数掠走，以策万全。

“这背后的名堂不止于此，”克林顿爵士被再三追问，也只肯吐这一句。

===Original===
Before any more could be said, the door opened again. This time it was Michael Clifton who entered the museum.

“You’ve got him, Michael?” cried Joan. “Who was he?”

Michael shook his head.

“He got away from us. It’s a damned mysterious business how he managed it; but he slipped through our fingers, Joan.”

“Well, tell us what happened﻿—quick!” Joan ordered. “I didn’t think you’d botch it, Michael.”

Michael obeyed her at once and launched into an account of the moonlight chase of the fugitive. Sir Clinton listened attentively, but interposed no questions until Michael had finished his story.
===Chinese===
话还没来得及往下说，门又开了。这回进博物馆来的，是迈克尔·克利夫顿。

“抓到他了，迈克尔？”琼喊道。“他是谁？”

迈克尔摇了摇头。

“他脱身了。他怎么逃出去的，真是邪门透顶；反正他从咱们指缝里溜了，琼。”

“那你快讲讲经过——快！”琼下令。“我没想到你会把事情办砸，迈克尔。”

迈克尔当即从命，一口气讲起月色下追捕逃犯的经过。克林顿爵士凝神细听，直到他把故事讲完，才插进一个问题。

===Original===
“Let’s have this quite clear,” the Chief Constable said, when the tale had been completed. “You had him hemmed in at the cliff top; you heard a splash, but there was no sign of anyone swimming in the lake; you discovered a rope tied to the balustrade and lying down the cliff-face to the cave-mouth; he wasn’t in the cave when you looked for him there. Is that correct?”

“That’s how it happened.”

“You’re sure he didn’t break back through your cordon?”

“Certain.”

“And you found Maurice in one of the Fairy Houses in the spinney?”

“Yes. He seemed in a queer state.”
===Chinese===
“咱们把这几点理清楚，”故事讲完，郡警察局长说道。“你们在崖顶把他围住了；你们听见一声水响，湖面上却不见有人泅水；你们发现一条绳子系在栏杆上，顺着崖面垂到洞口；你们到洞里找他，他不在。是这么回事吗？”

“经过就是这样。”

“你敢肯定，他没有回身冲破你们的封锁线？”

“肯定。”

“还有，你们在小树林里一座仙女屋中找到了莫里斯？”

“是的。他那样子有点古怪。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton, glancing at Cecil’s face, was surprised to see on it the same expression of almost malicious glee which he had surprised on the day when they examined that very Fairy House during their walk. Quite obviously Cecil knew something more than the Chief Constable did.

“Does that suggest anything to you, Cecil?” he demanded point-blank.

At the query, Cecil’s face came back to normal suddenly.

“To me? No, why should it?”

“I merely wondered,” said Sir Clinton, without seeming to notice anything.

It was clear that whatever Cecil knew, it was something which he was not prepared to tell.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士瞥了塞西尔的脸，惊讶地看到那上面又是一副几乎称得上幸灾乐祸的得意——同那天两人散步、察看那座仙女屋时被他撞见的神情如出一辙。明摆着，塞西尔知道些郡警察局长所不知道的东西。

“这让你想起什么了吗，塞西尔？”他劈面便问。

这一问之下，塞西尔的脸色霎时恢复了常态。

“我？没有，怎么会呢？”

“不过随口一问，”克林顿爵士说，像是全无所觉。

事情很清楚：不论塞西尔知道什么，那都是他不打算说出来的东西。

===Original===
Foxy had listened intently to Michael’s narrative, and as the Chief Constable seemed to have come to the end of his interrogations, Foxy put a question of his own.

“You say Maurice was wearing a white Pierrot costume? So was the fellow you were chasing. So was the man next me at the case when the lights went out.”

“I suppose you’re suggesting that Maurice is at the bottom of the business, Foxy,” Michael replied at once. “I’ll swallow that if you’ll answer one question. Why should a man burgle his own house?”

“Lord alone knows,” Foxy admitted humbly. “I’ve no brainwave on the subject.”

“It seems rather improbable,” observed Sir Clinton. “I think you’ll have to produce a motive before that idea could be accepted.”
===Chinese===
福克西一直凝神听着迈克尔的叙述；见郡警察局长似乎问完了，福克西提了一个自己的问题。

“你说莫里斯穿的是白色皮埃罗戏服？你们追的那家伙也是。灯灭的时候，柜子边上挨着我的那个人，还是。”

“我猜你是想暗示，这桩事的根子在莫里斯身上，福克西，”迈克尔当即回道。“只要你能答上一个问题，这个说法我便照单全收。一个人为什么要偷自家的宅子？”

“只有天知道，”福克西老老实实地承认。“这上头我可没有什么灵光妙悟。”

“这看上去相当站不住，”克林顿爵士评道。“我看，这个想法要立得住，你们先得拿出一个动机来。”

===Original===
He glanced round at the door as he spoke and added:

“Here’s Maurice himself.”

Maurice Chacewater had entered the room while the Chief Constable was speaking. He had discarded his fancy costume and wore ordinary evening-dress, against the black of which his face looked white and drawn. He came up to the group and leaned on the showcase as if for support.
===Chinese===
说话间，他朝门那边扫了一眼，又加了一句：

“瞧，莫里斯本人这不来了。”

郡警察局长说话那会儿，莫里斯·蔡斯沃特已经进了屋。他脱下了化装服，换了一身寻常晚礼服；衬着一片黑色，他的脸显得苍白而憔悴。他走到众人跟前，倚在展柜上，仿佛要借个支撑。

===Original===
“So you’ve muddled it, Michael,” he commented, after a pause. “You didn’t get your hands on the fellow, after all?”

Dismissing Michael with almost open contempt, he turned to Sir Clinton.

“What’s the damage? Did the fellow get away with anything of value?”
===Chinese===
“这么说，你们把事情搅砸了，迈克尔，”他顿了顿，说道。“到头来，你们还是没能拿住那家伙？”

他把迈克尔撇在一边，鄙夷几乎不加掩饰，随即转向克林顿爵士。

“损失几何？那家伙可卷走了什么值钱的东西？”

===Original===
“Nothing much: only your three replicas of the Leonardo medallions, so far as we can see.”

As he spoke, his glance telegraphed a warning to the rest of the group. It seemed unnecessary that Maurice should know all the ins and outs of the night’s doings.

But Foxy evidently failed to grasp the meaning of the Chief Constable’s look.
===Chinese===
“没什么大不了的：就眼下看来，只丢了你那三枚列奥纳多徽章的复制品。”

说话间，他递给其余众人一个眼色，示警之意昭然。今夜这番勾当的来龙去脉，似乎不必让莫里斯尽知。

可福克西显然没能领会郡警察局长这一瞥的用意。

===Original===
“We saved the real medallions for you, Maurice. Vote of thanks to us, eh?”

“How did you manage that?” Maurice demanded, with no sign of gratitude in his voice.

Quite oblivious of the warning looks thrown at him by the rest of the group, Foxy launched at once into a detailed account of the whole practical joke and its sequel. Maurice listened frowningly to the story. When it was completed, he made no direct comment.
===Chinese===
“我们替你把真徽章保住了，莫里斯。是不是该对我们提案致谢呀，嗯？”

“你们怎么做到的？”莫里斯追问，话音里听不出半点感激。

福克西全然没觉出众人投向他的警告眼色，当即详详细细讲起整场恶作剧及其后文。莫里斯皱着眉听完了这个故事。故事讲完，他没有正面置评。

===Original===
“Who’s got the medallions? You, Joan? I’ll take them.”

When she had handed them over, he scrutinized them carefully.

“These seem to be the Leonardo ones,” he confirmed.

Sir Clinton interposed a question.

“Were the medallions and the replicas in their usual places tonight, Maurice? I mean, were the real things in the top row and the electros down below?”
===Chinese===
“徽章在谁手里？你吗，琼？给我吧。”

她交出徽章之后，他细细验看。

“这几枚看来是列奥纳多真品，”他确认道。

克林顿爵士插进一问。

“今晚徽章和复制品都在平常的位置上吗，莫里斯？我的意思是，真品在上排，电铸复制品在下排？”

===Original===
Maurice gave a curt nod of assent. He weighed the three medallions unconsciously in his hand for a moment, then moved over to the safe in the wall of the museum.

“These things will be safer under lock and key, now,” he said.

He opened the safe, inserted the medallions, closed the safe-door with a clang, and busied himself with the combination of the lock.
===Chinese===
莫里斯简短地点头称是。他无意识地把三枚徽章在手里掂了片刻，随后走向博物馆墙上嵌着的保险柜。

“这些东西，如今锁起来才稳妥，”他说。

他打开保险柜，把徽章放进去，哐当一声关上柜门，接着便忙着拨弄那把密码锁。

===Original===
Before saying anything further, Sir Clinton waited until Maurice had returned to the group.

“There’s one thing,” he said. “I shall have to look into this affair officially now. It’s essential that things shall be left as they are. Especially the place where that fellow gave you the slip, Clifton. Nobody must be wandering about there, up at the spinney, until I’ve done with the ground. There may be clues left, for all one can tell; and we can’t run the risk of their being destroyed.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士没有急着再开口，直等到莫里斯回到众人中间。

“有一件事，”他说。“事到如今，我不得不正式查办这桩案子了。现场务必保持原样，这一点至关紧要。尤其是那家伙甩掉你们的地方，克利夫顿。在我验完那片场地之前，谁也不得到小树林那一带走动。说不准还会留有什么线索；线索被毁的这个险，我们冒不起。”

===Original===
Maurice looked up gloomily.

“Very well. I’ll give orders to the keepers to patrol the wood and turn everyone back. That do?”

“So long as no one sets foot on anything beyond the wood, I’ll be quite satisfied. But it’s important, Maurice. Impress that on your keepers, please.”

Maurice indicated his comprehension with a nod.
===Chinese===
莫里斯阴郁地抬起头来。

“好吧。我吩咐管理员们巡守林子，把人一律挡回去。这样行吗？”

“只要没人踏进林子以外任何一处，我便完全满意了。但这事要紧，莫里斯。请务必向你那些管理员们叮咛到位。”

莫里斯点头，示意领会。

===Original===
“I’ll begin dragging the lakelet up there tomorrow morning,” Sir Clinton added. “Something must have gone into the water to make the splash that was heard; and perhaps we shall find it. I don’t mind anyone going down by the lake side. It’s the top of the cliff that I want kept intact.”

He looked at his watch.

“You’re on the phone here? I must ring up the police in Hincheldene now and make arrangements for tomorrow. Show me your phone, please, Joan. And as I must get some sleep tonight, I’ll say goodbye to the rest of you now. Come along, Ariel. Lead the way.”
===Chinese===
“明天一早，我就开始打捞上面那片小湖，”克林顿爵士补了一句。“既然有人听见了那么一声水响，必有东西落了水；兴许我们能找着它。湖边那一带，谁下去走走我不管。我要原封不动的，是崖顶。”

他看了看表。

“府上通着电话吧？我现在得给欣切尔迪恩（Hincheldene）的警方挂个电话，安排明天的事。请领我去电话那儿，琼。今晚我还得睡上一觉，这就向各位道别了。走吧，爱丽儿，头前带路。”

## VII What Was in the Lake / 第七章 湖中之物

===Original===
“I was afraid of it,” Sir Clinton observed, as he lifted the dripping pole with which he had been sounding the water of the lakelet. “The net will be no good, Inspector. With these spikes of rock jutting up from the bottom all over the place, you couldn’t get a clean sweep; and if there’s anything here at all, it’s pretty sure to have lodged in one of the cavities between the spikes.”

It was the morning after the masked ball at Ravensthorpe. The Chief Constable had made all his arrangements overnight, so that when he reached the shore of the artificial lake, everything was in readiness. The decrepit raft had been strengthened; a large net had been brought for the purpose of dragging the pool; and several grapnels had been procured, in case the net turned out to be useless. Sir Clinton had gone out on the raft to sound the water and discover whether the net could be utilized; but the results had not been encouraging.

Inspector Armadale listened to the verdict with a rather gloomy face.
===Chinese===
“我早就担心会这样，”克林顿爵士（Sir Clinton）说着，提起那根湿淋淋的、方才用来探测小湖水深的竿子。“渔网派不上用场，探长。湖底到处都是一根根戳出来的岩石尖，没法干干净净地拖上一网；这水里要真有什么东西，十有八九卡在岩尖之间的坑洼里了。”

这是雷文索普（Ravensthorpe）假面舞会过后的第二天早晨。郡警察局长头天夜里就把一切安排停当，因此他一到人工湖岸边，万事俱备。那只破旧的木筏已经加固过；为打捞这片池水，还特意运来了一张大网；此外又备下了几只抓钩，以防渔网全然无用。克林顿爵士乘木筏出去测了测水深，想看看渔网能不能派上用场；结果却不乐观。

阿玛代尔探长（Inspector Armadale）听着这个结论，脸色颇为阴沉。

===Original===
“It’s a pity,” he commented regretfully. “Dragging with the grapnel is a kind of hit-or-miss job, Sir Clinton; and it’ll take far longer than working with the net.”

Sir Clinton acquiesced with a gesture.

“We’d better start close in under the cliff-face,” he said. “If anything came down from the top, it can’t have gone far before it sank. One of the people last night was watching the pool and he saw nothing on the surface after the splash, so it ought to be somewhere near the cave-mouth. You can pole over to the shore now, Constable; we’ve done with this part of the business.”

The constable obeyed the order and soon Sir Clinton rejoined the Inspector on the bank.
===Chinese===
“真遗憾，”他不无惋惜地评道。“用抓钩打捞，全凭撞大运，克林顿爵士；比起用网来，可要费事久得多。”

克林顿爵士做了个手势，表示认可。

“咱们最好从紧贴崖壁的地方开始，”他说。“要有什么东西从崖顶掉下来，沉底之前必走不远。昨夜有个人一直盯着这片池水，落水响之后他没看见水面上浮起任何东西，所以那物件沉下的地方应该离洞口不远。你现在可以把筏子撑回岸边去了，警员；这部分活儿咱们做完了。”

那警员遵命照办，不一会儿，克林顿爵士便回到岸上与探长会合。

===Original===
“It’s likely to be a troublesome business,” the Chief Constable admitted as his subordinate came up. “The bottom’s very irregular and the chances are that the grapnel will stick, two times out of three. However, the sooner we get to work, the better.”

He considered for a moment or two.

“Tack a light line to the grapnel as well as the rope. Get the raft out past the cave and let a constable pitch the grapnel in there. Then when you’ve dragged, or if the grapnel sticks, he can pull the hook back again with the light line and start afresh alongside the place where he made the last cast. But it’s likely to be a slow business, as you say.”
===Chinese===
“这活儿怕是相当麻烦，”下属走上前来时，郡警察局长承认道。“湖底高低不平，抓钩很可能三回里有两回要卡住。不过，动手越早越好。”

他思忖了片刻。

“除了缆绳，再给抓钩拴上一根细绳。把木筏撑过山洞那头，让一名警员在那儿把抓钩抛下去。这样拖过一遍之后，或者抓钩卡住的时候，他就能靠细绳把钩子拽回来，在上一次下钩的地方旁边重新开始。不过就像你说的，这活儿怕是快不了。”

===Original===
The Inspector agreed and set his constables to work at once. Sir Clinton withdrew to a little distance, sat down on a small hillock from which he could oversee the dragging operations, and patiently awaited the start of the search. His eyes, wandering with apparent incuriosity over the group at the water’s edge, noted with approval that Armadale was wasting no time.

Having made his instructions clear, the Inspector came over to where the Chief Constable was posted.

“Sit down, Inspector,” Sir Clinton invited. “This may take all day, you know, and it’s as cheap sitting as standing.”

When the Inspector had seated himself, the Chief Constable turned to him with a question.
===Chinese===
探长表示同意，随即把手下的警员们派上了阵。克林顿爵士退开几步，在一座小土丘上坐下——从那儿正好俯瞰打捞作业——耐着性子等候搜索开始。他的目光看似漫不经心地掠过水边那群人，却满意地注意到：阿玛代尔半点工夫也没耽搁。

交代清楚指示之后，探长走到郡警察局长驻脚的地方。

“坐下吧，探长，”克林顿爵士招呼道。“这活儿说不定要耗上一整天呢，坐着可不比站着多花钱。”

探长落座后，郡警察局长转过身来问了他一句。

===Original===
“You’ve seen to it that no one has gone up on to the terrace?”

Inspector Armadale nodded affirmatively.

“No one’s been up on top,” he explained, “I’d like to go and have a look round myself; but since you were so clear about it, I haven’t gone.”

“Don’t go,” Sir Clinton reiterated his order. “I’ve a sound reason for letting no one up there.”
===Chinese===
“露台上没人上去过，这一点你安排妥当了？”

阿玛代尔探长肯定地点了点头。

“上头没人去过，”他解释道。“我自己倒想上去转一圈看看；可既然您把话交代得那么明确，我就没去。”

“别去，”克林顿爵士重申他的命令。“我不放任何人上去，自有充足的理由。”

===Original===
He glanced for a moment at the group of constables.

“Another thing, Inspector,” he continued. “There’s no secrecy about that matter. In fact, it might be useful if you’d let it leak out to the public that no one has been up above there and that no one will be allowed to go until I give the word. Spread it round, you understand?”

Slightly mystified, apparently, the Inspector acquiesced.

“Do you see your way through the case, Sir Clinton?” he demanded. “You’ve given me the facts, but we’ll need a good deal more, it seems to me.”
===Chinese===
他朝那群警员瞥了一眼。

“还有件事，探长，”他接着说。“这件事本身用不着保密。实际上，你若把风声漏给外界——上头没人去过，而且我发话之前谁也不许上去——反倒可能有用。四下里传开去，明白吗？”

探长显出一副似感困惑的样子，但还是应承了下来。

“这案子您已经看出门道了吗，克林顿爵士？”他问道。“事实您是给我了；可依我看，咱们还差得远。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton pulled out his cigarette-case and thoughtfully began to smoke before answering the question. When he spoke again, his reply was an indirect one.

“There’s an old jurist’s saying that I always keep in mind,” he said. “It helps to clarify one’s ideas in a case:

Quis, quid, ubi, quibus auxiliis, cur, quomodo, quando?

That puts our whole business into a nutshell.” He glanced at the Inspector’s face. “Your Latin’s as feeble as my own, perhaps? There’s an English equivalent:

What was the crime, who did it, when was it done, and where,

How done, and with what motive, who in the deed did share?

How many of these questions can you answer now, offhand, Inspector? The rest of them will tell you what you’ve still got to ferret out.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士掏出烟盒，若有所思地吸起烟来，没有立刻回答。再度开口时，他的回答是绕着弯的。

“有句老法学家的话，我向来记在心上，”他说。“办起案子来，它能帮人理清思路：

Quis, quid, ubi, quibus auxiliis, cur, quomodo, quando?

咱们这桩事，一句话就装进去了。”他瞥了探长一眼。“你的拉丁文想必跟我一样拿不出手？好在有个英文的对句：

所犯何罪，何人所为，何时何地，

如何施行，动机何在，何人同案？

这几个问题，你现在能脱口答上几个，探长？答不上来的那些，就告诉你还有哪些东西要挖。”

===Original===
Inspector Armadale pulled out a notebook and pencil.

“Would you mind repeating it, Sir Clinton? I’d see through it better if I had it down in black and white.”

The Chief Constable repeated the doggerel and Armadale jotted it down under his dictation.

“That seems fairly searching,” he admitted, rereading it as he spoke.

“Quite enough for present purposes. Now, Inspector, how much do you really know? I mean, how many answers can you give? There are only seven questions in all. Take them one by one and let’s hear your answers.”

“It’s a pretty stiff catechism,” said the Inspector, looking again at his notebook. “I’ll have a try, though, if you give me time to think over it.”

Sir Clinton smiled at the qualification.

“Think it over, then, Inspector,” he said. “I’ll just go and set them to work with the dragging. They seem to be ready to make a start.”
===Chinese===
阿玛代尔探长掏出笔记本和铅笔。

“麻烦您再念一遍，克林顿爵士。白纸黑字记下来，我看得更透。”

郡警察局长把那首顺口溜重念了一遍，阿马代尔照着口述记了下来。

“这几问可真够犀利的，”他一面重读一面承认。

“眼下这够用了。那么，探长，你到底知道多少？我是说，你能答出几条？一共不过七个问题。一条一条来，说说你的答案。”

“这份问答可真够苛刻的，”探长说着，又看了看笔记本。“不过，只要您给我时间琢磨琢磨，我愿意试试。”

克林顿爵士对这句保留话微微一笑。

“那就琢磨吧，探长，”他说。“我去叫他们开始打捞。看样子他们已经准备动手了。”

===Original===
He rose and walked down to the group at the edge of the pool.

“You know what’s wanted?” he asked. “Well, suppose we make a start. Get the raft out to about ten yards or so beyond the cave-mouth and begin by flinging the grapnel in as near the cliff-edge as you can. Then work gradually outwards. If it sticks, try again very slightly off the line of the last cast.”

He watched one or two attempts which gave no result and then turned back to the hillock again.

“Well, Inspector?” he demanded as he sat down and turned his eyes on the group engaged with the dragging operations. “What do you make of it?”
===Chinese===
他起身走向池边那群人。

“该干什么都知道了吧？”他问。“好，那就动手。把木筏撑到洞口外大约十码的地方，先把抓钩尽量往崖根附近抛。然后逐渐往外推移。要是卡住了，就在偏离上一钩稍稍一点的地方再试。”

他看着他们试了一两钩，毫无所获，便折回土丘上去了。

“怎么样，探长？”他一面坐下，一面把目光投向忙着打捞的那群人。“你琢磨出什么来了？”

===Original===
Inspector Armadale looked up from his notebook.

“That’s a sound little rhyme,” he admitted. “It lets you see what you don’t know and what you do know.”

Sir Clinton suppressed a smile successfully.

“Or what you think you know, perhaps, Inspector?”

“Well, if you like to put it that way, sir. But some things I think one can be sure of.”

Sir Clinton’s face showed nothing of his views on this question.

“Let’s begin at the beginning,” he suggested. “ ‘What was the crime?’ ”

“That’s clear enough,” the Inspector affirmed without hesitation. “These three electrotypes have been stolen. That’s the crime.”
===Chinese===
阿玛代尔探长从笔记本上抬起头来。

“这顺口溜还真编得在理，”他承认道。“它能让你看清哪些是自己不知道的，哪些是知道的。”

克林顿爵士忍住笑，忍得很成功。

“或许还有哪些是你自以为知道的呢，探长？”

“唉，您要那么说也行，长官。不过有些事，我看还是可以有把握的。”

对这个问题的看法，克林顿爵士脸上什么也没流露。

“咱们还是从头开始吧，”他提议道。“‘所犯何罪？’”

“这一条再清楚不过，”探长毫不犹豫地断言。“三件电铸复制品被偷了。这就是罪案。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton seemed to be engrossed in the dragging which was going on methodically below them.

“You think so?” he said at length. “H’m! I’m not so sure.”

Inspector Armadale corrected himself.

“I meant that I’d charge the man with stealing the replicas. You couldn’t charge him with anything else, since nothing else is missing. At least, that’s what you told me. He wanted the real medallions, but he didn’t pull that off.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士似乎全神贯注于脚下那井然有序进行着的打捞。

“你这么想？”他终于开口。“嗯！我可就不那么有把握了。”

阿玛代尔探长更正自己。

“我是说，起诉他的罪名会是偷窃复制品。别的什么也告不上，因为别的什么都没丢。至少，您是这么告诉我的。他想要的是真徽章，可那一手没能得逞。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton refused to be drawn. He resorted to one of his indirect replies.

“ ‘What was the crime?’ ” he repeated. “Now, I’ll put a case to you, Inspector. Suppose that you saw two men in the distance and that you could make out that one of them was struggling and the second man was beating him on the head. What crime would you call that? Assault and battery?”

“I suppose so,” Armadale admitted.

“But suppose, further, that when you reached them, you found the victim dead of his injuries, what would you call the crime then?”

“Murder, I suppose.”

“So your view of the crime would depend upon the stage at which you witnessed it, eh? That’s just my position in this Ravensthorpe affair. You’ve been looking at it from yesterday’s standpoint, and you call it a theft of three replicas. But I wonder what you’ll call it when we know the whole of the facts.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士不肯上钩。他又祭出了那套迂回作答的本事。

“‘所犯何罪？’”他重复道。“这么着，我给你设一个案子，探长。假设你远远看见两个人，看得出其中一个在挣扎，另一个在拿东西砸他的脑袋。你会管这叫什么罪？殴打伤害罪？”

“大概会吧，”阿马代尔承认。

“再假设，等你赶到跟前，发现那受害的人已经伤重死了，那时你又管这罪叫什么？”

“谋杀，我猜。”

“这么说，你对罪名的看法，全看你目击的是哪一阶段了，嗯？在雷文索普这桩案子上，我的处境正是如此。你一直站在昨天的立场上看它，管它叫三件复制品失窃。可等我们掌握了全部事实，不知你又会管它叫什么。”

===Original===
The Inspector declined to follow his chief to this extent.

“All the evidence we’ve got, so far, points to theft, sir. I’ve no fresh data that would let me put a new name to it.”

“Then you regard it as a completed crime which has partly failed in its object?”

The Inspector gave his acquiescence with a nod.

“You think it’s something else, Sir Clinton?” he inquired.

The Chief Constable refused to be explicit.

“You’ve got all the evidence, Inspector. Do you really think a gang would take the trouble to steal replicas when they could just as easily have taken the three originals—that’s the point. The replicas have no intrinsic value beyond the gold in them, and that can’t be worth more than twenty or thirty pounds at the very outside. A mediocre haul for a smart gang, isn’t it? Hardly Trade Union wages, I should think.”

“It seems queer at first sight, sir,” he admitted, “but I think I can account for that all right when you come to the rest of your rhyme.”
===Chinese===
探长不肯跟上司走到这么远。

“到目前为止，我们掌握的全部证据都指向盗窃，长官。我没有新的凭据，没法给它换个别的新名目。”

“那么你把它看作一桩已然完成、只是目标落了一半空的罪案？”

探长点头表示同意。

“您觉得它是别的名堂，克林顿爵士？”他问道。

郡警察局长不肯把话说实。

“证据你都到手了，探长。一伙贼明明可以一样轻巧地拿走那三件真品，你当真以为他们肯费这个事去偷复制品吗？——症结就在这儿。复制品除了里头的金子，本身没有任何价值；而那点金子往宽里算，也值不过二三十镑。对一伙手段利落的贼来说，这票买卖未免寒碜，是不是？我看连工会标准的工资都抵不上。”

“乍看确实蹊跷，长官，”他承认道，“不过等您问到那顺口溜余下的几问，我想这一点我满能解释得通。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton showed his interest.

“Then let’s go on,” he suggested. “The next question is: ‘Who did it?’ What’s your answer to that, Inspector?”

“To my mind, there seems to be only one possible thief.”

Sir Clinton pricked up his ears.

“You mean it was a single-handed job? Who was the man, then?”

“Foxton Polegate,” asserted the Inspector.

He watched Sir Clinton’s face narrowly as he brought out the name, but the Chief Constable might have been wearing a mask for all the change there was in his features as he listened to the Inspector’s suggestion. As if he felt that he had overstepped the bounds of prudence, Armadale added hastily:

“I said ‘possible thief,’ sir. I don’t claim to be able to bring it home to him yet.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士露出了兴味。

“那就接着来，”他提议道。“下一问是：‘何人所为？’这一条你怎么答，探长？”

“依我看，作贼的可能只有一个人。”

克林顿爵士竖起了耳朵。

“你是说，这是单枪匹马干的？那么，那人是谁？”

“福克斯顿·波尔盖特（Foxton Polegate），”探长断言。

他说出这个名字时，紧紧盯着克林顿爵士的脸；可听着探长这个推测，郡警察局长脸上纹丝不动，跟戴了面具似的。阿马代尔仿佛自觉失了分寸，赶忙补上一句：

“我说的是‘可能的贼’，长官。我还不敢说眼下就能把罪名坐实到他头上。”

===Original===
“But you think it might even be ‘probable’ instead of only ‘possible,’ Inspector? Let’s hear the evidence, please.”

Inspector Armadale turned over the leaves of his notebook until he reached some entries which he had previously made.

“First of all, sir, Polegate must have known the value of these medallions—the originals, I mean. Second, he learned that they would be on show last night; and he knew where they’d be placed in the museum. Third, it was after Polegate came by this knowledge that the practical joke was planned. Fourth, who suggested the sham burglary? Polegate. Then fifth, who gave himself the job of actually taking the medallions? Polegate again. Sixth, where was Polegate immediately after the robbery? We’ve only his own word for it that he was strolling about, having a smoke. He might have been elsewhere, easily enough. Seventh, he was dressed up as a Harlequin when you saw him: but he might quite easily have slipped on a white jacket and a pair of Pierrot’s trousers over his Harlequin costume. He could disguise himself as a Pierrot in a couple of ticks and come out as a Harlequin again just as quick. So he might quite well have been the man in white that they were all busy chasing last night. Eighth, he knows the ground thoroughly and could give strangers the slip easily enough at the end of the chase. And, ninth, he didn’t appear when you wanted him last night. He only turned up when he’d had plenty of time to get home again, even if he’d been the man in white. That’s a set of nine points that need looking into. Prima facie, there’s a case for suspicion, if there’s no more. And there isn’t anything like so strong a case against anyone else, Sir Clinton.”
===Chinese===
“可在你看来，它没准儿已经不止是‘可能’，而称得上‘很可能’了，探长？请说说凭据。”

阿玛代尔探长翻动笔记本，翻到他先前记下的一些条目。

“首先，长官，波尔盖特必定知道这几枚徽章的价值——我说的是真品。第二，他得知了徽章昨晚会展出；他还知道它们会摆在博物馆的哪个位置。第三，正是波尔盖特获知这些内情之后，那场恶作剧才定了下来。第四，是谁提议假扮窃案？波尔盖特。第五，是谁把真正动手取徽章的差事派给自己？还是波尔盖特。第六，窃案发生后波尔盖特人在哪里？说他当时在四处溜达、抽烟，不过是他一面之词。他完全可能身在别处。第七，您见到他时，他扮的是哈里昆（Harlequin）；可他满可以往哈里昆戏服外头套上一件白上衣、一条皮埃罗（Pierrot）裤子。一转眼的工夫他就能扮成皮埃罗，再一转眼又能变回哈里昆。所以说，昨夜人人忙着追赶的那个白衣人，就完全可能是他。第八，这一带的地形他了如指掌，追到末了，想甩开生人易如反掌。第九，昨夜您要找他的时候，他偏偏不露面。等他终于现身，时间已绰绰有余——哪怕他就是那白衣人，也尽够赶回宅子了。一共九条，条条需要查究。单看表面，嫌疑已然立得起来，纵使仅此而已。而其他人身上，没有谁的嫌疑及得上这一半扎实，克林顿爵士。”

===Original===
“Well, let’s take the rest of the first line,” said the Chief Constable, without offering any criticism of the Inspector’s statement of the case. “ ‘When was it done, and where?’ ”

“At 11:45 p.m. and in the museum,” retorted Armadale. “That’s beyond dispute. It’s the clearest thing in the whole evidence.”

“I should be inclined to put it at 11:44 p.m. at the latest, or perhaps 11:43 p.m.,” said Sir Clinton, with an air of fastidiousness.

The Inspector looked at him suspiciously, evidently feeling that he was being laughed at for his display of accuracy.
===Chinese===
“好，咱们来看看第一行的后半段，”郡警察局长说着，对探长的案情陈述未置一词褒贬。“‘何时何地？’”

“晚上11:45，地点在博物馆，”阿马代尔应声道。“这无可争辩。全部证据里，就数这一条最清楚。”

“我倒倾向于认为至迟是晚上11:44，兴许还是11:43呢，”克林顿爵士说，一副考究到挑剔的神气。

探长狐疑地看着他，分明觉得自己那份精确劲儿遭到了取笑。

===Original===
“I go by Miss Rainhill’s evidence,” he declared. “She was the only one who had her eye on her watch, and she said she pulled out the switch at 11:45 precisely.”

“I go by the evidence of Polegate and young Chacewater,” said Sir Clinton, with a faint parody of the Inspector’s manner. “They were taken by surprise when the light went out, although they expected it to be extinguished at 11:45 p.m.”

“Oh, have it your own way, sir, if you lay any stress on the point,” conceded the Inspector. “Make it 11:44 or 11:45; it’s all the same, so far as I’m concerned.”

Armadale seemed slightly ruffled by his chief’s method of approaching the subject. Sir Clinton turned to another side of the matter.
===Chinese===
“我依据的是雷恩希尔小姐（Miss Rainhill）的证词，”他声明道。“只有她一个人盯着手表，她说她是在11:45整拉下的开关。”

“我依据的是波尔盖特和小蔡斯沃特（Chacewater）的证词，”克林顿爵士说，隐隐模仿起探长的腔调。“灯灭的时候他们都吃了一惊，尽管他们原本料到灯会在晚上11:45熄掉。”

“唉，要是您抠这个点，那就随您的便吧，长官，”探长让步道。“就算是11:44或11:45；在我这边，横竖一个样。”

上司探讨这桩事的路数，似乎让阿马代尔有点窝火。克林顿爵士转到了事情的另一面。

===Original===
“I suppose you say the crime has been committed in the museum?” he inquired.

The Inspector looked at him suspiciously.

“You’re trying to pull my leg, sir. Of course, it was committed in the museum.”

Sir Clinton’s tone became apologetic.

“I keep forgetting that we’re not talking about the same thing, perhaps. Of course, the theft of the replicas was committed in the museum. We’re quite in agreement there.”
===Chinese===
“我猜，你的说法是罪案发生在博物馆里？”他问道。

探长狐疑地望着他。

“您这是在耍我，长官。案子当然是发生在博物馆里的。”

克林顿爵士的语气转为歉意。

“我总是忘记，咱们说的恐怕不是同一件事。当然，偷复制品这桩罪案是发生在博物馆里的。这一点上，咱们意见完全一致。”

===Original===
He threw away his cigarette, selected a fresh one, and lighted it before continuing.

“And on that basis, I suppose there’s no mystery about the next query in the rhyme: ‘How done?’ ”

“None whatever, in my mind,” the Inspector affirmed. “Polegate could take what he wanted, once the light was out.”

Sir Clinton did not dispute this point.

“Of course,” he said. “And now for the next query: ‘With what motive?’ Where do you stand in that matter, Inspector?”

But here Armadale evidently felt himself on sure ground.

“Polegate’s a rackety young fool, sir. This is where local knowledge comes in. He’s got no common sense—always playing practical jokes. He’s been steadily muddling away the money his father left him. I shouldn’t be surprised if he’s hard up. That’s the motive.”
===Chinese===
他丢掉烟头，另拣了一支，点上，才继续说下去。

“在这个基础上，我想顺口溜里的下一问就没有什么玄妙了：‘如何施行？’”

“在我看来毫无玄妙，”探长断言。“灯一灭，波尔盖特要拿什么就能拿什么。”

这一点克林顿爵士没有争。

“当然，”他说。“接下来一问：‘动机何在？’这一条你站在哪儿，探长？”

不过到了这一条，阿马代尔显然自觉脚下扎实。

“波尔盖特是个轻狂的年轻蠢货，长官。这就得靠本地情况了。这人毫无常识——一天到晚搞恶作剧。他父亲留给他的钱，叫他一分不落、稳稳当当地糟蹋得差不多了。他要不是手头拮据，我倒要奇怪。动机就在这儿。”

===Original===
“And you think he’d steal from his oldest friends?”

“Every man has his price,” retorted the Inspector, bluntly. “Put on the screw hard enough in the way of temptation, and any man’ll fall for it.”

“Rather a hard saying that, Inspector; and perhaps a trifle too sweeping.” Sir Clinton turned on Armadale suddenly. “What would be *your* price, now, if I asked you to hush up this case against young Polegate? Put a figure on it, will you?”

Armadale flushed angrily at the suggestion; then, seeing that he had been trapped, he laughed awkwardly.

“Nobody knows even their own price till it’s put on the table, Sir Clinton,” he countered, with a certain acuteness.

The Chief Constable turned away from the subject.
===Chinese===
“你认为他会偷相交最久的老朋友？”

“人人都有个价钱，”探长直截了当地回敬。“诱惑的螺丝只要拧得够紧，谁都得栽进去。”

“这话未免太狠了点，探长；只怕也说得太绝。”克林顿爵士突然转过脸来逼视阿马代尔。“那么，倘若我求你把小波尔盖特这桩案子压下去，你自己的价钱是多少？开个数吧，嗯？”

这话惹得阿马代尔涨红了脸；随即他明白自己中了圈套，尴尬地笑了。

“自己的价钱是多少，不摆上桌面，连本人也不知道，克林顿爵士，”他回敬道，倒也不失机敏。

郡警察局长把话题岔开了。

===Original===
“You’re depending on there being a fair chance of Polegate getting away with the medallions without being suspected. But when young Chacewater and Miss Rainhill were in the scheme as well as Polegate, suspicion was sure to light on him when the medallions vanished. The other two were certain to tell what they knew about the business.”
===Chinese===
“你指望的是：波尔盖特带走徽章而不受怀疑的机会相当大。可是，除了波尔盖特，小蔡斯沃特和雷恩希尔小姐也都参与了那个计划；徽章一失踪，嫌疑必定落到他头上。另外两个人肯定会把知道的和盘托出。”

===Original===
Inspector Armadale glanced once more at his notebook in order to refresh his memory of the rhyme.

“That really comes under the final head: ‘Who in the deed did share?’ ” he pointed out.

“Pass along to the next caravan, then, if you wish,” Sir Clinton suggested. “What animals have you in the final cage?”

The Inspector seemed to deprecate his flippancy.
===Chinese===
阿玛代尔探长又看了一眼笔记本，好把那首顺口溜记起来。

“这一条其实归在最末一问底下：‘何人同案？’”他指出。

“那么，要是你愿意，就请移步下一节大篷车吧，”克林顿爵士提议。“最后一格兽笼里关的都是什么动物？”

探长似乎不以为然，嫌他轻浮。

===Original===
“It’s been very cleverly done,” he said, seriously. “You objected that suspicion was bound to fall on young Polegate; and so it would have done, if he hadn’t covered his tracks so neatly. He’s set everyone on the hunt for a gang at work, or at least for an outside criminal. Now I believe it was a one-man show from the start, worked from the inside. Polegate planned the practical joke—that gave him his chance. Then he forced himself forward as the fellow who was to do the actual stealing—and that let him get his hands on the medallions while young Chacewater held the keeper up for him. Without the holdup of the keeper, the thing was a washout. The joke helped young Polegate to enlist innocent assistance.”
===Chinese===
“这事干得非常巧妙，”他一本正经地说。“您方才提出，嫌疑必定落到小波尔盖特头上——本来也确实会如此，若不是他把踪迹抹得那么干净。他让所有人都去追查一伙作案的匪帮，至少也是一个外来罪犯。而我相信，这事从头到尾就是一个人的独角戏，从内部做成。波尔盖特筹划了那场恶作剧——机会由此而来。接着他又抢着当那个真正下手偷的人——这就让他摸到了徽章，由小蔡斯沃特替他逼住看管人。若没有逼住看管人这一手，整件事就泡了汤。那场恶作剧帮着小波尔盖特驱使了一帮无辜的帮手。”

===Original===
“But still suspicion would attach to him,” Sir Clinton objected.

“Yes, except for a false trail,” the Inspector agreed. “But he laid a false trail. Instead of waiting for the switch to be pulled out, he fired his shot from the bay, extinguished the light and then rushed out of the bay and went for the medallions.”

“Well?” said Sir Clinton in an encouraging tone.

“When he’d smashed the glass of the case, he took out the whole six medallions, and not merely three of them as he told you he’d done.”

“And then?”

“He pocketed the replicas and stuck the real things under the case with plasticine. Then he continued the false trail by bolting out of the house. He was the man in white. When he got clear of the people who were chasing him, he came back to the house again, ready to play his part as an innocent practical joker. And he had his tale ready, of how someone was beside him at the case, wearing a Pierrot costume. That stamped the notion of an outside gang on everybody’s mind. Both sets of medallions had gone. He—the innocent practical joker—could have produced the replicas from his pocket and sworn they were all that the gang had left in the case by the time he got to it.”
===Chinese===
“可嫌疑照样会缠着他，”克林顿爵士反驳。

“是，除非有一条假线索，”探长表示同意。“而他恰恰布了一条假线索。他没有干等着别人去拉闸，而是在凹室里开了枪，打灭了灯，随即冲出凹室，直奔徽章而去。”

“后来呢？”克林顿爵士用鼓励的语气说。

“他砸开展柜玻璃后，取出的是全部六枚徽章，而不是他跟您说的只有三枚。”

“然后呢？”

“他把复制品揣进衣袋，把真品用橡皮泥粘在展柜底下。随后他把假线索接着演下去：夺门而出，逃离宅子。他就是那个白衣人。等甩脱了追赶他的人，他又回到宅子里，准备接着扮演无辜恶作剧者的角色。他的一套说辞也是现成的：他赶到展柜跟前时，旁边另有一人，穿着皮埃罗戏服。这一下，外来匪帮的念头就烙进了每个人的脑子。两套徽章都不见了。他——这位无辜的恶作剧者——满可以把复制品从衣袋里掏出来，赌咒发誓说，等他赶到时，匪帮留在柜里的就只有这几件了。”

===Original===
“And … ?”

“And then, a few days later, he’d have managed to get into the museum on some excuse—he’s a friend of the family—and he’d have had no difficulty in taking the real medallions from under the case where he’d left them. He’d have to take the chance that they’d been overlooked. The false trail would help in that. He’d hardly expect a close search of the museum after the man in white had got clear away. And by running the business on these lines, he’d avoid any chance of being caught with the stuff actually in his pocket at any time.”
===Chinese===
“还有……？”

“再往后，过个几天，他总能寻个由头进博物馆——他是这家人的朋友——把真徽章从展柜底下他藏的地方取走，毫不费事。他得赌一把，赌东西没被人瞧见。假线索正好帮这个忙。白衣人既已脱身，他料想不会有人细细搜查博物馆。照这个路数运作，他任何时候都不会被人赃并获。”

===Original===
“But in that case, why did he hand over the real things to me like a lamb as soon as I challenged him?”

The Inspector was ready for this.

“Because as soon as he came into the museum last night, he found that you apparently knew everything—or a good deal more than he’d counted on. Anyhow, he didn’t know how much you knew; and he felt he’d got into a tight corner. He just let the whole thing slide and made up his mind to get out before things got too hot. So he pretended that so far as he was concerned, the practical joke was the thing; and he gave up the real medallions and kept the replicas in his pocket.”

“Why? He might as well have given up the lot.”

“No,” the Inspector contradicted. “He’d got to keep the false trail going, for otherwise there would have been awkward questions as to why he diverged from the prearranged programme. I mean the shooting out of the light, the lies about the man in white, and so forth. So he stuck to the replicas and made out that there was an outsider mixed up in the affair. But thanks to the practical joke, the outsider had missed the real stuff; and Polegate was really the saviour of the Leonardo set.”

Sir Clinton seemed to be pondering over Armadale’s version of the affair. At last he gave his own view.

“A jury wouldn’t look at that evidence,” he pointed out.

“I don’t suppose they would,” Armadale admitted. “But there may be more to come yet.”

“I expect so,” Sir Clinton agreed.

He rose as he spoke, and, followed by the Inspector, went down to the edge of the lakelet.
===Chinese===
“可要是那样，为什么我一发话，他就乖乖地把真品交了给我？”

这一问探长早有准备。

“因为昨夜他一进博物馆，就发现您显然什么都知道了——至少比他盘算的多得多。无论如何，他不知道您知道多少；他觉得自己钻进了死角。他索性撒手认栽，拿定主意趁局面还没太烫手之前脱身。于是他装出一副样子：就他而言，要紧的只是那场恶作剧；真徽章他交了出来，复制品却留在了衣袋里。”

“为什么？索性全交了岂不更好。”

“不，”探长反驳。“假线索他必须接着演下去，不然就会冒出难堪的质问：他为什么不照原先排好的戏路走。我指的是开枪灭灯、编造白衣人的谎话等等。所以他咬定复制品不放，一口咬定案子里有外人搅和。多亏那场恶作剧，外人错过了真家伙；波尔盖特反倒成了抢救达·芬奇那套徽章的功臣。”

克林顿爵士似乎在掂量阿马代尔这套说法。末了他才亮出自己的看法。

“这种证据，陪审团连看都不会看，”他指出。

“我也料他们不会，”阿马代尔承认。“不过后头也许还有新的东西。”

“我也这么想，”克林顿爵士附和。

他边说边站起身，探长跟在后面，一同走下小湖岸边。

===Original===
“No luck yet?” he inquired.

“None, sir. It’s a very difficult bottom to work a grapnel over. It sticks three times out of four.”

Sir Clinton watched the line of the drag which they were making.

“It’ll take a while to cover the ground at this rate,” he commented, noting the smallness of the area they had searched up to that moment.

As he turned away from the waterside, he noticed Cecil Chacewater approaching round the edge of the lakelet, and leaving the Inspector to superintend the dragging, he walked over to meet the newcomer. As he came near, he could see that Cecil’s face was sullen and downcast.
===Chinese===
“还没有收获？”他问道。

“没有，长官。这湖底太不适合抓钩作业。四回里能卡三回。”

克林顿爵士望着他们拖捞的路线。

“照这个速度，要捞完全片水域还早呢，”他评道，注意到他们到那时为止搜过的水面还很有限。

他转身离开水边时，看见塞西尔·蔡斯沃特（Cecil Chacewater）正沿着小湖边缘走来；他把监督打捞的事交给探长，迎着来人走了过去。走近了才看清，塞西尔的脸色又阴又颓。

===Original===
“ ’Morning, Sir Clinton. I heard you were here, so I came across to say goodbye before I clear out.”

Sir Clinton could hardly pretend astonishment in view of what he knew about the state of affairs at Ravensthorpe; but he did not conceal his regret at the news.

“There was a row-royal between Maurice and me this morning,” Cecil explained, gloomily. “Of course this medallion business gave him his chance, and he jumped in with both feet, you know. He abused me like a fishwife and finally gave me permission to do anything except stay at Ravensthorpe after tonight. So I’m off.”
===Chinese===
“早啊，克林顿爵士。听说您在这儿，我就过来道个别，我这就动身离开了。”

以克林顿爵士对雷文索普家事的了解，他装不出惊讶；但听到这消息，他并不掩饰惋惜。

“今天早上，我和莫里斯（Maurice）大吵了一场，”塞西尔阴郁地解释道。“徽章这档子事当然给了他机会，他就双脚一齐跳了进去，您知道的。他骂我骂得像个骂街的泼妇，末了恩准我干什么都行，唯独不许今晚过后再留在雷文索普。所以我走人。”

===Original===
“I wish you hadn’t got mixed up with that silly practical joke,” Sir Clinton said in some concern. “I can’t forgive that young blighter for luring you into it.”

Cecil’s resentment against his brother was evidently too deep to let him look on the matter from this point of view.

“If it hadn’t been that, it would have been something else. Any excuse would have served his turn, you know. He’d have flung me out sooner or later—probably sooner. I’ve felt for long enough that he was itching to clear me off the premises. Foxy’s little show only precipitated things. The root of the trouble was there long before.”

“Well, it’s a sad business.” Sir Clinton saw that it was useless to dwell on the subject. “You’re going up to town? Any address you can give me?”
===Chinese===
“你要是没搅进那场愚蠢的恶作剧就好了，”克林顿爵士不无忧虑地说。“那个小混蛋把你诱了进去，我不能原谅他。”

塞西尔对兄长的怨气显然太深，没法从这个角度看这件事。

“若不是这件事，也会是别的什么事。随便什么借口都够他用，您知道的。他迟早得把我撵出去——多半是赶早。他憋着劲儿要把我从这宅子里清出去，这感觉我受得够久了。福克西（Foxy）那场小把戏不过是让事情提前发作。祸根早就种下了。”

“唉，这事真令人难过。”克林顿爵士看出在这事上多谈无益。“你要上伦敦去？能留个地址给我吗？”

===Original===
“I’ll probably put up with a man for a day or two. He’s been inviting me to his place once or twice lately, but I’ve never been able to fit it in; so I may as well take him at his word now. I’ve got to look round for something to do, you know.”

“If you want someone to speak for you, Cecil, refer them to me when you apply for anything. And, by the way, if you happen to run short, you know my address. A letter will always find me.”

Cecil thanked him rather awkwardly.

“I hope it won’t come to that,” he wound up. “Something may turn up sooner than one hopes.”

Sir Clinton thought it well to change the subject again.

“By the way, Cecil,” he asked, “do you know anything about this man Foss? What sort of person is he?”
===Chinese===
“我大概会在一个人家里搭住一两天。近来他邀我去他那儿也不止一两回了，我一直没腾出空；现在正好顺水推舟，应了他的约。您知道的，我得寻点事做。”

“要是需要有人替你担保，塞西尔，你去谋什么事的时候，让他们来找我打听就是。另外，万一你手头紧了，你知道我的地址。一封信总能寻到我。”

塞西尔道了谢，神色颇为局促。

“我盼着不至于到那一步，”他收了话头。“说不定机会来得比指望的还快呢。”

克林顿爵士觉得最好再换个话题。

“对了，塞西尔，”他问道，“这位福斯（Foss）先生其人，你了解吗？他是个什么样的人？”

===Original===
It seemed an unfortunate topic. Cecil’s manner was anything but gracious as he replied:

“Foss? Oh, you know what sort of a fellow he is already. A damned eavesdropper on his hosts and a beggar with a tongue hinged in the middle so that he can talk with both ends at once. I’d like to wring his neck for him! What do they call the breed that runs off and splits to the police? Copper’s narks, isn’t it?”

“It wasn’t exactly that side of him that I wanted to hear about, Cecil. I’m quite fully acquainted with his informative temperament already. What I want to know is the sort of man he is socially and so forth.”

Cecil curbed his vexation with an effort.

“Oh, he seems to have decent enough manners—a bit Yankee, perhaps, in some things. He must do well enough out of this agent business of his, acting for Kessock and the like, you know. He arrived here with a big car, a chauffeur, and a man. Except for his infernal talebearing, I can’t say he’s anything out of the ordinary.”
===Chinese===
这话题似乎撞得不巧。塞西尔回话时的态度说不上半点客气：

“福斯？哼，他是什么货色，您早就知道了。一个偷听自家东道主说话的该死家伙，一条舌头中间装了铰链、好两头同时开腔的家伙。我真想拧断他的脖子！那种撒腿开溜、转头就向警察告密的下作胚子，人们管它叫什么来着？‘条子的眼线’，对不对？”

“我想听的倒不完全是这一面，塞西尔。他那爱告密的脾性，我已经领教得十足了。我想知道的是他在社交场上是个什么样的人物，诸如此类。”

塞西尔强压下火气。

“哦，他的举止倒还算体面——有些地方或许带了点美国佬气。他做的那份代理人营生，替凯索克（Kessock）之流办差，想必进账不坏，您知道的。他到这儿来，开着一部大轿车，带着一名司机、一名随仆。除了他那该死的长舌告密，我得说这人并无出奇之处。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton, apparently feeling that he had struck the wrong vein in the conversational strata, contented himself with a nod of comprehension and let Cecil choose his own subject for the next stage in their talk. He was somewhat surprised when it came.

“Have you heard the latest from the village?” Cecil demanded.

Sir Clinton shook his head.

“I’ve had very little time to collect local gossip this morning, Cecil. I’ve been busy getting things started for this bit of work in the lake, you see.”

“If you’d been down in Hincheldene village you could hardly have missed it. I went down this morning to get some tobacco and I found the whole place buzzing with it. That was before I’d seen Maurice, luckily.”

“Suppose you tell me what it is,” Sir Clinton suggested, drily.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士显然自觉在谈话的矿层里凿错了矿脉，只会意地点了点头，任由塞西尔自选下一段谈资。待话题一出，他倒有些意外。

“村里最新的消息您听说了吗？”塞西尔问道。

克林顿爵士摇了摇头。

“今天早上我一直没什么工夫收罗本地的闲话，塞西尔。您瞧，我忙着张罗湖里这桩活计开工呢。”

“您要是下过欣切尔迪恩（Hincheldene）村，想不听都难。我今早下去买烟丝，发现全村都在嗡嗡地议论这件事。还算凑巧，那是在我见莫里斯之前。”

“那就不妨讲给我听听，”克林顿爵士淡淡地说。

===Original===
“Do you remember my telling you about the family spectre, the White Man?” Cecil asked. “Well, it seems that the village drunkard, old Groby, was taking a shortcut through our woods last night—or rather this morning, for he’s a bit of a late going-to-rooster—and he got the shock of his life in one of the glades. He swears he saw the White Man stealing about from tree to tree. By his way of it, he was near enough to see the thing clearly—all white, even the face. What a lark!”

“You certainly seem to take your family spectre a bit lightly, Cecil. What’s the cream of the jest?”

Cecil’s face took on a vindictive expression.

“Oh, it gave me a chance of getting home on Maurice, after he’d given me the key of the street. I told him all about it and I rubbed in the old story. You know what I mean? The White Man never appears except when the head of the family’s on his last legs. Maurice didn’t like it a bit. He looked a bit squeamish over it; and I came away leaving that sticking in his gills.”
===Chinese===
“您还记得我跟您讲过我们家那位幽灵——白衣人（White Man）吗？”塞西尔问。“嘿，看来昨夜——不如说今晨，因为那老兄有点儿像只晚进窝的鸡——村里的酒鬼老格罗比（Groby）抄近道穿过我们的林子，在一处林间空地上，这辈子没这么吓过。他赌咒说亲眼看见白衣人在树与树之间鬼鬼祟祟地挪动。照他的说法，他离得够近，看得分明——浑身皆白，连脸也是白的。真有意思！”

“你们家的幽灵，你显然没太放在心上啊，塞西尔。这笑话的妙处在哪儿？”

塞西尔的脸上浮起一种挟怨报复的神情。

“哦，他把我扫地出门之后，这倒给了我一记回敬莫里斯的机会。我把这事原原本本告诉了他，还把那老传说使劲渲染了一番。您明白我的意思吧？白衣人只在当家人行将就木之时现身。莫里斯听了大为不爽。他的脸色都发白了；我走的时候，就让这话卡在他喉咙里下不去。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton hardly concealed his distaste for this kind of thing.

“You flatter yourself, I expect. Maurice is hardly likely to waste any thought over superstitions of that sort.”

Cecil’s expression still showed a tinge of malice.

“You’d wonder,” he said. “It’s all very well for you to sneer at these affairs; but it looks a bit different when you yourself happen to be the object of them, I guess. It’s easy to say ‘Superstition’ in a high-minded way; but if there’s one percent chance that the superstition’s going to hit you personally, then, you know, it rankles a bit. Anything to give pain is my motto where Maurice is concerned.”

Quite oblivious of Sir Clinton’s rather disgusted expression, he laughed softly to himself for a moment or two.

“And the funniest thing in the whole affair,” he went on, “is that I know all about this White Man. Can’t you guess what it was?”

Sir Clinton shook his head.

“Why, don’t you see?” Cecil demanded, still laughing. “What old Groby came across must obviously have been Maurice himself in his white Pierrot dress, coming back from the burglar-hunt! That’s what makes it so damned funny. Fancy Maurice getting the creeps on account of himself! It’s as good a joke as I’ve heard for a while.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士对这类行径的厌恶，几乎毫不掩饰。

“我看你是在自我陶醉。莫里斯不至于为那类迷信白白费神。”

塞西尔的神情里仍透着一丝恶意。

“那可说不准，”他说。“您尽可以高姿态地笑话这些事；可一旦您自己恰巧成了它们冲着来的人，滋味就不大一样了，我猜。‘迷信’两个字，说起来清高得很；可这迷信若有百分之一的机会落到您自己头上，您知道的，心里就扎得慌。凡能叫莫里斯难受的，都是好东西——这是我待他的格言。”

他全没留意克林顿爵士脸上那副大倒胃口的神情，自顾自低声笑了片刻。

“整件事里最逗的是，”他接着说，“这白衣人的底细我一清二楚。您猜不出那是什么吗？”

克林顿爵士摇了摇头。

“哎呀，您还不明白？”塞西尔追问，还在笑。“老格罗比撞见的，分明就是莫里斯本人——穿着他那身白色皮埃罗戏服，从追捕窃贼的队伍里回转！这才是整件事最他妈滑稽的地方。想想看，莫里斯居然叫自己吓出一身鸡皮疙瘩！这样的笑话，我好久没听过第二个了。”

===Original===
He laughed harshly.

“You don’t seem to see it. Well, well. Perhaps you’re right. And now I must be getting back to the house. I’ve a lot of stuff to collect before I go off.”

He shook hands with Sir Clinton and moved off towards Ravensthorpe. The Chief Constable gazed after him for a moment or two.

“That young man’s in a most unpleasant frame of mind,” he commented to himself. “He’s obviously quite off his normal balance when he’d make a point of that kind of thing. I can’t say I take much stock in brotherly love; but this is really overdoing the business. Both of them seem to have taken leave of ordinary feelings. It’s just as well they’re parting, perhaps.”
===Chinese===
他干笑了几声，笑声刺耳。

“您好像不觉得好笑。唉，也罢。兴许您是对的。现在我得回宅子去了。动身之前还有一大堆东西要收拾呢。”

他与克林顿爵士握了握手，朝雷文索普走去。郡警察局长目送了他片刻。

“这年轻人的心境委实很不健康，”他暗自评道。“一个人竟然处心积虑地拿这种事扎人，可见心思全然失了常态。手足之情嘛，我谈不上多看重；可这样做实在是过分了。他们两个似乎都把寻常人的情感抛到了脑后。分开，或许倒也好。”

===Original===
Rather moodily he retraced his steps to where the Inspector was directing the operations by the bank of the lakelet; but by the time he reached the group his face had taken on its normal expression.

“Fishing still poor?” he demanded, as he came up.

“Nothing so far, sir,” the Inspector confessed. “These rocks are the very deuce to work amongst. I’ve been running the grapnel over the same track two or three times, just in case we miss the thing the first shot. We’ve had no luck at all—unless you count this as a valuable find: a bit of limestone or something like that.”

He kicked a shapeless mass of white stone as he spoke. Sir Clinton stooped over it: a dripping mass about the size of a man’s fist. The Inspector watched him as he examined it; but Sir Clinton’s face suggested neither interest nor satisfaction.

“Might be a bit of marble that got swept over the top when they were putting up the balustrade in the old days,” the Inspector hazarded.

Sir Clinton looked at it again and shook his head.

“I doubt it,” he said. “However, since it’s the only thing you’ve fished up, you’d better keep it, Inspector. One never knows what may be useful. I might make a paperweight out of it as a souvenir.”
===Chinese===
他心事重重地折返回去，走向探长督阵的小湖岸边；待到走近那群人，他脸上已恢复了平常的神色。

“打捞的收成还是不行？”他走近时问道。

“到现在还一无所获，长官，”探长坦白道。“在这些乱石中间作业，真是活受罪。同一条路线我让抓钩拖了两三遍，就怕头一遍漏了那东西。运气坏到家了——除非您把这件算作值钱的收获：一块石灰岩，还是什么类似的东西。”

说话间他踢了踢一块不成形状的白石头。克林顿爵士俯身细看：一团湿淋淋的东西，约莫拳头大小。探长注视着他察看；可克林顿爵士的脸上，既看不出兴味，也看不出满意。

“兴许是早年间装栏杆的时候，从崖顶上扫下去的一块大理石，”探长猜测道。

克林顿爵士又看了看，摇了摇头。

“我看未必，”他说。“不过，既然这是你们捞上来的唯一物件，最好还是收着，探长。谁也说不准什么东西会派上用场。没准我还能拿它做个镇纸，留作纪念。”

===Original===
The Inspector failed to see the point of the joke, but he laughed as politely as he could.

“Very well, Sir Clinton, I’ll see that it’s put aside.”

He glanced over the Chief Constable’s shoulder.

“Here’s Mr. Clifton coming, sir.”

Sir Clinton turned round to find that Michael Clifton had approached while he was engaged with the dragging operations. Leaving the group by the bank, he walked slowly to meet the advancing figure.

“Good morning, Mr. Clifton. Come up to see how we’re getting on, I suppose. There’s nothing to report, I’m afraid.”

“Drawn blank?” Michael inquired, needlessly. “There ought to be something there, all the same.”

“It may have been only a stone,” Sir Clinton pointed out. “You heard a splash; that’s all we have to go on. And a stone would make that as well as anything else.”

“That’s true,” Michael admitted. “None of us saw the thing hit the water, so we’ve no notion what it was like. It might have been a stone for all we can tell. But why should the fellow pitch a brick into the water? That’s what puzzles me.”
===Chinese===
探长没听懂这笑话可笑在哪里，但还是尽量客气地陪着笑。

“好的，克林顿爵士，我让人把它收好。”

他朝郡警察局长身后望了一眼。

“克利夫顿（Clifton）先生来了，长官。”

克林顿爵士转过身，才发现迈克尔·克利夫顿（Michael Clifton）趁他顾着打捞作业的当口已经走近了。他离开岸边那群人，缓步迎向走来的人影。

“早上好，克利夫顿先生。我想，您是上来看我们进展如何的吧。恐怕没什么可报告的。”

“一无所获？”迈克尔多此一举地问。“话虽如此，那底下总该有点什么东西。”

“也可能不过是块石头，”克林顿爵士指出。“你们听见一声落水响；我们的凭据仅此而已。而一块石头，跟别的任何东西一样，都能弄出那声响。”

“这倒是真的，”迈克尔承认。“我们谁也没看见那东西落水，所以它是什么模样，谁也说不上来。就我们所知，它兴许真是块石头。可那家伙干吗要往水里扔块砖头呢？这一点我琢磨不透。”

===Original===
Before Sir Clinton could reply, a shout came from the bank, and the Inspector waved to them to come down.

“We’ve got something, sir,” he called, as they drew nearer.

Followed by Michael, Sir Clinton hurried up to the group at the water’s edge. The Inspector was kneeling down, carefully disentangling the grapnel from something white. At last he rose and held out his capture. Michael gave an exclamation.

“A white jacket!”

A little further shaking of the material showed that it was a complete white Pierrot costume, except for the cap and shoes. The Inspector spread it out on the grass to dry, after holding the jacket outspread in the air so that they could gauge its size by comparison with his own body.

“That’s what I’ve been hoping to get hold of, Inspector,” Sir Clinton said. “I doubt if you’ll find much more in the pool. But perhaps you’d better go on dragging for a while yet. Something else might turn up.”

He examined the costume carefully; but it was quite evident that there were no identifying marks on it. During the inspection, Michael showed signs of impatience; and as soon as he could he unostentatiously drew Sir Clinton away from the group.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士还没来得及答话，岸边一声呼喊，探长朝他们招手，叫他们下去。

“我们捞着东西了，长官，”他们走近时他喊道。

克林顿爵士由迈克尔跟着，快步赶到水边那群人跟前。探长正跪在地上，小心地把抓钩从一团白色的东西里解出来。终于他站起身，举起捕获物。迈克尔失声一呼。

“一件白上衣！”

把那衣料又抖了几下，才看清这是一整套白色皮埃罗戏服，只缺帽子与鞋。探长把上衣张开提在半空，让他们拿他的身量比对出大小，随后把整套戏服摊在草地上晾晒。

“这正是我一直盼着到手的东西，探长，”克林顿爵士说。“池子里再能捞出多少，我拿不准。不过你们最好再拖上一阵。说不定还会冒出别的什么。”

他细细察看那套戏服；但很显然，上面没有任何可供辨认的标记。察看之际，迈克尔已露出不耐烦的迹象；一得着机会，他便不动声色地把克林顿爵士从人群中引开了。

===Original===
“Come up here, Mr. Clifton,” the Chief Constable suggested, as he turned towards the hillock he had chosen earlier in the morning. “We can keep an eye on things from this place.”

He sat down and Michael, after a glance to see that they were out of earshot of the dragging party, followed his example.

“What do you make of that?” he demanded eagerly.

Sir Clinton seemed to have little desire to discuss the matter.

“Let’s be quite clear on one point before we begin,” he reminded Michael. “I’m a Chief Constable, not a broadcasting station. My business is to collect information, not to throw it abroad before the proper time comes. You understand?”

Rather dashed, Michael admitted the justice of this.

“I’m a public servant, Mr. Clifton,” Sir Clinton pointed out, his manner taking the edge off the directness of his remarks, “and I get my information officially. Obviously it wouldn’t be playing the game if I scattered that information around before the public service has had the use of it.”

“I see that well enough,” Michael protested. “All I asked was what your own views are.”

Sir Clinton smiled and there was a touch of mischief in his eye as he replied.

“Seeing that my conclusions are based on the evidence—at least I like to think so, you know—they’re obviously part and parcel of my official knowledge. Hence I don’t divulge them till the right moment comes.”
===Chinese===
“到这儿上来，克利夫顿先生，”郡警察局长边说边转向他清早选定的那座土丘。“从这个地方，咱们能把底下的事尽收眼底。”

他坐下了；迈克尔瞟了一眼，确认打捞那伙人听不见他们说话，也跟着坐下。

“这事您怎么看？”他急切地问道。

克林顿爵士似乎没什么兴致讨论这事。

“开始之前，咱们先把一点说清楚，”他提醒迈克尔。“我是郡警察局长，不是广播电台。我的本分是收集情报，不是在时机未到之前四处播散。你明白？”

迈克尔像被浇了盆冷水，只得承认这话在理。

“我是公职人员，克利夫顿先生，”克林顿爵士指出，他的仪态把这番话的锋芒磨钝了几分，“我的情报来自公务渠道。在公务部门用上这些情报之前就四处散布，显然有失规矩。”

“这我完全明白，”迈克尔抗议道。“我不过是想知道您自己的看法。”

克林顿爵士微微一笑，答话时眼里闪过一丝狡黠。

“既然我的结论是以证据为根基的——至少我自己愿意这么想，您知道的——那它们显然也是我公务知识里不可分割的一部分。因此，时机未到之前，我不外泄。”

===Original===
He paused to let this sink in, then added lightly:

“That’s a most useful principle, I find. One often makes mistakes, and of course one never divulges them either, until the right time comes. It’s curious, but I’ve never been able yet to satisfy myself that the right time has come in any case of the sort.”

Michael smiled in his turn; and Sir Clinton went on:

“But there’s no reason why you shouldn’t draw your own conclusions and give me the benefit of them. I’m not too proud to be helped, you know.”

For a moment Michael kept silence, as if considering what his next move should be. Sir Clinton had given him what might have looked like a snub; but Michael had acuteness enough to tell him that the matter was one of principle with the Chief Constable and not merely a pretext devised on the spur of the moment to suppress inconvenient curiosity.

“It just occurred to me,” he confessed, “that there’s a possible explanation of that thing they’ve fished up. Do you remember that I found Maurice in the Fairy House up above there”—he indicated the cliff-top with a gesture—“and when I left him there he was still wearing a white costume like this one?”

“So you told us last night,” Sir Clinton confirmed.

“Now when Maurice turned up in the museum later on,” Michael continued, “he was wearing ordinary evening clothes. He’d got rid of the Pierrot dress in the meantime.”

“That’s true,” Sir Clinton agreed.

“Isn’t it possible,” Michael went on, “that after I left him, Maurice got over his troubles, whatever they were, and pitched his disguise over the edge here. This may quite well be it.”

“Rather a rum proceeding, surely,” was Sir Clinton’s comment. “Can you suggest any earthly reason why he should do a thing like that?”

“I can’t,” Michael admitted, frankly. “But the whole affair last night seemed to have neither rhyme nor reason in it; and after swallowing the escape of that beggar we were after, I’m almost prepared for anything in this neighbourhood. I just put the matter before you. I can’t fake up any likely explanation to account for it.”
===Chinese===
他顿了顿，好让这话落进对方心里，然后轻描淡写地补了一句：

“我发现这是一条极有用的原则。人常常出错，而错误嘛，时辰到了之前当然也一样不外泄。说来奇怪，可在这一类事情上，我还从来没能让自己信服过：合适的时辰到了。”

迈克尔也笑了；克林顿爵士接着说：

“不过，你自己下结论、再让我沾沾光，这倒没有什么不可以。我不是那种傲慢到不肯受助的人，您知道的。”

迈克尔一时默然，像在盘算下一步该怎么走。克林顿爵士这番话，乍看像是不留情面的回绝；但迈克尔够机敏，看得出这在郡警察局长是原则问题，而不是临时编出来压制令人不便的好奇心的托词。

“我倒是忽然想到，”他坦白道，“他们捞上来的那东西，有一个说得通的解释。您还记得吧，我在上头的仙女屋（Fairy House）里找到了莫里斯”——他朝崖顶一挥手示意——“我离开他的时候，他还穿着一身和这件一样的白衣裳？”

“你昨夜是这么跟我们说的，”克林顿爵士确认道。

“可是后来莫里斯在博物馆露面的时候，”迈克尔接着说，“他穿的是寻常的晚礼服。那身皮埃罗戏服，他中途已经脱手了。”

“这倒是真的，”克林顿爵士附和。

“有没有这种可能，”迈克尔继续道，“我走之后，莫里斯熬过了他那阵烦恼——不管那烦恼是什么——然后把化装衣裳从这里扔下了崖边。这件没准就是。”

“这举动可真够古怪的，”克林顿爵士评道。“你能想出半点说得通的理由，他为什么要干这种事吗？”

“想不出，”迈克尔坦率地承认。“可昨夜整桩事本来就似乎毫无道理可言；连我们追的那家伙都逃脱了，这口气我都咽下了，如今这左近再冒出什么事，我几乎都不会意外。我只是把情况摆给您听。像样的解释，我编不出来。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton seemed to be reflecting before he spoke again.

“To tell you the truth, I was rather disappointed with the result of that drag.
