Quite obviously—this isn’t official information, for you can see it with your own eyes—quite obviously that Pierrot costume must have been wrapped round some weight or other, or it wouldn’t have sunk to the bottom. And in the dragging the weight fell out. I could make a guess at what the weight was; but I wish we’d fished it up. It doesn’t matter much, really; but one likes to get everything one can.”

Michael, unable to guess what lay behind this, kept silent in the hope that there was more to come; but the Chief Constable swung off to a fresh subject.

“Did you take a careful note of the costumes of the gang who helped you in the attempt to round the beggar up? Could you make a list of them if it became necessary?”

Michael considered for the best part of a minute before answering.

“Some of them I could remember easily enough; but not all, I’m sure. It was a bit confused, you know; and some of the crew turned up pretty late, when all my attention was focused on the final roundup. I really couldn’t guarantee to give you an accurate list.”

Sir Clinton’s nod indicated approval.

“That’s what I like,” he said. “I’d rather have a definite No than a faked-up list that might mean nothing at all. But there’s one point that’s really important. Did you notice, among your assistants, anybody in white like the man you were hunting?”

Michael apparently had no need to pause before replying.

“No,” he said definitely, “I saw nobody of that sort. I suppose you mean Maurice. He certainly wasn’t in the cordon when it went into the spinney or when it came out on the terrace. I’m absolutely sure of my ground there. But of course he may have been one of the latecomers. Almost as soon as we got to the terrace we had to sprint off down to the lake side, you see; and he might quite well have been a bit slow in the chase and have reached the top only after we’d come down here.”

“That’s all I wanted to know,” said Sir Clinton, with a finality which prevented any angling for further information.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士似乎沉思了片刻，才又开口。

“实话说，刚才那一拖的战果，我是有些失望的。明摆着——这不算公务情报，因为你亲眼也看得见——明摆着，那身皮埃罗戏服必定裹着什么重物，不然沉不到湖底。而拖捞的时候，重物脱落了。那重物是什么，我猜得出个大概；可惜没能捞上来。其实关系不大；只是能到手的，总想都拿到手。”

迈克尔猜不透这话背后的文章，只盼着还有下文，便保持沉默；可郡警察局长已经转到一个新话题上去了。

“帮你围捕那家伙的那伙人，他们的戏服你仔细留意过吗？如有必要，你能开出一张清单来吗？”

迈克尔足足想了快一分钟，才作回答。

“有几位的我还记得清楚；但全部嘛，肯定记不全。当时场面有点乱，您知道的；有些人到得挺晚，那时我全部注意力都在最后收网上。准确无误的清单，我实在打不了包票。”

克林顿爵士点头表示认可。

“我就喜欢这样，”他说。“我宁可要一个干脆的‘不’，也不要一张什么都不是的编造清单。不过有一点真正要紧。在你那些帮手里，你留意到有没有穿白衣裳的，像你们追的那个人一样？”

迈克尔显然无须思索就能作答。

“没有，”他答得斩钉截铁，“那样的人我一个也没看见。您是指莫里斯吧。包围圈进小树林的时候，还有它出林上露台的时候，他肯定都不在圈里。这一点我十足有把握。当然，他也可能是后到的人之一。您瞧，我们几乎刚上露台，就得撒腿奔到湖边来；他若是追得慢了半拍，完全可能等我们下来之后才到崖顶。”

“我要知道的就是这些，”克林顿爵士说，语气干脆，断了再打探的念头。

===Original===
Michael evidently had no desire to outstay his welcome, for in a few minutes he rose to his feet.

“I think I’ll go over to Ravensthorpe now,” he said. “I suppose you’re not going to leave here for a while?”

The words recalled to Sir Clinton the fact that he had not yet congratulated Michael on his engagement. He hastened to repair the oversight.

“I was looking for you at the dance last night,” he explained, after Michael had thanked him, “but before I got hold of you, this burglary business cropped up, and I’ve had hardly a minute to spare since then. By the way, if you’re going over to the house, you might tell Joan that I shall probably have to pay them a visit shortly, but I’ll ring up and let them know when I’m coming.”

Michael nodded and turned away, skirting the lakelet on his way to Ravensthorpe. Sir Clinton sauntered over to the waterside and watched the dragging operations which were still going on. When he made his way back to the hillock again, Inspector Armadale followed him.

“There’s another point that occurred to me, sir,” he explained. “I think you told me that Polegate was wearing a Harlequin’s costume last night?”

“That’s correct,” Sir Clinton confirmed. “And what then?”

“One difficulty I’ve had,” the Inspector went on, “was to explain how the fellow in white got away from them all so neatly. I think I see now how it was done.”

Sir Clinton made no effort to conceal his interest.

“Yes, Inspector?”

Armadale obviously took this as complimentary.
===Chinese===
迈克尔显然不想多留惹人嫌，几分钟后便起身了。

“我想我现在就到雷文索普那边去，”他说。“您一时半会儿不会离开这儿吧？”

这话提醒了克林顿爵士：自己还没恭喜迈克尔订婚呢。他赶紧弥补这个疏忽。

“昨夜舞会上我一直在找你，”迈克尔道过谢后，他解释道，“可还没找着你，就冒出了窃案这档子事，从那时起我几乎没有一分钟空闲。对了，你要是去宅子那边，不妨告诉琼（Joan），我最近恐怕得登门拜访一趟，来之前我会打电话知会他们。”

迈克尔点点头，转身走了，沿着小湖边往雷文索普去。克林顿爵士踱到水边，观看仍在进行的打捞作业。待他再回到土丘上时，阿玛代尔探长跟了过来。

“我又想到一点，长官，”他解释道。“我记得您说过，波尔盖特昨夜穿的是一身哈里昆戏服？”

“没错，”克林顿爵士证实。“然后呢？”

“我一直有个疑难，”探长继续道，“就是想不通那白衣人怎么能脱身得那么干净。现在我想，我明白那是怎么做到的了。”

克林顿爵士这回毫不掩饰他的兴味。

“说说看，探长？”

阿马代尔显然把这当成了夸奖。

===Original===
“This is how I figure it out, sir. Polegate had a white jacket and Pierrot trousers on over his Harlequin costume. At the end of the chase he bolted into the spinney and out on to the terrace above here. That gave him a breathing-space. It took Mr. Clifton a minute or two to organize his cordon; and during that time the thief was hidden from them by the trees.”

“That’s obviously true,” Sir Clinton admitted. “If he did change his costume, it must have been at that moment.”

“I expect he had a weight of some sort ready on the terrace,” the Inspector continued. “When he’d stripped off his jacket and trousers, he wrapped them round the weight and pitched them over into the pool. That would make the splash they all heard.”

“And after that?”

The Inspector was evidently delighted with his idea.

“That leaves us with Polegate in Harlequin dress on the terrace, with a minute or two to spare before the cordon was ready to move forward into the spinney.”

“Admitted.”

“Do you remember the camouflaged ships in the War, Sir Clinton?”

“I sailed in one, if that’s what you mean.”

“Well, you know what they were like: all sorts of cockeyed streaks and colours mixed up in a regular tangle to destroy their real outlines. And what’s a Harlequin’s costume? Isn’t it the very same thing?”

Sir Clinton confirmed this with an historical allusion.

“You’re quite correct, Inspector. As a matter of fact, the Harlequin’s dress was originally designed to represent Invisibility. Nobody except Columbine was supposed to be able to see Harlequin, you know.”
===Chinese===
“我是这么琢磨出来的，长官。波尔盖特在哈里昆戏服外面，套了一件白上衣和一条皮埃罗裤子。追到末尾，他一头扎进小树林，又冲上这上头的露台。这就给了他一个喘息的空当。克利夫顿先生花了一两分钟才把包围圈组织起来；那段时间里，树木正好替那贼挡住了他们的视线。”

“这显然是实情，”克林顿爵士承认。“他若是换了装，必是在那个当口。”

“我估计他在露台上预先备好了什么重物，”探长接着说。“他把上衣和裤子扒下来，裹在重物上，抡起来扔进了池子。他们听见的那声落水响，就是这么来的。”

“那之后呢？”

探长显然为自己的想法得意得很。

“这么一来，波尔盖特就穿着哈里昆戏服站在露台上，离包围圈开进小树林，还富余一两分钟。”

“姑且承认。”

“大战里那些迷彩伪装船，克林顿爵士还记得吗？”

“我还真乘坐过一艘，如果你指的是这个。”

“那您就知道它们是什么模样了：各种歪七扭八的条纹和颜色乱糟糟搅作一团，把船的真实轮廓搅个粉碎。哈里昆的戏服又是什么？不正是同一路数吗？”

克林顿爵士以一则历史掌故印证了这一点。

“你说得完全对，探长。说起来，哈里昆那身戏服，原本就是用来表现‘隐身’的。除了科隆比娜（Columbine），谁都不该看得见哈里昆，您知道的。”

===Original===
Inspector Armadale hurried to his conclusion.

“What was to hinder Polegate, during that breathing-space, getting back into the spinney? It was a moonlight night. You know what the spinney would be like under a full moon: it would be all dappled with spots of moonlight coming through the trees. And against a setting of that sort the Harlequin costume would be next door to invisible. He’d only have to stand still in some chequered spot and no one would detect him. They were all hunting for a man dressed in white. None of them noticed him. None of them saw him, I guess.”

Much to the Inspector’s surprise, Sir Clinton shook his head.

“I’d be prepared to bet pretty heavily that someone saw him,” he affirmed.

The Inspector looked at his Chief for a moment, obviously taken aback.

“You think someone saw him?”

Then a flood of light from a fresh angle in his mind seemed to illuminate the question.

“You mean he had a confederate in the cordon? Someone who let him through and kept it dark? I never thought of that! You had me beaten there, Sir Clinton. And of course, now I see it, that’s the simplest solution of the whole affair. If we can get a list of the people in the cordon, we’ll be able to pick out the confederate before long.”

Sir Clinton damped his enthusiasm slightly.

“It won’t be so easy to get that list, Inspector. Remember the confusion of the whole business: the hurry, the effect of moonlight, the masks, the costumes, and all the rest of it. You may be able to put a list together; but you’ll have some difficulty yourself in believing that you’ve tracked down every possible person who was in the line. And if you miss one …”

“He may be the man, you mean? Well, there’s no harm in trying. I’ll turn a sergeant on to gather all the news he can get.”

“It’ll be a good test of his capacity, then, even if nothing else comes out of it,” Sir Clinton certified, carelessly.
===Chinese===
阿玛代尔探长赶紧奔向他的结论。

“在那个喘息的空当里，又有什么拦着波尔盖特退回小树林去呢？那晚月色正明。满月下的小树林是什么光景，您是知道的：月光从树隙间漏下来，满地斑斑点点。衬着那样的背景，哈里昆戏服就近乎隐形。他只消在某个光影斑驳的地点站定不动，就没有一个人能识破他。他们全都在搜一个穿白衣裳的人。谁都没留意到他。我猜，压根就没人看见过他。”

令探长大为意外的是，克林顿爵士摇了摇头。

“我倒愿意下重注赌有人看见了他，”他断言。

探长望着他的局长，怔了片刻，显然措手不及。

“您认为有人看见了他？”

随即，脑中仿佛有一道新的光从另一个角度照亮了这个问题。

“您是说，包围圈里有他的同谋？有人放他过去，还守口如瓶？我怎么没想到这一层！这一手您可把我将死了，克林顿爵士。当然，现在我一看就明白，这才是全案最简单的答案。只要能拿到包围圈里人员的名单，不久就能把那同谋挑出来。”

克林顿爵士给他的热乎劲儿稍稍降了降温。

“那张名单，可没那么容易到手，探长。想想整桩事乱成了什么样：仓促、月光迷离、面具、戏服，还有其余种种。名单你也许拼凑得出；可要让你自己相信，线上可能有的人你已一个不落地追了出来，那可就难了。而要是漏掉一个……”

“您是说，漏掉的那个没准就是那人？唉，试试总没坏处。我派一名巡佐去，能收罗到的消息都收罗来。”

“那倒正好考验考验他的本事，就算别的什么也捞不着，”克林顿爵士漫不经心地下了个断语。

## VIII The Murder in the Museum / 第八章 博物馆中的谋杀案

===Original===
Sir Clinton cut short the shrill ringing of his desk telephone by picking up the receiver.

“The Chief Constable speaking,” he informed his inquirer.

Michael Clifton’s voice sounded over the wire.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士（Sir Clinton）拿起听筒，止住了桌上电话一阵阵刺耳的铃声。

“我是郡警察局长，”他告知来电问讯的人。

话筒里传来迈克尔·克利夫顿（Michael Clifton）的声音。

===Original===
“Can you come up to Ravensthorpe at once, Sir Clinton, or send Inspector Armadale? There’s a bad business here. Mr. Foss has been murdered. I’ve taken care that no one has got off the premises; and I’ve seen to it that his body has been left as it was found.”

Sir Clinton glanced at his wristwatch.

“I’ll drive across as soon as possible. See that things are left undisturbed, please. And collect all the people who can give any evidence, so that we needn’t waste time hunting for them. Goodbye.”

He shifted the switch of his telephone and spoke again.

“Is Inspector Armadale here just now?” he asked the constable who answered his call. “Tell him I wish to see him in my room immediately.”
===Chinese===
“克林顿爵士，您能立刻来一趟雷文索普（Ravensthorpe）吗？或者派阿玛代尔（Armadale）探长来也行。这儿出了大乱子。福斯（Foss）先生被人谋杀了。我已安排妥当，没放一个人离开宅子；尸体也吩咐了照发现时的原样放着。”

克林顿爵士瞥了一眼腕表。

“我尽快开车过去。请务必保持现场原样不动。再把所有能提供情况的人都聚拢来，省得我们费工夫四处找人。再见。”

他扳过电话的切换开关，又开口说话。

“阿玛代尔探长这会儿在吗？”他问接电话的警士。“告诉他我要他立刻到我房间里来一趟。”

===Original===
While waiting for Armadale, Sir Clinton had a few moments in which to consider the information he had just received.

“This looks like Part II of the Ravensthorpe affair,” he reflected. “Foss’s only connection with Ravensthorpe was the business of these Medusa Medallions. First one has the theft of the replicas; now comes the murder of this American agent. It’s highly improbable that two things like that could be completely independent.”

His cogitation was interrupted by the entry of Armadale, and in a few words Sir Clinton gave him the fresh information which had come to hand.

“We’ll go up there at once in my car, Inspector. Get the necessary things together, please. Don’t forget the big camera. We may need it. And the constable who does photography for us had better come along also.”
===Chinese===
等阿玛代尔的工夫，克林顿爵士有几分钟可以琢磨刚接到的消息。

“这瞧着像是雷文索普事件的第二幕，”他思忖道。“福斯跟雷文索普的瓜葛，只有美杜莎徽章（Medusa Medallions）这桩买卖。先是复制品失窃；如今又出了这位美国代理人遇害。这样两桩事竟会全然互不相干，殊难置信。”

阿玛代尔进来，打断了他的沉思；克林顿爵士三言两语，把刚到手的新消息告诉了他。

“咱们这就坐我的车过去，探长。请把要用的东西收拾齐备。别忘了那台大照相机。兴许用得着。还有咱们队里管照相的那位警士，最好也一同前来。”

===Original===
Inspector Armadale wasted no time. In a very few minutes they were on the road. As he drove, Sir Clinton was silent; and Armadale’s attempt to extract further information from him was a complete failure.

“You know as much as I do, Inspector,” the Chief Constable pointed out. “Let’s keep clear of any preconceived ideas until we see how the land lies up yonder.”

When they reached Ravensthorpe, they found Michael Clifton waiting for them at the door.

“There are only two people who seem to know anything definite about things,” he replied to the Chief Constable’s first inquiry. “Joan’s one of them, but she really knows nothing to speak of. The other witness is Foss’s man﻿—Marden’s his name. Will you have a look at the body first of all, and then see Joan and this fellow?”
===Chinese===
阿玛代尔探长毫不耽搁。没过几分钟，两人已经上了路。开车的是克林顿爵士，一路一言不发；阿玛代尔想从他嘴里再掏出点消息，全然徒劳。

“你知道的跟我一样多，探长，”郡警察局长（Chief Constable）指出。“到了那头看清地势之前，咱们先别存任何先入之见。”

赶到雷文索普时，他们看见迈克尔·克利夫顿正在门口候着。

“眼下似乎只有两个人了解点确实情况，”他回答郡警察局长的头一个问话。“琼（Joan）算一个，可她实在说不上知道什么。另一个见证人是福斯的贴身男仆——名叫马登（Marden）。您是不是先验看一下尸体，然后再见琼和这个人？”

===Original===
Sir Clinton nodded his acquiescence and the party followed Michael to the museum. Mold, the keeper, was again on guard at the door of the room, and Sir Clinton made a gesture of recognition as he passed in, followed by Armadale.

A cursory glance showed Foss’s body lying in one of the bays formed by the showcases round the wall. The Inspector went forward, knelt down, and held a pocket-mirror to the dead man’s lips.

“Quite dead, sir,” he reported after a short time.

“The police surgeon will be here shortly,” Sir Clinton intimated. “If he’s dead, we can postpone the examination of the body for a short time. Everything’s to be left as it is until we come back. Turn the constable on to photograph the body’s position in case we need it, though I don’t think we shall. Now where’s Miss Chacewater? We’d better get her version of the affair first. Then we can question the valet.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士点头应允，一行人随迈克尔往博物馆去。看管人莫尔德（Mold）又守在那间屋子的门口；克林顿爵士进门时朝他打了个招呼的手势，阿玛代尔跟在身后。

粗粗一瞥，只见福斯的尸体躺在沿墙展柜围出的一处凹室里。探长走上前去，跪下身，把一面小镜子凑到死者唇边。

“死透了，先生，”过了片刻他报告道。

“警局法医很快就到，”克林顿爵士示意道。“人既已死了，验看尸身可以缓上一缓。我们回来之前，一切保持原样。叫那警士把尸体的位置拍摄下来，万一要用——虽然我看用不着。蔡斯沃特（Chacewater）小姐在哪儿？咱们最好先听听她这边的说法。然后再盘问那位男仆。”

===Original===
Without being acutely sensitive to atmosphere, Michael Clifton could not help noticing a fresh characteristic which had come into the Chief Constable’s manner. This was not the Sir Clinton with whom he was acquainted: the old friend of the Chacewater family, with his faintly whimsical outlook on things. Instead, Michael was now confronted by the head of the police in the district, engaged in a piece of official work and carrying it through in a methodical fashion, as though nothing mattered but the end in view.

Followed by the two officials, Michael led the way to the room where Joan was waiting. The Chief Constable wasted no time in unnecessary talk. In fact, he plunged straight into business in a manner which suggested more than a touch of callousness. Only later on did Michael realize that in this, perhaps, Sir Clinton displayed more tact than was apparent at the moment. By his manner, he suggested that a murder was merely an event like any other﻿—rather uncommon, perhaps, but not a thing which called for any particular excitement; and this almost indifferent attitude tended to relax Joan’s overstrained nerves.
===Chinese===
迈克尔·克利夫顿纵然对气氛不算特别敏感，也忍不住注意到郡警察局长的举止中透出一种新的气象。这已不是他熟识的那位克林顿爵士：那位蔡斯沃特家的老友，看事情总带着几分俏皮的豁达。此刻站在迈克尔面前的，是本郡的警察长官，正办着一桩公务，有条不紊地贯彻始终，仿佛除了眼前的目的，再没有什么要紧事。

迈克尔领着两位官员，走到琼等候的房间。郡警察局长半句多余的闲话也不说。说实在的，他开门见山就办公事，那副架势近乎有几分冷酷。直到后来迈克尔才醒悟：在这一点上，克林顿爵士所显的手腕，或许远比当时看上去的更为老到。他用自己的做派表明：谋杀不过是寻常诸事中的一件——也许少见些，却犯不着为之大惊小怪；这种近乎淡然的态度，倒使琼那绷得过紧的神经松了下来。

===Original===
“You didn’t see the crime actually committed, of course?”

Joan shook her head.

“Shall I begin at the beginning?” she asked.

Sir Clinton, by a gesture, invited her to sit down. He took a chair himself and pulled out a notebook. Inspector Armadale copied him in this. Michael remained standing near Joan’s chair, as though to lend her his moral support.

After thinking for a moment or two, Joan began her story.
===Chinese===
“您自然没有亲眼看见罪行发生的经过吧？”

琼摇了摇头。

“我从头说起好吗？”她问。

克林顿爵士以手势请她坐下。他自己也拉过一把椅子，掏出记事本。阿玛代尔探长依样照办。迈克尔仍旧站在琼的椅子旁边，像是要给她精神上的支撑。

琼思忖了片刻，开始讲述事情的原委。

===Original===
“Some time after lunch, I was sitting on the terrace with Mr. Foss. I forget what we were talking about﻿—nothing of any importance. Soon after that, Maurice came out of the house and sat down. I was surprised to see him, for he’d arranged to play golf this afternoon. But he’d sprained his right wrist badly after lunch, it seems, and had phoned to put off his match. He sat nursing his wrist, and we began to speak of one thing and another. Then, I remember, Mr. Foss somehow turned the talk on to some of the things we have. It was mostly about Japanese things that they spoke; and Mr. Foss seemed chiefly interested in some of the weapons my father had collected. I remember they talked about a Sukesada sword we have and about the Muramasa short sword. Mr. Foss said that he would like to see them some time. He thought that Mr. Kessock would be interested to hear about them.”
===Chinese===
“午饭后过了一阵，我正和福斯先生坐在露台上。谈的什么我忘了——都不是要紧事。不久莫里斯（Maurice）从宅子里出来，也坐下。我见了有些意外，因为他本约好今天下午打高尔夫。可看样子他午饭后把右手腕扭得不轻，打电话把球局推掉了。他坐在那儿护着手腕，我们就东一件西一件地闲聊起来。后来我记得，福斯先生不知怎么把话头引到了我们藏的某些东西上。他们谈的多半是日本货；福斯先生似乎对我父亲收藏的一些兵器格外感兴趣。我记得他们谈起我们的一把祐定（Sukesada）刀，还有那把村正（Muramasa）短刀。福斯先生说，改日想看一看。他觉得凯索克（Kessock）先生听了这些也会感兴趣。”

===Original===
She broke off and seemed to be trying to remember the transitions of the conversation. Sir Clinton waited patiently; but at last she evidently found herself unable to recall any details of the next stage in the talk.

“I can’t remember how it came up. It was just general talk about things in our collection and things Mr. Foss had seen elsewhere, but finally they got on to the Medusa Medallions somehow. Mr. Foss was telling Maurice how tantalizing it was to buy these things and pass them on to collectors when he’d like to keep them for himself if only he could afford it. Then it came out that he always took a rubbing of all the coins and medals he came across. I remember he made some little joke about his ‘poor man’s collection’ or something like that. I forget exactly how it came about, but either he asked Maurice to let him have another look at the Leonardo medallions or Maurice volunteered to let him take rubbings there and then. I can’t recall the exact way in which the suggestion was made. I wasn’t paying much attention at the time.”
===Chinese===
她停了下来，像是在努力回想谈话是怎么过渡的。克林顿爵士耐心等着；可到头来她显然想不起接下来的话头是怎么个来龙去脉了。

“我想不起这话题是怎么提起的。无非是泛泛闲谈，说些我们家收藏的东西、福斯先生在别处见过的东西，可不知怎么，最后谈到了美杜莎徽章上。福斯先生跟莫里斯说，干他们这行，买下这些东西再转手给藏家，心里有多熬人——他自己何尝不想留下，只是财力不许。后来又带出一条：他历来经手的钱币勋章，都要拓一张拓片。我记得他还就此打了个小小的趣，说是他的『穷人收藏』还是什么的。具体的来龙去脉我忘了，反正是要么他请莫里斯让他再看一眼达·芬奇（Leonardo）那几枚徽章，要么是莫里斯主动提出，就让他当场拓几张。话是怎么提起的，我记不清了。当时我没太留神。”

===Original===
She looked up to see if Sir Clinton showed any sign of annoyance at incomplete information; but his face betrayed neither dissatisfaction nor approval. Inspector Armadale, though following the evidence keenly and making frequent notes, seemed to think that very little of her information was to the point.

“Then,” Joan went on, “I remember Mr. Foss getting up from his chair and saying: ‘If you’ll wait a moment, I’ll get the things.’ And he went away and left Maurice and me together. I said: ‘What’s he gone for?’ And Maurice said: ‘Some paper to take rubbings of the medallions and some stuff he uses for that, dubbin or something.’ In a few minutes, Mr. Foss came back again with some sheets of paper and some black stuff in his hand. I was interested in seeing how he did his rubbing or whatever you call it, so I went with them to the museum.”
===Chinese===
她抬眼看看克林顿爵士，不知他会不会因为情况不全而露出不耐；可他脸上既无不满，也无嘉许。阿玛代尔探长虽热切地循着证词听，不时记上一笔，看神气却觉得她的话多半文不对题。

“后来，”琼接着说，“我记得福斯先生从椅子上起身，说：『稍等片刻，我去把东西取来。』他就走了，把莫里斯和我撂在一处。我问：『他取什么去了？』莫里斯说：『拓徽章用的纸，还有他擦拓用的什么料，油膏还是什么的。』没几分钟，福斯先生回来了，手里拿着几张纸，还有一团黑色的东西。我倒有点想看看他怎么个拓法，就跟着他们去了博物馆。”

===Original===
“And then?” Sir Clinton prompted. As they were evidently coming near the moment of the murder in Joan’s narrative, it was clear that he wished to leave her no time to think of the crime itself.

“We went into the museum. Since that night of the masked ball, Maurice has removed most of the smaller articles of value from the cases and put them into the safe; so in order to get the medallions he had to open the safe. It’s a combination lock, you know; and as I knew Maurice wouldn’t like us to be at his elbow while he was setting the combination, I took Mr. Foss under my wing and led him over to where the Sukesada sword is hung on the wall. We looked at it for a few moments. I remember taking it out of its sheath to show the blade to Mr. Foss. Then I heard Maurice slamming the door of the safe; and when we went into the bay where it is, Maurice was there with the Leonardo medallions in his hand.”
===Chinese===
“然后呢？”克林顿爵士提了一句。琼的叙述眼看就要挨近凶案发生的当口，他分明是有意不让她有工夫去想罪行本身。

“我们进了博物馆。打假面舞会那晚起，莫里斯就把柜里多数值钱的小件收进了保险柜；所以要取徽章，就得开柜。您知道，那是把转字密码锁；我晓得莫里斯拨密码的时候不喜欢别人在他肘边盯着，我就把福斯先生照应着带走，领他到墙上挂着祐定刀的地方。我们看了几眼。我记得还把刀抽出鞘，让福斯先生看了看刀身。后来我听见莫里斯砰地关上保险柜门；等我们走进柜子所在的那格凹室，莫里斯已站在那儿，手里托着达·芬奇那几枚徽章。”

===Original===
“One moment,” Sir Clinton interrupted. “You said it was a combination lock on the safe. Do you happen to know the combination?”

Joan shook her head.

“Maurice is the only one who knows that. He never told it to any of us.”

Sir Clinton invited her to continue.
===Chinese===
“且慢，”克林顿爵士插话。“你方才说保险柜上是把转字密码锁。那组密码你知道不知道？”

琼摇了摇头。

“只有莫里斯一个人知道。他从不告诉家里任何人。”

克林顿爵士请她继续讲下去。

===Original===
“Maurice handed Mr. Foss one of the medallions and Mr. Foss took it over to the big central case﻿—the one with the flat top. Then he began to take a rubbing of the medallion with his paper and black stuff. He didn’t seem quite satisfied with his first attempt, so he had a second try at it. As we were watching him, he seemed to prick up his ears, and then he said: ‘There’s someone calling for you, Miss Chacewater.’ I couldn’t hear anything myself; but he explained that the voice was pretty far off. He had extra good hearing, I remember he said. He seemed very positive about it, so I went off to see what it was all about.”
===Chinese===
“莫里斯递给福斯先生一枚徽章，福斯先生把它拿到中央那座大展柜那儿——就是平顶的那座。接着他就用纸和那黑色颜料拓起徽章来。头一回拓的他似乎不太满意，又试了第二回。我们正看着他拓，他忽然像是竖起了耳朵，随即说道：『有人在叫您，蔡斯沃特小姐。』我自己什么也没听见；可他解释说，那声音隔得相当远。他说自己耳朵格外灵——我记得他是这么说的。他一口咬定，我便去看看究竟。”

===Original===
“Was that the last time you saw him?”

“Yes,” said Joan, but she had obviously more to tell.

“And then?”

“As I was going away from the museum door, I met Mr. Foss’s man, Marden. He had a small brown-paper parcel in his hand. He stopped me and asked me if I knew where Mr. Foss was. Something about the parcel, I gathered, though I didn’t stop to listen to him. I told him Mr. Foss was in the museum; and I went on to see if I could find who was calling. I searched about and came across Mr. Clifton; but I didn’t hear anyone calling my name. Mr. Foss must have been mistaken.”
===Chinese===
“那是您最后一次见到他吗？”

“是的，”琼说，可她显然还有话要讲。

“后来呢？”

“我离开博物馆门口的时候，迎面碰上福斯先生的男仆马登。他手里拿着一个牛皮纸小包裹。他拦住我，问我知不知道福斯先生在哪儿。我揣摩他是为那包裹的事找我，不过我没停下听他讲。我告诉他福斯先生在博物馆里；接着我就继续去找是谁在叫。我四处寻了一遍，撞见了克利夫顿先生；可我没听见任何人叫我的名字。想必是福斯先生听岔了。”

===Original===
“And then?”

Michael Clifton evidently thought it unnecessary that Joan should bear the whole burden of giving evidence. At this point he broke in.

“Miss Chacewater and I were together in the winter-garden when I heard a shout of ‘Murder!’ I didn’t recognize the voice at the time. I left Miss Chacewater where she was and made my way as quick as I could towards the voice. It came from the museum, so I hurried there. I found Foss on the floor with a dagger of some sort in his chest. He was gone, so far as I could see, before I came on the scene at all. The man Marden was in the room, tying up his hand. It was bleeding badly and he said he’d cut it on the glass of a case. I kept him under my eye till I could get a couple of keepers; and then I rang you up at the station.”
===Chinese===
“后来呢？”

迈克尔·克利夫顿显然觉得，不必让琼一个人担尽作证的担子。说到这里，他插了进来。

“我和蔡斯沃特小姐正在温室里，忽听得一声『杀人啦！』的喊叫。当时我没听出那是谁的嗓音。我把蔡斯沃特小姐留在原地，自己朝声音那边尽快赶去。声音出自博物馆，我便直奔那里。只见福斯倒在地板上，胸口插着一把匕首模样的东西。依我看，没等我到场，人早就没了。那个叫马登的在屋里，正包扎自己的手。手上的血淌得很凶，他说是叫展柜的玻璃割的。我盯着他，直到找来两个看管人；然后才往局里给您挂了电话。”

===Original===
“What had become of Mr. Chacewater?” Sir Clinton asked, without showing that he attached more than a casual interest to the question.

“That’s the puzzle,” Michael admitted. “I didn’t see him anywhere in the museum at the moment and I’ve been hunting for him everywhere since then: but he’s not turned up. He may have gone out into the grounds, of course, and left Foss alone in the museum; and possibly he had got out of earshot before the cry of ‘Murder!’ was raised by the valet. I don’t know.”

Sir Clinton saw that the Inspector wished to ask a question, but he silenced him by a glance.

“One more point, and we’re done, I think,” he said, turning to Joan. “Can you give me a rough idea of the time when the cry of ‘Murder!’ was raised? I mean, how long was it after you had left the museum yourself?”
===Chinese===
“蔡斯沃特先生那时去哪儿了？”克林顿爵士问道，语气里不显出对这个问题有什么超出寻常的关切。

“这正是费解的地方，”迈克尔承认。“当时我在博物馆里哪儿也没见到他，此后我又四处找了个遍：可他始终没有露面。当然，他也许出到园子里去了，把福斯一个人撂在博物馆；也可能男仆喊『杀人啦』之前，他就已走出了听得见喊声的地方。我不知道。”

克林顿爵士看出探长想发问，却用眼色止住了他。

“再问一点，我想就问完了，”他转向琼说道。“『杀人啦』那一声喊起的大概时刻，你能给我个约数吗？我是说，距你自己离开博物馆过了多久？”

===Original===
Joan thought for a few seconds.

“It took me three or four minutes before I came across Mr. Clifton, and we were together﻿—how long would you say, Michael?﻿—before we heard the shout?”

“Not more than five minutes,” Michael suggested.

“That’s about it,” Joan confirmed. “That would make it about eight or nine minutes, roughly, between the time I left the museum and the time we heard the shout.”

“About that,” Michael agreed.
===Chinese===
琼想了想，不过几秒钟。

“我走了三四分钟才碰见克利夫顿先生，然后我们在一起待了——你说有多久，迈克尔？——才听见那声喊？”

“至多五分钟，”迈克尔估量道。

“差不多是这个数，”琼确认道。“这么算来，从我离开博物馆，到我们听见那声喊，前后大约八九分钟。”

“差不多，”迈克尔附和。

===Original===
Sir Clinton rose and closed his notebook.

“That’s all you have to tell us? Everything that bears on the matter, so far as you know?”

Joan paused for a moment or two before replying.

“That’s all that I can remember,” she said at last, after an evident effort to recall any fresh details. “I can’t think of anything else that would be of use.”

“You’ve no idea where your brother is?”

“None at all,” Joan answered. Then a thought seemed to strike her. “You don’t think Maurice had anything to do with this?” she demanded, anxiously.

“He’ll turn up shortly to speak for himself, I’ve no doubt,” Sir Clinton said, as though to reassure her. “Now that’s all we need just now, so far as you’re concerned. I’m going to take Mr. Clifton away for a few minutes, but he’ll be back again almost immediately.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士起身，合上记事本。

“你要说的就这些了？就你所知，凡与这事有关的，都说了？”

琼迟疑了一两息才作答。

“我记得起来的就这些了，”她终于说，此前显然还努力回想有无新的细节。“再想不出别的什么有用的了。”

“你不知道你哥哥在哪儿？”

“全然不知，”琼答道。随即她像蓦地想起什么。“您不会是疑心莫里斯跟这事有干系吧？”她急切地追问。

“他很快就会露面，自己来说清楚的，这一点我不怀疑，”克林顿爵士说，像是安抚她。“眼下要问你的，就到此为止。我要带克利夫顿先生出去几分钟，他几乎立刻就会回来。”

===Original===
With a reassuring smile, the Chief Constable excused himself and led the way to the door, followed by Michael and the Inspector. As soon as he was out of the room, he turned to Michael.

“You’re quite sure that Mr. Chacewater wasn’t in the museum when you reached it?”

Michael considered carefully before replying.

“I don’t see how he could have been. I glanced into all the bays; and you know there isn’t cover enough for a cat in the place.”

“Was the safe door open or shut, did you notice?”

Michael again reflected before replying.

“Shut, I’m almost certain.”
===Chinese===
郡警察局长带着安抚的一笑，告了个便，领头向门口走去，迈克尔和探长跟在后面。一出屋门，他就转向迈克尔。

“你能肯定，你赶到时蔡斯沃特先生不在博物馆里？”

迈克尔仔细想了想才回答。

“我看他不可能在。每格凹室我都扫了一眼；您知道，那地方连只猫都藏不下。”

“保险柜的门是开着还是关着，你注意到了吗？”

迈克尔又思量了一番才作答。

“关着，我几乎可以断定。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton in his turn seemed to reflect for a moment or two.

“We’ll have a look at this fellow Marden, now, I think, Inspector, if you’ll bring him along to the museum. We’d better hear his tale on the spot. It’ll save explanations about the positions of things.”

Inspector Armadale departed on his quest while Michael and the Chief Constable made their way to the scene of the crime. Suddenly Sir Clinton turned and confronted Michael.

“Have you any notion whatever as to where Maurice has gone? I want the truth.”

Michael was manifestly taken aback by the direct demand.

“I haven’t a notion,” he declared. “He wasn’t in the museum when I got there, so far as I know. You can put me on my oath over that, if you like.”

The Chief Constable scanned his face keenly, but made no comment on his statement. He led the way to the museum; and they had hardly passed through the door before Inspector Armadale returned with the valet.
===Chinese===
这回轮到克林顿爵士沉吟了一两息。

“我看，咱们现在就见见马登这个家伙吧，探长，请你把他带到博物馆来。他的话最好当场听。也省得再解释各样东西的方位。”

阿玛代尔探长领命去了，迈克尔和郡警察局长则往凶案现场走。克林顿爵士忽然转身，直面迈克尔。

“莫里斯到底去了哪儿，你有没有一点影子？我要实话。”

这直截了当的一问，显然让迈克尔吃了一惊。

“一点影子也没有，”他声明。“据我所知，我到的时候他不在博物馆。这一点您要叫我起誓，我也敢起。”

郡警察局长锐利地审视着他的脸，却对他的话不加评论。他领头往博物馆去；两人前脚进门，阿玛代尔探长后脚就带着那男仆回来了。

===Original===
Marden appeared to be a man of about thirty years of age. Sir Clinton noticed that he carried himself well and did not seem to have lost his head in the excitement of the past hour. When he spoke, it was without any appreciable accent; and he seemed to take pains to be perfectly clear in his evidence. Sir Clinton, by an almost imperceptible gesture, handed over the examination of the valet to the Inspector. Armadale pulled out his notebook once more.

“What’s your name?” he demanded.

“Thomas Marden.”

“How long have you been in Mr. Foss’s service?”

“Since he arrived here from America, about three months ago.”
===Chinese===
马登看样子三十岁上下。克林顿爵士注意到他举止挺拔，经过过去这一小时的骚动，似乎并未慌神。他开口说话时，听不出什么明显的口音；作证也字斟句酌，力求清楚。克林顿爵士以一个几乎察觉不到的手势，把对男仆的盘问交给了探长。阿玛代尔又掏出了记事本。

“你叫什么名字？”他问道。

“托马斯·马登。”

“你在福斯先生手下当差多久了？”

“打他从美国到这儿起，约莫三个月。”

===Original===
“How did he come to engage you?”

“Advertisement.”

“You knew nothing about him before that?”

“Nothing.”

“Where was he living then?”

“At 474a Gunner’s Mansions, S.W. It’s a service flat.”

“He still has that flat?”

“Yes.”
===Chinese===
“他是怎么雇下你的？”

“登报招的。”

“在那之前你对他一无所知？”

“一无所知。”

“他那时住在哪儿？”

“西南区冈纳公寓（Gunner’s Mansions）474a号。是套服务式公寓。”

“那套公寓他现在还留着？”

“留着。”

===Original===
“How did he spend his time?”

The valet seemed astonished by the question.

“I don’t know. None of my business.”

Inspector Armadale was not to be turned aside.

“You must have known whether he stayed in the flat or went out regularly at fixed times.”

Marden seemed to see what was wanted.

“You mean, did he go out to an office every day? No, he came and went just when it suited him.”
===Chinese===
“他平日怎么打发时间？”

男仆似乎觉得这问题问得离奇。

“这我不知道。不关我的事。”

阿玛代尔探长却不肯被岔开。

“他成天待在公寓里，还是定时出门，这个你总该知道。”

马登像是明白了问话的用意。

“您是问他每天去不去写字楼办公？不去，他来去全随自己的便。”

===Original===
“Had he much correspondence?”

“Letters? Just about what one might expect.”

The Inspector looked up gloomily. So far, he had not got much to go upon.

“What do you mean by: ‘Just what one might expect?’ ”

“He got some letters every day, sometimes one or two, sometimes half a dozen. Just what one might expect.”
===Chinese===
“他信件多不多？”

“信？跟寻常人收的差不多。”

探长阴郁地抬眼看了看。到这时为止，他手上还没摸着多少凭据。

“你那句『跟寻常人差不多』，是什么意思？”

“他每天都收几封信，有时一两封，有时五六封。跟寻常人收的差不多。”

===Original===
“Have you any idea whether they were business letters or merely private correspondence?”

Marden seemed annoyed by the question.

“How should I know?” he demanded, stiffly. “It’s not my business to pry into my employer’s affairs.”

“It’s your business to read the addresses on the envelopes to see that the postman hasn’t left wrong letters. Did you notice nothing when you did that? Were the addresses mainly typewritten or written by hand?”

“He got bills and advertisements with the address typewritten﻿—like most of us. And one or two letters came addressed by hand.”
===Chinese===
“那些信是买卖上的信还是私人通信，你有个数吗？”

马登似乎被这问题惹得不快。

“我哪儿知道？”他生硬地反问。“窥探东家的私事，不归我管。”

“可看清信封上的地址、别叫邮差投错了信，这就归你管了吧。你收信的时候就没留意过什么吗？那些地址多是打字打的，还是手写的？”

“账单和广告都是打字的——跟咱们大多数人一样。另有一两封信，地址是手写的。”

===Original===
“Did you notice the stamps?”

“Some were American, of course.”

“So it comes to this,” Inspector Armadale concluded, “he was not carrying on a big business from the flat; most of his letters were ordinary bills and so forth; but he had some private correspondence as well; and part of his correspondence was with America? Why couldn’t you tell us that straight off, instead of having it dragged out of you?”

The valet was quite unruffled by the Inspector’s tone.

“I hadn’t put two and two together the way you do. They were just letters to me. I didn’t think anything about them.”
===Chinese===
“邮票你留意过吗？”

“有几张自然是美国的。”

“这么说来，事情就归结成这样，”阿玛代尔探长归结道，“他没在那套公寓里做什么大买卖；信件多半是寻常账单之类；可他也有私人通信；而且有一部分通信是跟美国通的？这些话你怎么不爽爽快快说出来，偏要人一句句往外挤？”

男仆对探长的口气毫不动气。

“我不像您那样会把二加二凑成四。在我眼里那就是些信。我没多想过。”

===Original===
Inspector Armadale showed no appreciation of this indirect tribute to his powers.

“Had he many visitors?”

“Not at the flat. He may have met his friends in the restaurant downstairs for all I know.”

“Do you remember any visitors at the flat?”

“No.”
===Chinese===
对这句拐着弯捧他本事的话，阿玛代尔探长毫不领情。

“他家客人多吗？”

“公寓里没有。他是不是在楼下餐馆会朋友，我就不知道了。”

“公寓里来过什么客人，你记得吗？”

“不记得。”

===Original===
The Inspector seemed to recollect something he had missed.

“Did he get any telegrams?”

“Yes.”

“Frequently?”

“Fairly often.”

“You’ve no idea of the contents of these wires?”

Marden obviously took offence at this.

“You asked me before if I pried into his affairs; and I told you I didn’t.”
===Chinese===
探长像是想起了一处漏问的。

“他收不收电报？”

“收。”

“勤吗？”

“不算稀。”

“这些电报里写的是什么，你就全无概念？”

马登显然被这话得罪了。

“您方才问过我，是不是窥探他的私事；我告诉过您，我不干那个。”

===Original===
“How often did these wires arrive?” the Inspector demanded, taking no notice of Marden’s annoyance.

“Perhaps once or twice a week.”

“Did he bet?” the Inspector inquired, as though it had just struck him that the telegrams might thus be explained.

“I know nothing about that.”
===Chinese===
“这些电报隔多久来一回？”探长追问，全不理会马登的不快。

“兴许一礼拜一两回。”

“他赌钱吗？”探长又问，像是蓦地想到，电报之事或许可以由此解释。

“这我一概不知。”

===Original===
Armadale went off on a fresh tack.

“Did he seem to be well off for money?”

“He paid me regularly, if that’s what you mean.”

“He had a car and a chauffeur, hadn’t he?”

“Yes.”

“Were they his own or simply hired?”

“I don’t know. Not my business.”

“The Gunner’s Mansions flats are expensive?”

“They get the name of it. I don’t know what he paid.”
===Chinese===
阿玛代尔换了个路数。

“他手头看着宽裕吗？”

“他给我发工钱从不拖欠，倘若您问的是这个。”

“他有一辆汽车，还雇着司机，是吧？”

“是。”

“车是他自个儿的，还是租的？”

“不知道。不归我管。”

“冈纳公寓的房租贵吧？”

“名声是贵。他付多少，我不知道。”

===Original===
“You don’t seem to have had much curiosity, Marden.”

“I’m not paid for being curious.”

The Inspector put down his pencil and reflected for a moment or two.

“Have you any idea of his address in America?”

“Not my business.”

“Did he write many letters?”

“I couldn’t say. None of my business.”

“You can at least say whether he gave you any to post.”

“He didn’t.”
===Chinese===
“你这份好奇心似乎不重啊，马登。”

“没人给我发好奇的钱。”

探长放下铅笔，寻思了一两息。

“他在美国的地址，你有数吗？”

“不归我管。”

“他写信写得多吗？”

“说不上。不归我管。”

“他交没交过信让你去寄，这你总说得上吧。”

“没有过。”

===Original===
“Have you anything else you can tell us about him?”

Marden seemed to think carefully before he replied.

“All his clothes were split new.”

“Anything else?”

“He carried a revolver﻿—I mean an automatic.”

“What size was it?”

“About that length.”

The valet indicated the length approximately with his hands, and winced slightly as he moved the bandaged one.
===Chinese===
“关于他，你还有什么能告诉我们的？”

马登回答之前，似乎细细想了想。

“他的衣裳件件崭新。”

“还有呢？”

“他随身带一把左轮——我是说自动手枪。”

“多大个头？”

“约莫这么长。”

男仆用双手比了个大概的长短，挪动那只裹着绷带的手时，微微皱了皱眉。

===Original===
“H’m! A .38 or a .45,” Armadale commented. “Too big for a .22, anyway.”

He took up his pencil again.

“Now come to this afternoon. Begin at lunchtime and go on.”

Marden reflected for a moment, as though testing his memory.

“I’d better begin before lunch. Mr. Foss came to me with a parcel in his hand and asked me to take it over to Hincheldene post office. He wanted it registered. He offered to let me take the car if I wished; but I preferred to walk over. I like the fresh air.”
===Chinese===
“哼！不是 .38 的，就是 .45 的，”阿玛代尔评了一句。“反正比 .22 的大。”

他重新拿起铅笔。

“现在说今天下午。从午饭时说起，往下讲。”

马登寻思了片刻，像是在检核自己的记性。

“我最好从午饭前说起。福斯先生手里拿着个包裹来找我，让我送到欣切尔迪恩（Hincheldene）邮局去。他要寄挂号。他说我愿意的话，可以开车去；可我宁可走着去。我喜欢透透新鲜空气。”

===Original===
“And then?” demanded the Inspector with an unconscious plagiarism of his Chief.

“Immediately after lunch, I set out and walked through the grounds towards Hincheldene village. I didn’t hurry. It was a nice afternoon for a walk. By and by I met a keeper, and he told me I couldn’t go any farther in that direction. He’d orders to turn back anyone, he said. I talked to him for a minute or two, and explained where I was going; and I pulled the parcel out of my pocket as a guarantee of good faith. He didn’t know me, you see. And when I got the parcel out, I noticed the label quite by chance.”
===Chinese===
“后来呢？”探长追问，不知不觉学了他上司的腔。

“午饭后我立刻动身，穿过园子往欣切尔迪恩村子走。我不赶急。那天下午正宜散步。走着走着迎面遇到一个看管人，他说朝那个方向我不能再往前走了。他说他奉了命，见人就往回撵。我跟他搭了一两分钟话，说明我要上哪儿去；还把包裹从口袋里掏出来，以表心迹。您想，他不认得我。我把包裹掏出来的时候，纯属偶然地瞥见了那标签。”

===Original===
“Ah, you do look at addresses after all!” interjected the Inspector.

“Quite by chance,” Marden went on, without taking any notice of the thrust. “And I saw that Mr. Foss had made a mistake.”

“How did you know that,” Inspector Armadale demanded, with the air of a cat pouncing on a mouse. “You said you’d taken no interest in his correspondence and yet you knew this parcel was directed to a wrong address. Curious, isn’t it?”

Marden did not even permit himself to smile as he discomfited the Inspector.

“He’d left out the name of the town. An obvious oversight when he was writing the label.”

“Well, go on,” growled the Inspector, evidently displeased at losing his score.
===Chinese===
“啊哈，敢情你到底还是看地址的！”探长插了一句。

“纯属偶然，”马登接着往下说，全不理会这一刺。“我就看出福斯先生弄错了一处。”

“这你怎么知道的，”阿玛代尔探长质问道，那副神气活像猫扑老鼠。“你说过你对他的通信毫不上心，可你却知道这包裹写错了地址。蹊跷不蹊跷？”

马登反过来将了探长一军，脸上连一丝笑意也不肯露。

“他漏写了镇名。写标签的时候，一眼可见的疏忽。”

“行，往下说，”探长嘟囔道，丢了这一分，显然心头不快。

===Original===
“As soon as I saw that, I knew it was no good taking the thing to the post office as it was. So I asked the keeper a question or two about the shortest way to Hincheldene without getting on to the barred ground. Then I turned and came home again, intending to ask Mr. Foss to complete the address on the parcel.”

“What time was it when you reached here again?”

Marden considered for a while.

“I couldn’t say precisely. Sometime round about half-past three or a bit later. I didn’t look at the time.”
===Chinese===
“我一见这个，就知道照原样把东西送到邮局也是白搭。于是我问了那看管人一两句，打听着不踩禁地的、去欣切尔迪恩最近的路。然后就转身往回走，打算请福斯先生把包裹上的地址补全。”

“你回到这儿是几点？”

马登想了半晌。

“说不准。三点半前后，也许略晚些。我没看表。”

===Original===
“What did you do then?”

“I hunted about for Mr. Foss, but he didn’t seem to be in the house. At last, when I was just giving it up, I met Miss Chacewater coming away from this room, and she told me that Mr. Foss was inside. She went away, and I came to the door. It was half-open and I could hear voices inside: Mr. Foss and Mr. Chacewater from the sound. I thought they’d soon be coming out and that I’d get Mr. Foss as he passed me; so I waited, instead of interrupting them.”

“How long did you wait?”

“Only a minute or two, so far as I can remember.”
===Chinese===
“然后你做了什么？”

“我四处找福斯先生，可他似乎不在宅子里。最后我正要作罢，恰遇上蔡斯沃特小姐从这间屋子出来，她告诉我福斯先生就在里面。她走开了，我便走到门口。门半开着，听得见里头有人说话：听嗓音是福斯先生和蔡斯沃特先生。我琢磨他们很快就会出来，等他打我跟前过时我就能截住他；于是我就等着，没去搅扰他们。”

“你等了多久？”

“顶多一两分钟，我记得是这样。”

===Original===
“You could hear them talking?”

“I could hear the sound of their voices. I couldn’t hear what they said. There’s an echo or something in this room and all I heard was the tone they were speaking in.”

“What sort of tone do you mean?”

Marden paused as though searching for an adjective.

“It seemed to me an angry tone. They raised their voices.”

“As if they were quarrelling?”

“Like that. And then I heard Mr. Chacewater say: ‘So that’s what you’re after?’ Then I heard what sounded like a scuffle and a gasp. I was taken aback, of course. Who wouldn’t be? I stood stock still with the parcel in my hand for a moment or two. Then I got my head back and I pushed open the door and rushed into the room.”
===Chinese===
“他们说话你听得见？”

“听得见说话的声音。说的什么听不清。这屋里有回声还是怎么的，我只听出他们说话的腔调。”

“你说的是什么腔调？”

马登顿了顿，像是在找一个合适的形容字眼。

“听着我觉着是动气的腔调。他们嗓门抬高了。”

“像是吵架？”

“就是那样。接着我听见蔡斯沃特先生说：『原来你打的是这个主意？』随后我听见一阵厮扭似的动静，还有一声抽气。我自然吃了一惊。谁个不吃惊？我攥着包裹在原地呆立了一两息。然后我回过神来，推开门冲进了屋里。”

===Original===
“Be careful here,” Sir Clinton interrupted. “Don’t try to force your memory. Tell us exactly what comes back into your mind.”

Marden nodded.

“When I got into the room here,” he went on, “the first thing I saw was Mr. Chacewater. He had his back to me and was just turning the corner here.”

Marden walked across and indicated the end of the bay beyond the one which contained the safe, the last recess in the room at the end opposite from the door.

“He went round this corner in a hurry. That’s the last I saw of him.”
===Chinese===
“这里要仔细，”克林顿爵士插话。“不要硬逼自己的记性。想起什么，就一字不差地告诉我们。”

马登点了点头。

“我进到这屋里，”他接着说，“头一眼看见的就是蔡斯沃特先生。他背朝着我，正拐过这边的墙角。”

马登走过去，指了指保险柜所在那格再往里的一处凹室的尽头，也就是屋子最里头、正对着房门的那最后一格。

“他急匆匆拐过了这个角。那是我见他的最后一眼。”

===Original===
Marden’s face betrayed his amazement even at the recollection.

“Never mind that just now,” said Sir Clinton. “Tell us what you did yourself.”

“I couldn’t see Mr. Foss at the first glance; but when I got near the corner where I’d seen Mr. Chacewater, I saw Mr. Foss lying on the floor. I thought he’d slipped or something; and I went over to give him a hand up. Then I saw a big knife or a dagger through his chest and some blood on his mouth. As I was hurrying over to his side, I slipped on the parquet﻿—it’s very slippery﻿—and down I came. I put out my hand to save myself and my fist broke the glass in one of these cases. When I got up again, my hand was streaming with blood. It’s a nasty gash. So I pulled out my handkerchief and wrapped it around my hand before I did anything else. It was simply gushing with blood and I thought of it first of all.”
===Chinese===
即便事后再想，马登脸上仍掩不住那份惊诧。

“这个先不去管它，”克林顿爵士说。“说说你自己做了什么。”

“我头一眼没瞧见福斯先生；可等我走近我看见蔡斯沃特先生拐过的那个角，就看见福斯先生躺在地板上。我当他滑了一跤还是怎么的；便走过去想拉他一把。跟着我看见一把大刀还是匕首的东西穿透了他的胸口，嘴上还有些血。我急急赶到他身边去时，脚下一滑——这地板滑得很——就摔倒了。我伸手一撑，拳头砸破了这些展柜中一柜的玻璃。等我爬起来，手上血流如注。这口子不轻。于是我先掏出手帕，把手缠上，别的什么都顾不得。血一个劲儿地涌，我只能先顾这个。”

===Original===
Marden held up his roughly swathed hand in proof.

“I got to my feet again and went over to Mr. Foss. By that time he was either dead or next door to it. He didn’t move. I didn’t touch him, for I saw well enough he was done for. Then I went to the door and shouted ‘Murder!’ as hard as I could. Then while I was shouting, it struck me as queer that Mr. Chacewater had disappeared.”

“It didn’t occur to you that he might have slipped out of the room while your back was turned﻿—when you were busy over Mr. Foss?” demanded Inspector Armadale in a hostile tone.

Marden shook his head.

“It didn’t occur to me at all, because I knew it hadn’t happened. No one could have got out of the room without my seeing him.”
===Chinese===
马登举起那只草草缠着布的手，以为凭据。

“我重新爬起来，走到福斯先生跟前。那时候他要不是已经死了，也差不多了。他一动不动。我没碰他，因为我看得很清楚，人是没救了。我便走到门口，拼尽全力喊『杀人啦！』喊着喊着，我忽然觉得不对劲：蔡斯沃特先生不见了。”

“你背着身忙福斯先生那会儿，他就许溜出屋去了——你就没想到这个？”阿玛代尔探长带着敌意质问。

马登摇了摇头。

“压根儿没想到，因为我知道没那回事。谁也不可能出这间屋还不叫我瞧见。”

===Original===
“Go on with your story, please,” Sir Clinton requested.

“There’s nothing more to tell. I kept shouting ‘Murder!’ and I searched the room here while I was doing it. I found nothing.”

“Was the safe door closed when you saw it first?” Sir Clinton inquired.

“Yes, it was. I thought perhaps Mr. Chacewater might be inside, with the door pulled to; so I tried the handle. It was locked.”
===Chinese===
“请继续讲你的经过，”克林顿爵士要求道。

“再没什么可讲的了。我一路喊着『杀人啦』，一边喊一边把这屋子搜了一遍。什么也没找着。”

“你初见保险柜时，柜门是关着的？”克林顿爵士问道。

“关着的。我琢磨蔡斯沃特先生兴许就在柜里，门虚掩着；便转了转把手。锁着呢。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton put a further inquiry.

“You heard only two voices in the room before you burst in?”

A new light seemed to be thrown by this question across Marden’s mind.

“I heard only two people speaking: Mr. Foss and Mr. Chacewater; but of course I couldn’t swear that only two people were in the room. That’s what you meant, isn’t it?”

Inspector Armadale caught the drift of the inquiry.

“I suppose if one man can disappear in a mysterious way, there’s nothing against two men vanishing in the same way,” he hazarded. “So all you can really tell us is that Mr. Foss and Mr. Chacewater were here at any rate, and possibly there were other people as well?”

“I couldn’t swear to anyone except these two,” Marden was careful to state.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士又提一问。

“你闯进去之前，屋里只听得见两个嗓音？”

这个问题像是往马登心里投进了一线新的光亮。

“我只听见两个人说话：福斯先生和蔡斯沃特先生；可我自然不敢起誓，屋里就只有两个人。您指的是这个，对吧？”

阿玛代尔探长听出了这一问的用意。

“我想，倘若一个人能悄没声地消失，没道理两个人就不能照这么消失，”他试探着说。“所以你真正能告诉我们的就是：福斯先生和蔡斯沃特先生至少在这儿，兴许还有别的人在场，对吧？”

“除了这两位，我不敢给任何人作保，”马登措辞谨慎地声明。

===Original===
“Another point,” Sir Clinton went on. “Have you any idea whether Mr. Foss came into contact with a person or persons outside the house during his stay here? I mean people known to him before he came to Ravensthorpe?”

“I couldn’t say.”

“None of your business, I suppose?” Inspector Armadale put in, with an obvious sneer.

“None of my business, as you say,” Marden returned, equably. “I wasn’t engaged as a detective.”
===Chinese===
“还有一点，”克林顿爵士继续说。“福斯先生住在这儿期间，跟宅子外头的什么人有没有过接触——你有个数吗？我指的是他来雷文索普之前就认识的人。”

“说不上。”

“又不归你管了，我猜？”阿玛代尔探长插进来，讥诮之意昭然。

“诚如您所说，不归我管，”马登不动声色地回敬。“雇我的时候，可没雇我当侦探。”

===Original===
“Well, this question falls into your department,” Sir Clinton intervened, as Armadale showed signs of losing his temper. “What costume was Mr. Foss wearing on the night of the masked ball? You must know that.”

Marden replied without hesitation.

“He was got up as a cowpuncher. He hired the costume from London when he heard about the fancy dress. It was a pair of cowboy trousers, big heavy things with fringes on them; a leather belt with a pistol-holster on it; a coloured shirt; a neckcloth; and a flappy cowboy hat.”

“Rather a clumsy rig-out, then?”

Marden seemed to find difficulty in repressing a smile.

“It was as much as he could do to walk at all, until he got accustomed to the things. He told me it gave him a good excuse for not dancing. He wasn’t a dancing man, he said.”
===Chinese===
“这个嘛，倒归你的部门管，”克林顿爵士出面转圜，因为阿玛代尔已有动气的苗头。“假面舞会那晚，福斯先生穿的是什么行头？这个你总该知道。”

马登应声而答，毫不迟疑。

“他扮的是个牛仔。他打听到要办化装舞会，就从伦敦租了行头。一条牛仔裤，肥大厚重，边上缀着流苏；一条皮腰带，上头挂着手枪套；一件花衬衫；一方颈巾；还有一顶耷拉檐的牛仔帽。”

“那这身披挂可够笨重的喽？”

马登似乎憋笑憋得颇为吃力。

“起初他连路都走不利索，直到穿惯了这些东西才好些。他跟我说，这倒给了他一个不跳舞的好由头。他说自己不是个跳舞的人。”

===Original===
“He carried a revolver, you say. Did you ever see any sign that he was afraid of anything of this sort happening to him?”

“I don’t understand. How could I know what he was afraid of or what he wasn’t? It was none of my business.”

Sir Clinton’s smile took the edge off Marden’s reply.

“Oh, I think one might make a guess,” he said, “if one kept one’s eyes open. A terrified man would give himself away somehow or other.”

“Then either he wasn’t afraid or else I don’t keep my eyes open. I saw nothing of the sort.”
===Chinese===
“你说过他随身带左轮手枪。他有没有过什么怕遭这类暗算的形迹，你可曾见过？”

“我不明白这问的是什么。他怕什么不怕什么，我怎么会知道？那不归我管。”

克林顿爵士一笑，把马登这句回话的锋芒化去了。

“哦，依我看，一个人只要眼睛没闭着，总猜得出几分，”他说。“真要是怕得厉害，那人总会露出点马脚来。”

“那要么是他不怕，要么就是我眼睛没睁着。这类形迹我一点没见过。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton reflected for a moment or two. He glanced at Armadale.

“Any more questions you’d like to put? No? Then that will do, Marden. Of course there’ll be an inquest and your evidence will be required at it. You can stay on here until you’re needed. I’ll see Miss Chacewater about it. But for the present you’ve given us all the help you can?”

“Unless you’ve any more questions you want to ask,” Marden suggested.

Sir Clinton shook his head.

“No, I think I’ve got all I need for the present, thanks. I may want you again later on, of course.”

Marden waited for nothing further, but left the room pursued by a slightly vindictive glance from Inspector Armadale. When he had disappeared, Sir Clinton turned to Michael Clifton.

“Hadn’t you better go back to Joan, now? She must be rather nervous after this shock.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士沉吟了一两息。他瞥了瞥阿玛代尔。

“还有什么要问的？没有？那么就到这里，马登。自然还要开验尸审讯，到时候需要你出庭作证。你可以在这儿住下，等传唤。这事我跟蔡斯沃特小姐说。眼下嘛，你已把能帮的都帮到了吧？”

“除非您还有要问的，”马登说。

克林顿爵士摇了摇头。

“没有了，眼下要的我想都齐了，谢谢。当然，往后兴许还要找你。”

马登再无他话，转身出屋，身后跟着阿玛代尔探长一道略含怨毒的目光。他一消失，克林顿爵士便转向迈克尔·克利夫顿。

“你这就回琼那儿去吧，好不好？受了这场惊吓，她此刻必定相当不安。”

===Original===
Michael came to himself with a slight start when the Chief Constable addressed him. Hitherto his role had been purely that of a spectator; and he had been so wrapped up in it that it came as a faint surprise to find himself directly addressed. Throughout the proceedings he had been semi-hypnotized by the deadly matter-of-fact way in which the police were going about their work. When he had first heard of the murder, he had felt as though something unheard-of had invaded Ravensthorpe. Of course murders did take place: one read about them in the newspapers. But the idea that murder could actually be done in his own familiar environment had come to him with more than a slight shock. The normal course of things seemed suddenly diverted.
===Chinese===
郡警察局长一开口点他，迈克尔才微微一怔，回过神来。此前他纯粹是个旁观者；他全然沉在这个角色里，冷不丁被直接点名，竟有几分讶异。整个过程中，警方办事那股子要命的一板一眼，把他催眠了个半酣。初闻凶讯时，他只觉得有什么闻所未闻的东西侵入了雷文索普。凶案当然时有发生：报上常见。可凶杀竟会真的出在自己朝夕熟悉的环境里，这念头带给他的不止是轻微的震动。日常生活的正常轨道，仿佛蓦地被拨到了岔道上。

===Original===
But during the last ten minutes he had been a witness of the beginning of the police investigation; and the invincible impression of ordinariness had begun to replace the earlier nightmare quality in his mind. Here were a couple of men going about the business as though it were of no more tragic character than a search for a lost dog. It was part of their work to hunt out a solution of the affair. They were no more excited over it than a chess-player looking for the key-move in a problem. The cool, dispassionate way in which the Chief Constable had handled the affair seemed to strike a fresh note and to efface the suggestions of the macabre side of things which had been Michael’s first impression of the matter. The Dance of Death retreated gradually into the background in the face of all the minute questionings about letters, and visits, and parcels﻿—these commonplace things of everyday life.
===Chinese===
可过去十分钟里，他亲眼见证了警方调查的开端；那种无可抵挡的寻常之感，开始取代先前盘踞在他心里的梦魇味道。眼前这两个人办这桩事，就像它并不比寻找一条走失的狗更悲壮几分。寻出这桩案子的谜底，本是他们分内的工作。他们为之动容的程度，并不比一位棋手在排局中搜寻关键一着时更多。郡警察局长处理此事时那份冷静、不动声色的做派，像是敲出了一个别样的音调，抹去了这事阴森可怖的一面——那原是迈克尔对它的第一印象。面对这一连串关于信件、来访、包裹的细枝末节的盘问——这些日常生活中平平无奇的东西——死亡之舞渐渐退到了幕后。

===Original===
“If I can be of no use here,” he said, “I think I’d better go.”

He hesitated for a moment as a fresh thought struck him.

“By the way, how much of this is confidential?”

Sir Clinton looked at him with an expressionless face.

“I think I may leave that to your discretion. It’s not for broadcasting, at any rate.”

“What about Maurice?” Michael persisted.

“I’d leave Maurice out of it as far as possible,” said Sir Clinton, in obvious dismissal. “Now, Inspector, I think we’d better have a look at the late Mr. Foss.”

Michael retreated from the room as they turned towards the body on the floor.
===Chinese===
“要是我在这儿帮不上什么忙，”他说，“我想我还是走的好。”

他迟疑了一下，一个新的念头冒了出来。

“对了，这些话里头有多少是要保密的？”

克林顿爵士面无表情地看着他。

“这个嘛，我想可以交给你自己斟酌。总之，不是拿去广播的。”

“那莫里斯呢？”迈克尔追问。

“莫里斯，能不提就尽量不提，”克林顿爵士说，分明是送客的意思。“好了，探长，咱们还是去看看已故的福斯先生吧。”

两人转身朝地板上的尸体走去，迈克尔退出了房间。

===Original===
“Leave Maurice out of it!” he thought, as he walked at a snail’s pace towards the room where he had left Joan. “That’s a nice bit of advice! If you leave Maurice out of it, there seems to be nothing left in it. Now what the devil am I to say to her? If I say nothing, she’ll jump to the worst conclusion; and if I say anything at all, she’ll jump to the same.”
===Chinese===
“能不提就不提！”他一路朝琼所在的房间踱去，脚步慢得像蜗牛，心里翻着这个念头。“这忠告可真是妙极了！真要把莫里斯撇开，这案子里好像就什么都不剩了。这下我对她到底说什么才好？我什么都不说，她会往最坏处想；我随便说点什么，她想的还是那最坏的一头。”

## IX The Muramasa Sword / 第九章 村正刀

===Original===
As the door closed behind Michael Clifton, the Chief Constable turned to the Inspector.

“Now we can get to business, Inspector. Let’s have a look round the place at leisure, and perhaps the surgeon will turn up before we reach the body itself.”

Followed by Armadale, he stepped over to the bay containing the corpse of Foss and began methodically to inspect the surroundings.
===Chinese===
迈克尔·克利夫顿（Michael Clifton）出去，房门在他身后合上，郡警察局长（Chief Constable）便转向探长。

“这下咱们可以办正事了，探长。先从容地把这地方四下看一看，等咱们走到尸体跟前，法医说不定也就到了。”

他身后跟着阿玛代尔（Armadale），举步走向停放福斯（Foss）尸身的那处凹室，开始有条不紊地察看四周。

===Original===
“This must have been the case that Marden slipped against when he cut his hand,” the Inspector pointed out. “There’s a big hole in the glass and some blood on the broken edges of the gap.”

“Oh, yes, there’s blood enough to suit most people,” Sir Clinton admitted, with a glance towards the shattered case. But he seemed less interested in the glass than in the floor surface; for he moved slowly to and fro, evidently trying to place himself so that the sunlight from the window was reflected up to him from the parquet. After a moment or two, he seemed satisfied.

“That part of Marden’s story seems true enough. He did slip here. If you come across, you’ll see a line where the polish of the parquet has been taken off by some hard part of his shoe. You won’t be able to spot it unless you make a mirror of the floor.”
===Chinese===
“马登（Marden）割破手时滑倒撞上的，必定就是这口柜子，”探长指出。“玻璃上有个大窟窿，破口的边缘上还沾着血。”

“哦，是啊，这儿的血够大多数人受用的，”克林顿爵士（Sir Clinton）应道，朝那口砸破的柜子瞥了一眼。可他对玻璃的兴趣似乎不及对地面；只见他慢慢来回踱着，显然是在挪动身子，好让窗口的日光从镶木地板上反照到自己眼里。过了一两分钟，他像是满意了。

“马登的话里，这一节看来不假。他确实是在这儿滑倒的。你过来瞧，可以看见一道印子，是鞋上什么硬物把镶木地板的抛光蹭掉了。不把地板当镜子使，你是瞧不出来的。”

===Original===
The Inspector in his turn moved over and satisfied himself of the existence of the faint mark.

“That confirms part of his story,” he admitted, grudgingly. “There’s a lot of blood about, quite apart from the stuff from the body. One might make something out of that.”

“Suppose we try,” Sir Clinton suggested. “Assume that he cut his hand here on the glass. He’d be all asprawl on the floor; and the first thing he’d do would be to put his hands down to help himself up. That would account for these biggish patches here, under the case. Then a foot or so away you see those round marks of droplets with tiny splashes radiating from them with a fair regularity all round. These must have been made by drops falling from his hand while he stood still﻿—no doubt while he was feeling with the other hand for his handkerchief to stanch the bleeding.”
===Chinese===
探长也走过去，亲眼认明了那道淡淡的痕迹。

“这证实了他话里的一部分，”他不情愿地承认。“且不说尸体里流出的血，这四下里血迹也不少。说不定能从这上头做点文章。”

“那咱们就试试，”克林顿爵士提议。“姑且假定他是在这儿被玻璃割破了手。他当时准是手脚摊开摔在地板上；头一件事必定是双手撑地爬起来。柜子底下这几块稍大的血印，正可由此解释。再往外一尺上下，你瞧那些圆圆的滴痕，四周匀匀称称地溅着细小的血星。那必是他站定不动时从手上滴落的——不用说，正是他腾出另一只手摸手帕、要止住血的时候。”

===Original===
The Inspector indicated his agreement.

“After he’d got it fixed up, one might expect him to go over and look at Foss. He’d gone down on the floor, you remember, while he was hurrying to Foss’s assistance.”

“There’s no sign of that,” Armadale hastened to point out. “I can’t see any blood-drops round about the body.”

“Oh, don’t be in too much of a hurry, Inspector. Perhaps they fell in the pool of Foss’s own blood or, more probably, his handkerchief soaked up any blood that flowed just then.”
===Chinese===
探长表示同意。

“包扎停当之后，按理会指望他走过去瞧瞧福斯。您记得吧，他急着赶去救福斯那会儿，人是跪伏在地板上的。”

“那可没有迹象，”阿玛代尔连忙指出。“尸体四周我看不到一点血滴。”

“哦，探长，别太性急。兴许血滴落进了福斯本人那摊血泊里；再不然，多半是他那块手帕把当时流出的血都吸掉了。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton, still with his eyes on the ground, began to cast about in search of further traces.

“Ah, here are a couple of drops at the end of the bay. Have a look at them, Inspector.”

Armadale knelt down and examined the clots.

“Made on his way to the door, probably,” he suggested.

“They might have been, if he was swinging his arms as one does when one walks freely; but one doesn’t usually swing the arm when there’s a fresh wound in the hand, I think. These aren’t round blobs like the others; they’re elongated, and all the splashing from them is at one end﻿—the end towards the safe. His hand, when they were made, was moving towards the safe’s bay, whatever his body was doing.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士仍旧盯着地面，开始四处搜寻别的痕迹。

“啊，凹室这头有两滴血。来看看，探长。”

阿玛代尔跪下身，细察那两粒血块。

“大概是他往门口走的时候滴下的，”他揣测道。

“若是像常人自在行走时那样甩着胳膊，倒也可能；可依我看，手里带着新伤口，人通常是不会甩那胳膊的。这两滴不像方才那些滚圆的血珠，而是拉长了的，溅出的血星全集中在一头——朝着保险柜的那一头。滴下这两滴血时，他的手正向保险柜那处凹室移动，身子在做什么且不管它。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton made a rough measurement of the distance between the two drops.

“If they’d been nearer together or further apart, then each of them might have been made while his arm was going backwards in its natural swing while he was walking towards the door. But the distance between them won’t fit that. You’ll see at once if you try walking over the ground yourself, Inspector; for you’re just about Marden’s height and your stride must be nearly the same as his.”

“He said something about going to the safe and trying the handle,” the Inspector admitted, grudgingly. “So far, his tale’s got some support.”

Sir Clinton smiled covertly at Armadale’s obvious desire to pick holes in the valet’s narrative.

“Well, let’s find out how it happened,” Sir Clinton suggested. “He evidently passed this bay and went on towards the next one, where the safe is. We’ll follow his example.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士约略量了量两滴血之间的距离。

“这两滴若是再近些或再远些，那倒可能是他朝门口走时、胳膊顺势向后一甩各滴下一滴。可这个间距对不上。探长，你自己走一遍这块地面就立时明白了；你的个头跟马登差不多，步幅也必定和他相仿。”

“他倒是提到过去保险柜那儿试了试把手，”探长又不情愿地承认。“到眼下为止，他的话总算有了几分佐证。”

阿玛代尔分明一心想在这男仆的叙述里挑刺，克林顿爵士暗自莞尔。

“好，咱们来看看究竟是怎么个经过，”克林顿爵士提议。“他分明是走过这处凹室，继续朝隔壁那一处——保险柜所在的去处——去了。咱们也照他的路线走一遭。”

===Original===
They turned the corner of the showcase and stepped over to the safe door.

“There’s a trace of blood on the handle, true enough,” the Inspector admitted. “But I’m not sure he told the truth about why he came to the safe.”

Sir Clinton inspected the smear of blood on the handle, but he seemed to attach very little importance to it.

“I suppose one mustn’t jump to conclusions and assume that everything’s all aboveboard,” he conceded. “But even if we keep open minds, wouldn’t it be the most natural thing in the world for Marden to try the safe door? Remember what had happened according to his story. Mr. Chacewater was in the room, for Marden saw him with his own eyes. Mr. Chacewater turned the corner of a bay﻿—the one next this; and then Marden lost him for good. If you’d been in Marden’s place, wouldn’t you have searched about, and then, finding no trace of the missing man, wouldn’t you have jumped to the conclusion that he might be hidden in the safe? And wouldn’t you have given the handle a pull, just to make sure the safe was really locked and that Mr. Chacewater wasn’t hiding inside it?”
===Chinese===
两人绕过展柜的拐角，走到保险柜门前。

“把手上确有一道血痕，”探长承认。“可他去保险柜的缘由，我不敢保他说的是实话。”

克林顿爵士察看了把手上那抹血，却似乎不甚看重。

“我想，咱们自然不可贸然断定一切都光明磊落，”他让步道。“可就算不抱成见地想，马登去拉一拉柜门，岂不是天下最顺理成章的事？照他的说法，想想当时出了什么状况。蔡斯沃特（Chacewater）先生就在屋里，因为马登亲眼看见了他。蔡斯沃特先生拐过一处凹室的角——就是隔壁那一处；随后马登就再也没了他的踪影。你若处在马登的地位，能不四下搜寻？搜不到失踪人的半点痕迹，能不想到他兴许藏进了保险柜？能不拉上一把把手，好确证柜子当真锁着、蔡斯沃特先生没藏在里头？”

===Original===
“I suppose so,” conceded the Inspector, evidently dissatisfied.

“I expect his tale isn’t complete, of course. He could hardly give every detail. It would be a bit suspicious if he had, I think. If his tale had been absolutely complete in every detail, I’d be inclined to suspect a previously prepared recitation rather than an account of the facts. In a case of this sort, one could hardly expect a watertight narrative, could one?”

He continued his examination of the floor; but there seemed to be no other bloodstains of any importance.

“Now let’s have a glance at the body,” he suggested. “We needn’t shift it till the surgeon comes; but we can see what’s to be seen without altering its position in the meanwhile.”
===Chinese===
“我想是这么回事，”探长让了步，神色分明不服。

“他的叙述当然未必周全。他总难把每个细节都说到。要真说全了，我看反倒有点可疑。他的话若是每个细节都滴水不漏，我就要疑心这是一篇预先备好的背书，而不是对事实的陈述了。这类案子，谁还能指望一份天衣无缝的叙述呢？”

他继续察看地板；此外似乎再无要紧的血迹。

“现在咱们来瞧瞧尸体吧，”他提议。“法医来之前不必挪动它；不过在这当口，不动它的位置，能看的咱们也尽可以看。”

===Original===
The Inspector was the first to reach the spot, and as he knelt down beside the corpse he gave an exclamation of surprise.

“Here’s an automatic pistol, sir. It’s lying almost under the body, but I can see the muzzle. It looks like a .38 calibre.”

“Leave it there. We’ll get at it later.”

Sir Clinton examined the body itself. The cause of death seemed obvious enough, for the weapon still remained in the wound. A glance at it set the Chief Constable’s eye ranging over the museum cases. He retreated from the bay and searched for a time until he found what he was looking for: an empty sheath in an unlocked case. Without touching the sheath, he scanned the Japanese inscription on its surface.
===Chinese===
探长头一个走到尸身旁，跪下一看，不由得惊呼出声。

“这儿有一支自动手枪，先生。几乎压在身子底下，可我瞧得见枪口。瞧着像是 .38 口径的。”

“搁着别动。回头再取。”

克林顿爵士验看尸身本身。死因似乎够明显了，凶器还留在创口里。对那凶器一瞥之下，郡警察局长的目光便开始在博物馆的展柜间逡巡。他从凹室里退出来，找了一阵，终于找到了要找的东西：一口没上锁的柜子里，搁着一具空刀鞘。他不碰刀鞘，只细读鞘面上的日文铭文。

===Original===
“So that’s the thing?”

The Inspector had come across to his side and stood looking at the sheath.

“So the thing’s one of the specimens?” he asked.

“Yes. Don’t touch it, Inspector. We may as well see whose fingerprints are on it, though it’s quite on the cards that it’s been handled by other people lately as well as the murderer. It’s rather a show specimen, you see﻿—one of Muramasa’s making. This was the sword they were discussing when they were out on the terrace. Muramasa’s weapons have the name of being unlucky; and this one seems to bear out the legend.”
===Chinese===
“原来凶器是这个？”

探长已走到他身边，站着端详那具刀鞘。

“这么说，那玩意儿是展品之一？”他问。

“正是。别碰它，探长。咱们不妨看看上头有谁的指纹，尽管十有八九，近来除了凶手，旁人也摆弄过它。你瞧，这是件挺惹眼的展品——出自村正（Muramasa）之手。他们在露台上议论的，就是这把刀。村正的刀素有不祥之名；这一把，看来是应了这传说。”

===Original===
The Inspector looked at the sheath with apparent care, but his thoughts seemed to be elsewhere.

“Nobody could have got away from here through the windows,” he observed, rather irrelevantly. “They’re all barred outside, and the catches are fast on the sashes.”

Evidently Sir Clinton had noticed this in the course of his previous search, for he gave a tacit assent to the Inspector’s statement without even glancing up at the windows.

“Here are the sheets of rubbing-paper that Foss was using,” the Inspector went on, picking them up as he spoke. “They’ll have his fingerprints on them, so I’ll stow them away. We might need them. One never knows.”
===Chinese===
探长煞有介事地端详着刀鞘，心思却似乎在别处。

“谁也没法从窗户脱身，”他冒出一句，文不对题。“窗户外面全装着铁条，窗闩也牢牢扣死在窗扇上。”

克林顿爵士先前搜查时显然早已注意到了这一点，对探长这话只无声地默认，连眼皮都没朝窗户抬一抬。

“这几张是福斯用的拓印纸，”探长接着说，一面顺手拾起。“上头必有他的指纹，我收起来。兴许用得着。这可说不准。”

===Original===
“We can get actual prints from the body if we need them,” Sir Clinton pointed out. “You don’t suppose it’s a suicide case, do you?”

The Inspector was too wary to throw himself open to attack. He contented himself with putting the papers away carefully in his pocketbook.

“Fingerprints will be useful, though,” Sir Clinton went on. “At the earliest possible moment, Inspector, I want you to get prints from the fingers of everyone in the house. Start with Miss Chacewater. She’ll agree to let you take hers without any trouble; and after that you can go on to Mr. Clifton and so down the scale. We’ve no authority for insisting, of course; but you can make a note if anyone objects. I expect you’ll get the lot without difficulty.”
===Chinese===
“真需要，从尸体上取真指纹就是了，”克林顿爵士指出。“你总不至于以为这是桩自杀案吧？”

探长乖觉，不肯授人以柄，只管把那几张纸仔细收进记事本了事。

“不过指纹自有用处，”克林顿爵士接着说。“探长，我要你尽早把这宅子里每个人的指纹都取到手。先从蔡斯沃特小姐开始。她会痛痛快快让你取的；随后你就可以找克利夫顿先生，依次往下。当然，咱们无权强人所难；可谁要是不肯，你就记上一笔。我料你把他们全取到手，不会费事。”

===Original===
At this moment Mold opened the door to admit the police surgeon; and Sir Clinton broke off in order to explain the state of affairs to him. Dr. Greenlaw was a businesslike person who wasted no time. While Sir Clinton was speaking, he knelt down beside the corpse and made a cursory examination of it. When he rose to his feet again, he seemed satisfied.

“That sword appears to have entered the thorax between the fifth and sixth ribs,” he pointed out. “It’s pierced the left lung, evidently; you notice the blood-foam on his lips? And most probably it’s penetrated right into the heart as well. It looks as if it had; but of course I’ll need to carry out a P.M. before I can give you exact details.”

“I suppose we can take out the sword before we shift the body?” asked the Inspector. “We want to examine it before anyone else touches it.”

“Certainly,” Greenlaw replied. “You can see for yourselves what happened. He was struck from the front by a right-handed man﻿—a fairly heavy blow, I should judge from the depth to which that sword has buried itself. There’s no sign of a twist in the wound, which looks as though he went down under it at once. Quite possibly the base of the skull may have been fractured on the floor by the force of his fall. We’ll see when we come to the P.M. But in any case that wound alone would be quite sufficient to cause almost immediate death. It’s a blade almost as broad as a bayonet, as you can see. I’ll go into the whole thing carefully when I can make a thorough examination. You’ll have him sent down to the mortuary, of course?”
===Chinese===
正这时，莫尔德（Mold）开门引进警局法医；克林顿爵士顿住话头，向他说明情形。格林洛医生（Dr. Greenlaw）是个干脆利落的人，半点工夫也不虚掷。克林顿爵士说话的当儿，他已跪在尸旁粗粗查验。再站起身时，他像是成竹在胸了。

“那把刀看样子是从第五、第六两根肋骨之间刺进胸腔的，”他指出。“显然穿透了左肺——你们留意到他唇上的血沫了吧？而且十之八九也一直捅进了心脏。瞧着像是这样；不过确切细节，自然得等我做完验尸才能奉告。”

“搬动尸体之前，我们可以把刀取出来吧？”探长问。“我们要赶在旁人碰它之前查验一番。”

“当然可以，”格林洛答道。“事情经过你们自己看得明白。他是被人从正面、由一个惯用右手的人刺中的——看那刀没入的深度，我判断这一击分量不轻。创口没有拧转的痕迹，可见他当场就栽倒了。很可能他倒地的力道，还在地板上撞裂了颅底。到验尸时自见分晓。但无论如何，单是这一处创伤，就足以令人几乎当即毙命。你们看，这刀身几乎有刺刀那么宽。等我能做周密检查时，再把始末仔细查个明白。你们自然会把尸体送去太平间吧？”

===Original===
“As soon as we’ve finished our work here.”

“Good. I’ll make a note or two now, if you don’t mind. Then I’ll leave you to get on. As things are, there’s nothing there which you couldn’t see for yourselves.”

He took out a pocketbook and began to jot down his notes.

“Just a moment, doctor,” Sir Clinton interposed. “I’ve got a patient for you here. I’d like you to have a look at his hand and bandage up some cuts before you go.”

Greenlaw nodded in agreement and went on with his note-taking.
===Chinese===
“我们这边一完事就送。”

“好。不介意的话，我这就记一两笔。然后我就不打搅你们办事了。照眼下情形，那里的东西没有一样是你们自己看不到的。”

他掏出记事本，开始草草记录。

“稍等片刻，医生，”克林顿爵士插话。“我这儿有个病人交给你。想请你在走之前看看他那只手，把几处伤口包扎一下。”

格林洛点头应承，继续做他的记录。

===Original===
“Now, Inspector,” Sir Clinton continued, “we’d better get this sword out. Be sure to take all the care you can not to rub out any fingerprints.”

Armadale obeyed, and after some cautious manoeuvres he succeeded in withdrawing the weapon, which he laid carefully on the top of the central showcase.

“Now we can have a look at him,” Sir Clinton said. “You don’t mind our shifting the position of the body, doctor?”
===Chinese===
“好了，探长，”克林顿爵士接着说，“咱们最好把这刀取出来。千万仔细着，别抹掉任何指纹。”

阿玛代尔领命，小心翼翼地摆弄了一阵，终于把凶器抽了出来，轻轻搁在中央展柜的柜顶上。

“这下可以瞧瞧他了，”克林顿爵士说。“我们挪一挪尸体的位置，医生你不介意吧？”

===Original===
Greenlaw closed his notebook and prepared to assist them if necessary.

“Begin with the contents of his pockets, Inspector,” Sir Clinton suggested.

“The blade’s gone clean through his left breast pocket,” the Inspector pointed out. He felt the outside of the pocket gingerly with his fingers.

“Nothing there except his handkerchief, so far as I can feel. It’s all soaked with his blood. I’ll leave that to the last. I want to keep my hands clean while I go over the rest.”

He wiped his fingertips carefully on his own handkerchief and continued his search.

“Right-hand breast pocket: a notecase.”
===Chinese===
格林洛合上记事本，必要时准备搭把手。

“先从他口袋里的东西查起，探长，”克林顿爵士提议。

“刀身把他上衣左胸袋整个穿透了，”探长指出。他用手指隔着一层，小心翼翼地摸了摸袋子外面。

“摸着里头除了手帕什么都没有。全叫他自己的血浸透了。那个留到最后再说。查其余各处时，我想保持手干净。”

他仔细用自己的手帕擦净指尖，继续搜检。

“上衣右胸袋：一只钞票夹。”

===Original===
He drew it out and handed it to Sir Clinton, who opened it and counted the contents.

“Three hundred and fifty-seven pounds in notes,” he announced at length. “That’s a fair sum to be carrying about with one. Ten visiting cards: ‘J. B. Foss,’ with no address.”

He crossed over to the central case and put down the notecase thoughtfully.
===Chinese===
他掏出来递给克林顿爵士；爵士打开，数了数里面的东西。

“三百五十七镑钞票，”他半晌宣布。“随身带这么一笔，数目不小。名片十张：‘J. B. 福斯’，并无地址。”

他踱到中央展柜前，若有所思地把钞票夹放下。

===Original===
“The left-hand waistcoat pockets are saturated with blood,” Armadale continued. “I’ll leave them over for the present. Top right-hand waistcoat pocket, empty. Lower right-hand waistcoat pocket: a small penknife and a toothpick. Not much blood here; he was lying slightly on his left side and it must have flowed in that direction, I suppose. Right-hand jacket pocket, outside: nothing. I’ll take the trousers now. Right-hand pocket: key-ring and a purse.”

He handed them to Sir Clinton, who examined them in turn before putting them on the central case.

“Only keys of suitcases here,” the Chief Constable reported. “We haven’t come across the latchkey of his flat, if you notice.”

He counted the contents of the purse.
===Chinese===
“马甲左边的口袋全叫血浸透了，”阿玛代尔继续道。“这两个暂且搁下。马甲右上袋，空。右下袋：一把小折刀、一支牙签。这儿血不多；他是稍向左侧躺着的，血想必都朝那边流了。上衣右外袋：没有东西。现在查裤子。右袋：一只钥匙圈、一个零钱包。”

他递给克林顿爵士；爵士逐一验看，再搁到中央展柜上。

“这上头只有几把衣箱钥匙，”郡警察局长报告。“你们留意没有，他公寓的弹簧锁钥匙，咱们到现在还没见着。”

他数了数零钱包里的东西。

===Original===
“Eight and sixpence and one ten-shilling note.”

The Inspector proceeded with his examination.

“Here’s something funny! He’s got a smallish pocket over his hip, just below the trouser button. That’s unusual. But it’s empty,” he added, after an eager search.

“Let me look at that,” Sir Clinton demanded.

He stooped down and inspected the pocket closely, then stood up and passed his hand across the corresponding spot on his own clothes. As he did so, Armadale noticed a peculiar expression pass across the Chief Constable’s face, as though some new idea had dawned upon him and had cleared up a difficulty. But Sir Clinton divulged nothing of what was passing in his mind.
===Chinese===
“八先令六便士，另有一张十先令的纸币。”

探长继续搜检。

“这儿有个蹊跷！他胯骨上方、裤扣正下方，缝了个小口袋。这可少见。不过里头是空的，”他急急翻寻了一阵之后，又补上一句。

“这个我瞧瞧，”克林顿爵士说道。

他俯身细察那个口袋，随后站直，在自己身上相应的部位隔衣抚了一下。就在这时，阿玛代尔注意到郡警察局长脸上一闪而过的异样神情，仿佛一个新念头豁然降临，解开了一处疑窦。可克林顿爵士对心中所想只字不露。

===Original===
“Make quite sure it’s empty,” he said.

Armadale turned the little pocket inside out.

“There’s nothing there,” he pointed out. “It wouldn’t hold much﻿—it’s hardly bigger than a ticket pocket.”

He looked at the pocket again, evidently puzzled by the importance which the Chief Constable attached to it.

“It’s a silly place to have a pocket,” he said at last. “It’s not like the old-fashioned fob. That was kept tight shut by the pressure of your body. This thing’s mouth is loose and it’s simply a gift to a pickpocket.”
===Chinese===
“千万确认它是空的，”他说。

阿玛代尔把那小口袋翻了个里朝外。

“什么也没有，”他指出。“本来也装不下多少——比票袋大不了多少。”

他又看了看那口袋，对郡警察局长如此看重它，显然大惑不解。

“把口袋开在这儿，可真是蠢主意，”他终于开口。“这比不得老式的怀表袋。那表袋贴着身子，叫身子一压，关得严严实实。这玩意儿袋口松垮垮的，简直是白送给扒手的礼物。”

===Original===
“I think we’ll probably find another of the same kind on the other side,” Sir Clinton contented himself with saying. “Let’s get on with the rest of them.”

Armadale turned the body slightly and put his hand into the hip pocket.

“It’s empty, too,” he announced. “It’s a very loose pocket with no flap on it. I expect he carried his pistol there and he had the pocket built for easy handling of his gun.”

He looked at the .38 automatic which had been disclosed as he turned the body.

“That wouldn’t have fitted into the little pocket,” he pointed out. “The pistol’s far too big for the opening.”
===Chinese===
“另一边多半还有个一模一样的，”克林顿爵士只说了这么一句。“接着查其余的吧。”

阿玛代尔把尸体稍稍侧转，手伸进后袋。

“也是空的，”他宣布。“这口袋松垮垮的，又没有袋盖。我看他的手枪平日就插在那儿，这口袋是特为出枪利索才让裁缝做上的。”

他望着自己转动尸体时露出的那支 .38 自动手枪。

“那枪可塞不进那只小口袋，”他指出。“枪身比袋口大得太多。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton nodded his agreement with this view.

“He didn’t use it for his pistol. Now, the left-hand pockets, please. You can wash your hands as soon as you’ve gone through them.”

Inspector Armadale stolidly continued his investigation.

“Left-hand breast pocket in jacket,” he announced. “Nothing but his handkerchief, saturated with blood.”

He handed it to Sir Clinton, who inspected it carefully before putting it with the rest of the collection.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士点头，认同此说。

“那口袋不是给他放手枪用的。现在请查左边的口袋。查完这些，你就可以去洗手了。”

阿玛代尔探长不动声色，继续他的搜检。

“上衣左胸袋，”他报告。“只有一块手帕，浸满了血。”

他递给克林顿爵士；爵士仔细验看，才把它归入其余那堆东西。

===Original===
“No marks on it, either initials or laundry-mark,” he said. “Evidently been bought and used without marking.”

“Ticket pocket, empty,” the Inspector went on, withdrawing his fingers from it. “Top left waistcoat pocket: a self-filling Swan pen and a metal holder for same. Lower left waistcoat pocket: an amber cigarette-holder. Not much to go on there.”

He turned to the trousers.

“Left-hand trouser pocket: five coppers.”
===Chinese===
“上头没有任何标记，姓名缩写没有，洗衣房的记号也没有，”他说。“显然是买来就使，从没做记号。”

“票袋，空的，”探长接着说，把手指从里头抽出来。“马甲左上袋：一支自灌墨水的天鹅牌（Swan）钢笔，连同一只配它的金属笔套。左下袋：一支琥珀烟嘴。靠这些都摸不出什么门道。”

他转向裤子。

“裤左袋：五枚铜板。”

===Original===
Handing them over, he proceeded.

“Your notion’s quite right, sir. There’s another of these side pockets here. But it’s empty like the other one.”

Instead of replying, Sir Clinton gingerly picked up the automatic pistol from the floor and placed it along with the other objects on the central case.

“You’d better examine that for fingerprints, Inspector,” he suggested. “I leave you to make the arrangements about taking the body down to the mortuary. The sooner the better. Now, doctor, we’ll get your patient for you, if the Inspector will be good enough to bring him to the lavatory near by, where you can get his wounds patched up.”
===Chinese===
他递过铜板，又往下进行。

“先生，您料的一点不错。这边果然又有一只这样的旁袋。不过跟那只一样，也是空的。”

克林顿爵士并不答话，只小心翼翼地从地板上拾起那支自动手枪，同其余物件一并搁上中央展柜。

“你最好验一验它上头的指纹，探长，”他提议。“送尸体去太平间的事，也交给你安排。越快越好。好了，医生，我们这就把你的病人给你请来——有劳探长把他带到近旁的盥洗室去，你就可以在那儿给他包扎伤口了。”

===Original===
Inspector Armadale soon produced Marden, who seemed rather surprised at being summoned again.

“It’s all right, Marden,” Sir Clinton assured him. “It merely struck me that when there was a doctor on the premises you ought to have these cuts of yours properly fixed up.”

Dr. Greenlaw speedily removed the temporary bandage which the valet had improvised.

“I’ll need to put some stitches into this,” he said, as the extent of the injury became evident. “Luckily these glass cuts are clean-edged. You’ll hardly see the scar after a time.”

Sir Clinton inspected the wounds sympathetically.

“You’ve made a bit of a mess of your hand, Marden,” he commented. “It’s just as well I thought of getting Dr. Greenlaw to look after you.”
===Chinese===
阿玛代尔探长很快把马登带了来，马登再度被传唤，似乎颇感意外。

“没事，马登，”克林顿爵士宽慰他。“我只是想到，宅里既然来了位医生，你手上这几处伤总该正经处置一下。”

格林洛医生迅速解下这男仆临时凑合裹上的绷带。

“这个得缝几针，”伤口的全貌一露出来，他便说。“所幸玻璃割的伤口切缘齐整。过些日子，疤痕几乎看不出来。”

克林顿爵士同情地察看伤口。

“你这只手可弄得够呛，马登，”他说。“幸亏我想到请格林洛医生照看你。”

===Original===
Marden seemed to have been looking for an opening.

“I’m glad you called me up again, sir,” he explained. “I’ve just thought of two other points about this affair.”

“Yes?”

While the doctor was cleaning and disinfecting the wounds, Marden addressed himself to the Chief Constable.
===Chinese===
马登似乎一直在找机会开口。

“您又把我叫回来，我正高兴呢，先生，”他解释道。“我刚想起这桩事的另外两个情况。”

“哦？”

医生清洗、消毒伤口的当儿，马登向郡警察局长说了下去。

===Original===
“I forgot to say, sir, that when I got back to the house I found Mr. Foss’s car waiting for him. I said a word or two to the chauffeur as I passed. It only struck me afterwards that this might be important. I forgot about it at the time.”

“Quite right to tell us,” Sir Clinton confirmed.

“The second thing was what the chauffeur told me. He’d been ordered to wait for Mr. Foss, it seems; and he got the idea that Mr. Foss was leaving Ravensthorpe this afternoon for good. I was surprised by that; for I’d heard nothing about it from Mr. Foss.”

He flinched slightly with the smart of his wounds, as Greenlaw washed them carefully.
===Chinese===
“我忘了说，先生，我回宅子的时候，看见福斯先生的汽车在等他。我路过时跟司机搭了一两句话。过后才想起，这事兴许要紧。当时就忘了。”

“告诉我们，做得对，”克林顿爵士肯定道。

“第二件事是那位司机对我说的。看样子，他是奉命在那儿等候福斯先生；而且他落了个印象，福斯先生今天下午就要离开雷文索普（Ravensthorpe），一去不回了。我听了诧异；因为福斯先生对我只字未提。”

格林洛仔细清洗伤口时，他疼得微微一缩。

===Original===
Sir Clinton seemed to be struck by a fresh idea.

“Before the doctor bandages you up, would you mind if we took your fingerprints, Marden? I’m asking everyone to let us take theirs, and this seems to be the best chance we shall have of getting yours, you see? Of course, if you object, I’ve no power to insist on it.”

“I’ve no objections, sir. Why should I have?”

“Then you might take impressions of the lot, Inspector,” Sir Clinton suggested. “Don’t spend too much time over it. We must get the bandages on this hand as quick as possible.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士似乎又想起一个新主意。

“趁医生还没给你包扎，马登，我们取一下你的指纹，你可介意？我正请宅里每个人都让我们取指纹呢，看来这是取你指纹的最好时机，你明白吧？当然，你若不愿意，我也无权勉强。”

“我不介意，先生。有什么可介意的？”

“那你就把全套指纹都取下来吧，探长，”克林顿爵士提议。“别在这上头多费工夫。这只手得尽快缠上绷带。”

===Original===
Inspector Armadale hurried away for his outfit and soon set to work to take the valet’s fingerprints. While he was thus engaged a fresh suggestion seemed to occur to Sir Clinton.

“By the way, Marden, you have that parcel which Mr. Foss sent to the post?”

“I can give you it in a moment, sir, once the doctor has finished with my hand.”

“Very good. I’d like to see it.”
===Chinese===
阿玛代尔探长急忙去取他那套家什，很快动手取这男仆的指纹。他正忙着，克林顿爵士似乎又冒出一个新的主意。

“对了，马登，福斯先生送去付邮的那个包裹，还在你手里吗？”

“医生一弄完我的手，我立时就能取来，先生。”

“很好。我想看一看。”

===Original===
The Chief Constable waited patiently until Marden’s hand was completely bandaged; then he dispatched the valet for the parcel. When it was forthcoming, he dismissed Marden again. The doctor took his leave, and Armadale was left alone with Sir Clinton.

“Now let’s see what Foss was sending off, Inspector.”

Cutting the string, Sir Clinton unwrapped the paper and disclosed a small cardboard box. Inside on a layer of cotton-wool, was a wristwatch. Further search failed to bring to light any enclosed note.

“I suppose he was sending it to be cleaned,” the Inspector hazarded. “Probably he wrote a letter by the same post.”
===Chinese===
郡警察局长耐心等到马登的手完全包扎好，才差这男仆去取包裹。包裹一到手，他又把马登打发走了。医生告辞而去，屋里只剩下阿玛代尔和克林顿爵士。

“现在瞧瞧福斯寄出去的是什么，探长。”

克林顿爵士割断捆绳，剥开包装纸，露出一只小纸盒。盒内垫着一层棉絮，棉絮上躺着一块腕表。再搜也没搜出附带的什么便条。

“我猜是送去清洗的吧，”探长猜测道。“多半他还随同一班邮发过一封信。”

===Original===
“Let’s have a look at it, Inspector. Be careful not to mark it with your fingers.”

Sir Clinton took the watch up and examined it closely.

“It looks fairly new to need repair.”

He held it to his ear.

“It’s going. Not much sign of damage there.”
===Chinese===
“拿来我看看，探长。小心别让你的手指在上面留印子。”

克林顿爵士拿起腕表，凑近细看。

“瞧着还相当新，不像要修理的样子。”

他把表贴到耳边。

“还在走。看不出什么损伤的迹象。”

===Original===
“Perhaps it needed regulating,” Armadale suggested.

“Perhaps,” Sir Clinton’s tone was noncommittal. “Take a note of the time as compared with your own watch, Inspector; and just check whether it’s going fast or slow in a few hours. Try it for fingerprints along with the rest of the stuff.”

He replaced it gently in its bed of cotton-wool and closed the box, taking care not to finger the cardboard.

“Now, if you’ll send for the chauffeur, we may get something from him.”
===Chinese===
“兴许是需要校准快慢，”阿玛代尔猜测。

“兴许是，”克林顿爵士的口吻不置可否。“把它指示的时刻跟你自己表上的对下来记好，探长；过几个钟头再查一查它是快了还是慢了。连同其余物件，也试试上头的指纹。”

他轻轻把表放回棉絮的凹窝里，合上盒盖，留神不去碰那纸板。

“现在，你去把司机叫来，说不定能从他嘴里问出点什么。”

===Original===
But the chauffeur proved a most unsatisfactory witness. He admitted that Foss had ordered him to bring round the car at 3:15 and wait for further orders; but he was unable to give any clear account of the talk he had with his employer when the order was given.

“I can’t remember what he said exactly; but I got the notion he was leaving here today. I’m dead sure of that; for I packed up my own stuff and had it ready to go off at a moment’s notice. It’s on the grid of the car now. I was so taken aback that I haven’t thought of unpacking it.”

Sir Clinton could get nothing further out of the man, and he was eventually dismissed.
===Chinese===
可这司机却是个极不让人满意的见证人。他承认，福斯吩咐他 3:15 把车开过来，听候进一步的吩咐；可下令时他同主人说了些什么，他已复述不出个明白。

“他原话怎么说的我记不清了；可我落了个印象，他是今天要走。这一点我笃定无疑；因为我把自己的行李都捆好了，说走抬腿就走。眼下还捆在车尾的行李格栅上呢。我懵了半天，一直没想起来解开。”

克林顿爵士再也从他嘴里掏不出什么，末了把他打发走了。

===Original===
“Now we’ll have a run over the late Mr. Foss’s goods,” the Chief Constable proposed, when they had dismissed the chauffeur.

But the search of Foss’s bedroom yielded at first nothing of much interest.
