“This doesn’t look as if that chauffeur had been telling the truth,” Armadale pointed out, when they found all Foss’s clothes arranged quite normally in wardrobe and drawers. “Foss himself had made no preparations for moving, that’s evident. I’ll see that chauffeur again and go into the matter more carefully.”

“You might as well,” Sir Clinton concurred. “But I doubt if you’ll get him to shift from his story. He seemed to be very clear about the main point, though he was weak in details.”
===Chinese===
“现在咱们把已故福斯先生的东西过一遍，”打发走司机后，郡警察局长提议。

可搜检福斯的卧室，起初没翻出什么要紧东西。

“这么看，那司机说的话不像实在的，”发现福斯的衣物在衣柜和抽屉里都摆放如常，阿玛代尔指出。“福斯本人没做半点动身的准备，这是明摆着的。回头我再见见那司机，仔细问一问。”

“你尽管去问，”克林顿爵士赞同。“不过我疑心你问不动他改口。大关节上他似乎一清二楚，细节上却含糊。”

===Original===
They subjected all Foss’s belongings to a careful scrutiny.

“No name marked on any of the linen; no tags on any of the suits; no labels inside the jacket pockets,” Inspector Armadale pointed out. “He seems to have been very anxious not to advertise his identity. And no papers of any sort. It looks a bit queer, doesn’t it?”

As he spoke, he noticed a small leather case standing in a corner.

“Hullo, here’s an attaché case. Perhaps his papers are in it.”

He crossed over and picked up the case, but as he did so an expression of surprise crossed his face.

“This thing’s as heavy as lead! It must weigh ten or twelve pounds at least!”
===Chinese===
他们把福斯的全部衣物细查了一遍。

“衬衣物上没有一个绣的名字；西装上一枚标签也没有；上衣口袋里也没有任何名号，”阿玛代尔探长指出。“他似乎极不愿张扬自己的身份。而且片纸只字都没有。瞧着有点古怪，是吧？”

说话间，他看见墙角立着一只小皮箱。

“咦，这儿有只公文包。他的文件说不定就在里头。”

他走过去提起箱子，可这一提，脸上立时掠过惊讶之色。

“这玩意儿沉得像铅！总有十磅十二磅的分量，只多不少！”

===Original===
“It’s not an attaché case,” Sir Clinton pointed out. “Look at the ends of it.”

Armadale turned the case round in his hand. At the upper part of one end the leather had been cut away, disclosing a small ebonite disc rather more than an inch in diameter and pierced with a pattern of tiny holes. At the opposite end of the case there were two small holes side by side and a larger one above; and examination showed brass sockets inside which seemed meant for the reception of plugs.

“You’d better get his keys, Inspector. Probably the key of this thing will be on the ring.”

With his curiosity raised to an acute pitch, Armadale went off in search of the key-ring; and was soon back again with it in his hand.
===Chinese===
“这不是公文包，”克林顿爵士指出。“瞧瞧它的两端。”

阿玛代尔把箱子在手里转过来。一端的上部皮面被裁去了一块，露出一枚直径一英寸出头的胶木小圆盘，盘面上穿有一层细孔拼成的花纹。箱子另一端，两个小孔并排着，上头还有一个大些的；细看之下，孔内嵌着黄铜插座，看来是供插销插入之用。

“你最好把他的钥匙取来，探长。这东西的钥匙，多半就串在那钥匙圈上。”

阿玛代尔的好奇心被吊到了极点，跑去取钥匙圈，不多时便攥着它回来了。

===Original===
“Now we’ll see what it is,” he said, as he turned the key in the case’s lock and pressed the opening spring.

The lifting of the lid disclosed a wooden casing fitted with a couple of hinged doors, an open recess in which were two levers, and a hinged metal plate, on which was an inscription. Armadale read it aloud uncomprehendingly:

“ ‘Marconi Otophone. Inst. No. S/O 1164.’ What the deuce is this?”
===Chinese===
“这下看看它是个什么家伙，”他说着，把钥匙拧进箱锁，按开了弹簧。

箱盖一掀开，露出一具木壳，壳上装着两扇小铰链门，一处敞开的凹槽——槽里是两根拨杆——还有一块铰链金属板，板上带着一行铭文。阿玛代尔全然不解其意，大声念了出来：

“『马可尼奥托风（Marconi Otophone），仪器编号：S/O 1164。』这到底是个什么玩意儿？”

===Original===
Sir Clinton put out his hand and lifted the hinged metal plate, disclosing below two wireless valves in their sockets.

“Some wireless gadget,” the Inspector ejaculated. “Now what could he possibly have wanted with a thing like that?”

Sir Clinton examined the instrument with interest, then he closed the case.

“We’ll take this along with us, Inspector.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士伸手掀起那块铰链金属板，板下露出插在管座上的两只无线电真空管。

“什么无线电小玩意儿，”探长脱口而出。“他要这么个东西，究竟派什么用场呢？”

克林顿爵士兴致盎然地察看这仪器，随后把箱盖合上了。

“这个咱们带走，探长。”

===Original===
Then, with a sudden change of mind, he contradicted himself.

“No, we’ll leave it here for the present. That will be much better.”

Somewhat mystified by this change of intention, the Inspector agreed. Sir Clinton’s manner did not invite questions.

“I think we had better see Miss Chacewater again. There are one or two questions I’d like to put to her, Inspector; and you had better be there.”
===Chinese===
随即他念头一转，又自己推翻了自己。

“不，眼下还是留在这儿。那样好得多。”

探长对这意图的突变有些纳闷，却也应承了。克林顿爵士那副神气，不容人发问。

“我想咱们最好再见一见蔡斯沃特小姐。有一两个问题我要问她，探长；你最好也在场。”

===Original===
In a minute or two, Joan was found, with Michael Clifton in attendance. Sir Clinton did not think it worth while to sit down.

“Just a couple of points I want to ask about. First of all, is there any record of the combination which opens the lock of the safe in the museum?”

Joan shook her head.

“Maurice was the only one of us who knew it. My father did leave a note of it; but I remember that Maurice destroyed that. He specially wished to keep it to himself.”
===Chinese===
一两分钟后，找到了琼（Joan），迈克尔·克利夫顿陪在一旁。克林顿爵士觉得犯不着坐下。

“只想问两点事。首先，博物馆里那口保险柜转字密码锁的密码，可有留过什么记录？”

琼摇了摇头。

“我们家只有莫里斯（Maurice）一个人知道。父亲原先留过一张字条，可我记得让莫里斯毁掉了。他特意要把这密码独揽在自己手里。”

===Original===
“Another point,” Sir Clinton went on. “Did Foss know, on the night of the burglary, which of the rows contained the real medallions and which row the replicas were in?”

Joan reflected for a moment or two before replying.

“He must have known. Maurice had shown him the things once at least, if not oftener; and I know there was no secret as to which were the real things and which were the counterfeits.”

Sir Clinton seemed satisfied with this information.
===Chinese===
“再有一点，”克林顿爵士接着说。“失窃那晚，福斯知不知道哪一排放的是真徽章、哪一排放的是复制品？”

琼思忖了片刻才作答。

“他必定知道。莫里斯给他看过那些东西，少说也有一次；而且据我所知，哪些是真品、哪些是赝品，从来不是什么秘密。”

克林顿爵士对这答话似乎感到满意。

===Original===
“One last thing,” he continued. “I suppose you could show me where your brother keeps his correspondence. We must get hold of Kessock’s address and notify him about Foss’s death; and there seems no way of doing it as quick as this one. If the papers aren’t locked up, perhaps I could see them now?”

It appeared that the letters were available and Sir Clinton turned them over rapidly.

“Fifth Avenue? That’s satisfactory.”

He put the papers back in their place.
===Chinese===
“最后一件事，”他继续道。“你哥哥存放来往信件的地方，你能领我去看看吗？我们必须弄到凯索克（Kessock）的地址，把福斯的死讯通知他；看来再没有比这更快的法子了。要是那些信没上锁，我现在就可以看看吗？”

信件倒是现成的，克林顿爵士飞快地翻了翻。

“第五大道（Fifth Avenue）？这就妥了。”

他把信件放回原处。

===Original===
“There’s just one thing more. I’m going to put a constable on guard at the door of the museum for a while﻿—day and night for a day or two, perhaps. You won’t mind?”

“Certainly not. Do as you wish.”

Sir Clinton acknowledged the permission. Then, as though struck by an afterthought, he inquired:

“Have you Cecil’s address?”
===Chinese===
“还有一件小事。我要派一名警士在博物馆门口守上一阵子——兴许一两天，昼夜不离人。你们不会介意吧？”

“当然不会。随你的便。”

克林顿爵士谢过了这层应允。随后，像是临时又想起什么，他问道：

“塞西尔（Cecil）的地址，你有吗？”

===Original===
Joan shook her head.

“He said he’d let me know where he was staying, but he hasn’t written. Perhaps he hasn’t settled down yet. He may be staying at an hotel for a day or two.”

“Please ring me up as soon as he sends word.”

Joan promised to do this, and Sir Clinton continued:

“By the way, Inspector Armadale wishes to take the fingerprints of everyone in the house. Would you mind setting an example and having yours taken along with the rest? If you do it, then it will be easier for us to get the others. They won’t be suspicious when they hear that it’s a general inquisition.”
===Chinese===
琼摇了摇头。

“他说过会把落脚的地方告诉我，可至今没来信。也许他还没安顿下来。兴许还要在旅馆里住上一两天。”

“他一有音信，请立刻给我来电话。”

琼应承下来，克林顿爵士又接着说：

“对了，阿玛代尔探长要取宅里每个人的指纹。你肯不肯带个头，同大家一起也让取一份？你若带了头，我们取旁人的就容易了。他们一听说是人人有份的例行查问，就不会起疑。”

===Original===
Both Joan and Michael consented without ado.

“The Inspector will be with you in a moment or two,” Sir Clinton said, as he took his leave. “Just a word with you, Inspector.”

Armadale followed him from the room.

“Now, Inspector, there’s a lot for you to do yet. First of all, get these fingerprints. Then telephone to London and get Kessock’s business address. As soon as you get it, let me know.”
===Chinese===
琼和迈克尔二话不说都应允了。

“探长过一两分钟就到你们这儿来，”克林顿爵士说着告辞。“跟你说句话，探长。”

阿玛代尔随他出了房门。

“好了，探长，你还有一大堆事要办。首先，取齐这些指纹。然后打电话到伦敦，查凯索克的办公地址。一查到，立刻告诉我。”

===Original===
“But you got his address from the correspondence, sir, surely. It’s in Fifth Avenue.”

“I want his other address﻿—his office in New York, you understand?”

“His office will be shut by now, if you’re going to cable,” the Inspector pointed out, thoughtlessly.

“No, it won’t. You forget that their time is some hours behind ours. We’ll catch him in office hours if you hurry. Then when you’ve done that, get Foss’s face photographed; and arrange for a constable and reliefs to be posted at the museum door till further orders. The museum door is to be left open and the light is to be left burning at night, so that he can keep his eye on things.”
===Chinese===
“可是先生，他的地址您不是从信件里得到了吗。就在第五大道。”

“我要的是他另一个地址——他在纽约（New York）的办公处，你明白吗？”

“要是打算发电报，这会儿他办公室早关门了，”探长冒失地指出。

“不，关不了。你忘了他们那边的时间比我们晚好几个钟头。动作快些，正好赶在他的办公时间逮着他。办完这桩，再拍下福斯的面容照片；再安排警士带着轮班的人守在博物馆门口，直到另行通知。博物馆的门要敞着，夜里灯也要亮着，好让他盯得住里头的东西。”

===Original===
Inspector Armadale jotted some notes in his pocketbook. As he closed this, he seemed to think of something.

“There’s just one thing, sir. You want to get into the safe? Couldn’t we get the number of the lock combination from the makers? They must know it.”

Sir Clinton shook his head.

“Unfortunately the safe has no maker’s nameplate on it, Inspector. I looked at the time we examined it. It’s a fairly old pattern, though, I noticed; and if it hasn’t got a balanced fence arbour, I think I can guarantee to find the combination of it with a little assistance.”
===Chinese===
阿玛代尔探长在记事本上草草记下几条。合上本子时，他像是又想起了什么。

“还有一件事，先生。您想打开那保险柜？咱们不能向制造厂家要密码吗？他们必然知道。”

克林顿爵士摇了摇头。

“可惜那保险柜上没有厂家的铭牌，探长。我们查验时我看过了。不过我留意到，它是相当老式的货色；只要它没装平衡制栓轴（balanced fence arbour），稍有人搭把手，我敢担保能把密码找出来。”

===Original===
Armadale looked rather blank.

“I thought these things were too stiff to tackle,” he said.

Sir Clinton suppressed a smile.

“You ought to read Edgar Allan Poe, Inspector. ‘Human ingenuity cannot concoct a cipher which human ingenuity cannot resolve,’ was a dictum of his. If I’m not mistaken about that safe, I think I could guarantee to open it in less than ten minutes. The resources of science, and all that, you know. But I think it would be better to wait a while and see if Mr. Chacewater turns up to open it for us himself.”

“But perhaps Mr. Chacewater’s body is inside it now,” the Inspector suggested. “There may have been a double murder, for all we know.”

“In that case, we shall find him when we open it,” Sir Clinton assured him lightly. “If he’s inside, he’ll hardly be likely to shift his quarters.”
===Chinese===
阿玛代尔显得一脸茫然。

“我原以为这种锁具硬得没法下手呢，”他说。

克林顿爵士忍住一抹笑意。

“你该读读埃德加·爱伦·坡（Edgar Allan Poe），探长。『人类的巧智，造不出人类的巧智解不开的密码』，便是他的一句格言。要是我没看错那口保险柜，我敢担保十分钟之内打开它。科学的种种手段嘛，你懂的。不过我想，还是等一等的好——瞧瞧蔡斯沃特先生会不会自己回来，替咱们把它打开。”

“可说不定蔡斯沃特先生的尸首眼下就在里头，”探长提醒道。“据我们所知，兴许是桩双重凶案呢。”

“真那样，咱们开柜时自会找到他，”克林顿爵士轻描淡写地宽慰他。“他若在里头，大约也挪不了窝了。”

## X The Shot in the Clearing / 第十章 林中空地上的枪声

===Original===
When Sir Clinton reached his office on the morning after the murder at Ravensthorpe, he found Inspector Armadale awaiting him with a number of exhibits.

“I’ve brought everything that seemed worth while,” Armadale explained. “I thought you might care to look at some of the things again, although you’ve seen them already.”

“That’s very good of you, Inspector. I should like to see some of them, as a matter of fact. Now suppose we begin with the fingerprints. They might suggest a few fresh ideas.”
===Chinese===
雷文索普（Ravensthorpe）凶案发生后的次日清晨，克林顿爵士（Sir Clinton）走进办公室，发现阿玛代尔（Armadale）探长已带着一批物证在等他。

“凡是瞧着值得带来的，我都带来了，”阿玛代尔解释道。“虽说您都已经看过，我想您或许还想再看几样。”

“难为你了，探长。说实话，有几样我确实想看看。这样吧，咱们就从指纹入手。它们兴许能启发些新想法。”

===Original===
“They seem to suggest more notions than I have room for in my head,” the Inspector confessed ruefully. “It’s a most tangled case, to my mind.”

“Then let’s start with the fingerprints,” the Chief Constable proposed. “At least they’ll settle some points, I hope.”

Armadale unwrapped a large brown-paper parcel.

“I got the lot without any difficulty; and last night we photographed them all and enlarged the pictures. They’re all here.”

“You took Foss’s, I suppose?”

“Yes, and I managed to find some of Maurice Chacewater’s too.”
===Chinese===
“依我看，它们启发的念头多得我脑袋里都腾不出地方装了，”探长苦笑着招认。“这案子，在我眼里乱得像一团麻。”

“那就从指纹开始，”郡警察局长（Chief Constable）提议。“至少我希望，它们能把几处疑点敲定。”

阿玛代尔解开一个牛皮纸大包裹。

“我顺顺当当全取到了手；昨夜我们把它们统统拍了照，还放了像。都在这儿。”

“福斯（Foss）的你也取了吧，我料想？”

“取了，我还想法子弄到了莫里斯·蔡斯沃特（Maurice Chacewater）的一些。”

===Original===
“That’s pretty sharp work,” Sir Clinton complimented his subordinate. “How did you manage to make sure they were his?”

“I asked for his set of razors, sir, and took them from the blades. He’d left prints here and there of his finger and thumb, either on the blade or on the handle. Of course I couldn’t get anything else very sharp; but there are quite enough for the purpose, as you’ll see.”

He laid out three enlarged photographs on the desk before Sir Clinton; then, below each of the first two, he put down a second print.

“This first print,” he said, pointing to it, “represents the fingerprints we found on the automatic pistol. You can see that it’s the arch pattern on the thumb. Now here”—he indicated the companion print—“is Foss’s thumbprint; and if you look at it, you’ll see almost at a glance that it’s identical with the print on the pistol. They’re identical. I’ve measured them. And there are no other prints except Foss’s on the pistol.”
===Chinese===
“这手脚够利索，”克林顿爵士夸了下属一句。“你是怎么拿准那是他的指纹的？”

“我向他要了他那一套剃刀，先生，从刀片上取的印。他东一处西一处留下了指头和拇指的印子，有的在刀片上，有的在刀柄上。自然，再清晰些的别的印子我是弄不到了；可您会看到，办事尽够使了。”

他在克林顿爵士面前的桌上摆开三张放大照片；又在前两张各自底下，各配了另一份印痕。

“这第一张印痕，”他指着说，“是我们在那把自动手枪上找到的指纹。您看得出，拇指上是弓形纹。喏，这边这张”——他指了指旁边配的那张——“是福斯的拇指印；您看上一眼，差不多立刻就看得出来，它跟手枪上那枚一模一样。完全相同。我量过了。手枪上除了福斯的，再没有别人的印子。”

===Original===
“Good,” said Sir Clinton. “ ‘And that, said John, is that.’ We know where we are so far as the pistol’s concerned. Pass along, please.”

“I’ve examined the pistol,” the Inspector continued. “It’s fully loaded in the magazine and has an extra cartridge in the barrel; but it hasn’t been fired recently so far as I can see.”

“Now for the next pair of prints,” Sir Clinton suggested.

“This represents the thumbprint from the sword, or whatever you call it,” said the Inspector. “Also prints of the two middle fingers of the right hand, found on the weapon. The second print of the pair shows identical fingerprints from a different source. The thumbprints in the two cases are not exactly alike, because you get only the edge of the thumb marked in the grip of a sword, whereas the other specimen gives a full imprint. But I think you’ll find they’re the same. I’ve measured them, too. You can see that the thumb pattern is a loop type, quite different from Foss’s prints; and there’s a trace of a tiny scar at the edge of the thumb in both these prints. I’d like you to compare them carefully, sir.”
===Chinese===
“好，”克林顿爵士说。“『约翰说了：就是这么回事。』手枪这一节，咱们心里有数了。请接着往下。”

“那把手枪我查验过了，”探长接着说。“弹匣满装着子弹，枪膛里还额外有一发；可依我看，它近来到没开过。”

“来看下一对印痕，”克林顿爵士提议。

“这张是那把刀——或者随它叫什么名目吧——上面的拇指印，”探长说。“还有在那件兵器上找到的、右手中指两枚指印。这一对的第二张，是从另一处来源取的相同指纹。两处拇指印不完全相像，因为手握刀柄时，拇指只留下侧边一道印；而另一份样本是整个按实了的印。可我认为您会看出是同一只手。这一对我也量过。您看，拇指纹型是箕形纹，跟福斯的纹型全然两样；而且两份印痕的拇指边上，都有一点细小疤痕的痕迹。请您仔细比对一番，先生。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton took up the two prints and scanned them with care, comparing the images point by point.

“There’s no mistake possible,” he said. “The two sets are identical, so far as I can see; and the scar on the thumb is a clinching bit of evidence.”

“You admit they’re from the same hand?” asked the Inspector, with a peculiar look at Sir Clinton.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士拿起两份印痕，细细审看，逐点比对图样。

“错不了，”他说。“依我看，两组指纹完全相同；拇指上那道疤，更是板上钉钉的铁证。”

“您承认它们出自同一只手？”探长问，望着克林顿爵士的眼神有些异样。

===Original===
“Undoubtedly. Now whose are the second set?”

The Inspector continued to look at his superior with something out of the common in his expression.

“The second set of prints came from Maurice Chacewater’s razors,” he said.

The Chief Constable’s lips set tightly and a touch of grimness showed in his face.
===Chinese===
“毫无疑问。那么第二组是谁的？”

探长仍旧望着他的上司，神色间带着几分不同寻常。

“第二组指纹，取自莫里斯·蔡斯沃特的剃刀，”他说。

郡警察局长双唇紧抿，脸上透出一丝冷峻。

===Original===
“I see we shall have to be quite clear about this, Inspector,” he said, bluntly. “By the look of you, you seemed to think I’d be taken aback by this evidence, because Mr. Chacewater is a friend of mine. I was taken aback—naturally enough. But if you think it’s going to make any difference to the conduct of this case—and I seemed to see something of the sort in your face—you can put that out of your mind once for all. The business of the police is to get hold of the murderer, whoever he may be. Friendship doesn’t come into these affairs, Inspector. So kindly don’t suspect me of anything of that kind in future. You know what I mean; I needn’t put it into words.”
===Chinese===
“看来这一层咱们得说个透亮，探长，”他直截了当地说。“瞧你那副神气，你像是以为蔡斯沃特先生是我的朋友，这份证据准会叫我大吃一惊。我是吃了一惊——这也够自然。可你要是以为它会给这案子的办法定个什么调子——我看你脸上就挂着几分这意思——那你趁早把这个念头从心里撵出去，一劳永逸。警察的本分，就是捉拿凶手，不管他是谁。交情在这一类事里没有立足之地，探长。往后请你别再往这上头疑心我。你明白我的意思；用不着我掰开揉碎地说。”

===Original===
Without giving Armadale time for reply, he picked up the last print.

“What’s this?”

“It’s the set of prints I took from the valet’s fingers,” the Inspector hastened to explain. “It corresponds to nothing I’ve found anywhere else. You can see it’s a whorl type on the thumb.”
===Chinese===
不给阿玛代尔回话的工夫，他拿起了最后一份印痕。

“这是什么？”

“这是我从那男仆手指上取的一组印痕，”探长赶忙解释。“它跟我在别处找到的哪一份都对不上。您看得出，拇指上是斗形纹。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton examined the print for a moment or two, then put it down.

“What about the box and the wristwatch?” he asked.

Inspector Armadale’s face showed that here he was puzzled.

“There’s nothing on either of them—not a recent mark of any description. And yet the man who packed them up must have fingered both things.”

“With gloves on, evidently.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士把印痕察看了一两息，随即放下。

“那只盒子和那块腕表呢？”他问。

阿玛代尔探长的脸色表明，这一处他犯难了。

“两样上头什么都没有——新近的印痕一星半点也没有。可把它们打成包裹的那个人，总归两样都上手摸过。”

“显然是戴着手套。”

===Original===
“But why gloves?” the Inspector demanded.

“Why gloves?” Sir Clinton echoed, rather sarcastically. “To avoid leaving fingerprints, of course. That’s obvious.”

“But why avoid leaving fingerprints on a thing that you’re sending to a jeweller for repair?”
===Chinese===
“可为什么要戴手套？”探长质问。

“为什么戴手套？”克林顿爵士应了一声，带着讥诮。“自然是为了不留指纹。这还用说。”

“可一件要送去珠宝匠修理的东西，为什么偏要不留指纹？”

===Original===
“Think it over, Inspector. I won’t insult you by telling you my solution. Let’s take another point. Have you the watch itself here?”

The Inspector produced it and handed it over. Sir Clinton took out a pocketknife and opened the back of the case.

“No use,” he announced, after examining the back cover carefully. “It’s never been repaired. There are no reference marks scratched on the inside of the back as there usually are when a watch has gone back to the watchmakers. If there had been, we might have found out something about Foss in that way, by getting hold of the watchmakers. By the way, have you timed this thing as I asked you to do?”

“It’s running on time,” Armadale answered. “It hasn’t varied a rap in the last twelve hours.”
===Chinese===
“自己琢磨去吧，探长。我把我的答案说出口，那是小瞧你。咱们看另一处。那块表本身你带在身上吗？”

探长取出来递了过去。克林顿爵士掏出随身小刀，撬开了表壳后盖。

“没用处，”他把后盖仔细验看之后宣布。“这表从没修理过。表底盖内侧没有划上那种记号——通常一块表送回表匠手里，都会划下查考的记号。要是有，咱们顺着表匠那条线，兴许能摸出点福斯的底细。对了，我叫你给这表对时，你照办了吗？”

“走得准，”阿玛代尔回答。“这十二个钟头里，分毫不差。”

===Original===
“A practically new watch; running to time; never needed repair so far; dispatched by post with no fingermarks of dispatcher: surely you can see what that means?”

Inspector Armadale shook his head.

“It might be a secret message,” he hazarded, though without much confidence. “I mean a prearranged code.”

“So it might,” Sir Clinton agreed. “The only thing against that in my mind is that I’m perfectly sure that it wasn’t.”

Armadale looked sulky.

“I’m hardly clever enough to follow you, sir, I’m afraid.”
===Chinese===
“一块几乎全新的表；走时准；至今没修过；经邮局寄出，寄件人却半点指痕不留：这意味着什么，你总该看得出来吧？”

阿玛代尔探长摇了摇头。

“兴许是种密信，”他试着猜了一句，底气却不很足。“我是说，预先约定的暗号。”

“也说不定，”克林顿爵士表示同意。“只是我心里有一条驳它：我完全确定它不是。”

阿玛代尔面露怏怏之色。

“怕是我脑子不够使，跟不上您，先生。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton’s expression grew momentarily stern; but the shade passed from his face almost instantly.

“This is one of these cases, Inspector, where I think that two heads are better than one. Now if I tell you what’s in my mind, it might tempt you to look at things exactly as I do; and then we’d have lost the advantage of having two brains at work on the business independently. We’re more likely to be usefully employed if we pool the facts and keep our interpretations separate from each other.”

The tone of the Chief Constable’s voice went a good way towards soothing the Inspector’s ruffled feelings, the more so since he saw the weight of Sir Clinton’s reasoning.

“I’m sorry, sir. I quite see your point now.”

Sir Clinton had the knack of leaving no ill-feelings in his subordinates. By an almost imperceptible change of manner, he dismissed the whole matter and restored cordiality again.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士的神色一时严峻起来；可那层阴影几乎立刻便从他脸上褪去了。

“探长，这案子在我看来，属于那种『一人计短、二人计长』的题目。我要是把心里想的和盘托给你，你难免事事都照我的路子看；那咱们就白白丢了两个人各自独立动脑的好处。咱们把事实归拢到一处、把各自的解释分开来揣着，办事才更有指望。”

郡警察局长这番话的口气，很把探长受挫的火气抚平了几分，何况他也掂出了克林顿爵士话里的分量。

“对不住，先生。您的高见，我这回算是完全明白了。”

克林顿爵士素有本领，从不让芥蒂留在下属心里。他几乎是难以察觉地一换做派，便把这一节整个揭过，重又热络起来。

===Original===
“Let’s get back to the pure facts, Inspector. Each of us must look at them in his own way; but we can at least examine some of them without biasing each other. Did you get any more information out of that chauffeur?”

Inspector Armadale seemed glad enough to forget the slight friction between himself and his Chief, as the tone of his voice showed when he replied.

“I could get nothing out of him at all, sir. He seems a stupid sort of fellow. But it was quite clear that somehow or other he’d picked up the idea that Foss meant to leave Ravensthorpe for good yesterday afternoon. He stuck to that definitely; and the packing up of his traps shows that he believed it.”

“We can take it, then, that Foss gave reason for the man thinking that he was going away. Put your own interpretation on that, Inspector; but you needn’t tell me what you make of it.”
===Chinese===
“咱们回到光溜溜的事实上来，探长。各人有各人的看法；可至少有几处，咱们能一起过上一遍，谁也不带偏谁。那个司机身上，你还问出点别的没有？”

阿玛代尔探长巴不得把同上司之间这点小摩擦忘个干净，回话的口气里就听得出这份情愿。

“他身上我压根儿什么也没问出来，先生。瞧着是个脑筋不灵的家伙。可有一点明明白白：不知怎么的，他认准了福斯打算昨天下午一去不回地离开雷文索普。这一点他咬得死死的；行李都收拾停当，也可见他自己信了这一层。”

“那么咱们可以认定，福斯说了什么、做了什么，叫那人以为他要走了。这里头你作何解，探长，你自己掂量；不必说给我听。”

===Original===
The Inspector’s smile showed that ill-feeling had gone.

“Very well, Sir Clinton. And I’ll admit that I had my suspicions of the valet. He seems to have a clear bill now in the matter of the fingerprints on the weapon. Perhaps I was a bit rough on the man; but he annoyed me—a cheeky fellow.”

“Oh, don’t let’s use hard words about him,” Sir Clinton suggested chaffingly. “Let’s call him cool, simply.”

“Well, his fingerprints weren’t on the handle of the sword, anyhow,” the Inspector admitted.

“I hardly expected them to be,” was all the comment Sir Clinton saw fit to make. “Now what about friend Foss? By the way, I don’t mind saying that I still think these two affairs at Ravensthorpe are interconnected. And one thing’s clear at any rate: Foss wasn’t the man in white. You remember he was wearing a cowboy costume according to the valet’s evidence; and we found that costume in his wardrobe, which confirms Marden.”
===Chinese===
探长脸上绽出笑来，芥蒂消了。

“好的，克林顿爵士。我也承认，先前我疑过那男仆。如今在兵器指纹这一节上，他算是得了张清白单子。兴许我先前对那人凶了些；可他惹恼了我——一个没大没小的家伙。”

“哦，咱们别说他的重话，”克林顿爵士打趣道。“干脆说他沉着，就完了。”

“反正他的指纹不在那刀柄上，”探长承认。

“我本来就没指望在，”克林顿爵士觉得说这一句就够了。“现在说说咱们的朋友福斯吧。对了，我不妨明说：雷文索普这两桩事，我照旧认为是连着的。至少有一点清楚：福斯不是那白衣人。你记得，据男仆的证词，他那晚穿的是一身牛仔装；那身装束我们在他衣柜里找到了，这就印证了马登（Marden）的话。”

===Original===
The Inspector seemed to be taking a leaf out of Sir Clinton’s book. He refrained from either acquiescing in or contradicting the Chief Constable’s statement that the two cases were linked.

“Foss had more ready money in his pocket than most people carry; he was in a position to clear out of Ravensthorpe at any moment without needing to go back to his flat or even to a bank. I think these facts are plain enough,” he pointed out. “And they fit in with the chauffeur’s evidence, such as it is.”

“And he had no latchkey of his flat with him,” Sir Clinton supplemented. “Of course it was a service flat and he may have left the key behind him instead of carrying it with him. One could find that out if it were worth while.”

“There’s a good deal that needs explaining about Foss,” the Inspector observed. “I’ve got his photograph here, taken from the body yesterday.”
===Chinese===
探长像是学了克林顿爵士的乖。局长说两案相连，他既不附和，也不驳斥。

“福斯口袋里的现钱，比寻常人带的都多；他随时可以从雷文索普拔脚走人，不必回公寓，连银行都不必去。这几层，我想够明白了，”他指出。“也跟司机那份证词对得上——那证词原本就算不得什么。”

“他身上也没带着公寓的弹簧锁钥匙，”克林顿爵士补上一句。“那自然是带仆役服务的公寓，他兴许把钥匙撂在寓所，没随身带。值当的话，这一层可以打听明白。”

“关于福斯，要解释的地方多着呢，”探长说。“我这里有他的照片，昨天从尸体上拍的。”

===Original===
He produced it as he spoke.

“Send a copy to Scotland Yard, Inspector, please, and ask if they have any information about him. Considering everything, it’s quite likely we might learn something. You might send his fingerprints also, to see if they have them indexed there.”

“I’ll send Marden’s too, when I’m at it,” the Inspector volunteered, “and the chauffeur’s. We might as well be complete when we’re at it.”

Sir Clinton indicated his agreement without saying anything. He changed the subject when he next spoke.
===Chinese===
他一边说，一边取了出来。

“请寄一份给苏格兰场（Scotland Yard），探长，问问他们有没有此人的情报。方方面面掂量下来，咱们很可能会打听出点什么。他的指纹你也可以一并寄去，看他们那边有没有编档存着。”

“办这事的时候，马登的我也一并寄去，”探长自告奋勇，“还有司机的。既然办，索性办齐全。”

克林顿爵士没言语，表示了同意。再开口时，他换了话头。

===Original===
“We’ve agreed to pool the facts, Inspector, and I’ve got a contribution—two contributions in fact—towards the common stock. Here’s the first.”

He laid a telegraph form on the desk before Armadale, and the Inspector read the wording:

Have no agent named Foss am not negotiating for Leonardo medallions. Kessock.

“Well, that’s a bit of a surprise!” ejaculated the Inspector. “It was obvious that there was something fishy; but I hadn’t imagined it was as fishy as all that. Kessock knows nothing about him, then?”
===Chinese===
“咱们说定了凑事实，探长，我这儿有一份进项——实说是两份。这是头一份。”

他把一张电报单放在阿玛代尔面前的桌上，探长念出电文：

并无名叫福斯的代理人，亦未洽购达·芬奇（Leonardo）徽章。凯索克（Kessock）。

“嘿，这可有点意外！”探长脱口喊道。“明摆着这里头有鬼；可我没料到，鬼有这么大。这么说，凯索克压根儿不认识他？”

===Original===
“My cable was fairly explicit. It’s clear that friend Foss had no authority from Kessock.”

“But what about all that correspondence between Maurice Chacewater and Kessock that we saw?”

“Forgeries, so far as the Kessock letters were concerned, obviously. One of Kessock’s household must have been in league with Foss and intercepted Maurice Chacewater’s letters. Then replies were forged and dispatched. I’ve cabled Kessock about it this morning, so as to get the news in at once. The confederate may hear of Foss’s murder through the newspapers in four or five days when our papers get across there. He might bolt when he got the news. I’ve given Kessock a chance to forestall that if he wants to.”
===Chinese===
“我的电报写得够明白了。咱们的朋友福斯，分明没从凯索克那儿讨到任何授权。”

“那咱们见过的那一摞莫里斯·蔡斯沃特和凯索克的通信，又怎么说？”

“就凯索克那几封信而论，分明是伪造的。凯索克家里必定有人同福斯串通一气，把莫里斯·蔡斯沃特的去信半路截了，再伪造回信发出去。这事我今早又给凯索克去了电报，好叫消息立刻递到。那同党要等咱们这边的报纸运到，四五天后才见得着福斯被杀的新闻；他得了信，兴许就要开溜。我给了凯索克一个抢在前头的机会，就看他用不用。”

===Original===
“That puts a new light on things, certainly,” Armadale said when he had considered the new facts. “Foss was a wrong ’un masquerading here for some purpose or other—the medallions, probably. That fits in with all the unmarked linen and the rest of it. But why was he murdered?”

Sir Clinton disregarded the question.

“I’ve got another fact to contribute,” he went on. “You remember that Marconi Otophone in Foss’s room? I’ve made some inquiries about it. It’s a thing they make for the use of deaf people—a modern substitute for the ear-trumpet.”

The Inspector made a gesture of bewilderment.

“But Foss wasn’t deaf! He admitted to you that he had good enough hearing, when he was telling you about overhearing Foxton Polegate in the winter-garden.”
===Chinese===
“这确实把事情照出了另一番光亮，”阿玛代尔把新事实掂量过后说。“福斯原来是个歹人，为着这样那样的目的在这儿乔装打扮——多半就是冲着徽章来的。这跟他那套没有标记的衬衣等等，全对得上。可他为什么被人杀了呢？”

克林顿爵士不理会这个问题。

“我还有一条事实要入伙，”他接着说。“你记得福斯房里那架马可尼（Marconi）奥托风（Otophone）吗？我打听过了。那是给聋人做的东西——老式助听喇叭筒的现代替身。”

探长做了个莫明所以的手势。

“可福斯不聋啊！他跟您讲自己在温室里偷听到福克斯顿·波尔盖特（Foxton Polegate）说话的时候，还亲口承认过自己耳音好得很。”

===Original===
“That’s quite true,” Sir Clinton rejoined. “But he evidently needed an Otophone for all that.”

The Inspector pondered for a few moments before speaking.

“It beats me,” he said at last.

Sir Clinton dismissed the subject without further discussion.

“Now what about Maurice Chacewater?” he inquired. “There’s no great difficulty in suggesting *how* he disappeared from the museum. It’s common talk hereabout that Ravensthorpe has secret passages; and one of them may end up in the wall of the museum.”
===Chinese===
“这话不假，”克林顿爵士应道。“可尽管如此，他分明还是用得着一架奥托风。”

探长寻思了片刻才开口。

“我是服了，”他终于说。

克林顿爵士不再深议，把这一题揭了过去。

“那么莫里斯·蔡斯沃特呢？”他问道。“他是怎么从博物馆里消失的，给个说法并不难。这一带人人都这么传：雷文索普有暗道；其中一条，兴许就通到博物馆的墙里。”

===Original===
It was the turn of Armadale to contribute a fresh fact.

“He didn’t appear at any local station yesterday or this morning; and he didn’t use a motor of any sort that I’ve been able to trace. I’ve had men on that job and it’s been thoroughly done.”

“Congratulations, Inspector.”

“If he hasn’t got away, then he must be somewhere in the neighborhood still.”

“I should say that was indisputable, if not certain,” commented Sir Clinton, with a return of his faintly chaffing manner. “A man can only be in one place at once, if you follow me. And if he’s not there, then he must be here.”
===Chinese===
这回轮到阿玛代尔凑一条新事实了。

“从昨天到今早，本地哪个车站他都没露过面；凡追得上线索的汽车，他一辆也没坐过。这桩活计我派了人手，办得扎扎实实。”

“可贺可贺，探长。”

“他既然没走脱，那就准还藏在左近什么地方。”

“依我说，这话纵然算不得板上钉钉，也是无可置辩的了，”克林顿爵士评论道，又带上他那淡淡的打趣劲儿。“一个人一时只能待在一个地方，你明白我的意思。他既然不在那儿，那就必定在这儿。”

===Original===
“Yes. But where is ‘here,’ in this particular case?” inquired Armadale, following his Chief’s mood. “I expect he’s hiding somewhere around. It’s what anyone might do if they found themselves up to the hilt in a case of murder”—he paused for an instant—“or manslaughter, and got into a panic over it.”

Sir Clinton ignored the Inspector’s last sentence.

“I wish I could get into touch with Cecil Chacewater. He ought to be at home just now. He’s the only man in the family now, and he ought to take charge of things up there.”

“You haven’t got his address yet, sir?”

“Not yet.”

Sir Clinton put the subject aside.
===Chinese===
“是。可眼下这桩案子里的『这儿』，又指哪儿呢？”阿玛代尔顺着上司的口吻问。“我料他是躲在附近什么地方。换了谁，深陷在这么一桩凶案里”——他顿了一顿——“或者误杀案里，慌了神，都会这么办。”

克林顿爵士只当没听见探长那后半句。

“但愿我能联络上塞西尔·蔡斯沃特（Cecil Chacewater）。他眼下该在家。如今这一家就剩他一个男人，那边的事务，该他出面掌管。”

“您还没拿到他的地址，先生？”

“还没有。”

克林顿爵士把这一节搁下了。

===Original===
“Now, Inspector, let me remind you of what's wanted:

What was the crime, who did it, when was it done, and where,

How done, and with what motive, who in the deed did share?

You put it down as murder?”

“Or manslaughter,” corrected Armadale. “And we know When, How, and Where, at any rate.”

“Do we?” Sir Clinton rejoined. “Speak for yourself. I’m not so sure about When and Where yet, and How is still a dark mystery so far as I’m concerned. I mean,” he added, “so far as legal proof goes.”
===Chinese===
“好了，探长，让我提醒你，办案要问的是哪几桩：

所犯何罪，何人所为，作于何时何地，

如何施行，怀着何种动机，何人同谋？

“你把它归为谋杀？”

“或者误杀，”阿玛代尔纠正道。“反正『何时』『如何』『何地』，咱们是知道了的。”

“咱们知道吗？”克林顿爵士应道。“那是说你自个儿。『何时』和『何地』，我还不怎么有把握；至于『如何』，在我这儿仍是一团漆黑的谜。我的意思是，”他补了一句，“就法律证据而论。”

===Original===
The Inspector was about to say something further when a knock at the door was heard and a constable appeared in answer to Sir Clinton’s summons.

“The Ravensthorpe head keeper wants to see you, Sir Clinton, if you can spare him a moment. He says it’s important.”

The Chief Constable ordered the keeper to be admitted.

“Well, Mold, what’s your trouble?” he inquired, when the man appeared.
===Chinese===
探长正要再说什么，门外传来叩门声，应克林顿爵士的传唤，进来一名警士。

“雷文索普的总看管人想见您，克林顿爵士，您要能赏他一会儿工夫的话。他说有要紧事。”

郡警察局长吩咐放那看管人进来。

“喂，莫尔德（Mold），你有什么为难事？”那人进来后，他问道。

===Original===
“It’s this way, Sir Clinton,” Mold began. “Seein’ the queer sort o’ things we’ve seen lately, it seemed to me that maybe another queer thing that’s happened might be important. So I thought it over, and I made bold to come and tell you about it.”

He seemed to lose confidence a little at this point; but Sir Clinton encouraged him by a show of interest.

“Last night,” he went on, “I was goin’ through the wood at the back o’ the house—about eleven o’clock it was, as near as I can make it. At the back o’ the house there’s a strip of woodland, then a little bit o’ a clearin’, and then the rest of the wood. I’d come out o’ the bigger bit o’ the wood and got most o’ the way across the clearin’ when it happened. I can tell you just where it was, for I was passin’ the old ruin there—the Knight’s Tower they call it.”
===Chinese===
“是这么回事，克林顿爵士，”莫尔德开了腔。“瞧着近来咱们见识过的这些邪行事，我心里想，兴许又出了一桩邪行事，也是要紧的。我前前后后琢磨了一回，就大着胆子来向您回禀。”

讲到这儿，他像是泄了几分底气；克林顿爵士做出关切的样子，给他鼓劲。

“昨儿夜里，”他接着说，“我正打宅子后头那片林子里穿过去——约莫十一点钟吧，我记得差不离就是那个时辰。宅子后头是一条林带，再过去一小块空地，再过去又是林子。我刚从大的一片林子里出来，走到空地一大半的当口，那事就来了。那地点我能说得准，因为我正打那边一座老破楼跟前过——他们管它叫骑士塔（Knight's Tower）。”

===Original===
He paused for a moment or two, evidently finding continuous narrative rather a strain.

“The moon was well up by that time. It’s just past the full these days; and the place was as clear as day. Everythin’ was quiet, except an old owl that lives in a hollow tree up by there. I could hear the swish of my feet in the grass and mighty little else; for the grass was dewy and made a lot o’ noise with my stepping through it. Well, as I was goin’ along, all of a sudden I heard a shot. It sounded close by me; an’ I turned at once. There’s a poachin’ chap that’s given me a lot o’ trouble, an’ I didn’t put it past him to think he might be tryin’ to give me a scare. But when I turned round there was nothin’ to be seen. There was nothin’ there at all; an’ yet that shot had come from quite close by.”
===Chinese===
他停了一两息，显然一口气连着往下讲，于他有点吃力。

“那工夫月亮已经上得老高了。这几日月头刚过望，那地方亮得跟白昼似的。四下里静悄悄，只有一只老猫头鹰，就住在那一头一棵空树里。我听得见自己两脚蹭草的沙沙声，别的几乎没有；草上挂着露水，我一踩过去，响动不小。正走着，冷不丁我听见一声枪响。听着就在我近旁；我立马转过身去。有个偷猎的家伙，一向叫我伤透了脑筋，我心想，莫不是他存心吓唬我一家伙。可我一回头，什么也没瞧见。那儿压根儿什么都没有；可那一枪，分明就是打近处响的。”

===Original===
“Did it sound like the report of a shotgun?” Sir Clinton asked.

Mold seemed to be in a difficulty.

“Shotgun sounds I know fairly well. ’T weren’t from a shotgun. More like a pistol-shot it sounded, when I’d had time to think over it. An’ yet it weren’t altogether like a pistol-shot, neither. That’s a sharp sound. This was more booming-like, if you understand me.”

“I’m afraid I don’t quite see it yet, Mold,” Sir Clinton admitted. “I know how difficult it is to describe sounds, though. Have another try. Did it remind you of anything?”
===Chinese===
“那响声像猎枪放的吗？”克林顿爵士问。

莫尔德面有难色。

“猎枪的响动我熟。那不是猎枪。等我回过味来，倒更像手枪。可又不大像手枪。手枪是脆响。这一声发闷，嗡嗡的，您明白我的意思吧。”

“怕是我还没听真切，莫尔德，”克林顿爵士承认。“不过我知道，形容声音这事最难。再试一回。它叫你想起什么来着？”

===Original===
A light seemed to flicker for a moment in Mold’s memory.

“I know!” he exclaimed. “It was like this. I’ve got it! Did you ever stand at the door of our Morris-tube range in the village while there was firin’ goin’ on inside? Well, this was somethin’ like that, only more so. I mean as if they’d fired somethin’ a bit heavier than a miniature rifle. That’s it! That’s just how it sounded.”

He was evidently relieved by having found what he considered an apt simile.
===Chinese===
莫尔德的记忆里像是倏地闪过一线光亮。

“有了！”他喊道。“是这么回事。我逮着了！您在村里莫里斯管靶场（Morris-tube range）门口站过没有——正赶上里头放枪的工夫？对，就跟那个有点像，还更沉些。我是说，像放了件比小口径步枪沉一点的东西。对了！就是那么个响法。”

找着了自以为贴切的比方，他分明松了口气。

===Original===
“What happened after that?” Sir Clinton demanded.

“When I saw nobody near me I’ll admit I felt a bit funny. Here was a shot comin’, so it seemed, out o’ the empty air, with nothin’ to account for it. Straight away, I’ll admit, sir, I began thinkin’ of that Black Man that little Jennie Hitchin has been spreadin’ the story about lately …”

Sir Clinton pricked up his ears.

“We’ll hear about the Black Man later on, Mold, if you please. Tell us what you did at that moment.”
===Chinese===
“后来呢？”克林顿爵士追问。

“我一瞧身边一个人没有，说实话，心里有点发毛。凭空一声枪响，像是打虚空里来的，没有一点来由。说实话，先生，我立马就想起小珍妮·希钦（Jennie Hitchin）近来逢人便讲的那桩『黑人』的事……”

克林顿爵士竖起了耳朵。

“那个『黑人』，莫尔德，请容后再讲。先说说你当时做了些什么。”

===Original===
“Well, sir, I searched about. The moon was clear of clouds and the place was just an open glade. The shot had come from quite near by, as I said. But when I hunted I could find nothing. There wasn’t a track in the dew on the grass. My own tracks showed up in the moonlight as clear as clear. There wasn’t anyone hiding in the old ruin; I went through and around it twice. There wasn’t a sound; for the shot had frightened the owl. I found nothing. And yet I’d take my oath that shot was fired not more than ten or a dozen yards away from me.”

“Did you hear any whistle of shot or a bullet?”

“No, sir.”

“H’m! That’s the whole story? Now, tell us about this Black Man you mentioned.”
===Chinese===
“是这样的，先生，我四下里搜了一遍。月亮明晃晃的没一丝云，那地方就是一片敞亮的林间草地。那一枪，我说过，响处就在近旁。可我搜来搜去，什么也找不着。带露的草上没有一条脚印。我自己的脚印，在月光底下清清楚楚。老破楼里也没藏着人；我里里外外绕着搜了两遍。一点声息也没有；那一枪把猫头鹰吓着了。我什么都没找着。可我敢起誓，那一枪就响在离我不过十来码远的地方。”

“听见弹丸或者子弹的啸声没有？”

“没有，先生。”

“唔！就这么些？好，说说你提到的那个『黑人』吧。”

===Original===
Mold seemed rather ashamed.

“Oh, that’s just child’s chatter, Sir Clinton. I oughtn’t to have mentioned it.”

“I’m quite willing to listen to ‘child’s chatter,’ Mold, if it happens to be unusual.”

Mold evidently decided to take the plunge, though obviously he regretted having mentioned the matter at all.
===Chinese===
莫尔德显得有点讪讪。

“嗐，那不过是娃娃家的闲话，克林顿爵士。我不该提的。”

“只要是异乎寻常的，莫尔德，『娃娃家的闲话』我也乐意听。”

莫尔德分明横下心要说了，尽管看得出，他懊悔不该提起这桩事。

===Original===
“This Jennie Hitchin’s a child that lives with her grandmother on the estate. The girl’s there at night in case anything goes wrong with the old woman. Old Mrs. Hitchin was taken ill one night lately, about the middle of the night. Pretty bad she seemed; and Jennie had to dress and go off for the doctor in a hurry. That took her through the woods—it’s a shortcut that way and the moonlight was bright. An’ as she was goin’ along …”

“What night was this,” Sir Clinton interrupted.

The keeper thought for a moment or two.

“Now I come to think of it,” he said, “’twas the night of that robbery up at Ravensthorpe. So it was. An’ as Jennie was goin’ along through the woods she saw—so she says—a Black Man slippin’ about from tree to tree.”
===Chinese===
“这个珍妮·希钦，是个跟祖母住在庄园里的小姑娘。夜里老太太那边要是出了什么事，也好有个照应。希钦老太不久前的一个夜里病倒了，正赶上半夜。瞧着病得不轻；珍妮只好穿衣裳，急急忙忙跑去请医生。那条路要穿过林子——那样走是近道，月光又亮。她正走着……”

“那是哪一夜？”克林顿爵士插话。

看管人想了一两息。

“这么一想，”他说，“正是雷文索普失窃的那一夜。没错，就是那夜。珍妮正打林子里走着，她瞧见——她是这么说的——一个黑人，在树与树之间躲躲闪闪地挪动。”

===Original===
“A man in dark clothes?”

“No, sir. If I understood rightly, ’twas a black man. I mean a naked man with a black skin, black all over.”

“Did he molest the child?”

“No, sir. He seemed to be tryin’ to keep out of her road if anythin’. But o’ course it gave her a start. She took and ran—and small blame to her, I think. She’s only eleven or so, an’ it gave her a dreadful fright. An’ of course next day this tale was all over the countryside. I wonder if you didn’t hear it yourself, sir.”
===Chinese===
“一个穿深色衣裳的男人？”

“不是，先生。要说我听明白的，那是个黑人。我是说，一个光着身子的男人，一身黑皮肤，浑身都黑。”

“他骚扰那孩子没有？”

“没有，先生。瞧那样子，他反倒像是在躲着她的道儿。可这自然叫她吃了一惊。她撒腿就跑——依我说，跑得一点不冤。她才十一岁上下，可把她吓坏了。第二天，这事儿自然就传遍了四乡。我倒纳闷，您自己就没听说过吗，先生。”

===Original===
“It’s news to me, Mold, I’m afraid. Even the police can’t know everything, you see. Now before you go I want something more from you. That night when you were on guard in the museum, you remember. Do you recall seeing anyone there at any time during the evening dressed in cowboy clothes? You know, the kind of thing in the Wild West films.”

Mold pondered for a time, evidently racking his memory.

“No, sir. I remember nobody like that. I think I’d have recalled it if I had. I’m rather keen on films about cowboys myself, and if I’d seen a cowboy I’d have had a good look at him, just out o’ curiosity.”
===Chinese===
“这个我还真是头回听说，莫尔德。你瞧，连警察也不能什么都知道。你走之前，我还要问你一件事。你在博物馆守夜那一晚，记得吧。那天晚上不论哪个时辰，你记不记得见过谁穿一身牛仔装？就是西部片里那种打扮。”

莫尔德想了一阵，显然在苦搜记忆。

“没有，先生。那样的人，我记得一个也没有。要是有，我想我不会忘。我自己就好牛仔电影这一口，真见了牛仔，光出于好奇，我也得好好瞧他几眼。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton had apparently got all he needed from Mold just then; and he sent him away quite reassured that his visit had not been wasted.

“What do you make of all that, Inspector?” he inquired with a faintly quizzical expression on his face, as soon as the door had closed behind the keeper.

Armadale shook his head. Then, seeing a chance of scoring, he smiled openly.

“I was to keep my ideas to myself, you remember, Sir Clinton.”
===Chinese===
看样子，克林顿爵士当时要从莫尔德嘴里问的都问齐了；他打发那人走时，叫他确信这一趟没算白来。

“这些你怎么看，探长？”看管人的身影刚在门外消失，他便问道，脸上带着几分似笑非笑的神气。

阿玛代尔摇了摇头。随即瞅出个扳回一城的机会，他坦然笑了。

“您吩咐过，叫我把自己的想法憋在肚里，您还记得吧，克林顿爵士。”

===Original===
The Chief Constable gave him smile for smile.

“That arrangement must be especially useful when you’ve no ideas at all, Inspector.”

Armadale took the thrust with good humour.

“Give me time to think, Sir Clinton. You know I’ve only a slow mind, and perhaps this isn’t one of my bright days.”

Before Sir Clinton could retort the desk telephone rang and the Chief Constable lifted the receiver.
===Chinese===
郡警察局长以笑还笑。

“这约定对你想必格外有用，探长——赶你压根儿没有想法的时候。”

阿玛代尔挨了这一下，倒不恼。

“容我些工夫想想，克林顿爵士。您知道我这脑子转得慢，兴许今天也不是我灵光的日子。”

克林顿爵士还没来得及回敬，桌上的电话响了，郡警察局长拿起听筒。

===Original===
“Yes, I am … Thanks very much. I’ll take down the address if you’ll read it to me.”

He jotted something down on a sheet of paper.

“Thanks. Goodbye, Joan.”

He flicked the note over to Armadale.

“Would you mind seeing if we can get on to that house by phone, Inspector? Hunt up the London Directory for it.”
===Chinese===
“对，是我……多谢。你念给我听，我把地址记下来。”

他在一张纸上记了几笔。

“谢谢。再见，琼（Joan）。”

他把纸条弹给阿玛代尔。

“探长，麻烦你看看那户人家能不能用电话联系上。去查一查伦敦电话簿。”

===Original===
“It’s Cecil Chacewater’s address?” said Armadale, glancing at the slip.

“Yes. The man he’s staying with may be on the phone.”

In a few minutes the Inspector came back with the number and Sir Clinton rang up. After a short talk he put down the receiver and turned to Armadale.

“He says he can’t come today. You heard me explaining that we want that secret passage opened, if there is one. But he doesn’t seem to think there’s any hurry. He has some business which will keep him till tomorrow.”
===Chinese===
“是塞西尔·蔡斯沃特的地址？”阿玛代尔扫了一眼纸条说。

“对。他借住的那家人，兴许装有电话。”

不几分钟，探长拿着号码回来了，克林顿爵士拨了过去。简短谈了几句，他放下听筒，转向阿玛代尔。

“他说他今天来不了。你听见我解释了，那条暗道——当真有的话——咱们要它打开。可他似乎不觉得有什么可急的。他有点事缠身，要到明天才得脱开。”

===Original===
“I heard you tell him that his brother’s disappeared,” the Inspector commented. “I’d have thought that would have brought him back quick enough.”

“It hasn’t, evidently,” was all that Sir Clinton thought it necessary to say. There seemed to be no reason for admitting the Inspector into the secret of the Ravensthorpe quarrels.
===Chinese===
“我听见您告诉他，他弟弟失踪了，”探长说。“我还当这一下，总够把他立刻叫回来的。”

“显然没有，”克林顿爵士觉得说这一句就够了。雷文索普那几场争吵的内情，似乎没有道理让探长也知晓。

## XI Underground Ravensthorpe / 第十一章 雷文索普的地下世界

===Original===
When Inspector Armadale presented himself at the Chief Constable’s office next morning he found Sir Clinton still faithful to his proposed policy of pooling all the facts of the case.

“I’ve just been in communication with the coroner,” Sir Clinton explained. “I’ve pointed out to him that possibly we may have further evidence for the inquest on Foss; and I suggested that he might confine himself to formalities as far as possible and then adjourn for a day or two. It means keeping Marden and the chauffeur here for a little longer; but they can stay at Ravensthorpe. Miss Chacewater has no objections to that. She agreed at once when I asked her.”

“The jury will have enough before them to bring in a verdict of murder against someone unknown,” the Inspector pointed out. “Do you want to make it more definite while we’re in the middle of the case?”

Sir Clinton made a noncommittal gesture as he replied:

“Let’s give ourselves the chance, at least, of putting a name on the criminal. If we don’t succeed there’s no harm done. Now here’s another point. I’ve had a telephone message from Scotland Yard. They’ve nothing on record corresponding to the fingerprints of Marden or the chauffeur. Foss was a wrong ’un. They’ve identified his fingerprints; and his photograph seems to have been easily recognizable by some of the Yard people who had dealings with him before. He went by the name of Cocoa Tom among his intimates; but his real name was Thomas Pailton. He’d been convicted a couple of times, though not recently.”
===Chinese===
第二天早上，阿玛代尔探长（Inspector Armadale）到郡警察局长（Chief Constable）办公室报到，发现克林顿爵士（Sir Clinton）依然恪守他提出的那套方针——把本案的事实尽数凑拢共享。

“我方才跟验尸官（coroner）通了话，”克林顿爵士解释道。“我向他指出，福斯（Foss）的验尸审讯，我们或许还能拿出新的证据；我建议他尽量只走走过场，随后休会一两天。这就意味着马登（Marden）和司机还得在此地多留些时日；不过他们可以住在雷文索普（Ravensthorpe）。蔡斯沃特小姐对此并无异议。我一开口问她，她当场就应允了。”

“陪审团面前的材料，已足够作出『凶手不明的谋杀』裁决了，”探长指出。“案子正办到半截，您倒想把它弄得更明确些？”

克林顿爵士不置可否地一扬手，答道：

“至少给咱们自己留个机会，给罪犯安上个名字。成不了，也无伤大雅。再说另一件事。苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）来了电话。他们的档案里，没有一份指纹能跟马登或司机的对上号。福斯却是个来路不正的家伙。他们认出了他的指纹；他的照片，苏格兰场里几个从前跟他打过交道的人，一眼就认了出来。在亲近人中间，他用的诨名是可可·汤姆（Cocoa Tom）；本名却是托马斯·佩尔顿（Thomas Pailton）。他被判过两回罪，不过不是近些年的事。”

===Original===
“What was his line?” the Inspector inquired.

“Confidence trick in one form or another, they say. Very plausible tongue, apparently.”

“Did they say anything more about him?” asked the Inspector. “Anything about working with a gang usually, or something like that? If he did, then we might get a clue or two from his associates.”

“He usually played a lone hand, it seems,” Sir Clinton answered. “Apparently he used to be on the Halls—the cheaper kind. ‘The Wonderful Wizard of Woz’ he called himself then. But somehow they made the business too hot for him and he cleared out into swindling.”
===Chinese===
“他是吃哪碗饭的？”探长问。

“说是无非各式骗术，换着花样来。瞧样子，那条舌头挺能把人说得信以为真。”

“他们还说了他点别的没有？”探长问。“比方说，他平时跟不跟帮派合伙，这一类的？若是合伙，咱们兴许能从他那些同伙身上摸出一两条线索。”

“看样子他惯常单干，”克林顿爵士答道。“显然他从前是在杂耍剧场里混饭的——还是不入流的那种场子。那时他自称『沃兹国的奇妙巫师』（The Wonderful Wizard of Woz）。可不知怎么，那一行容不下他了，他便抽脚溜走，改行行骗去了。”

===Original===
“Ah!” Armadale evidently saw something which had not occurred to him before. “Those pockets of his—the ones that puzzled me. They might have been useful to a man who could do a bit of sleight-of-hand. I never thought of that at the time.”

He looked accusingly at Sir Clinton, who laughed at the expression in the Inspector’s eyes.

“Of course I admit I saw the use of the pockets almost at once,” he said. “But that’s not a breach of our bargain, Inspector. The facts are all that we are pooling, remember; and the fact that Foss had these peculiar pockets was as well known to you as to myself. This notion about sleight-of-hand is an interpretation of the facts, remember; and we weren’t to share our inferences.”

“I knew pretty well at the time that you’d spotted something,” Armadale contented himself with saying. “But since you put it in that way I’ll admit you were quite justified in keeping it to yourself as special information, sir. I take it that it’s a race between us now; and the one that hits on the solution first is the winner. I don’t mind.”
===Chinese===
“啊！”阿玛代尔显然想到了一桩先前没想到的事。“他那几个口袋——就是叫我犯嘀咕的那几个。对一个会点儿手上戏法的人来说，它们兴许大有用处。当时我可没想到这一层。”

他带着责备的神气望着克林顿爵士；爵士瞧着探长眼里那副神情，笑了。

“当然，我承认那几个口袋的用处，我几乎一眼就看出来了，”他说。“可这不算毁咱们的约，探长。记住，咱们凑拢共享的只是事实；福斯有这么几个古怪口袋，这一事实你我知晓得一样清楚。而这手彩的主意，记住，是对事实的一种解读；咱们可说定了不交流各自的推断。”

“当时我就差不多料定，您瞧出了什么，”阿玛代尔只拿这一句自安自慰。“不过您既然这么讲，我就承认，您把它当作独家情报捂着，完全在理，先生。我理会，如今咱们俩是赛跑了；谁先想到答案谁赢。我没意见。”

===Original===
“Then there’s one other bit of information needed to bring us level. I’ve just had a message over the phone from Mr. Cecil Chacewater. It appears he’s just got home again; came by the first train in the morning from town, apparently. He’s waiting for us now, so we’d better go up to Ravensthorpe. I have an idea that he may be able to throw some light on his brother’s disappearance. At least he may be able to show us how that disappearing trick was done; and that would always be a step forward.”
===Chinese===
“不过还得添上一条消息，咱们才算打个平手。我刚刚接到塞西尔·蔡斯沃特（Cecil Chacewater）先生的电话。看样子他刚回到家里；显然是今早搭头班火车从城里回来的。他这会儿正等着咱们，所以咱们最好上雷文索普去一趟。我有个想法：他弟弟失踪的事，他也许能照出几分光亮来。至少，那套失踪的戏法是怎么变的，他兴许能演示给咱们看；那总归是往前迈了一步。”

===Original===
When they reached Ravensthorpe Cecil was awaiting them. The inspector noticed that he seemed tired and had a weary look in his eyes.

“Been out on the spree,” was Armadale’s silent inference; for the Inspector was inclined to take a low view of humanity in general, and he put his own interpretation on Cecil’s looks.

Sir Clinton, in a few rapid sentences, apprised Cecil of the facts of the case.

“I’d heard some of that before, you know,” Cecil admitted. “Maurice’s disappearance seems to have caused a bit of a stir. I can’t say he’s greatly missed for the sake of his personality; but naturally it’s disturbing to have a brother mislaid about the place.”

“Very irksome, of course,” agreed Sir Clinton, with a faint parody of Cecil’s detached air.
===Chinese===
他们到达雷文索普时，塞西尔已在那里等候。探长注意到，他像是乏了，眼里带着倦色。

“外头寻欢作乐去了，”这是阿玛代尔心里的无声推断；原来这位探长对芸芸众生向来看得低，塞西尔这副形容，他便自行给了一套解说。

克林顿爵士三言两语，把案情的种种事实向塞西尔交代了一遍。

“这些话有的我先前就听说了，你是知道的，”塞西尔承认。“莫里斯（Maurice）失踪这事，似乎掀起了一点波澜。论他那个人本身，可算不上多么叫人想念；可自家的兄弟在这宅子里下落不明，自然叫人心神不定。”

“那自然够烦心的，”克林顿爵士应道，淡淡地模仿了一把塞西尔那副置身事外的派头。

===Original===
Cecil seemed to think that the conversation had come to a deadlock, since the Chief Constable made no effort to continue.

“Well, what about it?” he demanded. “I haven’t got Maurice concealed anywhere about my person, you know.”

He elaborately felt in an empty jacket pocket, ending by turning it inside out.

“No,” he pointed out, “he isn’t there. In fact, I’m almost certain I haven’t got him anywhere in this suit.”

Cecil’s studied insolence seemed to escape Sir Clinton’s notice.
===Chinese===
塞西尔似乎觉得谈话已经陷入僵局，因为郡警察局长没有接着往下说的意思。

“那么，怎么样呢？”他诘问道。“要知道，我可没把莫里斯藏在我身上哪个角落。”

他煞有介事地往一只空上衣口袋里摸索了一气，末了索性把口袋翻了出来。

“喏，”他指出，“他不在这儿。说实话，我几乎可以肯定，这一身衣裳里哪儿都没藏着他。”

塞西尔这番刻意装出来的无礼，克林顿爵士似乎全然不曾留意。

===Original===
“There was a celebrated historical character who said something of the same sort once upon a time. ‘Am I my brother’s keeper?’ you remember that?”

“Good old Cain? So he did. And his name begins with a *C*, just like mine, too! Any other points of resemblance you’d like to suggest?”

“Not just now,” Sir Clinton responded. “Information would be more to the purpose at present. Let’s go along to the museum, please. There are one or two points which need to be cleared up as soon as possible.”
===Chinese===
“从前有位鼎鼎大名的古人，说过一句差不多的话。『我是看守我兄弟的吗？』记得这一句吧？”

“老伙计该隐？他还真说过。而且他名字也是 C 打头，跟我的一样！您还打算指出哪几点相似之处么？”

“眼下不必，”克林顿爵士答道。“目前更要紧的是问情况。请随我们上博物馆去吧。有一两处疑点，得尽快弄清楚。”

===Original===
Cecil made no open demur; but his manner continued to be obviously hostile as they made their way along the passages. At the museum door the constable on guard stood aside in order to let them pass in.

“Wait a moment,” Sir Clinton ordered, as his companions were about to enter the room. “I want to try an experiment before we go any further.”

He turned to Cecil.

“Will you go across and stand in front of the case in which the Muramasa sword used to be kept? You’ll find the sheath still in the case. And you, Inspector, go to the spot where we found Foss’s body.”

When they had obeyed him he swung the door round on its hinges until it was almost closed, and then looked through the remaining opening.
===Chinese===
塞西尔没有公然表示异议；但一路沿着过道走去，他的做派依旧明摆着充满敌意。博物馆门口，守卫的警士侧身让开，放他们进去。

“等一等，”同伴们正要进屋，克林顿爵士下令道。“再往里走之前，我想做个试验。”

他转向塞西尔。

“请你走过去，站在原先陈列村正（Muramasa）刀的那口柜子前头。刀鞘还在柜子里。探长，你呢，到我们发现福斯尸首的那块地方去。”

两人依言照办后，他把门绕着合页推过去，推到几乎关拢，再从留下的一道缝往里张望。

===Original===
“Say a few words in an ordinary tone, Inspector. A string of addresses or something of that sort.”

“William Jones, Park Place, Amersley Royal,” began the Inspector, obediently; “Henry Blenkinsop, 18 Skeening Road, Hinchley; John Orran Gordon, 88 Bolsover Lane …”

“That will be enough, thanks. I can hear you quite well. Now lower your voice a trifle and say ‘Muramasa,’ ‘Japanese,’ and ‘sword,’ please. And mix them into the middle of some more addresses.”

The Inspector’s tone as he spoke showed plainly that he was a trifle bewildered by his instructions.

“Fred Hall, Muramasa, Endelmere; Harry Bell, 15 Elm Japanese Avenue, Stonyton; J. Hicky, sword, The Cottage, Apperley … Will that do?”

“Quite well, Inspector. Many thanks. Think I’m mad? All I wanted was to find out how much a man in this position could see and hear. Contributions to the pool. First, I can see the case where the Muramasa sword used to lie. Second, I can hear quite plainly what you’re saying. The slight echo in the room doesn’t hinder that.”
===Chinese===
“用平常的声调说几句话，探长。念一串地址，或者诸如此类的。”

“威廉·琼斯，帕克坊，皇家阿默斯利（Amersley Royal），”探长依言开了口；“亨利·布伦基索普，斯基宁路 18 号，欣奇利；约翰·奥兰·戈登，博尔索弗巷 88 号……”

“够了，多谢。我听得相当清楚。现在把嗓子压低一点儿，请说『村正』『日本』『刀』。把它们掺在另外几条地址中间说。”

探长念的时候，语气分明透着几分茫然，叫这些吩咐闹的。

“弗雷德·霍尔，村正，恩德尔米尔；哈里·贝尔，榆树日本大道 15 号，斯托尼顿；J. 希基，刀，村舍，阿珀利……这样行么？”

“满好，探长。多谢。当我发疯了？我只想弄明白，一个人站在这个位置，能看见多少、听见多少。给凑份子添点进项。第一，原先搁村正刀的那口柜子，我望得见。第二，你说的话，我听得一清二楚。屋里那点轻微的回声，碍不着这个。”

===Original===
He swung the door open and came into the museum.

“Now, Cecil,” he said—and the Inspector noticed that all sign of lightness had gone out of his tone, “you know that Maurice disappeared rather mysteriously from this room? He was in it with Foss; there was a man at the door; Foss was murdered in that bay over there; and Maurice didn’t leave the room by the door. How did he leave?”

“How should I know?” demanded Cecil, sullenly. “You’d better ask him when he turns up again. I’m not Maurice’s nursemaid.”

Sir Clinton’s eyes grew hard.

“I’ll put it plainer for you. I’ve reason to believe that there’s an entrance to a secret passage somewhere in that bay beyond the safe. It’s the only way in which Maurice could have left this room. You’ll have to show it to us.”
===Chinese===
他把门推开，走进了博物馆。

“听着，塞西尔，”他说——探长留意到，他话音里那点轻松劲儿已经荡然无存——“你知道莫里斯是从这间屋里相当蹊跷地失踪了的吧？当时他和福斯同在屋里；门口守着一个人；福斯在那边的凹室里被害了；而莫里斯没有从房门出去。他是怎么出去的？”

“我怎么会知道？”塞西尔沉下脸诘问。“等他再露面，您顶好去问他本人。我又不是莫里斯的保姆。”

克林顿爵士的目光变得冷硬。

“我把话给你挑明了吧。我有理由相信，保险柜过去那边的凹室里，某个地方有一条暗道的入口。这是莫里斯离开这间屋子的唯一途径。你得把它指给我们看。”

===Original===
“Indeed!” Cecil’s voice betrayed nothing but contempt for the suggestion.

“It’s for your own benefit that I make the proposal,” Sir Clinton pointed out. “Refuse if you like. But if you do I’ve a search-warrant in my pocket and I mean to find that entrance even if I have to root out most of the panelling and gut the room. You won’t avert the discovery by this attitude of yours. You’ll merely make the whole business public. It would be far more sensible to recognize the inevitable and show us the place yourself. I don’t want to damage things any more than is necessary. But if I’m put to it I’ll be thorough, I warn you.”

Cecil favoured the Chief Constable with an angry look; but the expression on Sir Clinton’s face convinced him that it was useless to offer any further opposition.

“Very well,” he snarled. “I’ll open the thing, since I must.”
===Chinese===
“是嘛！”对这提议，塞西尔的嗓音里只透着轻蔑。

“我提这个，是为你自己着想，”克林顿爵士指出。“拒绝也随你。可你若拒绝，我兜里就揣着搜查证，哪怕撬掉大半镶板、把这屋子掏个底朝天，我也非要找到那入口不可。你摆出这副态度，也挡不住人家发现。你只会把整桩事情闹得尽人皆知。识时务，自己把地方指给我们看，才算明智得多。我不想多毁坏东西，能不动的就不动。可真要逼我下手，我会做得干净彻底，这可是先提醒你。”

塞西尔朝郡警察局长投去一记怒目；但克林顿爵士脸上的神气叫他确信，再作抗拒也是枉然。

“很好，”他从牙缝里挤出一句。“既然非开不可，我就开。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton took no notice of his anger.

“So long as you open it the rest doesn’t matter. I’ve no desire to pry into things that don’t concern me. I don’t wish to know how the panel opens. Inspector, I think we’ll turn our backs while Mr. Chacewater works the mechanism.”

They faced about. Cecil took a few steps into the bay. There was a sharp snap; and when they turned round again a door gaped in the panelling at the end of the room.

“Quite so,” said Sir Clinton. “Most ingenious.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士没理会他的火气。

“只要你把它打开，其余的都不打紧。与我无关的事，我没有打探的兴致。这镶板怎么开，我不想知道。探长，蔡斯沃特先生操纵机关的时候，咱们还是背过身去吧。”

两人转过身去。塞西尔向凹室里走了几步。只听咔哒一声脆响；待他们再回过身，屋子尽头的镶板上已豁然张开一扇门。

“正是，”克林顿爵士说。“真是精巧之至。”

===Original===
His voice had regained its normal easy tone; and now he seemed anxious to smooth over the ill-feeling which had come to so acute a pitch in the last few minutes.

“Will you go first, Cecil, and show us the way? I expect it’s difficult for a stranger. I’ve brought an electric torch. Here, you’d better take it.”

Now that he had failed in his attempt, Cecil seemed to recover his temper again. He took the torch from the Chief Constable and, pressing the spring to light it, stepped through the open panel.

“I think we’ll lock the museum door before we go down,” Sir Clinton suggested. “There’s no need to expose this entrance to anyone who happens to come in.”

He walked across the museum, turned the key in the lock, and then rejoined his companions.

“Now, Cecil, if you please.”
===Chinese===
他的嗓音已恢复了平日那副从容的调子；方才几分钟里恶感陡然升到剑拔弩张的地步，这会儿他似乎急于把它弥缝过去。

“塞西尔，请你头前带路，好不好？生人走这条道，怕是不容易。我带了一支手电筒。来，你拿着吧。”

抵抗既然失败，塞西尔的火气倒像是平复了下来。他从郡警察局长手里接过手电，按下弹簧点亮，跨过了敞开的暗板。

“下去之前，咱们还是把博物馆的门锁上吧，”克林顿爵士提议。“这个入口，犯不着叫偶然进来的人瞧见。”

他穿过博物馆，在锁眼里拧转钥匙锁了门，然后回到同伴身边。

“那么，塞西尔，请吧。”

===Original===
Cecil Chacewater led the way; Sir Clinton motioned to the Inspector to follow him, and brought up the rear himself.

“Look out, here,” Cecil warned them. “There’s a flight of steps almost at once.”

They made their way down a spiral staircase which seemed to lead deep into the foundations of Ravensthorpe. At last it came to an end, and a narrow tunnel gaped before them.

“Nothing here, you see,” Cecil pointed out, flashing the torch in various directions. “This passage is the only outlet.”
===Chinese===
塞西尔·蔡斯沃特头前引路；克林顿爵士示意探长跟着走，自己殿后。

“这儿留神，”塞西尔提醒他们。“紧跟着就是一段台阶。”

他们顺着一道螺旋楼梯往下走，楼梯似乎直通雷文索普地基的深处。最后楼梯到了头，一条窄地道在眼前张开大口。

“这儿什么也没有，你们瞧，”塞西尔一面把手电朝各个方向晃照，一面指出。“这条道是唯一的出口。”

===Original===
He led the way into the tunnel, followed by the Inspector. Sir Clinton lagged behind them for a moment or two, and then showed no signs of haste, so that they had to pause in order to let him catch up.

The tunnel led them in a straight line for a time, then bent in a fresh direction.

“It’s getting narrower,” the Inspector pointed out.

“It gets narrower still before you’re done with it,” Cecil vouchsafed in reply.

As the passage turned again Sir Clinton halted.

“I’d like to have a look at these walls,” he said.
===Chinese===
他领头进了地道，探长跟上。克林顿爵士落后了一两步，随后也不露一点赶急的意思，两人只得停下来等他跟上。

有一段路地道笔直，随后拐了个新方向。

“越来越窄了，”探长指出。

“没走完之前，还要更窄呢，”塞西尔屈尊赏了一句回答。

地道又拐弯时，克林顿爵士停住了。

“我想瞧瞧这几面墙，”他说。

===Original===
Cecil turned back and threw the light of the torch over the sides and roof of the tunnel.

“It’s very old masonry,” he pointed out.

Sir Clinton nodded.

“This is a bit of old Ravensthorpe, I suppose?”

“It’s older than the modern parts of the building,” Cecil agreed. He seemed to have overcome his ill-humour and to be making the best of things.

“Let’s push on, then,” Sir Clinton suggested. “I’ve seen all I wanted to see, thanks.”
===Chinese===
塞西尔转回身，把手电的光投到地道的两壁和顶上。

“很古的砖石，”他指出。

克林顿爵士点头。

“这就是老雷文索普的一角了吧，我猜？”

“比宅子里那些新式的部分还要老，”塞西尔附和。他那股坏脾气像是消了，这会儿正既来之则安之。

“那就接着走吧，”克林顿爵士提议。“该看的我都看过了，多谢。”

===Original===
As they proceeded, the tunnel walls drew nearer together and the roof grew lower. Before long the passage was barely large enough to let them walk along it without brushing the stones on either side.

“Wait a moment,” Sir Clinton suggested, as they reached a fresh turning. “Inspector, would you mind making a rough measurement of the dimensions here?”

Somewhat mystified, Inspector Armadale did as he was bidden, entering the figures up in his notebook while Cecil stood back, evidently equally puzzled by these manoeuvres.

“Thanks, that will do nicely,” Sir Clinton assured him when the task had been completed. “Suppose we continue?”
===Chinese===
一路前行，地道两壁越凑越近，顶也越压越低。不久，通道已窄得勉强容他们走过，稍不留神就要蹭上两侧的石头。

“等一等，”又到了一个新拐弯处，克林顿爵士提议。“探长，麻烦你把这里的尺寸大略量一量？”

阿玛代尔探长有几分发懵，仍旧依言照办，一面把数目记进笔记本；塞西尔退在一旁，对这套调度显然同样犯疑。

“多谢，这就很好，”量完之后，克林顿爵士向他保证。“咱们继续走？”

===Original===
Cecil advanced a few steps. Then a thought seemed to strike him.

“It gets narrower farther on. We’ll have to go on hands and knees, and there won’t be room to pass one another. Perhaps one of you should go first with the torch. There’s nothing in the road.”

Sir Clinton agreed to this.

“I’ll go first, then. You can follow on, Inspector.”

Inspector Armadale looked suspicious at this suggestion.

“He might get away back and shut us in,” he murmured in Sir Clinton’s ear.
===Chinese===
塞西尔往前走了几步。忽然像是想起什么。

“前头更窄。咱们只好手脚并用地爬，而且腾不出彼此错身的空儿。兴许该哪位拿着手电走前头。路上没别的东西。”

克林顿爵士表示同意。

“那我走前头。探长，你随后来。”

听了这话，阿玛代尔探长露出疑忌之色。

“他兴许会退回去，把咱们关死在里头，”他凑在克林顿爵士耳边低声道。

===Original===
The Chief Constable took the simplest way of reassuring the Inspector.

“That’s an ingenious bit of mechanism in the panel, up above,” he said to Cecil. “I had a glance at it as I passed, since it’s all in plain sight. From this side, you’ve only to lift a bar to open it, haven’t you?”

“That’s so,” Cecil confirmed.

Armadale was evidently satisfied by the information which Sir Clinton had thus conveyed to him indirectly. He squeezed himself against the wall and allowed the Chief Constable to come up to the head of the party. Sir Clinton threw his light down the passage in front of them.

“It looks like all-fours, now,” he commented, as the lamp revealed a steadily diminishing tunnel. “We may as well begin now and save ourselves the chance of knocking our heads against the roof.”
===Chinese===
郡警察局长用了最省事的一法，打消探长的疑虑。

“上头那块暗板里的机关，真是件精巧的物件，”他对塞西尔说。“我经过时瞟过一眼——反正一抬眼就尽收眼底。从这一边，只要抬起一根横杆就能开门，是不是？”

“正是，”塞西尔证实。

克林顿爵士这样拐着弯递给他的消息，显然叫阿玛代尔放了心。他把身子紧贴墙根，让郡警察局长走上队首。克林顿爵士把灯光朝前面的通道投下去。

“看来这会儿该手脚并用了，”灯光照出一条不断收窄的地道，他这般评论。“索性这就趴下吧，省得回头一不留神拿脑袋撞上洞顶。”

===Original===
Suiting the action to the word, he got down on hands and knees and began to creep along the passage.

“At least we may be thankful it’s dry,” he pointed out.

The tunnel grew still smaller until they found more than a little difficulty in making their way along it.

“Have we much farther to go?” asked the Inspector, who seemed to have little liking for the business.

“The end’s round the next corner,” Cecil explained.
===Chinese===
说着便做，他手脚伏地，开始沿着通道往前爬。

“至少有一点值得庆幸，这儿是干的，”他指出。

地道缩得愈发小了，到最后他们往前挪一步都得费不小的劲。

“还有多远？”探长问，他似乎对这差事没多大兴味。

“拐过下一个弯就到头了，”塞西尔解释。

===Original===
They soon reached the last bend in the passage, and as he turned it Sir Clinton found himself at the entrance to a tiny space. The roof was even lower than that of the tunnel, and the floor area was hardly more than a dozen square feet. A stone slab, raised a few inches from the ground, seemed like a bed fitted into a niche.

“A bit wet in this part,” Sir Clinton remarked. “If I’d known that we were in for this sort of thing I think I’d have put on an old suit this morning. Mind your knees on the floor, Inspector. It’s fairly moist.”

He climbed into the niche, which was no bigger than the bunk of a steamer, and began to examine his surroundings with his torch. Inspector Armadale, taking advantage of the space thus made clear, crept into the tiny chamber.

“This place looks as if it had been washed out, lately,” he said, examining the smooth flagstones which formed the floor. He turned his attention to the roof, evidently in search of dripping water; but he could find none, though the walls were moist.
===Chinese===
他们不久便到了通道最后一道弯，克林顿爵士一转过去，就发现自己站在一个小小空间的入口处。顶比地道的还低，地面拢共不过十来平方英尺。一块石板离地垫起几英寸，像嵌在壁龛里的一张床。

“这一段有点儿潮，”克林顿爵士说。“早知道要受这份罪，今早我就换一身旧衣裳了。探长，膝盖当心，地上相当湿。”

他爬进那个壁龛——大不过轮船上的一个铺位——便用手电四下察看起来。阿玛代尔探长趁着腾出来的空当，爬进了这间小室。

“这地方瞧着像是不久前冲洗过，”他说，一面端详铺作地面的平整石板。他又转而留意洞顶，显然想找滴水的痕迹；可墙壁虽潮，滴水却一滴也寻不见。

===Original===
Suddenly Sir Clinton bent forward and brought his lamp near something on the side of the niche.

The Inspector, seeing something in the patch of light, craned forward to look also, and as he did so he seemed to recognize what he saw.

“Why, that’s …” he ejaculated.
===Chinese===
忽然，克林顿爵士俯下身，把手电凑近壁龛侧壁上的什么东西。

探长看见那片光斑里有个东西，也探过身去同看；看着看着，他像是认出了那是什么。

“咦，那是……”他失声喊道。

===Original===
Sir Clinton’s lamp went out abruptly, and Inspector Armadale felt his arm gripped warningly in the darkness.

“Sorry,” the Chief Constable apologized. “My finger must have shifted the switch on the torch. Out of the way, Inspector, please. There’s nothing more to be seen here.”

Inspector Armadale wriggled back into the passage again as Sir Clinton made a movement as though to come out of his perch in the recess.

“So this is where Maurice got to when he left the museum?” the Chief Constable said, reflectively. “Well, he isn’t here now, that’s plain. We’ll need to look elsewhere, Inspector, according to your scheme. If he wasn’t elsewhere he was to be here. But as he isn’t here he’s obviously elsewhere. And now I think we’ll make our way up to the museum again. Wait a moment! We’ve got to get back into that passage with our heads in the right direction. Once we’re into the tunnel there won’t be room to turn round.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士的灯骤然熄了；黑暗里，阿玛代尔探长觉得自己的手臂被人一把攥住，那是警告。

“对不住，”郡警察局长致歉。“准是我的指头蹭着了电筒的开关。劳驾让让路，探长。这儿再没什么可看的了。”

克林顿爵士在凹龛里那个位置上做出个要出来的动静，阿玛代尔探长便蠕动着又退回通道里去。

“这么说，莫里斯离开博物馆后，落脚的就是这儿？”郡警察局长若有所思地说。“唔，他如今不在这儿，这明摆着。按你的方案，探长，咱们得往别处去找。他若不在别处，本该在这儿。可他既然不在这儿，显然便在别处。好了，我想咱们这就回博物馆去吧。慢着！得先把脑袋掉对了方向，再退回那条通道去。一旦进了地道，可就没有转身的余地了。”

===Original===
It took some manoeuvring to arrange this, for the tiny chamber was a tight fit for even three men; but at last they succeeded in getting back into the tunnel in a position which permitted them to creep forwards instead of backwards. They finally accomplished the long journey without incident, and emerged through the gaping panel into the museum once more.

“Now we’ll turn our backs again, Inspector, and let Mr. Chacewater close the panel.”

Again the sharp click notified them that they could turn round. The panelling seemed completely solid.
===Chinese===
这一番调度颇费了些手脚，因为那间小室里挤三个人都嫌转不开；但末了他们总算回到了地道里，摆好了姿势，能朝前爬而不必倒退。这段漫长的归途，他们终于有惊无险地走完，从洞开的暗板里重新钻进了博物馆。

“现在咱们再背过身去，探长，让蔡斯沃特先生关上暗板。”

又是咔哒一声脆响，通知他们可以回头了。那面镶板看上去严丝合缝，浑然无痕。

===Original===
“There are just a couple of points I’d like to know about,” Sir Clinton said, turning to Cecil. “You don’t know the combination that opens the safe over there, I believe?”

Cecil Chacewater seemed both surprised and relieved to hear this question.

“No,” he said. “Maurice kept the combination to himself.”

Sir Clinton nodded as though he had expected this answer.

“Just another point,” he continued. “You may not be able to remember this. At any time after you and Foxton Polegate had planned that practical joke of yours, did Foss ask you the time?”
===Chinese===
“正有两处我想问个明白，”克林顿爵士说着，转向塞西尔。“那边那口保险柜的开柜密码，你并不知道，我没猜错吧？”

听了这一问，塞西尔·蔡斯沃特似乎又惊讶，又松了口气。

“不知道，”他说。“开柜的密码，莫里斯从来独揽着，不告诉人。”

克林顿爵士点了点头，像是早料到有此一答。

“还有一点，”他继续说。“这个你或许记不得了。你同福克西·波尔盖特（Foxy Polegate）定下你们那个恶作剧之后，福斯可曾问过你时刻？”

===Original===
Cecil was obviously completely taken aback by this query.

“Did he ask me the time? Not that I know of. I can’t remember his ever doing that. Wait a bit, though. No, he didn’t.”

Sir Clinton seemed disappointed for a moment. Then, evidently, a fresh idea occurred to him.

“On the night of the masked ball, did anyone ask you the time?”
===Chinese===
这一问，显然把塞西尔打了个措手不及。

“他问我时刻？我不记得有这回事。他不曾这么问过，我记得。不过等一等。不，他没有。”

克林顿爵士似乎失望了片刻。随即，显然又一个新念头冒了出来。

“假面舞会那晚，可有什么人问过你时刻？”

===Original===
Cecil considered for a moment or two.

“Now I come to think of it, a fellow dressed as a cowboy came up and said his watch had stopped.”

“Ah! I thought so,” was all Sir Clinton replied, much to the vexation of Inspector Armadale.
===Chinese===
塞西尔寻思了片刻。

“这么说起来，倒有个穿牛仔装的家伙凑上来，说他的表停了。”

“啊！我就料是这么着，”克林顿爵士只回了这一句，叫阿玛代尔探长好不气闷。

===Original===
“By the way,” the Chief Constable went on, “I’d rather like to get to the top of one of those turrets up above.” He made a gesture indicating the roof. “There’s a stair, isn’t there?”

Armadale had difficulty in concealing his surprise at this unexpected demand. Cecil Chacewater made no difficulties, but led them upstairs and opened the door of the entrance to a turret. When they reached an open space at the summit, Sir Clinton leaned on the parapet and gazed over the surrounding country with interest. As the space was restricted, Cecil remained within the turret, at the top of the stair; but the Inspector joined his Chief on the platform.

“Splendid view, isn’t it, Inspector?”

“Yes, sir. Very fine.”

Armadale was evidently puzzled by this turn of affairs. He could not see why Sir Clinton should have come up to admire the view instead of getting on with the investigation. The Chief Constable did not seem to notice his subordinate’s perplexity.

“There’s Hincheldene,” Sir Clinton pointed out. “With a decent pair of glasses one could read the time on the clock-tower on a clear day. These woods round about give a restful look to things. Soothing, that greenery. Ah! Just follow my finger, Inspector. See that white thing over yonder? That’s one of these Fairy Houses.”
===Chinese===
“对了，”郡警察局长接着说，“我倒挺想到上头哪座角楼的顶上去一趟。”他做了个手势，指指屋顶。“有一道楼梯上去，是吧？”

这没来由的要求，叫阿玛代尔好不容易才藏住惊讶。塞西尔·蔡斯沃特倒不作难，领他们上了楼，打开了通往一座角楼的门。到了顶上一方露天的空处，克林顿爵士便倚着矮墙，兴致盎然地眺望四野风光。平台上地盘有限，塞西尔便留在角楼里、楼梯顶端；探长却上去与他的上司同站一处。

“好一片景致，是不是，探长？”

“是的，先生。非常好。”

事态这一转，显然叫阿玛代尔摸不着头脑。他不明白，克林顿爵士为什么不上紧查案，倒跑上来赏景。郡警察局长似乎没有留意下属的困惑。

“那是欣切尔迪恩（Hincheldene），”克林顿爵士指出。“有一副像样的望远镜，晴天上连钟楼上的时刻都认得出。四下这一圈树林，给景物添了几分幽静。那一片绿，养眼安神。啊！就顺着我的手指看，探长。瞧见那边那个白色的物件没有？那就是仙女屋（Fairy Houses）中的一座。”

===Original===
He searched here and there in the landscape for a moment.

“There’s another of them, just where you see that stream running across the opening between the two spinneys—yonder. And there’s a third one, not far off that ruined tower. See it? I wonder if we could pick up any more. They seem to be thick enough on the ground. Yes, see that one in the glade over there? Not see it? Look at that grey cottage with the creeper on it; two o’clock; three fingers. See it now?”

“I can’t quite make it out, sir,” the Inspector confessed.

He seemed bored by Sir Clinton’s insistence on the matter; but he held up his hand and tried to discover the object. After a moment or two he gave up the attempt and, turning round, he noticed his Chief slipping a small compass into his pocket.

“Quite worth seeing, that view,” Sir Clinton remarked, imperturbably, as he made his way towards the turret stair. “Thanks very much, Cecil. I don’t think we need trouble you any more for the present; but I’d like to see your sister, if she’s available. I want to ask her a question.”
===Chinese===
他又在四野间这里那里搜寻了片刻。

“那边又是一座，就在你瞧见那条溪水横穿两片小树林之间豁口的地方——喏。还有第三座，离那座倾颓的塔不远。瞧见没有？不知咱们还能不能再寻出几座来。瞧这地面上，它们够稠密的。对了，瞧见那边林中空地上的那一座没有？没瞧见？看那座爬满藤蔓的灰色村舍；两点钟方向；伸出三根手指。这下瞧见了？”

“我认不大真切，先生，”探长招认。

克林顿爵士在这事上这般执着，他似乎有些意兴阑珊；但他还是举起手，尽力去找那个目标。过了一两分钟，他放弃了这番努力，一转身，却瞧见他的上司正把一只小罗盘塞进衣袋。

“那片景致，确然值得一看，”克林顿爵士一边朝角楼楼梯走去，一边不动声色地说。“多谢了，塞西尔。眼下我想不必再劳动你；不过我想见见你妹妹，她要是有空。我有话要问她。”

===Original===
Cecil Chacewater went in search of Joan, and after a few minutes she met them at the foot of the stair.

“There’s just one point that occurred to me since you told us about that interview you and Maurice had with Foss before you went to the museum. You were sitting on the terrace, weren’t you?”

“Yes,” Joan confirmed.

“Then you must have seen Foss’s car drive up when it came to wait at the front door for him?”

“I remember seeing it come up just before we went to the museum. I didn’t say anything about it before. It didn’t seem to matter much.”

“That was quite natural,” Sir Clinton reassured her. “In fact, I’m not sure that it matters much even yet. I’m just trying for any evidence I can get. Tell me anything whatever that you noticed, no matter whether it seems important or not.”
===Chinese===
塞西尔·蔡斯沃特去找琼（Joan）了；几分钟后，她在楼梯脚下同他们碰了面。

“自从你给我们讲过、去博物馆前你和莫里斯同福斯那番会面之后，我想起了一点。你们当时坐在露台上，是不是？”

“是，”琼证实。

“那么，福斯那辆车开上来、在前门口候着他的情形，你一定看见了？”

“我记得临去博物馆之前，正看见它开上来。先前我没提起过。这事好像顶不了什么用。”

“那再自然不过，”克林顿爵士宽她的心。“说实话，就是到这会儿，我也说不准它要紧不要紧。我不过是凡能寻着的证据都要寻。不管看着要紧不要紧，凡是你留意到的，都请讲给我听。”

===Original===
Joan thought for almost a minute before replying.

“I did notice the chauffeur putting the hood up, and I wondered what on earth he was doing that for on a blazing day.”

“Anything else?”

“He had his tool-kit out and seemed to be going to do some repair or other.”

“At the moment when he’d brought the car round for Foss?” demanded the Inspector, rather incredulously. “Surely he’d have everything spick and span before he left the garage?”

“You’d better ask him about it, himself, Inspector,” said Joan, tartly. “I’m merely telling what I saw; and I saw that plain enough. Besides, he may have known he’d plenty of time. Mr. Foss was going away with us and obviously he wasn’t in a hurry to use the car.”
===Chinese===
琼想了将近一分钟才回答。

“我确实注意到司机在支车篷；我当时还纳闷，这么大晴天，他支篷究竟为的什么。”

“还有别的么？”

“他把工具箱取了出来，像是要做什么修理似的。”

“偏偏赶在他把车开出来给福斯使的时候？”探长问道，颇不敢信。“出了车库之前，他总该把样样都收拾得妥妥帖帖了吧？”

“这件事您顶好去问他本人，探长，”琼尖刻地说。“我不过讲我看见的；这一层我看得够清楚了。再说，他兴许知道时间宽裕得很。福斯先生是要同我们一起出门的，明摆着不急等着用车。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton ignored the Inspector’s interruption.

“I’ve got my own car at the door,” he observed. “Perhaps you could go out on to the terrace and direct me while I bring it into the same position as you saw Foss’s car that afternoon.”

Joan agreed; and they went down together.

“Now,” said Sir Clinton as he started the engine, “would you mind directing me?”

Joan, from the terrace, indicated how he was to manoeuvre until he had brought his own car into a position as near as possible to that occupied by Foss’s car on the afternoon of the murder.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士没有理会探长的插话。

“我自己的车就在门口，”他说。“或许你可以到露台上去，趁我把车挪到你那天下午瞧见福斯那辆车所停的位置时，给我指划。”

琼应承了；两人一同下去。

“好了，”克林顿爵士发动引擎时说，“有劳你指划指划？”

琼在露台上比画着，指点他如何挪转，直到他把自己的车摆到尽可能接近凶案当天下午福斯那辆车所停的位置。

===Original===
“That’s as near as I can get it,” she said at last.

Sir Clinton turned in his seat and scanned the front of Ravensthorpe.

“What window is this that I’m opposite?” he inquired.

“That’s the window of the museum,” Joan explained. “But you can’t see into the room, can you? You’re too low down there.”

“Nothing more than the tops of the cases,” Sir Clinton said. “You’d better get aboard, Inspector. There’s nothing more to do here.”
===Chinese===
“我只能对到这个份上了，”她末了说。

克林顿爵士在座位里转过身，端详雷文索普的正面。

“我正对着的是哪扇窗？”他问。

“那是博物馆的窗，”琼解释道。“可您瞧不进屋里去，对不对？您那位置太低了。”

“只瞧得见展柜的顶，”克林顿爵士说。“你上车吧，探长。这儿没事可干了。”

===Original===
He waved goodbye to Joan as Armadale stepped into the car, and then drove down the avenue. The Inspector said nothing until they had passed out of the Ravensthorpe grounds and were on the high road again. Then he turned eagerly to the Chief Constable.

“That was a splash of blood you found on the wall of the underground room, wasn’t it? I recognized it at once.”

“Don’t get excited about it, Inspector,” said Sir Clinton soothingly. “Of course it was blood; but we needn’t shout about it from the housetops, need we?”

Armadale thought he detected a tacit reproof for his exclamation at the time the discovery was made.

“You covered up that word or two of mine very neatly, sir,” he admitted frankly. “I was startled when I saw that spot of blood on the wall, and I nearly blurted it out. Silly of me to do it, I suppose. But you managed to smother it up with that bungling with your lamp before I’d given anything away. I’d no notion you wanted to keep the thing quiet.”
===Chinese===
阿玛代尔跨进车里，他向琼挥手作别，随即驱车驶下林荫道。出了雷文索普的地界、重新上了大路之前，探长一言不发。到了路上，他才急切地转向郡警察局长。

“您在地下那间屋子的墙上发现的，是一摊血，对吧？我当时一眼就认了出来。”

“这桩事就不必激动了，探长，”克林顿爵士宽慰道。“那当然是血；可咱们犯不着站到房顶上高声嚷嚷，对不对？”

阿玛代尔觉得，这话里藏着一句不点破的责备，责他发现当时那一声惊呼。

“我那一两句话，您遮掩得干净利落，先生，”他坦然承认。“瞧见墙上那摊血，我吃了一惊，差一点脱口嚷出来。我想，那样就蠢了。可我还没露半点口风，您就摆弄那盏灯装了一回笨拙，把事情捂了过去。我原先并不知道您打算把这事按住不说。”

===Original===
“No harm done,” Sir Clinton reassured him. “But be careful another time. One needn’t show all one’s cards.”

“You certainly don’t,” Armadale retorted.

“Well, you have all the facts, Inspector. What more do you expect?”

Armadale thought it best to change the subject.
===Chinese===
“无损大局，”克林顿爵士宽他的心。“不过下回留点儿神。手中的牌，用不着全亮出去。”

“您可确实不亮，”阿玛代尔回敬道。

“呃，事实你已经全有了，探长。你还指望些什么？”

阿玛代尔觉得，还是换个话题为妙。

===Original===
“That water that we saw down there,” he went on. “That never leaked in through the roof. The masonry overhead was as tight as a drum and there wasn’t a sign of drip-marks anywhere. That water came from somewhere else. Someone had been washing up in that cellar. There had been more blood there—lots of it; and they’d washed it away. That tiny patch was a bit they’d overlooked. Isn’t that so, sir?”

“That’s an inference and not a fact, Inspector,” Sir Clinton pointed out, with an expression approaching to a grin on his face. “I don’t say you’re wrong. In fact, I’m sure you’re right. But only facts are supposed to go into the common stock, remember.”

“Very good, sir.”

But the Inspector had something in reserve.
===Chinese===
“咱们在底下瞧见的那滩水，”他接着说。“那绝不是从顶上渗漏下来的。头顶的砖石严实得像一面鼓，哪儿也寻不见滴水的印子。那水是别处来的。有人在那间地窖里冲洗过。那儿原先还有血——多着呢；叫人冲洗净了。那一小片，是他们漏网的一处。是不是这么回事，先生？”

“这是一条推断，不是一条事实，探长，”克林顿爵士指出，脸上带着一副近乎咧嘴而笑的神情。“我并不是说你错了。说实话，我确信你对。可是别忘了，准许入公共份子的，只有事实。”

“是，先生。”

不过探长手里还存着一张牌。

===Original===
“I’ll give you a fact now,” he said with ill-suppressed triumph. “As you came away, you happened to ask Mr. Chacewater if he’d come by the first train this morning.”

“Yes.”

“And he said he did?”

“Yes.”

“Well,” said Armadale, with a tinge of derision in his voice, “he took you in, there; but he didn’t come over me with that tale. He didn’t come by the first train; he wasn’t in it! And what’s more, he didn’t come by train to our station at all, for I happened to make inquiries. I knew you were anxious for him to come back, and I thought I’d ask whether he’d come.”
===Chinese===
“这会儿我给你一条事实，”他说，那份得意劲儿怎么压也压不住。“临走的时候，您顺口问蔡斯沃特先生，是不是搭今早头班火车来的。”

“是。”

“他说他是？”

“是。”

“哼，”阿玛代尔说，话音里带着一丝讥诮，“在那上头，他把你蒙混过去了；可他那套话到我这儿行不通。他没搭头班火车；那趟车里压根儿没有他！更有甚者，他压根儿没坐火车来咱们这个车站，因为我恰好打听过。我知道您巴望他回来，便想着去问问，他到了没有。”

===Original===
“That’s very interesting,” said Sir Clinton.

He made no further remark until they reached the police station. Then, as they got out of the car, he turned to the Inspector.

“Care to see me do a little map-drawing, Inspector? It might amuse you.”
===Chinese===
“这可很有意思，”克林顿爵士说。

一路上到警察局，他再没多说什么。随后，两人下车时，他转向探长。

“想不想看我画一幅小地图，探长？兴许能给你解个闷。”

## XII Chuchundra’s Body / 第十二章 楚春德拉的尸体

===Original===
Sir Clinton’s map-drawing, however, was destined to be postponed. Hardly had they entered his office when the telephone bell rang. After a few moments’ conversation he put down the receiver and turned to Armadale.

“That’s Mold, the keeper. He’s found Maurice Chacewater’s body. He’s telephoning from his own cottage, so I told him to wait there and we’ll go up in the car. The body’s in the woods and we’ll save time by getting Mold to guide us to it instead of hunting round for the place.”
===Chinese===
然而，克林顿爵士（Sir Clinton）的绘图活计注定要往后推了。两人刚进他的办公室，电话铃就响了。交谈了几句，他放下听筒，转向阿玛代尔（Armadale）。

“是莫尔德（Mold），那个看管人。他发现了莫里斯·蔡斯沃特（Maurice Chacewater）的尸体。他是在自家小屋里打的电话，我叫他在那儿候着，咱们坐车上去。尸体在林子里，让莫尔德领咱们去，省得四下里摸索找地方，也省工夫。”

===Original===
It did not take long to reach the head keeper’s cottage, where they found Mold in a state of perturbation.

“Where is this body?” Sir Clinton demanded, cutting short Mold’s rather confused attempts to explain matters. “Take us to it first of all and then I’ll ask what I want to know.”
===Chinese===
不多时便到了看管长的小屋，只见莫尔德在那里心神不宁。

“尸体在哪儿？”克林顿爵士喝问，打断了莫尔德那番语无伦次的解释。“先带我们去验看，然后我要问什么再问。”

===Original===
Under the keeper’s guidance they made their way through the woods, and at last emerged into a small clearing in the centre of which rose a few ruined walls.

“This is what they call the Knight’s Tower,” Armadale explained.

Sir Clinton nodded.

“I expected something of the sort. Now, Mold, where’s Mr. Chacewater’s body?”

The keeper led them round the Tower, and as they turned the corner of a wall they came upon the body stretched at full length on the grass.
===Chinese===
在看管人引领下，他们穿林而行，末了走进一小片林中空地，空地正中矗立着几堵残垣断壁。

“这就是他们叫做骑士塔（Knight's Tower）的地方，”阿玛代尔解释道。

克林顿爵士点了点头。

“我料到就是这么个去处。好了，莫尔德，蔡斯沃特先生的尸体在哪儿？”

看管人领他们绕塔而行，转过一堵墙的墙角，他们便撞见了那具直挺挺躺在草地上的尸体。

===Original===
“The turf’s short,” said Armadale, with some disappointment. “There’s no track on it round about here.”

“That’s true,” said Sir Clinton. “We’ll have to do without that help.”

He walked over to where Maurice Chacewater was lying. The body was on its back; and a glance at the head was enough to show that life must be extinct.

“It’s not pretty,” Sir Clinton said as he pulled out his handkerchief and covered the dead face. “Shot at close range, evidently. I don’t wonder you were a bit upset, Mold.”
