===Chinese===
“这儿的草皮短，”阿玛代尔说，语气里带着几分失望。“周围地面上没有脚印。”

“不错，”克林顿爵士说。“这份助力咱们是指望不上了。”

他走到莫里斯·蔡斯沃特躺卧的地方。尸体仰面朝天；朝头部看一眼便足以断定，性命必定早已断绝。

“这光景不好看，”克林顿爵士说着掏出手帕，盖住了那张死人的脸。“显然是近距离开的枪。难怪你有些惊魂不定，莫尔德。”

===Original===
He glanced round the little glade, then turned again to the keeper.

“When did you find him?” he demanded.

“Just before I rang you up, sir. As soon as I came across him, I ran off to my cottage and telephoned to you.”
===Chinese===
他环顾了一下这片小小的林间空地，随即又转向看管人。

“你什么时候发现他的？”他问道。

“就在给您打电话之前一会儿，先生。我一撞见他，就立刻跑回小屋，给您挂了电话。”

===Original===
“When were you over this ground last?—before you found him, I mean.”

“Just before dusk, last night, sir. He wasn’t there, then.”

“You’re sure?”

“Certain, sir. I couldn’t have missed seeing him.”
===Chinese===
“你上回巡到这片地是什么时候？——我是说，在你发现他之前。”

“昨儿天擦黑之前，先生。那时候他还不在。”

“你拿得准？”

“拿得准，先生。我要是看见了他，决不至于漏过。”

===Original===
“You haven’t touched the body?”

Mold shuddered slightly.

“No, sir. I went off at once and rang you up.”

“You met no one hereabouts this morning?”

“No, sir.”

“And you saw no one last night, either?”

“No, sir.”
===Chinese===
“你没碰过尸体吧？”

莫尔德微微打了个哆嗦。

“没碰，先生。我当下就走开，给您挂了电话。”

“今天早上这一带你没碰见什么人？”

“没有，先生。”

“昨儿夜里你也没瞧见什么人？”

“没有，先生。”

===Original===
“It was somewhere round about here, wasn’t it, that you heard that mysterious shot you told us about?”

“Yes, sir. I was just here at the time.”

Mold walked about twenty yards past the tower, to show the exact position. Sir Clinton studied the lie of the land for a moment.
===Chinese===
“你跟我们讲过的那声蹊跷枪响，就是在这一带听见的吧？”

“是，先生。那会儿我就在这儿。”

莫尔德往塔那边走了约二十码，指出确切的地点。克林顿爵士把这一带的地势端详了片刻。

===Original===
“H’m! Have you any questions you want to ask, Inspector?”

Armadale considered for a moment or two.

“You’re sure you haven’t moved this body in any way?” he demanded.

“I never put a finger on it,” Mold asserted.

“And it’s lying just as it was when you saw it first?” Armadale pursued.

“As near as I can remember,” Mold replied, cautiously. “I didn’t wait long after I saw it. I went off almost at once to ring up the police.”
===Chinese===
“嗯！你还有什么要问的吗，探长？”

阿玛代尔斟酌了一两息。

“你拿得准没有挪动过这具尸体？分毫都没有？”他问道。

“我一根指头也没碰过它，”莫尔德一口咬定。

“它还照你头一回见时的原样躺着？”阿玛代尔追问。

“凭我记得的，差不离是原样，”莫尔德谨慎作答。“见着它之后我没多耽搁，几乎立刻就走开去给警察挂电话了。”

===Original===
Armadale seemed to have got all the information he expected. Sir Clinton, seeing that no more questions were to come, turned to the keeper.

“Go off to the house and tell Mr. Cecil Chacewater that his brother’s found and that he’s to come here at once. You needn’t say anything about the matter to anyone else. They’ll hear soon enough. And when you’ve done that, ring up the police station and tell them to send up a sergeant and a couple of constables to me here. Hurry, now.”

Mold went without a word. Sir Clinton waited till he was out of earshot and then glanced at Armadale.
===Chinese===
看样子，阿玛代尔想要问的都问齐了。克林顿爵士见不会再有问题，便转向看管人。

“快去宅子里告诉塞西尔·蔡斯沃特（Cecil Chacewater）先生，他弟弟找到了，叫他立刻到这儿来。这件事不必对其余任何人提起。他们很快就会听说的。办完这件事，再给警察局挂个电话，叫他们派一名警佐、两名警员到我这儿来。快去。”

莫尔德一声不吭地走了。克林顿爵士等他走得听不见了，才瞥了阿玛代尔一眼。

===Original===
“One thing stares you in the face,” the Inspector said in answer to the look. “He wasn’t shot here. That wound would mean any amount of blood; and there’s hardly any blood on the grass.”

Sir Clinton’s face showed his agreement. He looked down at the body.

“He’s lying on his back now; but after he was shot he lay on his left side till rigor mortis set in,” he pointed out.
===Chinese===
“有一桩事明摆在眼前，”探长像是回应那一眼，说道。“他不是在这儿挨的枪。那样一处枪伤，必然血流满地；可草地上几乎见不着血。”

克林顿爵士的脸色表示他也这么看。他低头望着尸体。

“他现在仰面躺着；可中枪之后，他原本是朝左侧卧的，直到尸僵定形才止，”他指出。

===Original===
The Inspector examined the body carefully.

“I think I see how you get that,” he said. “This left arm’s off the ground a trifle. If he’d been shot here and fell in this position, the arm would have relaxed and followed the lie of the ground. Is that it?”

“Yes, that and the hypostases. You see the marks on the left side of the face.”

“A dead man doesn’t shift himself,” the Inspector observed with an oracular air. “Someone else must have had a motive for dragging him about.”
===Chinese===
探长细细查验了尸体。

“我想我明白您是怎么看出来的，”他说。“这条左臂稍稍离地。倘若他是在这儿挨的枪、又以这个姿势倒下，胳膊本该松垂下来，贴着地面的走势。是不是这个理？”

“正是，再者还有尸斑。你瞧他左半边脸上的痕迹。”

“死人自己是不会翻身的，”探长一副神谕似的口气说道。“必是另有其人，怀着什么缘由，把他拖动过。”

===Original===
“Here’s a revolver,” Sir Clinton pointed out, picking it up gingerly to avoid marking it with fingerprints. “You can see, later on, if anything’s to be made out from it.”

He put the revolver carefully down on a part of the ruined wall near at hand and then returned to the body.

“To judge by the rigor mortis,” he said, after making a test, “he must have been dead for a good while—a dozen hours or more.”

“What about that shot that the keeper said he heard?” queried Armadale.

“The time might fit well enough. But rigor mortis is no real criterion, you know, Inspector. It varies too much from case to case.”
===Chinese===
“这儿有一把左轮手枪，”克林顿爵士说着，小心翼翼地把它拾起来，免得印上自己的指纹。“回头你可以看看，能不能从它身上琢磨出点什么。”

他把左轮枪仔细搁在手边一段残垣上，随后回到尸体旁。

“从尸僵来判断，”他验看了一下，说道，“他必定已死了好一阵子——十二个钟头，或者更多。”

“那看管人说听见的那声枪响，又怎么讲？”阿玛代尔问道。

“时辰上或许对得拢。不过尸僵实在算不得可靠的准绳，这你是知道的，探长。它因案而异，出入太大。”

===Original===
Inspector Armadale pulled out a small magnifying glass and examined the dead man’s hand carefully.

“Those were his fingerprints on that Japanese sword right enough, sir,” he pointed out. “You can see that tiny scar on the thumb quite plainly if you look.”

He held out the glass, and Sir Clinton inspected the right thumb of the body minutely.

“I didn’t doubt it from the evidence you had before, Inspector; but this certainly clinches it. The scar’s quite clear.”
===Chinese===
阿玛代尔探长掏出一柄小小的放大镜，细细察看死者的手。

“那把日本刀上的指纹，千真万确是他的，先生，”他指出。“您细看就知道，拇指上那点细疤瞧得相当清楚。”

他递过放大镜，克林顿爵士把尸体的右手拇指细细察看了一番。

“凭你先前掌握的证据，我本就不曾怀疑，探长；不过这一下更是铁定了。疤痕相当清楚。”

===Original===
“Shall I go through the pockets now?” Armadale asked.

“You may as well,” Sir Clinton agreed.

Inspector Armadale began by putting his fingers into the body’s waistcoat pocket. As he did so his face showed his surprise.

“Hullo! Here’s something!”

He pulled out the object and held it up for Sir Clinton’s inspection.

“One of the Leonardo medallions,” Sir Clinton said, as soon as he had identified the thing. “Let me have a closer look at it, Inspector.”
===Chinese===
“这会儿我把他口袋翻检一遍？”阿玛代尔问。

“翻吧，”克林顿爵士应允。

阿玛代尔探长先把手指伸进尸体的马甲口袋。这么一来，他脸上露出讶色。

“嚯！这儿有东西！”

他掏出那件东西，举起来给克林顿爵士验看。

“是达·芬奇徽章里的一枚，”克林顿爵士一认出那物件便说道。“给我细瞧瞧，探长。”

===Original===
He examined the edge with care.

“This seems to be the genuine article, Inspector. I can’t see any hole in the edge, which they told me was drilled to distinguish the replicas from the real thing. No, there’s no mark of any sort here.”

He handed it back to the Inspector, who examined it in his turn. Sir Clinton took it back when the Inspector had done with it, and placed it in his pocket.

“I think, Inspector, we’ll say nothing about this find for the present. I’ve an idea it may be a useful thing to have up our sleeve before we’ve done. By the way, do you still connect Foxton Polegate with this case?”
===Chinese===
他把边缘细细验看了一番。

“这一枚像是真品，探长。边上我看不出有什么孔——他们告诉过我，复制品为与真品区分，边上钻了孔。没有，这儿任何记号都没有。”

他把它交还给探长，探长也验看了一遍。探长看完，克林顿爵士又收回来，放进了自己口袋。

“我看，探长，这项发现眼下咱们谁也不声张。我有个预感，事情没完之前，袖里藏着这张牌兴许大有用处。顺便问一句，你还把福克斯顿·波尔盖特（Foxton Polegate）跟这案子连在一块儿吗？”

===Original===
Armadale looked the Chief Constable in the eye as he replied.

“I’m more inclined to connect Cecil Chacewater with it, just now, sir. Look at the facts. It’s been common talk that there was ill-feeling between those two brothers. Servants talk; and other people repeat it. And the business that ended in the final row between the two of them was centred in these Leonardo medallions. That’s worth thinking over. Then, again, Cecil Chacewater disappeared for a short while. You couldn’t get in touch with him. And it was just at that time that queer things began to happen here at Ravensthorpe. Where was he then? It seems a bit suggestive, doesn’t it? And where was he last night? If you looked at him this morning, you couldn’t help seeing he’d spent a queer night, wherever he spent it. That was the night when this body was brought here from wherever the shooting was done. And when you asked Cecil Chacewater how he’d come home, he said he’d arrived by the first train this morning. That was a lie. He didn’t come by that train. He’d been here before that.”
===Chinese===
阿玛代尔答话时，直直望着郡警察局长的眼睛。

“眼下方才，我倒更想把塞西尔·蔡斯沃特跟它连在一块儿，长官。您看这些事实。弟兄俩不睦，早是人人挂在嘴上的闲话。仆人们嚼舌根，旁人再添油加醋。而他们俩最后那场决裂，起因恰恰就在这几枚达·芬奇徽章上。这值得一想。再说，塞西尔·蔡斯沃特还失踪过一小阵子。您怎么也联络不上他。而偏偏就在那当口，雷文索普（Ravensthorpe）这儿开始出些蹊跷事。他那时在哪儿？这里头透着点意思，是不是？还有，昨儿夜里他又在哪儿？您今儿早上要是瞧过他，就免不了看出，他这一夜过得古怪，不管是在哪儿过的。尸首正是在那一夜，从行凶的地方运到这儿来的。而您问塞西尔·蔡斯沃特是怎么回家的，他说他是今儿早上头班火车到的。那是撒谎。他没坐那班车。在那之前他就到了这儿了。”

===Original===
To the Inspector’s amazement and disgust Sir Clinton laughed unaffectedly at this exposition.

“It’s nothing to laugh at, sir. You can’t deny these things. I don’t say they prove anything; but you can’t brush them aside by merely laughing at them. They’ve got to be explained. And until they’ve been explained in some satisfactory way things will look very fishy.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士听了这一番宏论，竟毫不做作地朗声笑了出来，叫探长又惊又恼。

“这没什么可笑的，长官。这些事您否认不了。我不是说它们证明了什么；可您也不能光靠一笑，就把它们撇到一边。总得有个解释。而不等它们得到个像样的解释，事情瞧着就非常可疑。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton recovered his serious mask.

“Perhaps I laughed a little too soon, Inspector. I apologize. I’m not absolutely certain of my ground; I quite admit that. But I’ll just give you one hint. Sometimes one case looks as if it were two independent affairs. Sometimes two independent affairs get interlocked and look like one case. Now just think that over carefully. It’s perhaps got the germ of something in it, if you care to fish it out.”

“Half of what you’ve said already sounds like riddles to me, sir,” Armadale protested, fretfully. “I’m never sure when you’re serious and when you’re pulling my leg.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士重新板起严肃的面孔。

“兴许我笑得早了点儿，探长。我赔个不是。我的立足点并非十拿九稳，这我完全承认。不过我给你一句提点。有时候，一桩案子瞧着像两件不相干的勾当。有时候，两件不相干的勾当又咬合在一处，瞧着像一桩案子。你细细琢磨琢磨这句话。这里头兴许埋着点东西的胚芽——就看你肯不肯把它抠出来了。”

“您说的话，眼下已有一半在我听来像打哑谜了，长官，”阿玛代尔烦躁地抗议。“我从来拿不准您哪句是正经、哪句是在耍弄我。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton was saved from the embarrassment of a reply by the arrival of Cecil Chacewater. He nodded curtly to the two officials as he came up. The Inspector stepped forward to meet him.

“I’d like to put one or two questions to you, Mr. Chacewater,” he said, ignoring the look on Sir Clinton’s face.

Cecil looked Armadale up and down before replying.

“Well, go on,” he said, shortly.
===Chinese===
恰在此时塞西尔·蔡斯沃特到了，克林顿爵士才算免去了回话的尴尬。他走上前来，朝两位官员生硬地点了点头。探长迎上前去。

“我想向您提一两个问题，蔡斯沃特先生，”他说，只当没看见克林顿爵士脸上的神色。

塞西尔回话前，把阿玛代尔上上下下打量了一番。

“那便问吧，”他简短地说。

===Original===
“First of all, Mr. Chacewater,” the Inspector began, “I want to know when you last saw your brother alive.”

Cecil replied without the slightest hesitation:

“On the morning I left Ravensthorpe. We’d had a disagreement and I left the house.”

“That was the last time you saw him?”

“No. I see him now.”
===Chinese===
“首先，蔡斯沃特先生，”探长开腔道，“我想知道，您最后一次见到您弟弟活着，是什么时候。”

塞西尔毫不迟疑地答道：

“我离开雷文索普的那天早上。我们闹了点意见，我便离开了宅子。”

“那是您最后一次见到他？”

“不是。我现在就见到他了。”

===Original===
The Inspector looked up angrily from his notebook.

“You’re giving the impression of quibbling, Mr. Chacewater.”

“I’m answering your questions, Inspector, to the best of my ability.”

Armadale made a fresh cast.
===Chinese===
探长从笔记本上愤然抬头。

“您这样子像是在支吾搪塞，蔡斯沃特先生。”

“我是在尽我所能，回答您的问题，探长。”

阿玛代尔另起了一个话头。

===Original===
“Where did you go when you left Ravensthorpe?”

“To London.”

“You’ve been in London, then, until this morning?”

Cecil paused for a moment or two before answering.
===Chinese===
“您离开雷文索普之后去了哪儿？”

“去了伦敦。”

“那么，直到今天早上，您一直在伦敦？”

塞西尔迟疑了一两息，方才作答。

===Original===
“May I ask, Inspector, whether you’re bringing any charge against me? If you are, then I believe you ought to caution me. If you aren’t, then I don’t propose to answer your questions. Now, what are you going to do about it?”

Armadale was hardly prepared for this move.

“I think you’re injudicious, Mr. Chacewater,” he said in a tone which he was evidently striving not to make threatening. “I know you didn’t arrive by the first train this morning, though you told us you did. Your position’s rather an awkward one, if you think about it.”

“You can’t bluff me, Inspector,” Cecil returned. “Make your charge, and I’ll know how to answer it. If you won’t make a charge, I don’t propose to help you with a fishing inquiry.”
===Chinese===
“我可不可以问一句，探长，您是不是要对我提出什么指控？若是，那么依我看，您就该先对我宣读警诫。若不是，那我不打算回答您的问题。怎么样，您打算如何处置？”

这一手阿玛代尔殊未料及。

“我认为您这样做失于轻率，蔡斯沃特先生，”他说，语气显然竭力不使它带上威胁的意味。“我知道您今早并非乘头班火车到的，尽管您对我们说是。您细想想，您的处境相当不利。”

“您唬不住我，探长，”塞西尔回敬道。“提出您的指控来，我自有应对之道。您若不肯指控，就休想我陪着您做这种撒网钓话的盘问。”

===Original===
The Inspector glanced at Sir Clinton’s face, and on it he read quite plainly the Chief Constable’s disapproval of his proceedings. He decided to go no further for the moment. Sir Clinton intervened to make the situation less strained.

“Would you mind looking at him, Cecil, and formally identifying him?”

Cecil came forward rather reluctantly, knelt down beside his brother’s body, examined the clothes, and finally, removing the handkerchief, gazed for a moment or two at the shattered face. The shot had entered the right side of the head and had done enough damage to show that it had been fired almost in contact with the skin.

Cecil replaced the handkerchief and rose to his feet. For a few moments he stood looking down at the body. Then he turned away.

“That’s my brother, undoubtedly.”
===Chinese===
探长瞥了一眼克林顿爵士的脸，从上面清清楚楚读出郡警察局长对他这一套做法的不以为然。他决定此刻就此打住。克林顿爵士出面转圜，好让局面松缓些。

“塞西尔，可否请你看看他，正式认一认尸？”

塞西尔颇为勉强地上前，在弟弟的尸体旁跪下，验看衣裳，末了掀开手帕，对着那张破碎的脸凝望了一两息。子弹从头部右侧射入，造成的破坏足以表明，开枪时枪口几乎贴着皮肤。

塞西尔把手帕盖回去，站起身来。他低头对着尸体站了片刻，随后转身走开。

“那是我弟弟，毫无疑问。”

===Original===
Then, as if speaking to himself, he added in a regretful tone:

“Poor old Chuchundra!”

To the Inspector’s amazement Sir Clinton started a little at the word.

“Was that a nickname, Cecil?”

Cecil looked up, and the Inspector could see that he was more than a little moved.

“We used to call him that when we were kids.”
===Chinese===
随后，他像是自言自语，又用追悔的语调添了一句：

“可怜的老楚春德拉（Chuchundra）！”

克林顿爵士听到这个字眼，微微一怔，叫探长好生诧异。

“那是他的外号吗，塞西尔？”

塞西尔抬起头来，探长看得出，他心里的激动不止一丝半缕。

“我们小时候常这么叫他。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton’s next question left the Inspector still further bemused.

“Out of *The Jungle Book* by any chance?”

Cecil seemed to see the drift of the inquiry, for he replied at once:

“Yes. ‘Rikki-Tikki-Tavi,’ you know.”

“I was almost certain of it,” said Sir Clinton. “I can put a name to the trouble, I think. It begins with *A*.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士的下一问，更叫探长如坠云雾。

“莫非出自《丛林之书》（The Jungle Book）？”

塞西尔似乎咂摸出了这一问的用意，当即答道：

“正是。您知道的，『里基-蒂基-塔维』那一篇。”

“我差不多拿得准了，”克林顿爵士说。“这桩祸事，我想我能给它安上个名目。它以『A』打头。”

===Original===
Cecil reflected for a moment before replying.

“Yes. You’re right. It does begin with *A*.”

“That saves a lot of bother,” said Sir Clinton, thankfully. “I was just going to fish in a fresh direction to get that bit of information. I’m quite satisfied now.”

Cecil seemed to pay little attention to the Chief Constable’s last remark. His eyes went round to the shattered thing that had been his brother.

“I’d no notion it was as bad as all this,” he said, more to himself than to the others. “If I’d known, I wouldn’t have been so bitter about things.”
===Chinese===
塞西尔思忖了片刻，才回话。

“是。您说得对。它确以『A』开头。”

“这就省去许多周折了，”克林顿爵士如释重负地说。“我正要换个方向下钩，钓出这一点消息来。如今我是完全满意了。”

塞西尔似乎全没留意郡警察局长末了那句话。他的目光又转回那具残破的躯体——那曾是他弟弟的人。

“我没料到事情糟到这般地步，”他说，与其说是讲给旁人，不如说是讲给自己。“早知道如此，我就不会把话说得那么狠了。”

===Original===
The sergeant and constables appeared at the edge of the clearing.

“Seen all you want to see, Inspector?” asked Sir Clinton. “Then in that case we can leave the body in charge of the sergeant. I see they’ve got a stretcher with them. They can take it down to Ravensthorpe.”

Armadale rapidly gave the necessary orders to his subordinates.

“Now, Inspector, I think we’ll go over to Ravensthorpe ourselves. I want to see that chauffeur again. Something’s occurred to me.”
===Chinese===
警佐和警员们出现在空地边上。

“要看的是不是都看齐了，探长？”克林顿爵士问。“那么，尸体可以交给警佐看管了。我瞧他们带了担架来。可以把他抬到雷文索普去。”

阿玛代尔火速向手下交代了必要的指令。

“好了，探长，咱们自己也到雷文索普走一趟。我要再见见那个司机。我想起了一件事。”

===Original===
As the three men walked through the belt of woodland Sir Clinton turned to Cecil.

“There’s one point I’d like to have cleared up. Do you know if Maurice had any visitors in the last three months or so—people who wanted to see the collection?”

Cecil reflected for a time before he could recall the facts.

“Now you mention it, I remember hearing Maurice say something about a fellow—a Yankee—who was writing a book on Leonardo. That chap certainly came here one day and Maurice showed him the stuff. The medallions were what he chiefly wanted to look at, of course.”

“You didn’t see him?”

“No. None of us saw him except Maurice.”
===Chinese===
三人穿行在林带间，克林顿爵士转向塞西尔。

“有一处疑点，我想弄个明白。你可知道，最近三个月上下，莫里斯可曾接过什么来客——那些想参观收藏的人？”

塞西尔回想了好一阵，才把事实记起来。

“经您一提，我记得听莫里斯说起过一个家伙——一个美国佬——正在写一部关于达·芬奇的书。那人确实有一天来过这儿，莫里斯把藏品给他看了。他主要想看的，自然是那几枚徽章。”

“你没见到他？”

“没有。除了莫里斯，我们谁都没见着他。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton made no comment; and they walked on in silence till they came to the house. Inspector Armadale was by this time completely at sea.

“Find that chauffeur, Inspector, please; and bring him along. I’ve got one or two points which need clearing up.”

When the chauffeur arrived it was evident that Armadale had not been mistaken when he described him as stupid-looking. Information had to be dragged out of him by minute questioning.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士未置一词；三人默然前行，一直走到宅子跟前。到这时，阿玛代尔探长已是彻底如在云里雾中。

“劳驾，探长，去把那个司机找来，带到我这儿。我有一两处疑点需要澄清。”

司机到得面前，显然阿玛代尔说他一副蠢相，并不冤枉他。每一点情况都得靠掰开了揉碎了的细问，才能从他嘴里抠出来。

===Original===
“Your name’s Brackley, isn’t it?” Sir Clinton began.

“Yes, sir. Joe Brackley.”

“Now, Brackley, don’t be in a hurry with your replies. I want you to think carefully. First of all, on the day that Mr. Foss was murdered, he ordered you to bring the car round to the front door.”

“Yes, sir. I was to wait for him if he wasn’t there.”
===Chinese===
“你叫布拉克利（Brackley），是不是？”克林顿爵士开腔道。

“是，先生。乔·布拉克利（Joe Brackley）。”

“听着，布拉克利，回话别急着开口。我要你仔细想一想。首先，福斯先生遇害那天，他吩咐你把车开到正门前。”

“是，先生。他要是不在那儿，我就得等他。”

===Original===
“You pulled up the car here, didn’t you?”

Sir Clinton indicated the position in front of the house.

“Yes, sir. It was there or thereabouts.”

“Then you put up the hood?”

“Yes, sir.”
===Chinese===
“你把车停在这儿了，对不对？”

克林顿爵士指了指宅子前面的位置。

“是，先生。就在那一带，差不离。”

“随后你支起了车篷？”

“是，先生。”

===Original===
“What possessed you to do that on a sunny day?”

“One of the fastenings was a bit loose and I wanted to make it right before going out.”

“You didn’t think of doing that in the garage?”

“I didn’t notice it, sir, until I’d brought the car round. My eye happened to fall on it. And just then I saw Mr. Foss going off into the house with some people. He didn’t seem in a hurry, so I thought I’d just time to make the repair before he came out.”
===Chinese===
“大晴天的，你怎么想起支车篷来了？”

“有个篷扣有点松了，我想着出车前把它弄妥帖。”

“没想过在车库里弄？”

“我把车开出来之后才留意到的，先生。眼睛正好瞟见了。正那会儿，我瞧见福斯先生领着几个人进了宅子。他瞧着不赶时间，我想正好趁他出来前把活儿干完。”

===Original===
“You got on to the running-board to reach the hood, didn’t you?”

“Yes, sir.”

“Which running-board? The one nearest the house?”

“No, sir. The other one.”

“So you could see the front of the house as you were working?”

“Yes, sir.”
===Chinese===
“你是踩上踏脚板去够车篷的，对不对？”

“是，先生。”

“哪一侧的踏脚板？靠宅子近的那侧？”

“不是，先生。是另一侧。”

“这么说，你干活时瞧得见宅子的正面？”

“是，先生。”

===Original===
“Did you see anything—anything whatever—while you were at work? You must have raised your eyes occasionally.”

“I could see the window opposite me.”

“By and by, I think, Marden, the valet, came up and spoke to you?”

“Yes, sir, he did. He’d been going to the post, he said, but there had been some mistake or other and he’d come back.”

“He left you and went into the house?”

“Yes, sir.”
===Chinese===
“干活的时候，你可瞧见什么动静——什么动静都算上？你总不免偶尔抬抬眼吧。”

“我瞧得见正对着我的一扇窗。”

“过了不大工夫，我想，那个贴身男仆马登（Marden）走上前来，跟你搭话了？”

“是，先生，他说过话。他说他本要上邮局去，不知出了什么岔子，又折回来了。”

“他撇下你，进宅子去了？”

“是，先生。”

===Original===
“After that, did you see Marden again—I mean within, say, twenty minutes or so?”

“Yes, sir.”

“Where did you see him, if you can remember?”

“Up there, sir, at that window. He was talking to Mr. Foss.”

“When you were up on the running-board, you could just see into the room?”

“Yes, sir.”
===Chinese===
“在那之后，你再见着马登没有——我是说，比方说，二十分钟上下的工夫里？”

“见着了，先生。”

“在哪儿见着的，要是你还记得？”

“上头那儿，先生，就在那扇窗跟前。他正跟福斯先生说话。”

“你在踏脚板上站着的时候，正好能瞧见屋里？”

“是，先生。”

===Original===
“What happened after that?”

“I finished the repair; so I came down off the running-board and let down the hood again.”

“Anything else you can remember, Brackley?”

“No, sir.”
===Chinese===
“后来呢？”

“我把活儿干完了，就从踏脚板上下来，又把车篷放了下来。”

“还有别的记得的吗，布拉克利？”

“没有了，先生。”

===Original===
“Very well. That will do. By the way, Inspector,” Sir Clinton turned round, preventing the Inspector from making any comments while the chauffeur was standing by, “I’d clean forgotten the patrolling of the place up yonder. I’ve never found time to go up there; but it’s really a bit out of date now. I think we can dispense with the patrol after tonight. And the same holds for that guard on the museum. There’s no need for either of them.”

“Very good, sir,” Armadale responded, mechanically.

The Inspector was engaged in condemning his own stupidity. Why had he not seen the possibilities involved in that repair of the hood? With the extra foot of elevation of course the chauffeur could see further into the museum than a man standing on the ground. And here was the damning evidence that Marden’s story was a lie. And the Inspector had missed it. He almost gritted his teeth in vexation as he thought of it. The keystone of the case: and the Chief Constable had taken it under his nose!
===Chinese===
“很好。就问到这儿。顺带说一句，探长，”克林顿爵士转过身来，趁司机还站在旁边，不叫探长插话评论，“上头那处的巡夜，我忘了个干净。我一直腾不出工夫上去看看；不过如今它实在有点过时了。我想今夜过后，巡逻就可以撤了。博物馆门口那个岗哨也一样。两个都用不着了。”

“是，长官，”阿玛代尔机械地应道。

探长正忙着痛骂自己愚钝。修理车篷这一节里的种种可能，他怎么就没看出来？多了那一英尺的高度，司机自然比站在地上的人看得更进博物馆一层。而眼前这便是铁证：马登那套说辞是谎话。探长竟把它漏掉了。一想到这个，他几乎咬碎钢牙。全案的拱顶石——竟叫郡警察局长当着他的面、从他鼻子底下取走了！

===Original===
Sir Clinton turned to Cecil as the chauffeur retired.

“I shall be here about one o’clock in the morning, Cecil,” he said, lowering his voice. “I want you to be on the watch and let me in without anyone getting wind of my visit. Can you manage it?”

“Easily enough.”

“Very well. I’ll be at the door at one o’clock sharp. But remember, it’s an absolutely hush-hush affair. There must be no noise of any sort.”

“I’ll see to that,” Cecil assured him.
===Chinese===
司机退下后，克林顿爵士转向塞西尔。

“明天凌晨一点钟上下，我会到这儿来，塞西尔，”他压低声音说。“我要你留心守着，放我进来，还不能让任何人嗅到我此行的风声。你办得到吗？”

“绰绰有余。”

“很好。一点整我准到门口。不过记住，这是一件绝密的事。绝不能有半点声响。”

“这我自会料理，”塞西尔向他担保。

===Original===
Sir Clinton turned to the Inspector.

“Now I think we’ll go across to where we left my car.”

On the way to the police station Sir Clinton’s manner did not encourage conversation; but as they got out of the car he turned to Armadale.

“Map-drawing’s a bit late in the day now, Inspector; but we may as well carry on for the sake of completeness.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士转向探长。

“现在，我想咱们该到停车的地方去了。”

去警察局的一路上，克林顿爵士的神情不便于搭话；可下车时，他转向阿玛代尔。

“画地图如今已有点晚了，探长；不过为求齐全，不妨还是把它做完吧。”

===Original===
He led the way to his office, took a ruler and protractor from his desk, and set to work on a sheet of paper.

“Take this point as the museum,” he said. “This line represents the beginning of the tunnel. I took the bearing that time when I lagged behind you. At the next turn—this one here—I made a pretence of examining the walls and took the bearing as we were standing there. I got the third bearing when I asked you to measure the dimensions of the tunnel. As it has turned out, secrecy wasn’t really necessary; but it seemed just as well to keep the survey to ourselves. I got the distances by pacing, except the last bit. There I had to estimate it, since we were crawling on all fours; but I think I got it near enough.”

“And you carried all the figures in your memory?”

“Yes. I’ve a fairly good memory when I’m put to it.”

“You must have,” said Armadale, frankly.
===Chinese===
他领路进了办公室，从桌上取来直尺和量角器，在一张纸上动起手来。

“把这一点当作博物馆，”他说。“这条线代表地道的起点。那次我落在你后头的时候，我测了方位。到下一个拐弯——就是这儿这一处——我装作查验两壁，趁咱们站在那儿时又测了方位。第三次方位，是我请你丈量地道尺寸时测下的。事实证明，保密原也并非真有必要；不过把勘测攥在自己手里，终究稳妥些。距离是我步测出来的，只有末尾一小段除外。那一段只好估，因为咱们是手脚并用爬过去的；不过我想估得也差不离。”

“这些数目，您全装在脑子里带回来的？”

“是啊。真要用的时候，我这记性还算不坏。”

“那可真是得有这样的记性，”阿玛代尔由衷地说。

===Original===
“Now,” Sir Clinton went on. “By drawing in these lines we get the position of that underground room. It’s here, you see. The next thing is to find out where it lies, relative to the ground surface. I had a fair notion; so when I got to the top of the turret I took the bearing of the Knight’s Tower. I’ll just rule it in. You see the two lines cut quite near the cell. My notion is that there’s a second entrance into that tunnel from that ruined tower. In the old days it may have been a secret road into the outpost tower when a siege was going on.”

“I see what you’re getting at now,” Armadale interrupted. “You mean that Maurice Chacewater’s body was in the cell and that it was shifted from there up the other secret passage—the one we didn’t see—and left alongside the tower this morning?”

“Something of that sort.”

“And now we’ve got to find who killed Maurice Chacewater down there, underground?”

“There’s nothing in that, Inspector. He killed himself. It’s a fairly plain case of suicide.”

“But why did he commit suicide?”
===Chinese===
“现在，”克林顿爵士接着说。“把这几条线画进去，那间地下密室的位置便有了。就在这儿，你瞧。下一步是弄清它相对地面的位置。我原本心里有个大概；所以上到塔楼顶上时，我测了骑士塔的方位。我这就把它画上。你瞧，两条线相交的地方，离那间密室很近。我的想法是，那条地道还有第二个入口，就在那座破塔里头。早年间遇上围城，它或许是一条通往前哨塔的密道。”

“这下我明白您的用意了，”阿玛代尔插话道。“您是说，莫里斯·蔡斯沃特的尸体原本就在那密室里，后来从那儿经另一条暗道——咱们没见到的那条——被搬了上来，今儿早上撂在了塔旁？”

“差不多是这个意思。”

“那么现在，咱们得去查清，是谁在下面地下害了莫里斯·蔡斯沃特？”

“这一层没什么可查的，探长。他是自尽。这是一桩相当明白的自杀案。”

“可他为什么自杀？”

===Original===
Sir Clinton appeared suddenly smitten with deafness. He ignored the Inspector’s last inquiry completely.

“I shall want you tonight, Inspector. Come to my house at about half-past twelve. And you had better wear rubber-soled boots or tennis shoes if you have them. We’ll go up to Ravensthorpe in my car.”

“You’re going to arrest Marden, sir?”

“No,” was Sir Clinton’s reply, which took the Inspector completely aback. “I’m not going to arrest anybody. I’m going to show you what Foss was going to do with his otophone; that’s all.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士霎时像聋了一般。探长末了那一问，他全然置之不理。

“今儿夜里我用得着你，探长。十二点半上下到我家来。还有，你最好穿胶底靴，要不然网球鞋也行——要是有的话。咱们坐我的车上雷文索普去。”

“您要去逮捕马登，长官？”

“不，”克林顿爵士答道，这话叫探长彻底愣住了。“我不打算逮捕任何人。我要让你看看，福斯原本要用他那架奥托风（otophone）做什么——仅此而已。”

## XIII The Otophone / 第十三章 奥托风

===Original===
Punctually at half-past twelve the Inspector arrived at Sir Clinton’s house. The Chief Constable’s first glance was at the feet of his subordinate.

“Tennis shoes? That’s right. Now, Inspector, I want you to understand clearly that silence is absolutely essential when we get to work. We’ll need to take a leaf out of the book of the *Pirates of Penzance*:

With catlike tread
						

						Upon our prey we steal.

That’s our model, if you please. The car’s outside. We’ll go at once.”
===Chinese===
十二点半整，探长准时到了克林顿爵士（Sir Clinton）宅上。郡警察局长（Chief Constable）第一眼先朝他下属那双脚望去。

“网球鞋？这就对了。那么，探长，我要你明明白白地懂得：咱们动手的时候，绝对要保持肃静。这一手，咱们得学学《彭赞斯的海盗》（Pirates of Penzance）里的做派：

蹑着猫儿般的脚步，

我们悄悄潜近猎物。

请看，这就是咱们的榜样。车子就在外头。咱们即刻动身。”

===Original===
As preparations for an important raid, these remarks seemed to Armadale hardly adequate; but as Sir Clinton showed no desire to amplify them, the Inspector was left to puzzle over the immediate future without assistance. The hint about the otophone had roused his curiosity.

“Foss’s hearing was quite normal,” he said to himself, turning the evidence over in his mind. “He heard that conversation in the winter-garden quite clearly enough. So quite evidently one couldn’t call him deaf. And yet he was dragging an otophone about with him. I don’t see it.”
===Chinese===
作为一场重要突袭的部署，这些话在阿玛代尔（Armadale）听来未免不够分量；可克林顿爵士又无意多说，探长只得没人帮衬，独自对着眼下将临的事暗自琢磨。关于那架奥托风（Otophone）的提示，早已勾起了他的好奇。

“福斯（Foss）的听力分明正常，”他心里暗自掂量着这些证据。“温室里那番谈话，他听得够清楚的了。所以明摆着，谁也不能说他聋。可他偏偏拖着一架奥托风到处走。我实在想不通。”

===Original===
The Chief Constable pulled up the car in the avenue at a considerable distance from the house.

“Change here for Ravensthorpe,” he explained, opening the door beside him. “I can’t take the motor nearer for fear of the engine’s noise giving us away.”

He glanced at the illuminated clock on the dashboard.

“We’re in nice time,” he commented. “Come along, Inspector; and the less said the better.”
===Chinese===
郡警察局长把车停在林荫道上，离宅子还隔着老远。

“开往雷文索普（Ravensthorpe）的，请在此换乘，”他一边推开身旁的车门一边解释。“再往前车是开不得了，就怕引擎声走漏了咱们的行藏。”

他瞥了一眼仪表板上亮着的钟。

“时候赶得正好，”他说。“走吧，探长；话越少越好。”

===Original===
They reached the door of Ravensthorpe exactly at one o’clock. Cecil was waiting for them on the threshold.

“Switch off those lights,” Sir Clinton said in a whisper, pointing to the hall lights which Cecil had left burning. “We mustn’t give the show away if we can help it. Someone might be looking out of a window and be tempted to come down and turn them out. You’re supposed to be in bed, aren’t you?”

Cecil nodded without speaking, and, crossing the hall, he extinguished the lamps. Sir Clinton pulled an electric torch from his pocket.

“There’s a staircase giving access to the servant’s quarters, isn’t there?”

Cecil confirmed this, and Sir Clinton turned to the Inspector.
===Chinese===
一点整，他们到了雷文索普的门口。塞西尔（Cecil）正在门槛上候着他们。

“把那些灯关掉，”克林顿爵士耳语道，指的是大厅里塞西尔没熄的灯。“能不露马脚就不露。说不定有人正从哪扇窗口往外张望，一时起意下楼来关灯。照理你这会儿该在床上，不是么？”

塞西尔无声地点了点头，穿过大厅，把灯熄了。克林顿爵士从口袋里掏出一支电筒。

“有一道楼梯通到仆人的住处，是吧？”

塞西尔证实了，克林顿爵士便转向探长。

===Original===
“Which of your men is on duty at the museum door tonight?”

“Froggatt,” the Inspector answered.

“We’ll go along to him,” said Sir Clinton. “I want you, Cecil, to take the constable and post him at the bottom of that stair. Here’s the flash-lamp.”
===Chinese===
“今晚是你的哪个人在博物馆门口当值？”

“弗罗加特（Froggatt），”探长答道。

“咱们这就上他那儿去，”克林顿爵士说。“塞西尔，我要你带上这位警员，把他安置在那道楼梯脚下。手电筒在这儿。”

===Original===
Froggatt was surprised to see the party.

“Now, Froggatt,” the Chief Constable directed. “You’re to go with Mr. Chacewater. He’ll show you where to stand. All you have to do is to stick to your post there until you’re relieved. It’ll only be a matter of ten minutes or so. Don’t make the slightest sound unless anything goes wrong. Your business is to prevent anyone getting down the stair. There’ll be no trouble. If you see anyone, just shout: ‘Who’s there?’ That’ll be quite enough.”
===Chinese===
弗罗加特见了这一行人，好生诧异。

“听着，弗罗加特，”郡警察局长吩咐道。“你这就跟蔡斯沃特（Chacewater）先生走。他会指给你该站的位子。你只须钉在那个岗位上，直到有人来换班。前后不过十来分钟的事。不出岔子，就别弄出半点声响。你的职责，是不放任何人下那道楼梯。不会有什么乱子。要是瞧见有人，只管喊一声：『什么人？』这就尽够了。”

===Original===
The Inspector and Sir Clinton waited on the threshold of the museum until Cecil came back.

“Very convenient having these museum lights on all night,” Sir Clinton remarked. “We don’t need to muddle about with the flash-lamp. Now just wait here for a moment, and don’t speak a word. I’m going upstairs.”

He ascended to the first floor, entered Foss’s room and picked up the otophone, with which he returned to his companions.
===Chinese===
探长和克林顿爵士在博物馆门口候着，直等塞西尔回来。

“博物馆的灯通宵亮着，倒真方便，”克林顿爵士说。“咱们犯不着摸黑摆弄手电筒了。你们在这儿略等一等，一个字也别说。我上楼去一趟。”

他上了二楼，走进福斯的房间，取了那架奥托风，带着它回到同伴身边。

===Original===
“Now we can get to work,” he whispered, leading the way into the museum. “Just lock that door behind us, Inspector.”

Followed by the other two he stepped across the museum to the bay containing the safe. There he put the otophone on the floor and opened the case of the instrument. From one compartment he took an earphone with its headband. A moment’s search revealed the position of the connection, and he plugged the earphone wire into place in sockets let into the outside of the attaché case. A little further examination revealed a stud beside the leather handle, and this Sir Clinton pressed.

“That should start the thing,” he commented.
===Chinese===
“现在可以动手了，”他悄声道，领头走进博物馆。“探长，把咱们身后的门锁上。”

另外两人跟着他，穿过博物馆，走到安放保险柜的那处凹室。他把奥托风搁在地上，打开仪器的箱子。他从其中一个格层里取出一副带头箍的耳机。略一寻找，便找到了接线的位置，他把耳机线插进公文箱外壳上嵌着的插孔。再一细看，又寻见皮提手旁有颗按钮，克林顿爵士把它按了下去。

“这一下该把它开动了，”他说。

===Original===
He lifted the hinged metal plate slightly and peered into the cavity which contained the valves.

“That seems all right,” he said, as his eye caught the faint glow of the dull emitters.

Shutting down the plate again, the Chief Constable put his finger into the compartment from which he had taken the earphone, pressed a concealed spring, and pulled up the floor of the compartment.
===Chinese===
他微微掀起那块铰链金属板，朝装着电子管的空腔里窥看。

“看来没毛病，”他说，眼睛已瞥见那几支微光灯丝透出的淡淡光晕。

郡警察局长重新合上金属板，把手指伸进方才取耳机的那个格层，按住一根暗簧，把格层的底板掀了起来。

===Original===
“This is the microphone,” he explained, drawing out a thick ebonite disk mounted on the false bottom of the compartment. “It’s attached to a longish wire so that you can take it out and put it on a table while the case with the valves and batteries lies on the floor out of the way. Now we’ll tune up.”

He brought microphone and earphone together, when a faint musical note made itself heard. Then he handed the microphone to Cecil.

“Hold that tight against the safe door, Cecil. Get the base in contact with the metal of the safe and keep the microphone face downwards. It’s essential to hold it absolutely steady, for the slightest vibration will put me off.”
===Chinese===
“这就是话筒，”他解释道，从格层的夹层底上抽出一块厚厚的胶木圆盘。“它连着一根挺长的电线，你可以把它取出来搁在桌上，装电子管和电池的箱子则放在地上，不碍手脚。现在咱们来调音。”

他把话筒和耳机凑到一处，立时听到一声轻微的乐音。随后他把话筒递给塞西尔。

“塞西尔，把它紧紧贴在保险柜门上。底盘要贴住柜身的金属，话筒面朝下。务必拿得纹丝不动，哪怕最轻微的一丝震动，都会搅了我。”

===Original===
He fitted on the headband and moved the two tiny levers of the otophone until the adjustment of the instrument seemed to satisfy him. Then, very cautiously, he began to work the mechanism of the combination lock. For some time he seemed unable to get what he wanted; but suddenly he made a slight gesture of triumph.

“It’s an old pattern, as I thought. There’s no balanced fence arbour. This is going to be an easy business.”

Easy or not, it took him nearly a quarter of an hour to accomplish his task; for at times he obviously went astray in the work.

“Try to keep your feet still,” he said. “Every movement you make is magnified up to the noise of a pocket avalanche.”
===Chinese===
他戴上头箍，拨动奥托风上那两枚小小的操纵杆，直到仪器的调校令他满意。随后，他极其小心地开始拨弄转字密码锁的机括。有一阵子他似乎总摸不着窍门；可忽然间，他做了个小小的得胜手势。

“是老式样，跟我想的一样。没有平衡制栓轴（balanced fence arbour）。这趟活计要好办了。”

好办归好办，他费了将近一刻钟才把事情办成；因为干着干着，他不时显然走了岔路。

“脚千万稳住别动，”他说。“你每动一下，都放大成一场口袋雪崩般的响动。”

===Original===
At last the thing was done. The safe door swung open. Sir Clinton took off the headband, received the microphone from Cecil, and packed it away in the case of the otophone along with the earphone.

“You’d better jot down the number of the combination, Cecil,” he suggested. “It’s on the dial at present.”

While Cecil was busy with this, the Chief Constable switched off the otophone and put it in a place of safety.

“Now we’ll see what’s inside the safe,” he said.
===Chinese===
事情终于办妥。保险柜门应声荡开。克林顿爵士摘下头箍，从塞西尔手里接过话筒，连同耳机一并收进奥托风的箱子里。

“你最好把密码的数字记下来，塞西尔，”他提议。“这会儿还在字盘上。”

塞西尔忙着记数的当口，郡警察局长关掉了奥托风，把它安置到稳妥的地方。

“现在瞧瞧保险柜里都有什么，”他说。

===Original===
He swung the door full open and disclosed a cavity more like a strongroom than a safe.

“Have you any idea where the medallions were usually kept?” he inquired.

Cecil went over to one of the shelves and searched rapidly.

“Why, there are only two of them here!” he exclaimed in dismay.
===Chinese===
他把柜门整个拉开，露出一处空腔，与其说是保险柜，不如说更像一间保险库。

“你可知道徽章平日一般收在哪里？”他问。

塞西尔走到一处架子跟前，飞快翻找。

“咦，这儿只剩两枚了！”他懊丧地叫出声。

===Original===
“Hush!” Sir Clinton warned him sharply. “Don’t make a row. Have a good look at the things.”

Cecil picked up the medallions and scanned them minutely. His face showed his amazement as he turned from one to another.

“These are the replicas! Where have the genuine Leonardos gone?”

“Never mind that for the present. Put these things back again. I’m going to close the safe. We mustn’t risk talking too much here; and the sooner we’re gone the better.”
===Chinese===
“嘘！”克林顿爵士厉声警示他。“别弄出响动。把东西好好瞧个明白。”

塞西尔拿起徽章，细细察看。他把两枚翻来覆去地对看，脸上满是惊诧。

“这些是复制品！真正的达·芬奇原作哪儿去了？”

“眼下先别管这个。把东西放回原处去。我要把保险柜关上了。在这儿多说话犯不着冒这个险；咱们走得越早越好。”

===Original===
He picked up the otophone and led the way out of the museum.

“You might bring Froggatt back to his post here,” he said. “We don’t need him at the stair any longer. I must go upstairs again for a moment with this machine.”

Cecil piloted Froggatt back to his original post just as the Chief Constable rejoined them.

“I don’t want to talk here,” Sir Clinton said to Cecil. “Get a coat and walk with us down to the car. We’ve done our work for the night.”
===Chinese===
他拿起奥托风，领路出了博物馆。

“你把弗罗加特带回他原先的岗位吧，”他说。“楼梯那儿用不着他了。我还得带着这台机器再上楼去一趟。”

塞西尔刚把弗罗加特领回原岗，郡警察局长也回到了他们身边。

“我不想在这儿说话，”克林顿爵士对塞西尔说。“去拿件外套，陪我们走下去到车那儿。今晚的活计已经做完了。”

===Original===
The Chief Constable waited until they were well away from the house before beginning his explanation.

“That otophone is﻿—as I expect you saw﻿—simply a microphone for picking up sound, plus a two-valve amplifier for magnifying it. The sounds that reach the microphone are amplified by the valves set to any extent, within limits, that you like to set it for. You can make the crumpling of a piece of paper sound like a small thunderstorm if you choose; and it’s especially sensitive to clicks and sounds of that sort. The mere involuntary shifting of your feet on that parquet floor made a lot of disturbance.
===Chinese===
郡警察局长直等他们走出了老远、离开宅子好一程，才开口解释。

“那架奥托风——想必你们也看出来了——不过是一具采集声音的话筒，外加一具双电子管放大器来扩音。传进话筒的声音，经电子管放大，放大到什么地步，尽凭你在限度之内怎么调。你若高兴，能让揉皱一张纸的声响听上去像一场小小的雷暴；它对咔嗒声那一类的声响尤其灵敏。方才在那镶木地板上，你不由自主地挪一挪脚，就搅出好大一阵动静。

===Original===
“Now in the older type of combination locks, if the dial was carefully manipulated, a person with sharp hearing might just be able to detect a faint click when a tumbler fell into place in the course of a circuit; and by making a note of the state of the dial corresponding to each click the combination could finally be discovered. In the modern patterns of locks this has been got round. They’ve introduced a thing called a balanced fence arbour, which is lifted away from the tumblers as soon as the lock spindle is revolved; so in this new pattern there’s no clicking such as the older locks give.”
===Chinese===
“再说老一式的转字密码锁：只要把字盘细细拨弄，耳朵尖的人兴许恰好能听出一声轻微的咔嗒——那是转片在盘转一圈的半道上落进了槽位；把每一声咔嗒对应的字盘刻度记下来，最终就能把密码摸个一清二楚。新式样的锁把这一着给破了。他们添了个叫做平衡制栓轴的物件，锁轴一转，它便从转片上抬起来；所以这新式样里，再没有老式锁那种咔嗒声了。”

===Original===
“I see now,” said the Inspector. “That’s an old pattern lock; and you were using the otophone to magnify the sound of the clicks?”

“Exactly,” Sir Clinton agreed. “It made the thing mere child’s play. Each click sounded like a whip-crack, almost.”

“So that’s why Foss brought the otophone along? He meant to pick the lock of the safe and get the medallions out of it?”

“That was one possibility, of course,” Sir Clinton said, with a grave face. “But I shouldn’t like to say that it was the only possibility.”
===Chinese===
“这我就明白了，”探长说。“那是一把老式锁；您是在用奥托风把咔嗒声放大了听？”

“一点不错，”克林顿爵士表示同意。“它把这桩事变成了小孩子的把戏。每一声咔嗒，差不多都响得像一记鞭梢。”

“这么说，福斯带上奥托风就是为了这个？他是想撬开保险柜的锁，把徽章取出来？”

“那自然是一种可能，”克林顿爵士神色凝重地说。“可我不愿断言，它就是唯一的可能。”

===Original===
He smoked for a few moments in silence, then he turned to Cecil.

“Now I’ve a piece of work for you to do; and I want you to do it convincingly. First thing tomorrow morning you’re to find some way of spreading the news that you’ve recovered all the genuine medallions and that they’re in the safe. Don’t give any details; but see that the yarn gets well abroad.”

“But all the real medallions are gone!” said Cecil in disgust. “And whoever’s got them must know they’re gone.”

“There’s nothing like a good authoritative lie for shaking confidence,” Sir Clinton observed, mildly. “That’s your share in the business. You’d better mention it at breakfast time to as many people as you can; and you can telephone the glad news to me, with the door of the telephone box open so that anyone can hear it. Yell as loud as you please, or louder if possible. It won’t hurt me at the other end. In any case, see that the happy tidings wash the most distant shores.”
===Chinese===
他默默吸了几口烟，随后转向塞西尔。

“眼下我有一桩差事交给你办；我要你办得叫人信服。明天一早头一件事，你要想个法子放出风声，就说真的徽章你已尽数追回，眼下都在保险柜里。细节一概不要讲；只管把这套说辞散播得远远的。”

“可真的徽章全没了呀！”塞西尔厌恶地说。“落在谁手里，谁自然知道它们没了。”

“要动摇人的信心，再没有什么比一个权威口气的好谎话更灵，”克林顿爵士温和地说。“这是你在这桩事里该出的一份力。你最好趁早餐时候，对尽可能多的人提上一句；还可以给我打电话报这个喜讯，把电话亭的门敞开着，让谁都听得见。你只管放声去嚷，能嚷多响就多响。电话那头伤不着我。总而言之，务必叫这喜讯冲刷到最远的海岸。”

===Original===
“Well, since you say so, I’ll do it. But it’s sure to be found out, you know, sooner or later.”

“All I want is a single day’s run of it. My impression is that, if things go well, I’ll have the whole Ravensthorpe affair cleared up by this time tomorrow. But I don’t promise that as a certainty.”

“And this yarn is part of your scheme?”

“I’m setting a trap,” Sir Clinton assured them. “And that lie is the bait I’m offering.”
===Chinese===
“好吧，既然您这么说，我就照办。不过您知道，这事儿迟早是要露馅的。”

“我要的不过是让它撑上一天工夫。依我的感觉，倘若诸事顺遂，到明天这个时辰，整个雷文索普的案子就能了结个干净。但这我可不敢打包票。”

“这套谎话也是您计策里的一环？”

“我在布一个陷阱，”克林顿爵士向他们担保。“那句谎话，就是我下的饵。”

===Original===
As they reached the car, he added:

“See that your constable doesn’t say a word about this affair tonight﻿—to anyone. That’s important, Inspector.”
===Chinese===
走到车前，他又添了一句：

“务必盯住你的警员，今晚对这桩事一个字也不说——对谁都不说。这很要紧，探长。”

## XIV The Second Chase in the Woods / 第十四章 林中第二度追捕

===Original===
“I’ve made all the necessary arrangements, sir,” Inspector Armadale reported to the Chief Constable on the following evening. “A dozen constables﻿—two with rubber-soled shoes﻿—and a couple of sergeants. They’re to be at the Ravensthorpe gate immediately it’s dark enough. The sergeants have the instructions; the constables don’t even know where they’re going when they leave here.”

“That’s correct,” Sir Clinton confirmed. “Let’s see. That’s fourteen altogether. Less two, twelve. Plus you and myself, fourteen. I think we’ll add to our number. Nothing like being on the safe side. Mr. Chacewater’s personally interested in the affair; I think we’ll take him in also. And Mr. Clifton might reasonably claim some share in the business. That makes sixteen. You’re detaching two constables to watch that lakelet. Well, surely fourteen of us ought to be able to pick up the scoundrel without difficulty.”
===Chinese===
“必要的部署我都安排妥帖了，先生，”第二天傍晚，阿玛代尔（Armadale）探长向郡警察局长（Chief Constable）汇报。“十二名警员——其中两名穿胶底鞋——外加两名警佐。天一黑透，他们就到雷文索普（Ravensthorpe）大门口集合。指令只交到警佐手里；警员们离开这儿时，连自己要上哪儿去都不知道。”

“这就对了，”克林顿爵士（Sir Clinton）认可道。“咱们算算：一共十四个。减掉两个，十二。加上你和我，又是十四。我看还得再往里添人。稳妥点总没坏处。蔡斯沃特（Chacewater）先生跟这桩事有切身利害；我想把他也带上。克利夫顿（Clifton）先生也满有理由来分上一份。这就十六个了。你又要拨两名警员去看住那座小湖。好吧，咱们十四个人，把那恶棍拿下来，总该不在话下。”

===Original===
“You’re sure that he’ll make for the terrace over the pool, sir?”

“Nothing’s sure in this world, Inspector. But I think there’s a fair chance that he’ll make in that direction. And if he doesn’t, why, then, we can run him down wherever he goes.”

“If he goes up there, we’ll have him,” the Inspector affirmed. “There’ll be no amateur bungling this time, like the last affair. I’ll see to that myself. He won’t slip through a constabulary cordon as he did when he’d only a lot of excited youngsters to deal with.”

“I leave that part of the business entirely in your hands, Inspector,” the Chief Constable assured him.
===Chinese===
“您拿得准他一定往池子上方那道露台跑，先生？”

“这世上就没有拿得准的事，探长。不过依我看，他往那个方向逃的机会不小。他若不走那边，也不要紧——他逃到哪儿，咱们就追到哪儿。”

“只要他上了那边，他就跑不掉了，”探长断言。“这回可不会再像上回那样尽是外行误事。这一点我亲自把关。上回他应付的不过是一群激动的毛头小子；这一回，他休想再从警察的封锁线里溜过去。”

“那一摊子事我就全权交给你了，探长，”郡警察局长向他担保。

===Original===
“What I can’t see,” the Inspector continued, with a faint querulousness in his tone, “is why you’re going about the thing in this elaborate way. Why not arrest him straight off and be done with it?”

“Because there’s one little party you’ve omitted to take into your calculations, Inspector﻿—and that’s the jury. Suspicion’s not good enough for us at this stage. Criminal trials aren’t conducted on romantic lines. Everything’s got to be proved up to the hilt. Frankly, in this case, you’ve been scattering your suspicions over a fairly wide field, haven’t you?”
===Chinese===
“我就是想不通，”探长接着说，语气里带着一丝埋怨，“您何苦把事情办得这么周章。何不干脆把他抓起来，一了百了？”

“因为有一位小小的人物，你盘算的时候给漏掉了，探长——那就是陪审团。到了这一步，光有嫌疑顶不了用。刑事审判可不按传奇小说的路数来办。每一件事都得证明得滴水不漏。老实说，这个案子里，你那怀疑撒的面也够宽的了，是不是？”

===Original===
“It’s our business to be suspicious of everybody,” the Inspector pleaded in extenuation.

“Oh, within limits, within limits, Inspector. You started by suspecting Foxton Polegate; then you branched off to Marden; after that you hovered a bit round Maurice Chacewater; and at the end you were hot on Cecil Chacewater’s heels. There’s too much of the smart reader of detective stories about that. He suspects about six of the characters without having any real proof at all; and then when the criminal turns up clearly in the last chapter he says: ‘Well, that fellow was on my list of suspects.’ That style of thing’s no use in real criminal work, where you’ve got to produce evidence and not merely some vague suspicions.”
===Chinese===
“把人人都疑个遍，本就是咱们的本行，”探长辩解道。

“哦，那也得有个限度，有个限度，探长。你起先疑的是福克斯顿·波尔盖特（Foxton Polegate）；接着岔到马登（Marden）身上；随后又在莫里斯·蔡斯沃特（Maurice Chacewater）周围盘桓了一阵；到了末了，又紧咬住塞西尔·蔡斯沃特不放。这里头，侦探小说聪明读者的气味太重了。那种读者疑遍六七个角色，手里却连一点真凭实据也没有；等到最后一章罪犯清清楚楚露了面，他还要说：『瞧，那家伙本来就在我的嫌疑人名单上。』这一套在真正的刑案工作里毫无用处——干这一行得拿出证据来，光有些含含糊糊的嫌疑可不行。”

===Original===
“You’re a bit hard, sir,” the Inspector protested.

“Well, you criticized my methods, remember. If I were to arrest the fellow just now, I doubt if I could convince a jury of his guilt. And they’d be quite right. It’s their business to be sceptical and insist on definite proof. It’s that proof that I expect to get out of tonight’s work.”
===Chinese===
“您这话未免太苛刻了，先生，”探长抗议道。

“可你还挑剔过我的办法呢，记得吧。这会儿我要是把那家伙抓起来，只怕我说不服陪审团定他的罪。而且他们那样想也完全对。抱定怀疑、非确凿证据不认，本就是他们的本分。我指望从今晚这场行动里拿到手的，正是那证据。”

===Original===
“It will be very instructive for me, sir,” Inspector Armadale commented, with heavy irony.

“You take things too seriously,” Sir Clinton retorted, with an evident double meaning in the phrase. “What you need, Inspector, is a touch of fantasy. You’ll get a taste of it tonight, perhaps, unless my calculations go far astray. Now I’m going to ring up Mr. Chacewater and make arrangements for tonight.”
===Chinese===
“这对我必定大有教益，先生，”阿玛代尔探长说道，讥讽沉甸甸地压在话里。

“你把事情看得太较真了，”克林顿爵士回敬道，这话明摆着一语双关。“探长，你缺的是一点儿奇想。除非我的盘算错得离谱，今晚你兴许就能尝上一口。现在我得给蔡斯沃特先生打个电话，把今晚的事安排安排。”

===Original===
And with that he dismissed the Inspector.

Armadale retired with a grave face; but when he closed the door behind him his expression changed considerably.
===Chinese===
说完这句，他便打发探长走了。

阿玛代尔绷着一张凝重的脸退了出去；可等他掩上身后的房门，脸色却变了不少。

===Original===
“There he was, pulling my leg again, confound him!” he reflected. “A touch of fantasy, indeed! What’s he getting at now? And the worst of it is I haven’t got to the bottom of the business yet myself. He’s been quite straight in giving me all the facts. I’m sure of that. But they seem to me just a jumble. They don’t fit together anyhow. And yet he’s not the bluffing kind; he’s got it all fixed up in his mind; I’m sure of that, whether he’s right or wrong. Well, we’ll see before many hours are over.”

And with reflections like these Inspector Armadale had to content himself until nightfall.
===Chinese===
“瞧他又在那儿拿我寻开心了，真该死！”他心里琢磨。“还『一点儿奇想』呢！他这回又打的什么主意？最糟的是，连我自己都还没摸到这案子的底。事实他倒全都痛痛快快给我了。这一点我有数。可那些事实在我看来就是一团乱麻，怎么也拼不拢。话虽如此，他可不是个爱虚张声势的人；这一整套在他脑子里早摆布停当了；对不对另说，这一点我也有数。好吧，不出几个钟头，就见分晓。”

就这样左思右想，阿玛代尔探长只得捱到天黑。

===Original===
As they drove up to the Ravensthorpe gates the Inspector found Sir Clinton in one of his uncommunicative moods. He seemed abstracted, and even, as the Inspector noted with faint malice, a little anxious about the business before them. When they reached the gates they found the constabulary squad awaiting them. Sir Clinton got out of the car, after running it a little way up the avenue.
===Chinese===
车往雷文索普大门开去的一路，探长发觉克林顿爵士又犯了他那副缄口不言的老脾气。他显得心不在焉，甚至——探长略带几分恶意地留意到——对眼前这桩差事还有几分悬心。到了大门口，只见警察小队早已候着。克林顿把车顺林荫道开上一小段，才停下车来。

===Original===
“Now, the first thing you’ve got to remember,” he said, addressing the squad, “is that in no circumstances are you to make the slightest noise until you hear my second whistle. You know what you’re to do? Get up behind the house at the end opposite to the servants’ wing and stay there till you get my signal. Then you’re to come out and chase the man whom the Inspector will show you. You’re not to try to catch him. Keep a hundred yards behind him all the time; but don’t lose sight of him. The Inspector will give you instructions after you’ve chased for a while. Now which of you are the two with tennis shoes?”

Two constables stepped out of the ranks. Sir Clinton took them aside and gave them some special instructions.

“Now, you’d better get to your places,” he said, turning to the squad again. “Remember, not a sound. I’m afraid you’ll have a long wait, but we must take things as they come.”
===Chinese===
“听着，你们头一件必须记住的事，”他对着小队说道，“就是在听见我第二声哨响之前，无论如何不许弄出半点声响。你们知道自己的差事吧？绕到宅子背后、仆人厢房对面的那一头待着，一直等到我的信号。然后就出来追那个人——探长会指给你们看。不许试着去捉他。始终吊在他身后一百码；可也别让他脱了视线。追过一阵之后，探长自会向你们布置。那两个穿网球鞋的是哪两位？”

两名警员从队列里跨了出来。克林顿爵士把他们拉到一旁，单独交代了几句。

“好了，你们最好这就各就各位，”他又转身对全队说。“记住，一个声响也不许出。恐怕要等上好一阵子，不过事到临头，也只有随机应变。”

===Original===
As the squad was led off into the night, he moved over to where the Inspector was standing.

“I want something out of the car,” he said. The Inspector followed him and waited while Sir Clinton switched off the headlights and the tail lamp. The Chief Constable felt in a locker and handed something to Armadale.

“A pair of night-glasses, Inspector. You’ll need them. And that’s the lot. We’d better get to our position. There’s no saying when the fellow may begin his work.”
===Chinese===
小队被引进夜色里之后，他踱到探长站着的地方。

“我要从车里拿点东西，”他说。探长跟着他，候在一旁，克林顿爵士熄了车头灯和尾灯。郡警察局长在贮物箱里摸索了一阵，递给阿玛代尔一样东西。

“一副夜用望远镜，探长。你用得着。要拿的就这些。咱们最好这就上岗位。那家伙几时动手，谁也说不准。”

===Original===
Rather to the mystification of the Inspector, Sir Clinton struck across the grass instead of following the avenue up to the house. After a fairly long walk they halted under a large tree.

“A touch of fantasy was what I recommended to you, Inspector. I think a little tree-climbing is indicated. Sling these glasses round your neck as I’m doing and follow on.”

“Quite mad!” was the Inspector’s involuntary comment to himself. “I suppose, once we get up there, he’ll come down again and tell me I needed exercise.”
===Chinese===
让探长颇为纳闷的是，克林顿爵士不走林荫道去宅子，却斜穿过草地。走了相当一程，他们在一棵大树底下收住了脚。

“我劝你的就是那么一点儿奇想，探长。依我看，眼下正该来一点爬树。照我的样子，把望远镜挂上脖子，跟着来。”

“简直是疯了！”探长不由得在心里嘀咕。“我料着等咱们一爬上去，他准又要下来，对我说我缺的是运动。”

===Original===
He followed the Chief Constable, however; and was at last directed to a branch on which he could find a safe seat.

“Think I’m demented, Inspector?” Sir Clinton demanded with the accuracy of a thought-reader. “It’s not quite so bad as that, you’ll be glad to hear. Turn your glasses through that rift in the leaves. I was at special pains to cut it yesterday evening, in preparation for you. What do you see?”
===Chinese===
话虽如此，他还是跟着郡警察局长上去了；末了被指到一根树枝上，寻了个坐得安稳的位置。

“是不是当我疯癫了，探长？”克林顿爵士问道，那准头简直像会读心术。“幸而没有糟到那个地步，你听了可以宽心。把望远镜对准树叶间那道豁口。昨天傍晚我特意费了番手脚裁出来，就是给你预备的。你看见什么了？”

===Original===
The Inspector focused his glasses and scanned the scene visible through the fissure in the foliage.

“The front of Ravensthorpe,” he answered.

“Some windows?”

“Yes.”

“Well, one of them’s the window of the museum; and this happens to be one of the few points from which you can see right into the room. If the lights were on there, you’d find that we’re looking squarely on to the door of the safe.”
===Chinese===
探长调好焦距，扫视枝叶缝隙间望得见的景象。

“雷文索普的正面，”他答道。

“看见几扇窗子？”

“看见了。”

“好，其中一扇就是博物馆的窗；而这里恰好是仅有的几个位置之一，从这儿能把那间屋子看个通透。要是那边亮着灯，你就会发现，咱们正正对着保险柜的柜门。”

===Original===
With this help the Inspector was able to pick out the window which evidently he was expected to watch.

“It’ll be a slow business,” Sir Clinton said in a bored tone. “But one of us has got to keep an eye on that window for the next hour or two at least. We can take it in turn.”

They settled down to their vigil, which proved to be a prolonged one. The Inspector found his perch upon the branch anything but comfortable; and it grew more wearisome as the time slipped past.
===Chinese===
有了这个提示，探长认出了那扇显然要他盯住的窗子。

“这会是桩磨工夫的活儿，”克林顿爵士用一种腻烦的口气说。“可接下来至少一两个钟头，咱们总得有一双眼睛盯着那扇窗。可以轮班来。”

他们安顿下来守候，这一守竟守了很久。探长发觉树枝上那个栖身处怎么也说不上舒服；时间一点点溜过去，越发难捱。

===Original===
“Fantasy!” he commented bitterly to himself as he shifted his position for the twentieth time. “Cramp’s more likely.”

But at last their tenacity was rewarded. It was during one of the Inspector’s spells of watching. Suddenly the dark rectangle of the window flashed into momentary illumination and faded again.
===Chinese===
“奇想！”他第二十次挪动身子时，狠狠地自嘲了一句。“想的怕是抽筋哟。”

可他们的坚韧到头来终有报偿。那正是探长轮值守望的一班。忽然，窗子那一方黑黢黢的矩形闪过一瞬亮光，随即又暗了下去。

===Original===
“There he is!” exclaimed the Inspector. “He’s carrying a flash-lamp.”

Sir Clinton lifted his glasses and examined the place in his turn.

“I can see him moving about in the room,” the Inspector reported excitedly. “Now he’s going over towards the safe. Can you see him, sir?”

“Fairly well. What do you make of him?”
===Chinese===
“他在那儿！”探长失声道。“他带着手电筒。”

克林顿爵士举起望远镜，轮到自己朝那处察看了一番。

“我看见他在屋里走动，”探长兴奋地报告。“这会儿他朝保险柜过去了。您看得见他吗，先生？”

“看得还算清楚。你看他是个什么样人？”

===Original===
The Inspector studied his quarry intently for a while.

“That’s the otophone, isn’t it, sir? I can’t see his face; it seems as if he’d blackened it.﻿ ﻿… No, he’s wearing a big mask. It looks like﻿ ﻿…”

His voice rose sharply.

“It’s Marden! I recognize that waterproof of his; I could swear to it anywhere.”
===Chinese===
探长凝神盯了他的猎物好一阵。

“那不是奥托风（Otophone）吗，先生？我看不清他的脸；像是把脸涂黑了。……不对，他戴着一个大面罩。瞧那样子，像……”

他的嗓音陡然拔高。

“是马登！我认出他身上那件雨衣了；到哪儿我都认得出，可以起誓。”

===Original===
“That’s quite correct, Inspector. Now I think we’ll get down from this tree as quick as we can and I’ll blow my whistle. That ought to startle him. And I’ve arranged for that to be the signal for a considerable amount of noise in the house, which ought to give the effect we want.”

He slipped lightly down the branches, waited for the slower-moving Inspector, and then blew a single shrill blast on his whistle.

“That’s roused them,” he said, with satisfaction, as some lights flashed up in windows on the front of Ravensthorpe. “I guess that amount of stir about the place will flush our friend without any trouble.”
===Chinese===
“一点不错，探长。现在咱们得赶快从这棵树上下去，我来吹哨。那一声准会吓他一跳。我还安排好了，让它作信号，叫宅子里大大闹动一场，好收到咱们要的效果。”

他轻快地溜下树枝，等动作迟缓的探长也落了地，随后吹出一声尖利的哨音。

“这一下把他们惊起来了，”他说，颇为满意——这时雷文索普正面的几扇窗里灯光骤然亮起。“宅子里有这么一番动静，咱们那位朋友保管一惊就窜，毫不费事。”

===Original===
He gazed through his glasses at the main door.

“There he goes, Inspector!”

A dark figure emerged suddenly on the threshold, hesitated for a moment, and then ran down the steps. Armadale instinctively started forward; but the cool voice of the Chief Constable recalled him.

“There’s no hurry, Inspector! You’d better hang your glasses on the tree here. They’ll only hamper you in running.”

Hurriedly the Inspector obeyed; and Sir Clinton leisurely hung up his own pair. Armadale turned again and followed the burglar with his eyes.
===Chinese===
他举着望远镜盯住正门。

“他出来了，探长！”

一条黑影倏地出现在门槛上，迟疑了片刻，随即奔下台阶。阿玛代尔本能地抢步要追；郡警察局长冷静的嗓音把他唤了回来。

“用不着忙，探长！你最好把望远镜挂在这棵树上。跑起来它只会碍事。”

探长匆匆照办；克林顿爵士也不慌不忙把自己那副挂好。阿玛代尔重又转过身，用目光追住那窃贼。

===Original===
“He’s making for the old quarry, sir.”

“So I see,” Sir Clinton assured him. “I want the fellow to have a good start, remember. I don’t wish him to be pressed. Now we may as well get the chase organized.”

Followed by the Inspector, he hurried towards the front of Ravensthorpe.

“I think that’s a fair start to give him,” he estimated aloud. Then, lifting his whistle, he blew a second blast.
===Chinese===
“他奔老采石场去了，先生。”

“我也瞧见了，”克林顿爵士应道。“记住，我要那家伙占足先机。我不愿意他被人逼得太紧。现在咱们不妨把这场追捕组织起来。”

探长跟着他，快步朝雷文索普的正面走去。

“我看，让他占这样一个先机也算公道，”他出声估量道。随即举起哨子，吹响了第二声。

===Original===
Almost immediately the figures of Cecil Chacewater and Michael Clifton emerged from the main door, while a few seconds later the police squad rounded the corner of the house.

“Carry on, Inspector!” Sir Clinton advised. “I leave the rest of the roundup to you. But keep exactly to what I told you.”

Armadale hurried off, and within a few seconds the chase had been set afoot.
===Chinese===
几乎紧接着，塞西尔·蔡斯沃特（Cecil Chacewater）和迈克尔·克利夫顿（Michael Clifton）的身影便从正门里出来，几秒钟后，警察小队也绕过了宅子的墙角。

“接着办吧，探长！”克林顿爵士叮嘱道。“围捕余下的事都交给你。可务必严格照我说的办。”

阿玛代尔匆匆去了，不出几秒钟，追捕便已发动。

===Original===
“We must see if we can wipe your eye this time, Mr. Clifton,” the Chief Constable observed. “It’s a run over the old ground, you notice.”

Michael Clifton nodded in answer.

“If you’d let me run him down I’d be obliged to you,” he suggested. “You’ve given him a longish start, certainly; but I think I could pull him in.”

Sir Clinton made a gesture of dissent.

“Oh, no. We must give him a run for his money. Besides, it wouldn’t suit my book to have him run down too early in the game.”
===Chinese===
“克利夫顿先生，这一回咱们得瞧瞧，能不能让你扳回一城，”郡警察局长说。“你留意到没有，这一趟跑的还是老地界。”

迈克尔·克利夫顿点头作答。

“您若肯让我去追他，我就感激不尽了，”他提议道。“您放他的先机确实长了些；不过我想，我还能把他追回来。”

克林顿爵士做了个不同意的手势。

“哦，不行。总得让他这一场跑得值回票价。再说，这出戏里让他落网落得太早，也不合我的盘算。”

===Original===
The fugitive had reached the edge of the pinewood as they were speaking, and now he disappeared from their sight among the arcades of the trees.

“The moon will be down in no time,” Cecil pointed out as they ran. “Aren’t you taking the risk of losing him up in the woods there? It’ll be pretty dark under the trees.”

He quickened his pace slightly in his eagerness; but the Chief Constable restrained him.

“Leave it to Armadale. It’s his affair. We’re only spectators, really.”

“I want the beggar caught,” Cecil grumbled, but he obeyed Sir Clinton’s orders and slowed down slightly.
===Chinese===
他们说话这工夫，那逃亡者已经到了松林边上，转眼便消失在树木的重重拱廊之间，不见了踪影。

“月亮眼看就要落下去了，”塞西尔一边跑一边指出。“您这不是在冒跟丢他的风险吗？树林底下可要黑得厉害。”

他求成心切，脚下略略加快；郡警察局长却按住了他。

“交给阿玛代尔。那是他的事。说到底，咱们不过是看客。”

“我要那家伙被逮住，”塞西尔咕哝道，但他还是遵从克林顿爵士的吩咐，把脚步放慢了些。

===Original===
A few seconds brought them to the fringe of the wood; and far ahead of them they could see the form of the burglar running steadily up the track.

“Just the same as before?” Sir Clinton demanded from Michael.

“Just the same.”

Through the wood they went behind the police squad. At the brow of the hill, where the trees began to thin, Armadale called a halt. They could hear him giving orders for the formation of his cordon. When his men began to move off under his directions the Inspector came over to Sir Clinton.
===Chinese===
几秒钟后他们到了林边；远远望去，只见那窃贼的身影正沿着小径不紧不慢地往上跑。

“跟上一回一个样？”克林顿爵士向迈克尔问道。

“一个样。”

他们跟着警察小队穿林而行。到了山脊上、树木渐稀的地方，阿玛代尔下令停止前进。他们听得见他布置封锁队形的号令。等他手下的人按调度散开之后，探长便走到克林顿爵士跟前。

===Original===
“He’ll not slip through our hands this time, sir. I’ll beat every bit of cover in that spinney. He can’t get away on either side without being spotted. We’ll get our hands on him in a few minutes now. I suppose he’s armed?”

Sir Clinton shook his head.

“I should doubt that.”

The Inspector failed to conceal his surprise.

“Not armed? He’s sure to be.”

“We’ll see in a minute or two,” the Chief Constable answered. “You’d better get your beaters to work, hadn’t you?﻿ ﻿… Ah!”
===Chinese===
“这一回他休想再从咱们手里溜走，先生。那片小树林里，每一处藏身的地方我都要趟一遍。他往两边哪一侧窜，都躲不过眼睛。不出几分钟，咱们就能按住他。我猜他带着家伙？”

克林顿爵士摇了摇头。

“我看未必。”

探长掩饰不住诧异。

“没带家伙？他准定带了。”

“一两分钟内便见分晓，”郡警察局长答道。“你最好叫你的围捕手开工了，是不是？……啊！”

===Original===
In the silence they heard the sound of a faint splash from the direction of the quarry.

“History’s repeating itself pretty accurately, isn’t it?” said Sir Clinton, turning to Michael. “That’s the kind of thing you heard the other night?”

“Just the same,” Michael admitted.
===Chinese===
一片静默之中，他们听见采石场那边传来一声轻微的水响。

“历史正在一板一眼地重演，是不是？”克林顿爵士说着，转向迈克尔。“那晚你听见的，就是这一类声响吧？”

“一模一样，”迈克尔承认。

===Original===
But as the line of constables moved forward he could not help contrasting their methodical work with the rather haphazard doings of the pursuers on the earlier occasion. Armadale had evidently issued stringent orders, for not a tuft of undergrowth was left unexamined as the line slowly closed in upon the hunted man. Every possible piece of cover was scrutinized and beaten before the cordon passed beyond it.
===Chinese===
可当警员们的横列向前推进时，他不禁拿这番有条不紊的功夫，同上回追捕者们那阵乱无章法的忙乱作起了比较。阿玛代尔显然下过严令：横列缓缓向被追的人收拢，沿途连一丛一簇的灌木都不放过。每一处可能的藏身之地，都要在封锁线越过之前细细查过、趟过。

===Original===
“Very pretty,” Sir Clinton commented, as they moved up in the rear of the line. “The Inspector must surely have been training these fellows. They really do the business excellently.”

Michael suddenly left the path they were following and stepped across under the trees.

“I’m going to have a look at that Fairy House myself,” he declared. “That’s where I found Maurice after the last show. I want to be perfectly certain that it’s empty.”

He opened the door, leaned inside the building, and then came back to his companions. Something like disappointment was visible in his expression. He was taken aback to see glances of sardonic amusement exchanged between Cecil and the Chief Constable.
===Chinese===
“很漂亮，”克林顿爵士评论道，这时他们跟在横列后面推进。“探长必定操练过这班人。活儿干得实在地道。”

迈克尔忽然离开他们走的小径，跨进树下去了。

“我要亲自去那座仙女屋（Fairy House）看一眼，”他声明。“上一场收场之后，我就是在那儿找到莫里斯的。我要十足确准那屋里没人。”

他推开门，探身进那座小屋看了看，随后回到同伴身边。他脸上隐隐透出几分近似失望的神色。却见塞西尔和郡警察局长交换着眼色，彼此一副揶揄而乐的模样，他不由得一怔。

===Original===
“Drawn blank, have you?” Cecil inquired.

“There’s no one there at present,” Michael admitted.

“I don’t think the constables would have missed a plain thing like that,” Sir Clinton remarked mildly, though with a faint undertone of correction in his voice.
===Chinese===
“扑了个空，是吧？”塞西尔问道。

“这会儿屋里没人，”迈克尔承认。

“那么显眼的一样物事，我想警员们总不至于漏掉，”克林顿爵士温和地说，话音里却带着一丝纠正的底味。

===Original===
Before Michael had time to reply they heard Armadale’s voice. The cordon had passed completely through the spinney and was now on the edge of the marble terrace.

“Come along,” Sir Clinton urged. “We mustn’t miss the final scene.”

They hurriedly joined the line just as Armadale ordered a last advance.

“He’s somewhere on this terrace,” he told his men. “See that he doesn’t break away from you at the last moment.”
===Chinese===
迈克尔还来不及答话，他们就听见了阿玛代尔的声音。封锁线已经完全穿过那片小树林，此刻正抵着大理石露台的边沿。

“快来，”克林顿爵士催促道。“压轴的一幕可不能错过。”

他们急忙赶上横列，恰好赶上阿玛代尔下令最后推进。

“他就在这露台上的什么地方，”他对手下人说。“务必别让他在最后一刻从你们手里脱身。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton turned to Michael.

“Just the same as before?”

Michael made a gesture of assent.

“I’ll admit that this is more businesslike.”

The Constabulary line crept forward almost foot by foot, subjecting everyone of the marble seats to the most rigid scrutiny. Inspector Armadale’s anxiety was more and more apparent as the cordon advanced without securing the man for whom they were searching. At last the whole of the possible cover had been beaten, and the constables emerged on the open terrace. The fugitive had vanished, apparently, into thin air.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士转向迈克尔。

“跟上一回一个样？”

迈克尔做了个肯定的手势。

“我承认，这一回更像办事的样子。”

警察的横列几乎是逐尺逐寸地向前蹭，把每一张大理石座椅都置于最严格的检视之下。封锁线步步推进，却始终拿不到搜索的目标，阿玛代尔探长的焦灼愈来愈挂在脸上。最后，所有可能的藏身之处都趟遍了，警员们涌上了空阔的露台。那逃亡者，分明是凭空消失了。

===Original===
Michael Clifton turned to the Chief Constable with an ironical smile.

“*Just* the same as last time, it seems. How history repeats itself!”

The Inspector hurried across the terrace to where they were standing. It was obvious that he was completely staggered by the turn of events.

“He’s got away, sir,” he reported in a mortified voice. “I can’t think how he’s managed it.”
===Chinese===
迈克尔·克利夫顿带着讥诮的笑，转向郡警察局长。

“看来跟上回『就是』一个样。历史多么会重演啊！”

探长匆匆穿过露台，走到他们站立的地方。事态这一转折，显然把他彻底震懵了。

“他跑掉了，先生，”他懊恼地报告。“我想不出他是怎么做到的。”

===Original===
“I think we’ll repeat that last stage again, Inspector, if you don’t mind. Withdraw your men till they’re just in front of that last line of seats.”

While the Inspector was giving his orders Sir Clinton pulled his case from his pocket, opened it, and thoughtfully tapped a cigarette on the lid. Before lighting it he threw a glance up and down the empty spaces of the terrace from which the fugitive had so mysteriously vanished.
===Chinese===
“你要是不介意，探长，我想咱们把最后那一节再演一遍。把你的人撤回来，撤到最后一排座椅的正前方为止。”

探长下令的当口，克林顿爵士从口袋里掏出烟盒，打开，若有所思地拿一支烟在盒盖上磕了磕。点火之前，他把空荡荡的露台上上下下扫了一眼——那逃亡者正是从这片空场里如此神秘地消失的。

===Original===
“All plain and above board, isn’t it?” he said, turning to his two companions. “I’ve got nothing in my hands except a cigarette, and you can search my sleeves if you like. It is required, as Euclid would say, to produce a full-sized burglar for the satisfaction of the audience. It’s a stiff job.”

He glanced again over the wide white pavement of the terrace.

“A conjurer’s usually allowed a little patter, isn’t he? The quickness of the tongue distracts the eye, and all that. Just a question, then. Do you happen to remember what Medusa was able to do? Turned things into stone when she looked at them, didn’t she? That somehow brings the late Pygmalion to my mind﻿—a kind of association of opposites, in a way, I suppose. But I’ve often wondered what Pygmalion felt like when the statue came to life.”
===Chinese===
“一切光明磊落，是不是？”他说着，转向两位同伴。“我手里除了一支烟什么也没有，你们愿意，还可以搜搜我的袖子。现在——照欧几里得的口气说——须再造出一个原尺寸的窃贼，以餍观众之望。这是桩棘手的活儿。”

他又扫了一眼露台宽阔的白色石面。

“变戏法的照例总要许他说几句垫话，是不是？舌头一快，眼就花了，诸如此类。那么，问一个小问题。你可记得美杜莎（Medusa）有什么本事？她看谁一眼，谁就变成了石头，是不是？不知怎么，这倒叫我想起已故的皮格马利翁（Pygmalion）——我想，在某种意义上，这是一种相反相成的联想。可我一直纳闷，雕像活过来的时候，皮格马利翁心里是什么滋味。”

===Original===
He turned sharply on his heel.

“You can come down off that pedestal, my friend. The game’s up!”

To the amazement of the group around him, the white marble statue above him started suddenly into life. It leapt down from its base on to the pavement of the terrace, staggered as it alighted, and then, as Cecil and Michael grasped at its smooth sides, it shook itself clear and sprang upon the broad marble balustrade.

“Come back, you fool!” Sir Clinton snapped, as the figure faced outward to the gulf below.

But instead of halting, the white form gathered itself together for an instant and then dived headlong into the abyss. There was the sound of a splash; and an appalling cry came up through the night.
===Chinese===
他猛地一转身。

“你可以从那底座上下来了，我的朋友。戏收场了！”

他周围这一群人惊骇莫名：头顶上那尊白色大理石像骤然活了过来。它从基座上一跃而下，跳上露台的铺石，落地时一个趔趄；随即——塞西尔和迈克尔抓向它光滑的躯体两侧——它挣脱开来，纵身跳上了宽宽的大理石栏杆。

“回来，你这蠢货！”克林顿爵士厉声喝道，这时那身影已正对着下方的深渊。

可那白色的身影非但没有停住，反而凝身一蓄，随即头朝下扎进了深渊。一声水响；一声骇人的惨呼穿透夜色传了上来。

===Original===
Sir Clinton dashed to the rail.

“Below, there! Get out on that raft at once and pick him up. He’s badly hurt. He’ll drown if you don’t hurry.”

The Inspector hurried forward.

“Why didn’t you warn us, sir? We’d have had Marden as easy as anything. If you’d only told us what to expect.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士抢步扑向栏杆。

“下面的！赶紧上木筏，把人捞起来。他伤得很重。你们不快点儿，他就要淹死了。”

探长抢上前来。

“您为什么不预先告诉我们，先生？不然拿住马登还不容易。您只要早说一声，让我们有个防备。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton looked round.

“Marden? That’s not Marden. I tell you, Inspector, if that jump of his meant anything, it suggests that there’s no Marden at all.”

The Inspector’s amazement overbore his chagrin.

“I don’t understand﻿ ﻿…” he began.

“Never mind. I’ll explain later. Get away down to the waterside at once. See if he’s badly damaged. Quick, now.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士环顾四周。

“马登？那不是马登。我告诉你，探长，倘若他那一跳说明了什么，它说明的是：压根儿就没有什么马登。”

探长的惊异压过了懊恼。

“我不明白……”他开口道。

“先别管这个。回头我再解释。立刻下到水边去。看看他伤得重不重。快去。”

===Original===
As the Inspector hurried off, the Chief Constable turned to Michael Clifton.

“History doesn’t always repeat itself exactly, you see.”

He pulled out a matchbox and lit his cigarette in a leisurely fashion. Then, throwing away the vesta, he inquired:

“You see now how he got away from you last time?”
===Chinese===
探长匆匆去了，郡警察局长转向迈克尔·克利夫顿。

“你瞧，历史并不回回都分毫不差地重演。”

他掏出火柴盒，从容不迫地点燃香烟。随后，把火柴杆一扔，他问道：

“现在明白上一回他是怎么从你们手里逃脱的了？”

===Original===
Michael made no reply. He was examining the pedestal from which the living statue had taken its flight; and he could see the scores and cuts left by the chisel which had smoothed the standing-place of the original marble figure. Quite obviously, on the night of the masked ball, the same trick had been played; and while the pursuers were searching all around, the fugitive had stood rigid above them, unsuspected by anyone.
===Chinese===
迈克尔没有作声。他正察看那尊活雕像腾身而去的底座；他看得出，凿子削平底座上原有石像立足之处时，留下了道道凿痕切口。明摆着，假面舞会那一晚玩的就是同一套把戏；追捕的人四下搜寻的时候，逃亡者却僵直地立在他们的头顶上，无一人起疑。

===Original===
Cecil turned to the Chief Constable.

“Aren’t you going down to see if something can’t be done for the poor devil? He must have come a fearful smash on the rocks.”

“Poor devil?” Sir Clinton retorted. “That’s not a poor devil. That’s a wild beast, if you’re anxious for information. But if you’re a member of the Society for the Prevention of Cruelty to Animals, I suppose we’d better see that things are done decently and in order. We’ll go down, if you’re perturbed about him.”

It took them some little time to descend to the level of the lakelet. They could see, as they went down, the process of rescue; and when they reached the waterside, they found two constables stooping over a limp white figure, beside which the Inspector knelt solicitously. As the newcomers approached, Armadale rose and stepped over to them.
===Chinese===
塞西尔转向郡警察局长。

“您不下去看看，能不能给那可怜的家伙想点办法吗？他摔在岩石上，准摔得不成样子了。”

“可怜的家伙？”克林顿爵士回敬道。“那可不是什么可怜的家伙。那是一头野兽——如果你急着打听的话。不过，你若是防止虐待动物协会的会员，我想咱们最好还是把事情办得体体面面、有条有理。你要是不放心他，咱们就下去。”

他们下到小湖那一层高程，费了些工夫。一路下去，营救的经过都望得见；到得水边，只见两名警员俯身对着一个瘫软的白色身影，探长正关切地跪在旁边。见来人走近，阿玛代尔起身，迈步迎了过去。

===Original===
“He’s done for, sir,” he reported in a low voice to Sir Clinton. “His pelvis is smashed and I think his spine must have gone as well. He’s paralysed below the waist. I doubt if he’ll last long. It was a fearful smash.”

Cecil crossed over and peered down at the face of the dying man. For a moment he failed to recognize him; for the white greasepaint disguised the natural appearance of the features: but a closer scrutiny revealed the identity of the living statue.

“Why, it’s the chauffeur!”

“Of course,” was all that Sir Clinton thought it worth while to say.
===Chinese===
“他完了，先生，”他低声向克林顿爵士报告。“骨盆摔碎了，我看脊椎也断了。腰部以下都瘫了。只怕撑不了多久。摔得怕人。”

塞西尔走过去，俯身端详那垂死之人的脸。一时间他没有认出来；白色的油彩遮没了五官本来的模样：可再细看，这尊活雕像是谁便见分晓了。

“咦，是那个司机！”

“可不，”克林顿爵士觉得，说这一句就尽够了。

===Original===
Armadale brought something up from the waterside.

“Here’s the waterproof he was wearing, sir. It’s Marden’s, just as I told you when I saw him in the museum tonight. When he flung it over the edge of the cliff as we were coming up, it landed on a broad bit of rock instead of sinking like the Pierrot costume, the other night.”

Sir Clinton was silent for a moment. His glance wandered to the broken, white-clad figure on the ground, but no pity showed on his face. Then he turned back to Armadale.

“See if you can get a confession out of him, Inspector. He won’t live long at the best; and he might as well tell what he can. We can’t hang him now, unfortunately; and he may as well save us some trouble in piecing things together. For one thing, he’s got a bag or a suitcase lying around somewhere in the neighbourhood with a suit of clothes in it. You’d better find out where that is, and save us the bother of hunting for it. If you manage to get anything out of him, take it down and get it witnessed. Bring it down to Ravensthorpe at once.”
===Chinese===
阿玛代尔从水边拿起一样东西走来。

“这就是他身上穿的那件雨衣，先生。是马登的，正像我今晚在博物馆里看见他时所说。咱们上来的时候，他把它从崖边甩了下去；它落在一块宽岩石上，没像那晚的皮埃罗（Pierrot）戏装那样沉了底。”

克林顿爵士沉默了片刻。他的目光游移到地上那具残破的白衣身影上，脸上却不见一丝怜悯。随后他转回身，对着阿玛代尔。

“探长，看看你能不能让他说出点口供。往最好里说他活不长；他有什么能说的，也不妨都说了。可惜如今咱们没法绞死他了；他也正好省得咱们拼凑案情多费手脚。就说一件事：他在附近什么地方藏着一只提包或小行李箱，里头有一套衣裳。你最好问出它在哪儿，省得咱们四处去找。你若当真能问出什么来，记下来，找人见证。随即把口供带到雷文索普来。”

===Original===
He paused, then added as if by an afterthought:

“You’d better search these tights that he’s wearing. There ought to be five of the medallions concealed about him somewhere. Get them for me.”

He turned to Cecil and Michael.

“We’ll go back now to Ravensthorpe. Unless I’m far astray in my deductions, there’s been another murder there; and we must keep the girls from hearing about it, if we can.”
===Chinese===
他顿了顿，又像临时想起似的补了一句：

“他身上那套紧身衣，你们最好搜一搜。五枚徽章该就藏在他身上什么地方。给我取来。”

他转向塞西尔和迈克尔。

“咱们现在就回雷文索普去。倘若我的推论没有大错，那里又出了一桩命案；咱们得尽量别让姑娘们听见风声。”

===Original===
As they walked through the pinewood, Sir Clinton maintained a complete taciturnity, and neither of the others cared to break in on his silence. His last words had shown that ahead of them might lie yet another of the Ravensthorpe tragedies, and the shadow of it lay across their minds. It was not until they were approaching the house that the Chief Constable spoke again.
===Chinese===
穿过松林的一路，克林顿爵士始终一言不发，另外两人也不愿去打破他的沉默。他末了那句话表明，他们前头可能还横着又一桩雷文索普的惨剧，那片阴影压在他们心头。直到快到宅子的时候，郡警察局长才又开了口。

===Original===
“You’ve spun that yarn I gave you to the girls?”

“They know there was some stunt afoot,” said Cecil, “but they were to keep out of the way, in their rooms, until we were clear of the house.”

“One had to tell them something,” Sir Clinton answered. “If one hadn’t, they’d have been pretty uncomfortable when all that racket started. You managed to scare him out very neatly with the row you raised when I blew my whistle.”

“The girls are sitting up, waiting for us,” Cecil explained. “They said they’d have coffee ready when we came back.”

“The deuce they did!”

Sir Clinton was obviously put out.
===Chinese===
“我交给你的那套说辞，你讲给姑娘们听了？”

“她们知道有什么行动在进行，”塞西尔说，“不过已经说好，她们要避在各自房里，等咱们离开宅子再说。”

“总得跟她们说点什么，”克林顿爵士答道。“不然那一通喧腾起来，她们非吓得不轻。我吹哨之后你们掀起的那阵吵嚷，把他惊出去惊得干净利落。”

“姑娘们都熬着夜等咱们呢，”塞西尔解释道。“她们说好了，等咱们回来，咖啡就备下。”

“这可真是见鬼！”

克林顿爵士明显给弄得不痛快了。

===Original===
“I’d been counting on their going back to bed again. Then we could have got Marden’s body away quietly﻿—if he’s been murdered, as I think he has. There’s no use upsetting people if you can avoid it. Ravensthorpe’s had its fill of sensations lately and there’s no need to add another tonight.”

He reflected as he walked on, and at last he seemed to hit on an expedient to suit the circumstances.

“The bottom’s out of this case now,” he said, at last. “There’ll be no trial; so there’s no need for any more secrecy, so far as I can see. I’ll be giving nothing away that I shouldn’t, at this stage of the game.”

He threw away the end of his cigarette and looked up at the bulk of Ravensthorpe before them. Here and there on the dark front the yellow oblong of a window shone out in the night.

“Suppose I spin them a yarn,” Sir Clinton went on. “I can keep them up until dawn with it. After that, they’ll sleep sound enough; and while they’re asleep, we’ll get Marden’s body away in peace and comfort. It’ll spare them the shock of finding another corpse on the premises; and that’s always something gained.”
===Chinese===
“我原指望她们会回房去睡。那样，咱们就能悄悄把马登的尸体运走——如果他真像我料想的那样遭了毒手。能不叫人受惊，就不叫人受惊。雷文索普近来出的乱子也受够了，今晚犯不着再添一桩。”

他一路走一路盘算，末了似乎想出了一个应付眼下局面的办法。

“这案子如今已经见底了，”他终于说道。“不会有审判了；所以依我看，也不必再保密。事到如今这个地步，我不会泄露什么不该泄露的了。”

他把烟头一扔，抬头望向面前雷文索普那黑黢黢的巨大轮廓。幽暗的正面，东一处西一处，几扇窗子黄色的长方块在夜色里亮着。

“不如我来给她们编套故事，”克林顿爵士接着说。“我能拿它把她们一直留到天亮。过后她们会睡得很沉；趁她们睡着，咱们就从从容容、安安稳稳把马登的尸体运走。这也省得她们在宅院里又撞见一具尸首、受一场惊吓；这总归是落着的好处。”

===Original===
When they reached Ravensthorpe, Sir Clinton turned to Cecil.

“You’d better go and close the safe in the museum. No use leaving things like that open any longer than’s necessary. I must go up to Marden’s room now. I’ll be back again in a minute or two.”
===Chinese===
到了雷文索普，克林顿爵士转向塞西尔。

“你最好去把博物馆的保险柜关上。这类东西开着，能不多开一刻就不多开一刻。我现在得上马登的房间去一趟。一两分钟就回来。”

===Original===
Ascending the servants’ staircase, Sir Clinton made his way to the valet’s room. The door was locked; but when Sir Clinton tapped gently, a constable opened it and looked out. At the sight of the Chief Constable, he stood aside.

“He’s been murdered, sir,” the man explained in a whisper.

“I guessed it might be that,” Sir Clinton returned.
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士登上仆人用的楼梯，径直走向贴身男仆的房间。门锁着；可克林顿爵士轻轻一叩，一名警员开了门，探头外望。一见是郡警察局长，他便闪身让开。

“他被人谋害了，先生，”那人低声解释。

“我料着多半是这样，”克林顿爵士答道。

===Original===
“Whoever did it must have chloroformed him first,” the constable went on. “There was a pad of cotton-wool over his face; and his throat’s cut.”

The Chief Constable nodded in comprehension.

“That would prevent any sounds,” he said. “Brackley was a first-class planner, there’s no doubt.”
===Chinese===
“下手的人必定先用氯仿把他迷住了，”警员接着说。“他脸上还捂着一团药棉；喉咙割断了。”

郡警察局长会意地点了点头。

“这样就不会出一点声响，”他说。“布拉克利（Brackley）是个一流的谋划家，这毫无疑问。”

===Original===
The constable continued his explanation.

“We came up here as you told us, sir; and when we heard your whistle we slipped into the room, expecting to arrest him according to your orders. But he was dead by that time. It was quite clear that he’d been murdered only a short time before. Your orders didn’t cover the case, so we thought the best thing to do was to lock the door and wait till you came back. You’d said we were to keep him here till your return, anyhow; so that seemed to be the best course.”

“Quite correct,” Sir Clinton commended them. “You couldn’t have done better. Now you’ll need to wait here till morning. Keep the door locked, and don’t let any word of this affair get abroad. I’ll see about removing the body in due course. Until then, I don’t want any alarm on the subject.”

He stepped across the room, examined the body on the bed, and then, with a nod to the constables, he went downstairs once more.
===Chinese===
警员继续说明。

“我们照您的吩咐上这儿来了，先生；听见您的哨声，就溜进了房间，指望按您的命令逮捕他。可那时他已经死了。看得出，他遇害还是不久前的事。您的命令没料到这种情形，我们便想着，最好的办法是锁上门，等您回来。反正您说过，无论如何要把他留在这儿等您回来；这么做似乎最为妥当。”

“完全正确，”克林顿爵士夸奖他们。“你们办得再好不过了。现在你们得在这儿守到天亮。门保持锁上，这桩事一个字也不许外泄。搬走尸首的事，我会适时安排。在那之前，我不希望此事引起任何惊动。”

他跨过房间，验看床上那具尸体，然后朝警员们点了点头，再次下楼去了。

## XV Sir Clinton's Solution / 第十五章 克林顿爵士的解答

===Original===
“It’s a pleasure to meet Sir Clinton again,” Joan observed when they had finished their coffee. “For the last ten days or so, I’ve been dealing with a man they call the Chief Constable. I don’t much care for him. These beetle-browed officials are not my sort. Too stiff and overbearing for me, altogether.”
===Chinese===
“又见到克林顿爵士本人，真是一桩乐事，”喝完咖啡，琼说道。“这十来天，我一直同一位他们称作郡警察局长的人物打交道。我不大喜欢他。这号眉头紧锁的官老爷不合我的胃口。总的说来，对我是又生硬，又霸道。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton laughed at the hit.

“Sorry,” he said. “I’ve invited one of your aversions to join us. In fact, I think I hear him at the door now.”

“Inspector Armadale?” Joan demanded. “Well, I’ve nothing against him. You never let him get a word in edgeways at our interviews. Grasping, I call it.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士被这一击逗笑了。

“抱歉，”他说。“我请了你讨厌的人之一来加入我们。说起来，我想这会儿他已经到门口了。”

“阿玛代尔探长？”琼问。“唔，我跟他无冤无仇。只是咱们几回问话，你从不让他插上半句话。我管这叫独揽一切。”

===Original===
The door opened and the Inspector was ushered in. As he entered, a glance passed between him and Sir Clinton. In reply, Armadale made a furtive gesture which escaped the rest of the company.

“Passed in his checks,” Sir Clinton interpreted it to himself. “That clears the road.”
===Chinese===
门开了，探长被引进屋来。他进门时，同克林顿爵士交换了一个眼色。作为回应，阿玛代尔做了一个隐秘的手势，旁人都没有察觉。

“筹码交割了，”克林顿爵士心里如此解读，“这一来，路上就清净了。”

===Original===
Joan poured out coffee for the Inspector and then turned to the Chief Constable.

“Cecil promised that you’d tell us all about everything. Don’t linger over it. We’re all in quite good listening form and we look to you not to be boring. Proceed.”
===Chinese===
琼给探长倒上咖啡，随后转向郡警察局长。

“塞西尔答应过，你会把一切原原本本讲给我们听。别拖拖拉拉。我们都处于极佳的听讲状态，就指望你别讲得乏味。开始吧。”

===Original===
Sir Clinton refused to be disconcerted.

“Inspector Armadale’s the last authority on the subject,” he remarked. “He’s got the confession of the master mind in his pocket. I haven’t seen it yet. Suppose I give you my account of things, and the Inspector will check it for us where necessary? That seems a fair division of labour.”

“Very fair,” Una Rainhill put in. “Now, Joan, be quiet and let’s get on with the tale.”
===Chinese===
克林顿爵士偏不肯乱了方寸。

“在这个题目上，阿玛代尔探长才是最高的权威，”他说。“主谋的供词就揣在他口袋里。我还没有拜读过。不如由我把事情讲一遍，需要之处请探长替我们核对核对？这分工听起来还算公道。”

“公道得很，”尤娜·雷恩希尔插话道。“好了，琼，安静些，让故事讲下去。”

===Original===
“Before the curtain goes up,” Sir Clinton suggested, “you’d better read your programmes. First of all you find the name of Thomas Pailton, alias Cocoa Tom, alias, J. B. Foss, alias The Wizard of Woz: a retired conjurer, gaolbird, confidence-trick sharp, etc. As I read his psychology, he was rather a weak character and not over straight even in dealing with his equals. In the present play, he was acting under the orders of a gentleman of much tougher fibre.
===Chinese===
